# Expo HAS CHANGED

Read the exact versioned docs at https://docs.expo.dev/versions/v56.0.0/ before writing any code.

# Cruise FM — project context

A premium driving-companion app (React Native / Expo SDK 56, Expo Router). Users bring their own Spotify playlists; Cruise FM wraps them in a cinematic driving experience — mood stations, full-screen visual modes, atmosphere. "Spotify organises by artist/genre; Cruise FM organises by how a drive feels."

The owner does not code — describe changes in plain English, Claude implements everything. Keep explanations non-technical.

## Product decisions (fixed)
- Monetisation (locked 2026-07-22): £1.99/mo + £14.99/yr annual ("save 37%"), 7-day free trial; optional launch-week Founder lifetime £24.99 one-time. Launch FREE first, payments as fast-follow update. (RevenueCat SDK wired: sandbox test_ key in config.ts, entitlement "premium", safe no-op on web/old builds via src/utils/purchases.ts; real store products + keys still pending)
- FREE tier: Cassette + Equalizer modes, basic playback, limited custom stations (3), badges
- PREMIUM: Vinyl + Retro Radio modes, all mood themes, unlimited playlists, future additions
- Never premium: offline listening, badges, founder cosmetics, seasonal themes
- Live song titles are Spotify-only (decided 2026-07-23): companion-mode listeners (YouTube Music etc.) see the station's mood tagline — Google exposes no now-playing API. Possible future: Android media-notification reader (native module, fresh build, Android-only); iOS has no equivalent.
- `OWNER_MODE` in src/constants/config.ts bypasses all locks during development — set FALSE for launch (2026-07-23). `LAUNCH_FREE = true` (same file) is the public launch state: everyone premium, no locks/paywall, Sentry live; flip LAUNCH_FREE off only when real store products + platform RevenueCat keys ship (payments fast-follow)

## Architecture map
- `src/app/(tabs)/` — cruise (home w/ one-tap Start Drive), stations, modes, profile
- `src/app/premium.tsx` — amber paywall screen; `src/app/auth.tsx` — Spotify OAuth redirect handler (cruisefm://auth)
- `src/components/{Equalizer,Cassette,Vinyl,RetroRadio}Mode.tsx` — full-screen visual modes; all four share the same shell: full-bleed station image (blurRadius 3.5, `imageStyle={{width:'100%',height:'100%'}}` — REQUIRED, web falls back to intrinsic size without it), 5-stop dark gradient capped ~0.58, glow band tinted from station eqColors, "PLAYING FROM" header, bottom-left song title, white 6px progress bar, white 80px play circle (custom 8x30 pause bars), MaterialCommunityIcons skip-previous/next 48
- `src/components/StationDetailModal.tsx` — station page: full-bleed image, Add your playlist (Spotify picker), mode picker, Start Drive
- `src/constants/stations.ts` — 8 stations; each has cardGradient (muted card preview), eqColors (equalizer bar palette), iconName (white MCI icon)
- `src/utils/spotify.ts` — OAuth PKCE + playback API; `useSpotifyPlayback.ts` — live play/pause/skip + 5s now-playing poll used by all modes; `stationPlaylists.ts` — per-station linked playlist (AsyncStorage); `lastCruise.ts` — resume logic
- `src/components/DriveCheckCard.tsx` — "Are you driving?" honesty check: 45 min of untouched playback → card; ignored for 2 min → drive clock pauses (driveStats suspend/resume) until the next playback touch. Playback controls signal life via activityPing in NowPlayingContext
- `website/index.html` — self-contained waitlist landing page (Formspree form ID still a placeholder)
- Credentials in `.env` (EXPO_PUBLIC_SPOTIFY_*) — gitignored, copy manually between machines

## Gotchas learned the hard way
- Never define background JSX as an inline component inside render — new identity each render remounts the blurred image (visible twitching). Use a plain JSX const.
- Station images: assets/stations/*.jpg, must be ≥ ~1100px on the short side or web renders them at intrinsic size (bottom cut-off). Compress to ≤ ~600KB, JPEG only (no .avif/.webp/.png — bundler/perf). Source from Unsplash/Pexels, never Pinterest.
- Expo Go is a dead end (SDK 54 vs our 56 + no custom deep links). Android testing = EAS dev build; iOS on Windows blocked (needs paid Apple acct) — use Safari at the LAN IP for visuals, or the iOS Simulator on a Mac.
- Spotify playback API needs the user's Spotify app active on some device, and Premium.
- Spotify quota policy (confirmed 2026-07-18): extension requests are ORGANIZATIONS-ONLY since May 2025 and require an established business w/ 250k+ MAU — unreachable at launch. Dev-mode allowlist confirmed at 5 users (+ the owner account) — reserve slots for genuine music-testing testers; everyone else runs demo mode. Before public launch, build the no-API fallback: Start Drive deep-links the linked playlist into the Spotify app (spotify: URI, no auth needed) for non-allowlisted users; Cruise FM stays the visual layer.
- STANDING RULE (owner, 20.08, after two weeks of real fixes sitting unreleased): pushes to this branch auto-publish to PREVIEW only — that's the owner's phone and TestFlight testers, never the public. Production is deliberately manual, so it never moves the app under real users without someone choosing to. BUT THAT MEANS IT IS EASY TO FORGET, and it was — the production channel went two weeks without a publish while preview kept moving, so nobody with the app from the App Store received any of that work. THE FIX IS A HABIT, NOT A CODE CHANGE: after a run of fixes has been proven out on preview (the owner's phone, TestFlight), ASK/REMIND WHETHER TO PUBLISH TO PRODUCTION — don't wait to be asked. Sensible moments to raise it: the end of a working session, after several commits have landed, or whenever the owner asks "did this go out to everyone". Still respect the existing guards before doing it — never publish to production while a build is in App Store review, and always let the workflow's own "is a real build listening on this runtime" check run rather than skipping it.
- STANDING RULE (owner, 20.08, same conversation): "please do inform me of the updates whenever we create new changes for the app" — after any change, summarise plainly what changed and why, in the same non-technical terms as everywhere else in this file. Not a silent commit-and-move-on. This applies whether the change is a bug fix, a new feature, or ops work like a build/publish — the owner should never have to ask "what did you just do" or discover something only by noticing it on her phone.

## Current state / next steps
- THE KEYBOARD FIX WAS ONLY HALF-APPLIED, AND THE MOOD/MODE SHEETS WERE THE LAST DARK-ONLY SCREENS 2026-08-19 (OTA), owner on 1.3.1 with two screenshots. (1) "I'm trying to type to create my station but the tab keeps missing" — THE SAME REPORT AS 19.08 EARLIER, and build 32 does carry that fix, so the fix was wrong rather than missing. `sheet` got `flexShrink: 1`, but the `ScrollView` inside it had NO STYLE — and in React Native a ScrollView takes its CONTENT height and will not shrink unless told to, so that height became a floor the sheet could not go under; being bottom-anchored, the overflow went off the TOP and took the name field with it. Both levels have to be allowed to shrink, not just the outer one; the comment already claimed "the ScrollView inside then scrolls instead of pushing the top off", which was the intent and not the code. RULE: `flexShrink` on a container does nothing if its scrolling child cannot shrink too.
  WHY THE HARNESS PASSED BOTH WAYS, now written into scripts/harness/inputs.mjs: `KeyboardAvoidingView` is `Platform.OS === 'ios' ? 'padding' : undefined`, so on web it is INERT, and react-native-web resolves flex through CSS where a scroller shrinks without being asked. That file is evidence about contrast and gross layout only — it cannot prove a sheet survives a real keyboard, and it reported "ok" on the broken build. Shipped on the mechanism plus the device report, like the 03.08 gestureTrap.
- THE CREATE-STATION TYPE BAR, THIRD ROUND — `KeyboardAvoidingView` ITSELF WAS THE REMAINING SUSPECT 2026-08-20 (OTA), owner: "fix the bug that's still there with create station type bar." Checked FIRST, before touching any code, that the 19.08 ScrollView fix had genuinely reached her — it did: the push publish and a manual dispatch both completed successfully within minutes of the commit, so this is not the OTA-drought shape (30.07/02.08/13.08) again, it's a second real cause under the same symptom.
  `KeyboardAvoidingView`'s `behavior="padding"` works by measuring ITS OWN FRAME via `onLayout` and computing how much bottom padding to add from that. This sheet's copy of it sat inside a `Modal` (its own native window) wrapping an `Animated.View` that is mid-transform while it slides in — either one is a believable way for that internal measurement to land on a stale or wrong number, and it's a well-known class of flakiness for this exact component in exactly this situation (Modal + animated ancestor). None of that can be proven from a browser, which has no soft keyboard at all — inputs.mjs's squeezed-viewport test can only ever exercise the flexShrink chain, never KeyboardAvoidingView's own frame math, which is precisely the mechanism now suspected.
  FIX: `KeyboardAvoidingView` is gone from this sheet. In its place, a plain `Keyboard.addListener('keyboardWillShow'/'keyboardWillHide', ...)` effect reads the keyboard's own reported height and applies it directly as `marginBottom` on the sheet. Keyboard notifications are OS-level events — they reach every listener regardless of which window or transform sits between the input and the app root, so this sidesteps the measurement question entirely rather than trusting a component with a known-flaky history here. `flexShrink` on the sheet and its ScrollView (already fixed 19.08) is what lets the sheet actually give up that height instead of overflowing off the top, same as before — this round changes ONLY where the keyboard height number comes from.
  VERIFIED: tsc clean, inputs.mjs still green in both themes across all three surfaces, the 15-step health walk green, the contrast sweep clean (only the five known Stations-hero false positives), preflight 10/1(uncommitted)/0. NOT verified end-to-end on a real keyboard — nothing run from here can be, on either version of this fix — so this is shipped on the mechanism (a documented RN/Modal interaction, not a guess) plus removing the specific fragile dependency, the same standard this file already holds itself to for gestures no browser can drive. Owner is testing this round on her phone before deciding whether to publish it to production.
- WIDGETS, ROUND 1 — THE HALF THAT IS NOT SWIFT 2026-08-21 (OTA; owner: "i have limited credit at the moment, so build the first few features today"). The widgets themselves are a native extension and cannot ship over the air, but the two pieces they depend on both live in JS, can be tested here, and are worth having in place before any build is cut — the same JS-first order Apple Music was built in (29.07).
  (1) `src/utils/widgetData.ts` — the snapshot a widget reads. A widget cannot run our JavaScript (WidgetKit wakes it as its own process), so everything it shows has to be written down in advance in a shared App Group container; this is the app's side of that, and the Swift only ever reads. THE TIMELINE IS THE WHOLE TRICK, and it is what answers the owner's "can the CD animate" question honestly: iOS budgets a widget to a handful of refreshes a day, so polling is unavailable to everyone, but WidgetKit accepts a TIMELINE of future entries and renders them ITSELF with the app not running. Cruise FM already owns a real broadcast schedule, so the next 24h of changeovers are knowable in advance — `buildOnAirTimeline` walks hour by hour (the windows are whole hours, so an hourly step cannot miss a boundary) and collapses runs, leaving one entry per real change. The widget then genuinely changes through the day while never animating or waking us.
  COLOURS, NOT PHOTOGRAPHS, and it is a real constraint rather than a shortcut: a widget extension cannot read the app's bundled assets, and a custom station's photo lives in documentDirectory, outside the shared container. Pictures mean copying files into the App Group at save time — a later round. The Modes page is gradient glass anyway, so a coloured tile with the dial number and the station's icon is the house language.
  `scripts/test-widget-data.mjs` transpiles the shipped module AND the real schedule.ts rather than stubbing it, because the one claim worth testing is that the timeline never names a station that is not on air — checked at every hour of a whole week. A widget is read at a glance and believed; same honesty rule the notification budget is held to. Also covers the 13.08 kind split (a desk listener is counted in SESSIONS, never shown a drive count of zero), the cold-phone case, and that publishing is a safe no-op with no bridge. ONE ASSERTION OF MINE WAS WRONG AND THE CODE WAS RIGHT: "the last entry is 24h out" fails because changeovers are COLLAPSED, so the final change can land mid-evening and stay current to the end — the honest property is that the entry still current at +24h names the right station.
  (2) `src/app/drive.tsx` + `src/utils/driveRequest.ts` — where a widget tap lands (`cruisefm://drive?station=&mode=`). A ROUTE, not a Linking listener, because Expo Router already turns an incoming URL into one (auth.tsx has done the Spotify redirect this way since July). Nothing from the URL is trusted: the station is resolved and compared by id, the mode goes through `knownMode`, so a stale link (a station deleted since the widget drew it) falls back to a real drive rather than dead-ending.
  THE BUG THAT COST THE ROUND, and it is a structural fact worth remembering: **`NowPlayingHost` — which renders the fullscreen deck — is mounted in `src/app/(tabs)/_layout.tsx`, NOT at the root.** A deep link is a root route, so when a tap cold-starts the app into `/drive` the host does not exist yet: calling `np.open()` there produced a REAL session with the right station and no deck on screen at all (measured — the home header said NOW PLAYING · Sunset AM over the home page). Reordering open/navigate did not help, because the host is absent either way. FIX is the createStationRequest pattern: the link records what was asked for, hands over to the home tab, and cruise.tsx consumes it on focus — inside the tabs, where the host exists. A module flag rather than a route param for the documented reason (a param STICKS and would start a drive out of nowhere on a later visit); reading it takes it. Consumed through `npRef` because the focus callback is memoised.
  THE PROBE WAS WRONG TWICE BEFORE THE CODE WAS, and both are this file's standing lessons in new clothes. First run read ALL leaf text and reported `inMode=true` for every case — including the mode name — because Expo Router keeps every tab mounted, so those words exist off-screen in trees nobody can touch (the 19.08 phantom-text trap); filtering to reachable + on-screen flipped it to a real failure. Then it reported `deck=false` on a deck that WAS open, because every mode RESTS after ~6 untouched seconds and fades its chrome to zero opacity, which the reachability filter correctly rejects — a screenshot settled it in one look. A wake tap at (40,118) is the documented fix. VERIFIED end to end in the web build on five links: sunset+vinyl, night-run+disco, coastal+cd (exact mode match), a stale link, and a bare link — right station and right deck every time, zero page errors, plus screenshots of the Vinyl and Mirror Ball decks.
  WHAT IS LEFT FOR THE BUILD: the Swift. A `CruiseWidgets` Expo module exposing `setSnapshot(json)` (writes the App Group + `WidgetCenter.reloadAllTimelines()`), the WidgetKit extension itself, and an App Group entitlement — which preflight will fail until it is added to `preflight-allow.json`'s `entitlements` list, deliberately, so nobody adds a capability the provisioning profile lacks without noticing (builds 15 and 24). Deliberately NOT wired into app.json yet: the house rule is that native changes are batched at build time, and adding an entitlement now would fail preflight and block the OTA pipeline currently carrying the keyboard fix.

CONSEQUENCE OF THE SELF-STARTING CLOCK, which is correct but surprising: an install that predates this feature has `firstSeenAt` set the moment the update lands, so even a heavy existing user waits a day before being asked. Better a day late than asking the instant an update installs.

Full build-time checklist in `docs/launch/widgets-setup.md` — follow that rather than this entry when cutting the build.

Switching it on adds an App Group entitlement, which preflight fails BY DESIGN (builds 15 and 24 both died at signing over an entitlement the profile did not carry), and that would block the OTA pipeline in the meantime. The wiring is four lines and they are listed in the doc, including adding the entitlement to `preflight-allow.json` KNOWINGLY — that list is the record of what the profile is expected to carry, so it must only move when the Apple-side capability actually has.
  ALREADY TRUE AND WORTH KNOWING: `modules/cruise-widgets/` **autolinks** (verified with `npx expo-modules-autolinking search --platform apple`, which lists it beside cruise-music-kit), so the bridge compiles into the NEXT BUILD OF ANY KIND even without the target. Benign — `widgetsAvailable()` goes true, `setSnapshot` returns false with no App Group, nothing reads it and nothing throws — but it means the native side has quietly changed and preflight's package-lock check cannot see it.
  THE ICON PROBLEM, solved on the JS side rather than in Swift: a widget draws a CHARACTER, and stations store a MaterialCommunityIcons glyph NAME. `glyphChar()` resolves it against the glyphmap we already ship and the snapshot carries `iconChar`, so Swift never needs a second copy of that table. An unknown name gives '' — nothing drawn, rather than the hollow box a bad codepoint produces.
  NEW `scripts/test-widget-contract.mjs`, and it is the one worth keeping: the snapshot leaves TypeScript as JSON and arrives in Swift as a Codable struct, and Swift's decoder is ALL OR NOTHING — one required property missing and the whole decode returns nil, blanking every widget at once with nothing logged anywhere. Neither side knows about the other, so nothing catches a rename. The test reads the property names straight out of the shipped Swift, checks them against a snapshot built by the shipped TypeScript, and also asserts the App Group string and storage key MATCH ACROSS ALL THREE FILES (app module, extension, target config) — if those disagree the widgets read an empty container for ever with no error, the worst way for this to fail. It caught a real one on its first run: the app sends `icon` and the struct never declared it. Harmless at runtime (Swift ignores unknown keys) but it is the exact shape of a half-finished rename, so `icon` is now declared optional. RULE: a field added to the snapshot must be added to Snapshot.swift AS AN OPTIONAL — a widget binary already on a phone gets handed newer snapshots by an app that updated over the air.
  WHAT IT CANNOT TELL YOU: none of this has been compiled. Swift cannot be built from here, so this is written to the same standard as the MusicKit module was (03.08) — a documented contract, checked where it is checkable, and proven only when a build actually runs. The first build is where the Swift gets its real test.
  STILL NOT BUILT: the Live Activity (item 5), which is the one that also reaches CarPlay. Its open question is unchanged and is recorded in the entry below.
- WIDGETS/LIVE ACTIVITIES ADDED TO THE NATIVE BUILD QUEUE, RIGHT AFTER APPLE MUSIC 2026-08-20 (planning only, no code yet — this is a NEXT FRESH BUILD item, not OTA): owner asked about Lock Screen and Home Screen widgets, and specifically a spinning-CD-while-playing idea. Researched properly before answering rather than guessing at current iOS capability (WebSearch, sources in the reply to her). THE HONEST LIMIT: a Home Screen widget (WidgetKit) is refresh-budgeted by iOS to roughly every 15-60 minutes and the ONLY thing allowed to animate on its own is a countdown-style timer text — no continuous spin, ever, for any app, not just us. THE RIGHT TOOL for "feels alive while driving" is a **Live Activity** (ActivityKit) — the Lock Screen banner + Dynamic Island, which DOES support a limited, deliberate animation (a pulse/breathe reads fine; pushing updates faster than its own animation cycle causes visual jitter per Apple's own guidance, so no literal spin there either — the app's existing "breathing glow" language is the honest fit, not a rotating disc).
  THE FIND WORTH BUILDING FOR: a Live Activity now shows up AUTOMATICALLY on the CarPlay Dashboard once it exists on iPhone — no separate CarPlay app or entitlement work needed beyond the Live Activity itself. For a driving app that is the single best-fit surface available: one build gets Cruise FM onto the Lock Screen, the Dynamic Island, AND the car's own screen. Confirm this still holds (and re-check whatever iOS/CarPlay version is current) at BUILD TIME, not from this note — it was true as searched 20.08 but platform behaviour is exactly the kind of thing that's worth re-verifying right before cutting the build, not trusted from a session weeks earlier.
  BUILD ORDER, slotted in AFTER Apple Music (shipped) on the NEXT FRESH BUILD list: (1) Home Screen "Start Drive" tile — last station's art, deep-links straight into it, static; (2) Home Screen "On Air Now" tile — reads the existing broadcast schedule (schedule.ts), so its CONTENT changes through the day even though the widget itself never animates, which is the cheap way to make a static widget feel like a living radio; (3) Home Screen streak/stats tile, mirroring Profile's own strip; (4) Lock Screen widget — dial number in the app's own DSEG gauge font, one tap to start a drive; (5) Live Activity for an active drive — station, mode, a drive timer (native ticking text, no polling needed), the breathing-glow treatment, tap to reopen; this is the one that also lights up CarPlay.
  OPEN QUESTION FLAGGED, NOT YET ANSWERED: how reliably the Live Activity can be kept in step with the ACTUAL song as it changes. Cruise FM doesn't play audio itself (Spotify/Apple Music does), and since 04.08 Apple Music playback deliberately uses the SYSTEM player specifically so music survives the app being backgrounded/suspended — which means the app process may not be running to push a live update every time the song changes. A remote push (APNs) could keep it live from outside, but that needs a server, which contradicts the "nothing leaves the device, no Cruise FM server" privacy claim this app has held to throughout. The safe, buildable-now scope is a Live Activity for "drive in progress" (station + mode + timer, updated whenever the app itself is foregrounded) rather than a song-by-song live tracker; song-level live sync is a real open question to solve AT BUILD TIME, not assumed away here. Same mechanism as MusicKit: a small native Swift target(s) added via a config plugin (WidgetKit extension + ActivityKit), EAS cloud-buildable, no Mac required.
  (2) MOOD AND MODE SHEETS THEMED — `StationSheet` and `ModeSheet` had no palette at all, so on paper they came up as black slabs over a light page (owner: "can the tab that lets me choose the station, change to light mode?"). Both now themed, dark side byte-identical. Two things are NOT plain tokenisation because they were tuned against black: the ACTIVE mode chip INVERTS (a solid fill in the opposite of the page — a white pill on near-white paper is not a button, the same call the create sheet's save button makes), and StationSheet's accent marks go through `readableOn` because they come from the STATION rather than the palette, so a near-white station would otherwise vanish as a mark on a pale row. Verified by screenshot in both themes; the mode's own scene stays dark in both, which is the 31.07 Daylight decision and correct.
- THE MIRROR BALL COULDN'T BE TAPPED, AND ITS SWIPE WAS FIGHTING THE WAKE 2026-08-19 (OTA) — owner: "the mirror ball just keeps animating when i swipe across it. It doesn't pause when you tap on it either." BOTH REAL, one cause each, and the second was MY OWN MISS. `ballPan` declined the touch on START (`onStartShouldSetPanResponder: () => false`) and claimed only a clearly sideways MOVE, so (1) a TAP was never seen at all — the record and the disc both toggle play, making this the one deck you couldn't tap to pause — and (2) the root sniffer woke the chrome on ANY touch, and waking slides the scene back to its awake position UNDER THE FINGER, so the ball moved relative to the thumb and read as animating on its own. That second one is exactly the fault fixed for Vinyl and CD on 18.08 (deferred `requestWake` + `cancelWake` once the deck claims the touch); the ball was never given it. FIX: the responder claims on start and classifies like the disc — within ~35° of straight down drives `slideY` and dismisses on the same 120px/0.8 thresholds, anything else winds, under `TAP_SLOP = 12`px of travel is a tap. `scrub.begin()` moved OUT of grant and into the wind branch, because `scrub.end()` ALWAYS seeks and a tap must never pass through it (the CD lesson). A tap while RESTED only wakes, never toggles (the 18.08 rule: waking a deck must not pause the song). Rest countdown is held while winding and re-armed on release.
  A REGRESSION THE REST HARNESS CAUGHT, and it is the stale-closure trap in a new disguise: `requestWake`'s `setTimeout` captured the `wakeChrome` of the render that SCHEDULED it, so tap-to-pause re-armed the rest countdown believing the drive was still playing — Mirror Ball came back `paused 0 rested 1`, i.e. the controls faded out on someone who had just paused. The deferred wake now calls through `wakeChromeRef`, so the order of the timeout and the re-render stops mattering. RULE: anything deferred past the current tick must read its state through a ref.
  NEW HARNESS `scripts/harness/ball-touch.mjs` — tap toggles play, swipe turns the ball, pull-down still dismisses (that last one is the regression guard: claiming on start is exactly how a mode loses its dismiss). MEASUREMENT NOTE, two failed attempts before the third worked, both failing the same way — by assuming some part of the picture is quiet. Reading the elapsed time is VACUOUS here (no Spotify in the web build, so no track, no seek bar: it read `null -> null`). A pixel diff of the ball's box catches the glitter, fireflies and light rays, each on its own clock, so "held still" can never be true. Reading the full-size layers' opacities catches the colour reflections and spotlight as well as the six flipbook frames — nine layers, not six. What works is SIGNAL AGAINST NOISE over equal intervals: the ball changed 4.6-9.8x more while being dragged than while left alone. There is no still thing on this screen to compare against; compare like for like instead.
- "GO UPDATE FROM THE APP STORE" CARD 2026-08-19 (OTA): owner, after being told what OTA can and can't reach — "some app require you to go to the App Store and update from there". New `src/components/AppStoreUpdateCard.tsx` + `src/utils/appStoreUpdate.ts`, home page, above AlreadyPlayingCard. THE GAP IT CLOSES: OTA (expo-updates) can only carry JS and assets onto a binary ALREADY installed — it cannot put a new binary on anyone's phone, which is exactly the case that matters whenever a release changes something native (Apple Music, a permission, a module) and someone on an old binary has no way back into step without a fresh App Store download. NO SERVER, matching the privacy story: it asks Apple's own public listing (`itunes.apple.com/lookup?bundleId=...`) what version is live, the same public catalogue endpoint appleArtwork.ts already calls for album covers — "an anonymous, routine version check" is the whole of what leaves the device. Compares version segment-by-segment (`isNewer`, so "1.3.10" doesn't lose to "1.3.9" under a string compare), CACHES the check for 12h so it isn't a network call every launch, and DISMISSAL REMEMBERS THE VERSION rather than "never ask again" — so a driver who dismisses 1.3.1 still gets asked about 1.3.2 the day that ships. Tapping the card opens the App Store page via Linking; the card renders nothing on any failure (offline, blocked, malformed response) — same fail-quiet contract as the artwork lookup. NOT a replacement for AutoUpdateHost (14.08) — that one silently ships JS/asset updates to an EXISTING binary; this one is for the case OTA structurally cannot reach. THIS DOES NOT PUSH A NOTIFICATION WITHOUT THE APP BEING OPENED — that would need either a server (reverses the "nothing leaves the device" privacy claim) or a background-task scheduler (a native module not yet installed, so a fresh build) to check on a timer with the app closed; both were explained to the owner and deliberately deferred. Verified offline (`scripts/test-app-store-update.mjs`, transpiles the shipped module against a fake AsyncStorage/fetch — version compare, cache hit-once, dismissal-by-version, offline-fails-quiet) and visually in the real web build with a temporary stubbed return (reverted, confirmed by diff) — card renders cleanly above the mood-question card in both themes, doesn't crowd it. Health walk green with the real (blocked-from-here) network call caught and swallowed as designed.

Default release behaviour = check-on-launch, apply-on-next-launch (no runtime code added). REQUIRES ONE FRESH BUILD before OTA works — current installed builds lack the expo-updates native module and will NOT receive updates. Owner runs on their machine: (1) `eas build -p android --profile preview` once to bake in expo-updates; after that, JS/asset-only changes ship via `eas update --branch preview --environment preview -m "<note>"` (minutes, no store, no reinstall). Fingerprint policy auto-blocks OTA when native deps change → that's the signal a new build is needed, not an update. TRAP (bitten 2026-07-23): `eas.json` is a fingerprint source — ANY edit to it silently changes the runtime version, so existing builds stop receiving updates. Treat eas.json/app.json/package.json edits as "fresh build required" events; after such an edit, cut a new testflight-profile build for the owner's phone so preview OTA keeps flowing. **SUPERSEDED 2026-07-30** — the fingerprint policy is gone (it had started disagreeing with EAS's own build-time fingerprint, see the pinned-runtime entry below); everything in this entry about config files silently changing the runtime version no longer applies.

- DONE 2026-07-24 (late night, all OTA): new "Disco Ball" visual mode (owner's idea) — `src/components/DiscoBallMode.tsx`, premium, registered in modeCatalog/NowPlayingHost/Modes tab (icon `mirror-variant` — `disco-ball` glyph doesn't exist in MaterialCommunityIcons). Chunky geodesic cel-shaded mirror tiles (seam-tiling scroll trick for a true axis-spin with zero seam), station-mood colour-cycling wash (uses each station's own eqColors, not a fixed palette), two prominent on-ball sparkle stars + an orbiting light field, gentle hang/float. Iterated twice against owner references (video style match) before landing. Also: Vinyl mode fix — the album label wasn't actually desynced from the disc (both share one rotation value, mathematically impossible to drift), it just LOOKED slower because the disc's surface was almost pure concentric circles with no visual rotation cue; added small asymmetric scuff/fleck texture marks so the disc's spin reads as clearly as the label's, plus grew the album-art label ~12-13% (both fullscreen deck and preview card).
- DONE 2026-07-24 (Disco Ball premium visualizer pass, OTA): kept the stylized cel-shaded ball (not photoreal) but raised production value — beveled tiles (offset shadow + bright inner rim per facet), four independent neon cyan/magenta/purple/blue reflection streaks drifting across the surface (own speed/phase each, layered above the mood-colour wash), bloom halo bleeding outside the ball's own clip (layered-gradient trick, no real blur filter available), volumetric light shafts + floating bokeh particles in the room, a whisper-quiet lens flare, spin slowed 9s→14s/rev (reads as a real hanging ball, not a toy spinning fast). Glow/reflections now nudge gently on the beat (same simulated ~100BPM pattern as AmbientGlow, gated on real playback). All native-driver opacity/transform only, no new dependencies — normal OTA.
- DONE 2026-07-25 (Disco Ball round 4 — owner reference photo, OTA): the facets read as a flat brick wall because the old surface was a flat texture strip scrolling behind a circular mask — no perspective baked in at all. Rebuilt as a GENUINE SPHERE PROJECTION: each mirror is a quad between two latitudes/longitudes, projected through the sphere with a ~17° downward viewing tilt and back-face culled, so rows compress toward the poles, columns converge at the top pole, facets squash to slivers at the silhouette. Per-facet Lambert shading off each tile's own normal, quantised into a ramp (stylised, not photoreal) + a minority of mirrors given a neon cast. ~240 facets (ROWS 17 × COLS 32 after culling), built once, NEVER animated. KEY INSIGHT: the grid is now static and the light moves behind it — cheaper AND truer, since on a real mirror ball the grid barely appears to move; what travels is the light across the facets (scrolling patch layer beneath, carved into mirrors by the grid's seams). Also: ball turns ONLY while audio genuinely plays and coasts to a stop when it stops (resumes from where it left off — `spin` must stay 0..1 or it scrolls off its own two-copy strip, so every target is clamped); spin 14s→9.5s/rev; the two 4-point sparkle stars REMOVED (owner: "doesn't enhance the style"); room sparkle reworked into star dust (fine glitter, hairline cross-flares, densest around the ball, coarser particle layers dialled back to make room); specular highlight softened from a hard ellipse to a gradient (it was sitting on the ball like a painted blob and hiding the mirrors).
- DONE 2026-07-25 (OTA): BOTTOM-SEAM BUG FIXED (owner spotted a faint cut-off line near the home indicator "on almost every screen") — AmbientGlow's `ag.wrap` had `overflow:'hidden'` with `top: SCREEN_H*0.34 + height: SCREEN_H*0.62`, i.e. a hard clip at EXACTLY 0.96×screen height; the hazes breathe up to scale 1.18, so the scaled-up smoke was sliced against that edge into a straight horizontal line. Present in all 8 visual modes, hence "everywhere". Fixed by running the layer to `bottom: 0` with NO clip (smoke fades out on its own gradient; the screen edge is the only clip wanted) — comment left in the file, never re-add overflow:hidden there. Also: Recommended cards (StationCard CompactCard, used only by the home strip) restyled from a full-strength accent gradient slab to smoked glass — `rgba(15,17,29,0.55)` base + accent palette at 0.42 opacity, hairline rim 0.14, softer/deeper shadow (0.32/26) instead of the neon halo; variants rendered and compared before picking.
- DONE 2026-07-25 (Disco Ball round 5 — owner feedback + reference clip of a REAL mirror ball, OTA): bob REMOVED (hangs dead still now); turn 9.5s→7s/rev and REVERSED so the surface travels right — that direction is load-bearing, it makes "drag right = scrub forward" agree with the playback direction instead of fighting it; SWIPE-TO-SCRUB on the ball (like vinyl): surface tracks the finger 1:1, a ball-width of drag = 1/5 of the song, release seeks Spotify, only horizontal drags claimed so a downward swipe still dismisses, auto-turn suspends while a finger is down and resumes from the angle left behind. Facet CONTRAST raised so rotation actually reads (brighter/tighter hot spots, darker base, less opaque tile fills, WASH_PEAK_OPACITY 0.6→0.44 — the mood tint was flattening the facets into one flat colour). The owner's reference clip CONFIRMED the round-4 architecture: on a real mirror ball the grid barely appears to move and what travels is the flashes across the mirrors, which is exactly the static-grid + scrolling-light model. Light strip now drawn over 4 periods (k=-1..2) not 2, so the wrap is exactly seamless at the silhouette. NOTE for future rounds: the straight vertical line down the ball's centre is NOT a bug — it's the front meridian, which correctly projects to a straight line (measured: tiles either side are 123.9 vs 127.5 luminance, no discontinuity); don't "fix" it.
- DONE 2026-07-25 (Disco Ball round 6 — "still doesn't spin", OTA): it WAS spinning; it was invisible. Measured properly instead of guessing — rendered the composite offscreen at turn 0 vs turn 0.25 and diffed: median |Δlum| was 6/255, only 9% of the ball changing >20. Three FLAT layers were sitting on top of the travelling light and cancelling it: ColorCycleWash (solid slab, 0.44→0.22), dbShade's inner WHITE stop (flat 0.5 sitting exactly where the light travels → 0.12; the dark outer stops make the ball read round so they stayed), and the dbHot specular (0.5→0.26). Tile fills lowered too (0.28+0.38·nz → 0.18+0.26·nz) since they must pass light through, and FOUR large soft washes (lpBig) added to the scrolling layer — the small hot spots give per-mirror sparkle but are too fine to carry rotation on their own. Re-measured: median 17/255, 44% changing >20 (~3×). Timing/gating/geometry untouched. LESSON: for "is it animating?" questions on this ball, measure the composite diff — don't eyeball layer opacities. Also note the spin gate `playing && !musicSwitching && (track?.isPlaying ?? true)` is the SAME expression driving AmbientGlow's beat, which the owner has confirmed works — so the gate is sound.
- DONE 2026-07-25 (Disco Ball rounds 7-8 — THE SPIN BUG, finally found by MEASURING, OTA): after three rounds of "still not spinning" that I wrongly treated as a visibility problem (tuning wash/shade/tile opacity — all wasted), the owner's screen recording settled it. METHOD, reuse this: extract frames with the ffmpeg bundled in imageio-ffmpeg (`/usr/local/lib/python3.11/dist-packages/imageio_ffmpeg/binaries/ffmpeg-linux-x86_64-v7.0.2`; no system ffmpeg, and Chromium can't decode HEVC), then diff regions with PIL/numpy. A temporary SPIN_DEBUG strip (a green marker driven by the SAME spin value + a text readout of the gate flags) made the value observable; tracking it BY COLOUR gave the phase over time — but mask the marker's own row band, or the static green TEXT drags the centroid and fakes a frozen reading. Result: phase frozen at 0.851 for 4s while the gate said SPIN1, then a play/pause tap revived it and it ran at exactly 6.95s/turn. Ball pixel std: 0.43 frozen vs 13.0 running. ROOT CAUSE: resuming called `spin.stopAnimation(cb)` to read the current value — ASYNC for a native-driven value (NativeAnimatedAPI.getValue) — and everything that started the next animation lived inside that callback, so one dropped answer stopped the ball forever. FIX: the turn is linear at a known duration, so the phase is now derived from the wall clock (phaseRef/runStartRef/turningRef/readPhase) and NEVER read back; every branch starts an animation synchronously. NEVER reintroduce a native value read-back on this path.
- DONE 2026-07-25 (Disco Ball round 9 — the grid itself now turns, OTA): owner could see the light travelling but not the tiles. A sphere on its polar axis maps latitude circles onto themselves, so horizontal rings correctly DON'T move — only meridians do. KEY TRICK: a meridian's projection is the ball's own profile curve scaled horizontally by sin(longitude) plus a vertical shear proportional to that scaled x — i.e. `transform: [{skewY}, {scaleX}]`, and BOTH are in RN's native-driver SUPPORTED_TRANSFORMS allowlist (checked node_modules/react-native/Libraries/Animated/NativeAnimatedAllowlist.js). So the whole rotating grid is 24 animated views, not hundreds of animated facets. Verified by overlaying the transform output on the true projection — pixel-identical at every angle except within ~0.3° of centre, where the meridian is a straight vertical line anyway (clamp the skew to ±89° and give scaleX a 0.002 epsilon; a zero scaleX is a degenerate matrix). REJECTED on the renders, don't retry blind: (a) animating every facet as a moveable rectangle — poles break into overlapping blocks, silhouette goes ragged, because axis-aligned rects can't be the converging quads; (b) smoothing the fills so only seams move — becomes a wireframe globe and loses all mirror character.
- DONE 2026-07-25 (MIRROR BALL round 10 — renamed + beveled facets + its own icon, OTA): mode RENAMED "Disco Ball" → "Mirror Ball" everywhere user-facing (modeCatalog label, MODE_META, Modes-tab card title, the mode's own header) — the id stays `disco` and the file stays `DiscoBallMode.tsx`, because saved cruises and per-station picks store the bare string. Ball enlarged ~14% (`Math.min(winW*0.71, winH*0.39, 340)`). SURFACE TEXTURE: each mirror is now TWO quads, not one flat fill — a darker full-tile frame plus a brighter inner face inset ASYMMETRICALLY toward the light (`BEVEL = {u0:.13, u1:.79, v0:.21, v1:.87}`, thin lit rim top/left, thick shadow bottom/right; a symmetric inset just reads as smaller squares). Inner face found by bilinear interpolation of the tile's own four projected corners. 478 static paths, still built once and never animated, so no per-frame cost. Jitter 1.7→2.1. Combined opacity kept near the old 0.44 ceiling so the travelling light still shows through (that ceiling is what round 6 measured — don't raise it). BRICK BOND added at the same time (`bond = (j%2)*0.5` on longitude): with visible tile edges the 32 static columns were stacking into continuous vertical lines that competed with the 24 rotating meridians for "the grid that turns" — offsetting alternate rows kills the static verticals AND is how real mirror balls are built. Verified by rendering the real builders at three rotation phases with true-projection meridians overlaid. NEW ICON: `src/components/MirrorBallGlyph.tsx` — MCI has no mirror-ball glyph (`mirror-variant` reads as a hand mirror), so it's the same sphere projection at ROWS 7 × COLS 12 with each quad shrunk to 0.82 (the GAP is the grid; hairline strokes vanish below 1px), plus a hanging stem. Static, memoized, legible at 17px in the mini-player. CARD: frosted silver glass instead of the purple slab — white/blue-white gradient on the DIAGONAL (a neutral wash only reads as metal if it falls off corner-to-corner), bright rim, cool outward shadow on a WRAPPER view (the card clips its own children, and on iOS that clips the shadow too), plus 16 twinkling specks walking the card's PERIMETER — glitter scattered at random lands on the title and reads as dirt.
- DONE 2026-07-25 (Mirror Ball round 11 — the two owner reference photos, OTA): the round-10 bevel was structurally right but visually inverted. Fat frame (a third of each tile) + one jittered brightness for frame and face = small squares floating on a grey sphere; the references show the opposite — the mirror is nearly the whole tile and the seams are HAIRLINES. Three changes, all in `buildSphereTiles`: (1) `BEVEL` tightened to {u0:.10, u1:.94, v0:.06, v1:.90} so the frame is ~10% not ~34%; (2) frame and face now shade by DIFFERENT rules — the frame is continuous metal so it takes smooth lambert with NO jitter and a +2.6 lift (bright grid on the lit side, dark on the shadow side), while each mirror reflects its own bit of the room so the face takes lambert ±2.6 steps of jitter (neighbours land wildly apart). Driving both from one value was what made the whole surface speckle together and lost the lit grid; (3) colour on nearly every tile (0.10–0.44 cast from a palette of the station's three eqColors + the four NEONs) instead of the old 26% minority at a fixed 0.28 — `buildSphereTiles` now takes `eq` and memoizes on it. Plus a station-coloured rim light at the silhouette, and the latitude arcs dropped 0.45→0.26 (they were cutting through the newly-lit grid). CRITICAL: the frame path is the outer tile AND the face quad in one `d` with `fillRule="evenodd"`, so it paints only the border. As a full quad under the face its opacity lands on the WHOLE ball and cancels the travelling light — the round-6 failure exactly. Measured after: median |Δlum| between quarter-turns 36/255 with 70% of the ball changing >20, vs round 6's post-fix 17/255 / 44%, so the turn reads roughly twice as strongly as the version the owner signed off.
- DONE 2026-07-25 (Mirror Ball round 12 — "the tiles don't seem to be moving", OTA): round 11 caused it. Giving every tile a BRIGHT hairline frame drew each tile's VERTICAL edges too — and those are static meridian seams, which directly contradict the meridians that rotate. Bright and perfectly regular, they became the most legible structure on the ball and pinned the whole surface; the eye locks onto whatever doesn't move. NEW MEASUREMENT, reuse it for any "is the grid turning?" question (the round-6 composite-diff test can't answer this one — it happily reported 36/255 while the ball looked frozen, because the travelling LIGHT dominates that number): render N frames across a window, take the per-frame vertical-edge map `|∂x|`, then `min` over time = structure pinned in place vs `std` over time = structure travelling; report moving/static. TRAP that nearly sent this the wrong way: 24 meridians repeat every 1/24 turn, so sampling 12 frames at 1/12-turn steps landed on IDENTICAL meridian positions every time and scored the moving lines as static — sample a window that is NOT a multiple of the meridian period (now 24 frames over 1/6 turn). FIX: `BEVEL.u0/u1` are 0 and 1, so the frame is top/bottom edges only, no vertical sides — never give it a horizontal inset again. Meridians then take over as the ball's only vertical seams: count 24→32 to match the tile columns, width 0.0035→0.004, and each gained a paired pale stroke (offset in PATH space inside the same animated view, so no extra animated views; scaleX shrinks the gap toward the front meridian, which is correct — face-on you don't see a seam's edge). Measured static 2.19→0.81, moving 6.73→10.49, ratio 3.1→13.0. Rejected on the render: meridians at width 1.8 / opacity 0.62 — reads as a wire cage over the mirrors.
- DONE 2026-07-26 (Mirror Ball round 13 — flashing mirrors + rim fix, OTA): owner, on the round-12 build (confirmed from their recording: seam repeat measured 200ms ≈ 7000/32, vs 292ms for the old 24), said "I see the coloured tiles staying still but the shape moving". Correct, and the diagnostic that shows it: diff two frames ~0.35s apart and look at WHAT changed — only the thin vertical seams did, the tile bodies were frozen. The smooth light layer does vary each tile (measured face temporal std 13.7 with no flash layer at all) but it slides over the mosaic as a soft glow instead of being carved up by it, so no mirror ever changes as a mirror. FIX: a MirrorFlash layer — a second, brighter copy of every mirror face, filed into 26 phase buckets by screen x (plus ±0.13 per-tile jitter so tiles pop individually instead of in rigid columns), each bucket one Animated.View whose whole layer fades up and down. The bump uses CIRCULAR distance between the turn's phase and the bucket's, so the sweep wraps seamlessly — as the band leaves the right limb the left limb is already lighting, matching the two-copy light strip. Opacity-only, native driver, so 26 groups cost about one animation; ~239 extra static paths (~730 total, watch this if CPU reports return). Tuning that mattered: at WIDTH 0.12 the band faded over 1.7s and read as another soft glow — the metric barely moved (13.74→13.47). Sharp and narrow is the whole point: 0.07 now. RIM: the round-11 station-coloured stroke at the silhouette was the "outline of the shape that looks too noticeable" — ANY stroke there reads as a drawn outline. Replaced with a radial gradient that fades inward (transparent to 0.80, colour 0.16 at 0.955, white 0.10 at the edge); never put a hard ring back. REJECTED, with reasons, for making the tiles literally translate: (a) per-column affine (skewY+scaleX, the meridian trick applied to a strip) — a strip's two bounding meridians have different λ so one affine can't map both, and the error is catastrophic exactly at the front centre where scaleX→0 collapses the column to a line; (b) true 3D via [perspective, rotateX(tilt), rotateY(λ+τ), rotateX(-b), translateZ(R)] per tile with backfaceVisibility hidden — mathematically right, front-facing tiles never overlap so z-order wouldn't matter, and only rotateY animates, BUT it needs ~540 animated views and RN gives no `preserve-3d`, so nested/grouped 3D is unreliable and it can't be validated without a device. Not worth the gamble mid-launch; revisit only if the flash layer proves insufficient.
- DONE 2026-07-26 (EQUALIZER → retro segmented LED meter, OTA): owner references = a segmented VU meter (stacked lamps, unlit track visible above the level, mirrored reflection below). BUG FOUND doing it: the bars were never actually segmented in fullscreen. `GapStrips` faked the gaps by painting strips of the BACKGROUND colour over a solid gradient, and fullscreen passed `bgColor="transparent"` — so the strips painted nothing and the bars were solid gradients. Lamps are now real Views with real transparent gaps (station artwork shows between them, which is most of the retro look). Added: the unlit track behind the level (the strongest retro cue — a hi-fi meter always shows its whole scale), a white peak cap riding the top of each bar, per-lamp colour sampled up the station's 3-stop palette, segment pitch 7→8px (gap 2→3, chunkier), and animation targets snapped to whole lamps so the meter lands on a lamp instead of half way up one. Reflection re-uses the SAME Animated.Values, so it costs views but no extra animations. TWO TRAPS: (1) the reflection's flip needs NO vertical offset — scaleY(-1) already puts the bars' bottom lamps against the baseline; offsetting the box reveals the far end of the flip, so short bars vanish and tall ones float detached (that was the first render); (2) `fs.vizSection` CLIPS, so the meter must be sized for bars + reflection together — `FS_MAX_SEGS = (VIZ_H + 9) / (UNIT * (1 + REFLECT_RATIO))`, VIZ_H raised 0.26→0.30 of the screen. Verified fits at 926pt and 667pt. `EqualizerModeCard`/`EqualizerModePreview` are dead code (nothing imports them) — don't spend time on their layout.
- DONE 2026-07-26 (Mirror Ball round 14 + Equalizer trim, OTA): EQUALIZER — owner: "remove the black pills in the background and remove the reflection, just keeping the equalizer bars". Both gone (the unlit track read as a dark grid over the artwork, and the reflection's fade gradient painted a solid dark box across the photo). `Lamps` lost its `dim` variant, `Bars` lost the `track` prop, `BarsWithReflection` + `REFLECT_RATIO` deleted, and VIZ_H went back to 0.26 with the meter filling it (no reflection to make room for). Peak caps KEPT — the owner didn't name them and they read as part of the bars. FLASH PACE BUG (the "have the ball move at the same time as the reflective animation" note, and it was a real mismatch): the meridians are a true sphere projection, so a seam crosses the visible face in HALF a turn, sinusoidally; the flash band was keyed to screen x over a WHOLE turn, linearly — so highlights travelled at half the speed of the spinning grid. FIX: phase is now the tile's own LONGITUDE folded into `FLASH_PERIOD = 0.5` (two lit bands, one either side, so the face is never dark), which also gives the band the correct screen speed for free — quick across the middle, slowing into each limb, exactly like the surface. FLASH_CORE/FLASH_WIDTH are in turns so they scale with the period, NOT with 1 — halve them if the period ever changes. Verified by tracking the bright centroid: 0.65 of the radius per 1/6 turn vs 0.33 under the old mapping, i.e. the 2× the diagnosis predicted. Spin 7000→6000ms as asked. BRIGHTNESS — owner: "a bit dim". Round 13's rebalance overshot into gloom (median luminance 72, only 2.6% of the ball above 200). Lifted the shade ramp's dark end off near-black, faceStep/frameStep up, mirror colour cast 0.45→0.72, wash 0.10→0.14, underglow up. First attempt overshot the other way (median 121, 20% above 200 — washed out, no dark mirrors left); landed at median ~103 with 12-16% above 200. Reference clip is median 53 / 8.4% — deliberately NOT matched, because that ball is photographed in a black room and ours sits over lit station artwork.
- DONE 2026-07-26 (launch-eve sweep — 7 owner items, all OTA): (1) MIRROR BALL COLOURS ON-THEME — the mode carried a fixed club-neon set (cyan/magenta/purple/blue) blended alongside each station's own eqColors, so every ball got pinks and teals regardless of mood; Mountain Pass (pure white eqColors) came out speckled magenta. All colour now flows through `stationPalette(eq)` = the station's three stops plus a lighter and a deeper version of each, giving variety with no foreign hue. Also swept the same fix through NeonSweep (was 4 fixed neons), BokehField and bloomColor. The `NEON` constant is DELETED — don't reintroduce a fixed palette here. (2) SHINE — new `BallSheen`: a wide soft diagonal band sweeping across the ball on a 5.2s yo-yo, above the flashes. Deliberately a wide band, not a hotspot (round 4 already proved a hotspot reads as a painted blob); VolumetricRays lifted 0.16→0.26 so the ball also throws light into the room. (3) Ball raised via `paddingBottom: ballSize * 0.30` on its flex box — it hangs from above, so sitting high reads better than dead centre. (4) SOFTER ATMOSPHERE setting — new `softAtmosphere` in motionSettings/MotionContext, default ON, scaling AmbientGlow's whole layer to 0.5 via one wrapper View (scaling the layer, not each haze, keeps shapes and timing identical). Profile row sits directly under Atmosphere and hides when Atmosphere is off. (5) FREEZE BUG, partial — found and fixed a real one in `AutoDim`: the 30s timer fires and then AWAITS the brightness calls, so closing the mode in that gap left the continuation to run `setDimmed(true)` anyway, mounting the invisible full-screen catch Modal over the app the user had just returned to. clearTimeout is far too late for that; there's now a `cancelled` flag checked after each await. NOT confirmed to be the owner's symptom — no repro, so if "can't swipe after leaving a page" persists, ask WHICH page and whether the drive was ~30s idle. (6) CUSTOM STATION COLOURS — `CreateStationModal` never writes `eqColors`, and `StationDetailModal` receives custom stations RAW (customToStation only runs when a mode resolves one), so `eqColors?.[1] ?? theme.accentColor` fell through to the app's violet: every user-made station's mode chips and Start Drive button were purple regardless of the colour picked. Added a `(station as CustomStation).color` fallback, and `customToStation` now derives a light→base→deep ramp via `rampFromColor()` instead of three identical stops (three identical stops give the EQ and mirror ball nothing to work with). Derived, not stored, so stations saved earlier get it too. (7) MirrorBallGlyph is now PLAIN WHITE — tiles vary only in opacity, body gradient and specular removed; the silver/blue version read as a colour illustration among flat white glyphs.
- DONE 2026-07-26 (share pill + one shared action row, OTA): all eight modes carried a BYTE-IDENTICAL copy of the action-row markup and its four styles — the classic way things drift apart. Now one component, `src/components/ModeActionRow.tsx`: `[Change Mood] [playlist name] [share]`. The share pill renders ONLY when `spotify.track` exists — with no track there is nothing to share but a mood tagline, and a button that sometimes shares nothing is worse than no button. Icon is Ionicons `share-outline` on iOS (the box-with-arrow Apple users know) and `share-social-outline` on Android. Playlist label is capped at 18 chars AND flex-shrunk with `minWidth: 0` — minWidth is load-bearing on a flex row, without it the text sets the pill's minimum width and the share button gets pushed off the edge. Station name varies per mode (`station` / `currentStation` / `lockedStation`), so it's passed in. SPACING: pills' marginTop 18→26, and the five modes whose transport row had `marginTop: 18` (CD, Circular EQ, Disco/Mirror Ball, Horizon, Tuner) went to 14 so the buttons sit slightly higher — don't take that lower, the progress bar is directly above. Cassette/Vinyl/Equalizer already sat at `marginTop: 10, marginBottom: 8` and were left alone; their gap below grows from the pills' side anyway.
- DONE 2026-07-26 (SHARE CARD — Spotify-style now-playing pin, OTA): share now opens a preview sheet with a real card, then hands a PNG to the OS share sheet. KEY CONSTRAINT AND HOW IT WAS MET: turning a View into an image normally needs `react-native-view-shot`, a NATIVE module — installing it changes the fingerprint and every existing build stops receiving OTA until a new binary ships, which is unacceptable mid-launch. Instead the card is drawn ENTIRELY in react-native-svg (already in the build) and captured with `<Svg>.toDataURL()`, then written with `expo-file-system/legacy` (`writeAsStringAsync` + `EncodingType.Base64` into `cacheDirectory`) — expo-file-system ships inside `expo` itself, so it is already autolinked. Net: zero new dependencies, pure OTA. CONSEQUENCE: everything in `ShareCard.tsx` must be SVG primitives — no Views, no expo-linear-gradient, no MarqueeText. TWO IMPLEMENTATION TRAPS: (1) the capture copy is a SECOND full-size `<Svg>` parked off-screen (`left: -CARD_W*2`, opacity 0 — NOT display:none, a hidden view may not rasterise), because toDataURL rasterises at the view's own size and capturing the small preview would export a soft thumbnail; (2) both Svg roots are on screen at once, so gradient ids are namespaced per instance via a `uid` prop — duplicate ids across roots is a known way to get one rendering blank. The preview step also earns its keep by giving the album art time to load; capturing straight from the tap often exported an empty cover. SVG `<Text>` does not wrap, so `wrapLines()` estimates at 0.52×fontSize per char and errs short. Card is 1080×1350 (4:5 — iMessage/WhatsApp show it uncropped). Name comes from `getProfileName()` (Spotify `/me` display_name, cached in AsyncStorage); with no name the line reads "Now playing on <station>". Footer carries cruisefm.app so recipients without the app can install. ANDROID: React Native's `Share` cannot attach a file (needs a content:// provider), so Android shares the text form — expo-sharing would fix it but is a native module, so it waits for the next fresh build. Station colours drive the card, and pale stations (Mountain Pass is pure white) are mixed 0.68/0.82 toward black or white text can't sit on them.
- DONE 2026-07-26 (TUNER → dot-matrix head unit, OTA): owner references = a car head unit and an FM receiver, both dot-matrix LCDs. NEW `src/components/DotMatrix.tsx`: a real 5×7 matrix renderer (51 glyphs: A-Z, 0-9, punctuation), drawing LIT AND UNLIT dots — the visible unlit dots are the whole reason it reads as hardware, and no font can do that. No font file, so it ships OTA. TRAP while building it: the glyph table was first written as one 35-char string per letter and several were silently malformed (S, X, 5 came out mangled, two even needed `.slice(0,35)` to compile) — it's now seven 5-char rows per glyph joined at load, where a mistake is visible on sight, plus a length assertion. Always render the full character set before trusting it. LAYOUT per the owner's sketch: `[● ON AIR] SONG TITLE / ARTIST`, then `STEREO … TUNED`, then big `FM … 92.10`. The ON AIR lamp breathes only when `playing && lock > 0.9`. Long titles SCROLL (`DmLine`, transform-only on the native driver) rather than truncating, but the window width is SNAPPED to a whole number of character cells — a window ending mid-glyph instantly breaks the illusion. The duplicate song-title block below the dial is gone; it only appears now when there's no track, carrying the mood tagline. Retired: `onAirChip`/`onAirDot`/`onAirText`/`freqBig`/`freqUnit` styles and the MarqueeText import. Frequency now shows 2 decimals (92.10) to match a real receiver.
- DONE 2026-07-26 (share card → mode artwork, OTA): owner wanted the card to read as artwork for the mode and mood, not a Spotify clone in different colours. `ModeArt` in ShareCard.tsx draws a per-mode composition behind the cover, always in the STATION's eqColors — so the mode gives the shape and the station gives the palette. cd/vinyl = concentric grooves centred on the cover (the artwork becomes the label); disco = sphere latitudes + meridians + scattered light; cassette = shell, two reels and the tape run (deliberately WIDER than the cover and pushed low, or it hides behind the artwork entirely); equalizer = a bell-curved meter along the bottom; orb = concentric rings; radio = dial ticks with a needle; horizon = sun on a receding grid. `modeId` is threaded from ModeActionRow (session mode) alongside the existing label. Everything is SVG primitives — same OTA constraint as the rest of the card. PLATFORM LIMIT, unchanged and worth restating: iOS shares the real PNG; ANDROID shares text only, because React Native's `Share` cannot attach a file without a content:// provider. Fix is `expo-sharing`, a native module → next fresh build, which suits the Android timeline anyway (blocked on Google's 14-day closed test).
- RUNTIME VERSION IS NOW PINNED, NOT COMPUTED (2026-07-30) — read this before touching app.json. `runtimeVersion` was `{"policy":"fingerprint"}` and is now the literal string `"1.0.0"`. WHY: EAS's build-time fingerprint and eas-cli's publish-time fingerprint had DIVERGED — build 10 (EAS macOS builder, commit 247faef) reported `1a4deffd3fee3f9ad1ccc3d53843eefcf3e4705d` while this repo fingerprinted as `eb670d785ceebd4b977406ca67cab1364bf2df63` on both a Linux AND a macOS runner from the same commit. Consequence: every update published for two days succeeded, appeared on the dashboard, and reached ZERO phones. Ruled out first (don't re-check these): the OS, repo changes since that commit, leftover expo-audio. CONSEQUENCES OF PINNING, both load-bearing: (1) editing app.json/eas.json/package.json NO LONGER breaks OTA by itself — the old "any edit to eas.json silently changes the runtime version" trap is GONE, so the NEXT-FRESH-BUILD batching below is now about NATIVE code only, not about protecting a hash; (2) the automatic native-mismatch seatbelt is gone, so ANY change that alters native code (new native module, plugin adding an Info.plist key, new permission) MUST bump `version` AND `runtimeVersion` together in the same change, or an update will land on a build that cannot run it. `.github/workflows/ota-update.yml` now refuses to publish unless a FINISHED iOS build on that channel is listening on the current runtime version, so a silent no-op cannot recur; the temporary `diagnose` mode and macOS fingerprint job it was debugged with are removed.
- NEXT FRESH BUILD — BATCH THESE (do NOT do them early). These are NATIVE changes: an OTA update carries JS and assets only, so none of them can reach a phone without a new binary. (Since 30.07 they no longer break OTA merely by editing app.json — see the pinned-runtime entry above — but each still needs `runtimeVersion` bumped in the same change.) Owner's plan (26.07): the next build happens after Apple approval and once they're satisfied with functions/design.
  1. `expo-sharing` — lets Android attach the share-card PNG. React Native's own `Share` can only send text on Android (a file needs a content:// provider). iOS already works. One Android user today, so nothing is lost by waiting. After installing, swap the Android branch in `ShareCard.tsx`'s `shareCard()` for `Sharing.shareAsync(path)` and keep the text path as the fallback.
  2. `react-native-view-shot` — lets the share card be a REAL SCREENSHOT of the mode instead of a hand-drawn SVG lookalike (owner asked 27.07: "since this is a more visual app… share this like a screenshot but in a card form", comparing the actual Mirror Ball on device against the redrawn one and rightly preferring the real thing). This is THE reason the card is SVG at all — capturing a View needs a native module, which changes the fingerprint. Once it lands: capture the mode's own fullscreen view (minus transport/pills), inset it into the 1080x1350 card with the eyebrow, song block and cruisefm.app footer around it, and keep the SVG heroes as the fallback for when a capture fails or there's no live mode (e.g. sharing from the mini-player). `ShareModeArt.tsx` stays useful either way.
  3. Apple Music via MusicKit — OWNER-CONFIRMED PRIORITY (28.07): full in-app music for everyone on iOS, no quota. Native module + Apple Developer MusicKit key + config plugin; user needs an Apple Music subscription. Start with `expo-music-kit`-style community plugin or a small custom Swift module; entitlement `com.apple.developer.applemusic` + NSAppleMusicUsageDescription. Wire behind the existing platform abstraction (musicPlatform.ts) so Spotify and Apple Music are peers.
  4. Drop the `expo-audio` plugin from app.json — mic-reactive visuals were removed 22.07 and legal.ts now says the app "does not use the microphone", but the plugin still declares a microphone permission. Leaving it costs an unexplained permission on the store listing; it was kept only to protect the fingerprint.
  5. **DONE IN app.json, AWAITING A BINARY (06.08): `NSPhotoLibraryAddUsageDescription`** — without it iOS withholds "Save Image" from the share sheet, so the card cannot be saved to Photos (see the Save Image entry below for how that was diagnosed). The key is already written into app.json; it simply has no effect until a build carries it. Nothing else is needed — no new dependency, no code change.
  6. `expo-media-library` + a "Save to Photos" button in ShareCardSheet — OWNER APPROVED 06.08 ("save that for the next build"). One tap straight to the camera roll instead of share-sheet → Save Image. Needs the key in item 5 anyway, so the two ship together; keep the share route as well, since it is the path to Pinterest/Messages. `MediaLibrary.saveToLibraryAsync(path)` on the file the card already writes to `cacheDirectory` — no capture rework, the PNG exists by the time either button is pressed.
  7. **NOTIFICATIONS — `expo-notifications`, OWNER APPROVED 07.08 ("save this for the next build").** Full copy set and the anti-nag rules are drafted in `docs/notifications-copy.md` — build from that document, it is the spec. ALL LOCAL, scheduled on the phone: no server, no push tokens, nothing leaves the device, so the Privacy page's "no Cruise FM server" claim stays true (add a line saying notifications are scheduled on-device). THE RESTRAINT IS THE FEATURE and it is mechanical, not aspirational: max 2/week, ≥48h apart, nothing 22:30–06:30 unless late-night is opted into, never during a drive or for 6h after, never on a day they already drove, no line repeated within 8 weeks, none on install day. ADAPTIVE BACK-OFF is the centrepiece — 2 ignored in a row → 1/week, 4 → 1/fortnight, 6 → STOP until they open the app themselves, then resume silently with no "we missed you". Permission is asked after the THIRD drive, never on first launch, behind an honest pre-prompt card (iOS allows one ask; a denial is near-permanent). Each notification carries stationId + mode and deep-links straight into the drive. Budget/back-off counters live in AsyncStorage beside the drive log. WEATHER (Rain Drive FM) is PHASE TWO and the only piece with a privacy cost — it needs a rough position, so it ships later, default OFF, asking only when switched on, using iOS reduced-accuracy location or a typed town; disclose it like the artwork lookup already is. DONE ALREADY (OTA, 07.08): the fake `Premium Offers` toggle is deleted (component + `premiumOffers` pref key), `newStations` is relabelled "New Stations & Modes" and stays default ON (owner's call — it is the only way anyone learns the app grew, capped at one per release), and the sheet now prints the ceiling in plain sight. NOTE the two remaining toggles are still INERT until this build lands — they save a preference nothing reads.
  Bump `version` + `runtimeVersion` in app.json as part of the batch, then cut a `testflight`-profile build afterwards so the owner's phone stays on the preview channel and OTA keeps flowing.
- DONE 2026-07-26 (Tuner round 2 — dual-band dial, OTA): (1) the standard `PLAYING FROM <station>` header is BACK at the top of Tuner (it was the only mode without one), and the duplicate station name that sat between the display and the dial is gone. (2) NEEDLE is now a fixed neon red (`NEEDLE_RED = '#FF3B30'`), never the station accent — on every dial worth copying the pointer is the one element that doesn't change hue, which is what lets the eye find it instantly. Built as three stacked rects: a wide soft glow, a 3.2px core, a 1.2px near-white hairline. First cut at 14px/5px/2px read as a bar, not a pointer. (3) DUAL SCALE — FM numbers and ticks above the lit band, a decorative AM scale below it, both labelled in the dot-matrix type via the new `DotMatrixGroup` (a `<G>` rather than its own `<Svg>`, since react-native-svg can't nest Svg roots). The AM row is cosmetic — Cruise FM only tunes FM — and is mapped across the same travel at its own rate, so it drifts past at a different speed as you tune, exactly like a real printed dial. `AM_PX_PER_KHZ = PX_PER_MHZ / 220`: at the first value (/78) a 200kHz gap was wider than the screen and only one AM number was ever visible. (4) The space the removed song-title block left is taken by the display itself — padding 14→22, row gaps 10→18, and 28px between panel and dial. NOTE: `G` is the glyph table's name inside DotMatrix.tsx, so the SVG group import there is aliased `SvgG`.
- DONE 2026-07-26 (SHARE CARD → mode artwork, round 2 — the mode IS the picture, OTA): the first card drew the mode as a faint pattern BEHIND a big album cover, so every mode came out as the same Spotify pin in a different colour — which defeats the point, since the modes are the part nobody else has. New `src/components/ShareModeArt.tsx` owns a HERO stage (y 150..890 of the 1080x1350 card) and each mode draws a real illustration there with the ALBUM ART AS A COMPONENT OF IT, not on top of it: vinyl = the record's label (plus grooves, sheen, silver J tonearm), cd = the disc face under jewel-case plastic with the diffraction fan UNDERNEATH the art (a printed face is matte — same rule as the mode), cassette = the printed inlay in the shell above the reels, disco = a genuine sphere projection (same lat/long quads, back-face cull, Lambert shading + brick bond as the mode, ~150 visible facets, static) with beams falling onto the cover below, equalizer = the cover with the segmented LED meter standing in front of it, orb = the cover ringed by radial bars, radio = the head unit — real dot-matrix title/artist/frequency via DotMatrixGroup, cover in the panel, dual-band dial with the fixed red needle, horizon = the cover becomes the sliced retrowave sun over a receding grid. Card geometry (CARD_W/H, stage bounds, mixHex) moved INTO ShareModeArt so the two files don't import each other in a circle; ShareCard re-exports them. `STATION_FREQS` moved to constants/stations.ts (+ `stationFrequency()` with a hash fallback so custom stations tune somewhere stable) because the card draws the same dial and importing it from TunerMode would cycle mode -> action row -> card -> mode.
  VERIFICATION METHOD, reuse it: the components were rendered for real by transpiling their OWN source with `ts.transpileModule` under a JSX factory that emits SVG markup (tag map Circle->circle etc., camelCase->kebab attrs, `href={{uri}}`->href), then screenshotted in Chromium at 8 modes x 3 fixtures. Four traps if you rebuild that harness: the fragment factory arrives THROUGH the element factory as `h(F, null, ...kids)` so F must read props.children; child markup strings must be wrapped in a marker class or they get HTML-escaped into visible text; DotMatrix's glyph table is named `G` and collides with the SVG `<G>`; and name the factory `__h`, because a local named `h` in the component (a bar height, a rect height prop) silently shadows it.
  BUGS THE RENDER CAUGHT: CoverSquare only takes one `size`, so the cassette inlay drew an 804-SQUARE straight over the song title (added `CoverRect`); the horizon grid ran to the card's bottom edge and struck through the title; the vinyl tonearm was a short stick in the corner. PALE STATIONS: Mountain Pass's eqColors are literally three whites, and a white haze over half the card turned it light grey — every big soft glow now goes through `glowCol(eq)`, which deepens by perceived luminance, so pale stations keep their hue and come out charcoal-with-silver-hardware instead of washed out. Checked with no album art too (the tint fallbacks stand in fine). Still SVG primitives only and zero new dependencies, so it remains a normal OTA; ANDROID still shares text only until `expo-sharing` lands in the next fresh build.
- DONE 2026-07-26 (Tuner round 3 — use the space, OTA): (1) SONG TITLE moved onto its OWN full-width row instead of sharing row 1 with the ON AIR lamp. Sharing was what kept it small — the lamp plus its gap left about ten characters, so growing the dots just meant scrolling sooner. Full width buys bigger dots AND more of the name visible: TITLE 2.75/1.0 → 3.5/1.15 (glyph height 25 → 31px) with the same 11-12 characters, ARTIST 1.95/0.7 → 2.1/0.78. The lamp now sits alone above them, which is also what the owner's original sketch asked for. Title/artist are LEFT-aligned on their own row (`DmLine` gained an `align` prop) — right-alignment only made sense when they were the right-hand column of a two-column row. (2) BAND LABELS vs SCROLLING NUMBERS: "FM" sat directly in the FM numbers' travel path and collided into "FM02"; same for "AM" and "1000". Fixed in two parts, and the SECOND part is the one that matters — a backing plate ALONE is not enough, because a number half-hidden behind it reads as a different, shorter number ("1000" → "000"), which is worse than a gap. So any scale number whose glyph box would overlap the plate is now dropped outright (`underPlate()`), and the plate itself fades out on its right edge (`dialPlate` gradient) so the thin ticks dissolve under it rather than being sliced. (3) DIAL GAP is now responsive: `clamp((winH - 640) * 0.18, 14, 56)`. A flat 52 gave the owner the lower dial they asked for but ran off the bottom of a short phone — the panel and the dial are both fixed-height blocks inside one centred column, so there is no slack to absorb it. Panel paddingVertical trimmed 30 → 26 for the same reason. NOTE: on a 667pt screen (SE) the column still overflows; it did before this round too, so it's pre-existing, not a regression — worth a look if an SE user ever appears.
- DONE 2026-07-26 (STATIONS PAGE → a receiver, AM/FM bands, OTA): owner's note was that the page had a dial *motif* but the idea was "kind of forgotten", and it read as the Modes tab with different cards. It's now laid out like a real head unit. BANDS: `AM` = free stations + the user's own creations, `FM` = premium — one visible reason to pay instead of a PREMIUM badge repeated seven times (that per-card badge is now DELETED; the band header carries it). Both bands sort ASCENDING by dial number, which is the whole point of the metaphor, so custom stations interleave with the free ones by frequency rather than sitting in their own "MY STATIONS" block. New in constants/stations.ts: `STATION_AM` (night-run 810, sunset 1010, daylight 1240), `stationAm()` (hash into 540–1600 on round 10kHz steps, so a made-up station always lands in the same place), `stationDial(id, premium)` returning `{band,label,value}`. ROW = rail | number | card. The rail line is drawn INSIDE every row rather than once behind the list — the rows stack into one continuous line by themselves and the red marker lands on the right station with nothing to measure; it needs `top:-1/bottom:-1` or sub-pixel rounding leaves visible breaks at the seams. The red marker (horizontal, matching the Tuner's needle colour `#FF3B30`) parks on `np.session?.stationId`, falling back to the hour's on-air station. TRAP, applies to the marker box, the number and the lock overlay alike: the card carries `marginBottom:14` from `cardShadow`, so anything that wants to line up with the CARD must stop 14 short of the row's bottom — otherwise it centres on the gap. That also fixed a pre-existing bug where the premium lock panel hung 14px below the card it covered. CARD: name/tagline/tag now CENTRED, which means nothing may share the name's row (the right-hand block is padded to the icon's width so the middle is really the middle) and tags are capped at ONE — two long ones ("sunset glow" + "warm vibes") wrapped to a second line and made that station's card taller than its neighbours, which a dial can't have.
  (The Tuner was made dual-band the same day — see the entry above — so the page and the dial now agree.)
  Verified by running the real web build (`npx expo start --web`) and screenshotting in Chromium at 393pt — worth reusing for layout work; `/stations` goes straight there, and the platform-picker sheet needs "Skip for now" clicking first. Mouse-wheel scrolling does NOT move the RN ScrollView on web; set a tall viewport (2100) and take one shot instead.
- DONE 2026-07-26 (TUNER → genuinely dual-band, OTA): the Stations page's AM/FM split made the Tuner's FM-only dial a lie, so the dial now really has two bands and the AM row stopped being decoration. `BAND_CFG` holds each band's range, drag rate, tick size and lock window: FM 87.5-108.5 at 110px/MHz, AM 530-1600 at 2.2px/kHz — chosen so both bands take about the same thumb travel end to end, and so a tick lands ~22px apart on either. Both label every TENTH tick (1 MHz / 100 kHz), which is why one `MAJOR_EVERY` serves both. `DIAL` maps every station to `{band,value}` once via `stationDial`, and `nearestStation(band, freq)` only sees the current band's stations — that is what a band switch MEANS on a receiver. LAYOUT RULE: the LIVE band is always the top scale and carries the station markers; the other band is printed underneath and drifts at its own rate (its whole span laid across ~2.2 screens, labels thinned to at least 78px apart). TRAP: draw only halves and majors on the printed row — at full density it became a solid grey mass that out-shouted the scale actually being tuned. Also `Math.round(k*tick*1000)/1000` on every tick, or FM labels come out as 92.30000000000001. BAND BUTTON sits on the STEREO/TUNED row, where a head unit keeps it; tapping a band tunes to that band's first station and starts it, because on a real set the band and the audio move together. It's a TouchableOpacity inside the dial's PanResponder zone and works — RN asks the deepest child first on touch-start — but that's the thing to re-check if band taps ever stop responding on device. ROUND 2 (owner: "wasn't too obvious, I wouldn't have known it would work"): the first version used the same flat dot-matrix type as the STEREO/TUNED labels sitting beside it, so it read as printed text rather than a control. The keys now have a raised top bevel, a border, and a lamp dot that lights on the selected band — and the hint under the dial reads "tap am / fm · drag to tune" instead of just "drag to tune", because the dial now has two gestures and only one was named. MoodSheet picks that cross bands flip the dial instantly instead of sweeping (the two scales aren't one ruler, so there is nothing to travel across).
