Custom stations are deliberately NOT on the dial: MoodSheet doesn't list them either, so the mode stays consistent with itself.
- DONE 2026-07-26 (station page slides in from the right, OTA): `StationDetailModal` used to rise from the bottom like a sheet; it now pushes in from the RIGHT like turning to a page (`slideX` SCREEN_W->0, 300ms ease-out cubic — timing not spring, a page push should arrive and stop rather than wobble). Both exits are kept and each leaves the way it would have come in: an edge-swipe back slides it off right, the old pull-down still drops it, and `handleClose(down)` picks the axis. The dismiss PanResponder claims on MOVE only (capture-on-start is what killed the settings back button) and the rightward branch is EDGE-ONLY (`g.x0 < 44`) or it fights the horizontal Visual Mode strip; a `swipeAxis` ref locks the axis on the first few pixels so the two gestures can't argue mid-drag. Close now goes through a `closeRef` because the responder is built once and would otherwise hold the first render's copy. ALSO FOUND doing it: the sheet had NO visible way out at all — only the gesture — so there's now a back chevron at top-left, and the custom-station ... menu (plus its dropdown) moved to the right to make room.
- DONE 2026-07-26 (stations page -> SMOKED GLASS cards + the free stations are now AM, OTA): four card directions were mocked at real phone width against the real station colours, artwork and MCI icon font (harness in scratchpad: pull the fields out of stations.ts with a regex, copy the jpgs, @font-face the MaterialCommunityIcons.ttf and index the glyphmap for the icon chars — a quick way to compare directions without building them). A = the shipping mood-gradient slab, B = smoked glass, C = the station photo darkened, D = no cards, a printed listing. Owner picked B. The slab was the specific thing making Stations and Modes look like twins (Modes is gradient slabs too) and it shouted over the dial. `ListCard` now: `rgba(15,17,29,0.72)` glass base, the station's `eqColors[1]` as a wash off the LEFT EDGE only (2E -> 10 -> transparent at 0.42/0.72), rim at `accent+'44'`, icon ring `accent+'22'` on `accent+'66'`, and the neon halo shadow softened to 0.34/22 — the same move the Recommended cards had on 25.07. `CustomStationCard` got the identical finish so the user's own stations sit on the dial looking like everything else (it needs `overflow:'hidden'` on the inner view for the wash to clip). NAMES: the free stations are on the AM band, so "Sunset FM" contradicted the dial it sat on — night-run/sunset/daylight are now Night Run AM / Sunset AM / Daylight AM (also updated in website/index.html's station list and alt text, ThemeGenerator's sample text and HeroCard's doc comment). App Store screenshots still say FM; cosmetic, and worth refreshing whenever they're next regenerated.
- DONE 2026-07-27 (share card gets the real station photograph, OTA): owner compared the on-device Mirror Ball with the share card's redrawn one and asked for "a screenshot but in a card form". A true screenshot needs `react-native-view-shot` (native → next fresh build, now item 2 in that batch), but the biggest visible gap was OTA-able: every mode is a full-bleed BLURRED STATION PHOTO with the object on top, and the card was drawing a flat gradient instead. `ShareCardBody` now paints `station.imageBlur ?? station.image` full-bleed behind everything — react-native-svg's `<Image href>` takes a require()'d asset directly, so no new dependency — under a 4-stop scrim (0.72 → 0.60 → 0.74 → 0.93) shaped like the one the modes lay over their own backdrop: lighter where the object sits, deepest under the type. Custom stations have no artwork and fall through to the gradient. HARNESS NOTE: scratchpad/share/render.js needed two tweaks to see this — `href` may now be a bare string as well as `{uri}`, and the fixtures carry `imageBlur` as a base64 data URI of the real assets/stations/blur/*.jpg.
- DONE 2026-07-27 (stations page colour + orange dial numbers + left-aligned names, OTA): on device the smoked glass came out far darker than the web render — several stations read grey-brown rather than their own mood. Three changes: (1) COLOUR — glass base 0.72 -> 0.58 so less black sits over the wash, and the wash itself now travels the WHOLE card (82 -> 46 -> 1A across 0/0.55/1) instead of fading out at 0.72, plus a faint top-down white lift so it doesn't read as a flat fill; rim 44 -> 5C, icon ring 22/66 -> 38/99. Still glass, not the old slab. (2) DIAL NUMBERS are car-stereo amber `#FF9A2E` — grey read as UI text rather than a lit dial — and the tuned one burns brighter (`#FFD79B` + an orange textShadow glow); the red rail marker says WHERE, the bright number says which one is live. (3) NAMES AND TAGLINES LEFT-ALIGNED again, reversing the 26.07 centring at the owner's request: with the dial number sitting in its own column to the left, the name reads better hard against the icon than floating mid-card. The chevron block's fixed 42 width existed ONLY to balance the icon and keep the name centred — it is 26 now. Same treatment applied to `CustomStationCard`.
- DONE 2026-07-27 (paywall brought back in line with the real line-up, OTA): `src/app/premium.tsx` still sold "Vinyl, the Tuner, Horizon and the Circular Wave orb" — but Circular EQ went FREE on 25.07 and Mirror Ball + CD had never been added, and the premium FM band (26.07) was not mentioned anywhere. The FIX IS STRUCTURAL, not a copy edit: FEATURES and COMPARISON are now DERIVED from `MODE_CATALOG` (m.pro) and `STATIONS` (s.premium), so the paywall cannot go stale again when the line-up moves — the hand-typed duplicate list is exactly how it drifted. New rows for the AM/FM split ("AM band stations" free, "FM band - 7 stations" premium) since that is now a headline reason to pay. `PaywallShowcase` was also advertising Circular EQ as a premium teaser: its Orb miniature is replaced by a MIRROR BALL one (uniform tile grid clipped to a circle with alternate rows offset — how a real ball is built — plus a travelling two-band light, a top-left catch and a thick dark inner rim for roundness; the light moves, the grid does not, same trick as the full mode). Scene order Vinyl -> Tuner -> Mirror Ball -> Horizon. RULE recorded in that file: anything on the showcase stage must be pro:true in MODE_CATALOG.
- DONE 2026-07-27 (Mirror Ball round 15 — denser mirrors + a real gradient in each, OTA): owner sent two reference balls and asked for smaller/more tiles with "mini gradients", keeping the themed colours. GRID 17x32 -> 21x40 (~240 -> ~380 visible facets; a front-face tile is now ~8px). `buildLatitudeArcs` carries its own ROWS constant and MUST be changed with it or the arcs stop sitting on the row seams. PER-TILE GRADIENT: each mirror face is drawn twice — its own colour, then the same path filled `url(#dbFacet)`. That is ONE gradient definition for the entire ball, because SVG gradients default to objectBoundingBox units and so rescale to whatever path references them; per-tile defs (~380 nodes) was the obvious approach and the wrong one. REJECTED first: a flat two-tone split (light top-left triangle over a darker face) — rendered, and it read as a triangle drawn on every tile rather than a falloff. Stops are white 0.66 -> white 0.10 at 0.46 -> #05060d 0.42, i.e. lit corner top-left and shadow bottom-right, matching the key light. Face base pulled down 0.55 of a shade step to pay for the sheen. MEASURED before/after with the offscreen builder harness (scratchpad/ball/render.js — transpiles hash01..buildSphereTiles straight out of the component, so it tests the shipped geometry): median luminance 98 -> 103, share above 200 10.1% -> 12.0%, i.e. landed back on the round-14 numbers the owner signed off. Checked on Night Run, Sunset, Mountain Pass and Daylight — the white station still comes out silver, the warm ones stay warm. COST: 717 -> 1456 paths. All STATIC (built once, never animated; only the 26 flash groups animate), but that is 2x the shapes — if `cpu_resource` reports come back, ROWS/COLS is the first knob to turn down. Partly paid for already by skipping flash paths for mirrors with catchRoll <= 0.12, whose catch tops out near 0.09 opacity and is invisible.
- DONE 2026-07-27 (Mirror Ball round 16 — head on + gradients at different angles, OTA): (1) TILT 0.30 -> 0.05. The comment on that constant warned that dead-level would flatten the latitude rings into straight lines and read as a brick wall — that was TRUE OF THE OLD BUILD, which faked the surface with a scrolling texture strip, and is NOT true of the sphere projection: rendered at 0.30/0.10/0.04/0 side by side, roundness is identical because the columns still pinch toward the silhouette and the shading still falls off. Kept a sliver rather than exactly 0 so the poles never land on the silhouette, where z = 0 is the degenerate case for the back-face cull. TILT feeds the tiles, the latitude arcs AND the meridian transform from one constant, so they stay in agreement automatically. (2) PER-TILE GRADIENT DIRECTION: round 15 gave every facet the same top-left -> bottom-right ramp, so the whole surface leaned one way; on both reference balls the mirrors disagree, and that disagreement is most of what stops the surface looking printed. `FACET_DIRS` holds six directions and `facetDir(j,k)` picks one per tile, weighted toward the first two so the ball still reads as lit from the upper left rather than as noise. Still only six gradient nodes for ~380 facets (objectBoundingBox units). Measured: median 103 -> 107, >200 12.0% -> 11.9% — brightness unmoved.
- DONE 2026-07-27 (Mirror Ball round 17 — denser again, seams as hairlines, OTA): owner re-sent the reference ball with no words; the remaining gap at a glance was tile DENSITY. 21x40 -> 25x48. Paid for by DELETING the per-tile frame path (was ~380 paths): the BEVEL gap already exposes the ball's dark body between rows, so the seam draws itself. TRAP found immediately on the render: with the frame gone the gap showed near-black instead of dark metal, and at the higher row count those bands dominated and the ball read as STRIPES — the fix is that the gap must be a hairline, so BEVEL v0/v1 went 0.06/0.90 -> 0.03/0.955. u0/u1 stay 0/1 (no horizontal inset — vertical seams belong to RotatingMeridians alone; see round 12). Net 1445 -> 1540 paths for 37% more mirrors. Measured median 107 -> 106, >200 11.9% -> 12.5%, so brightness is where the owner left it. NOTE when reading the offscreen harness output: it renders the tile layer only, so tiles in a row appear to butt together — the 32 rotating meridians that draw the vertical seams are a separate layer the harness doesn't include.
- DONE 2026-07-27 (CRASH FIX — background timers were getting the app SIGKILLed, OTA): owner reported several crashes "leaving and entering the app again", with NOTHING in Sentry. Sentry was fine (DSN hardcoded so it can't go missing from a bundle, native module + plugin installed, OWNER_MODE false so nothing was being filtered). The four reports in Settings > Privacy > Analytics Data were all `bug_type 309`, `EXC_CRASH / SIGKILL`, exception codes 0x0/0x0 — i.e. the SYSTEM shot the process. A crash reporter can never see that: there is no signal to catch and no chance to flush, which is exactly why Sentry was silent. Don't chase Sentry when the reports say 309. ROOT CAUSE: `useSpotifyPlayback`'s 5-second poll had NO AppState gate, so a backgrounded app kept making a network request every 5s indefinitely; iOS gives a background app a few seconds of grace and then kills it for exactly this. FIX: the interval is now owned by `pollRef` with `startPoll`/`stopPoll`, and the existing AppState listener stops it on background and restarts it (plus an immediate refresh) on foreground. Same treatment for VinylMode's 3s "restart the spin" safety net via a new `useAppActive()` hook (src/utils/useAppActive.ts) — anything on a repeating timer must ask that hook. Checked: these were the ONLY two setIntervals in src/. rAF loops are fine, iOS doesn't run them in the background. NOT the mirror ball — the crash timestamps (17:25, 17:45, 18:56 and 01:28) bracket the whole day and the poll has been unguarded for far longer; the density work just meant a lot more leave/enter cycles that day.
- DONE 2026-07-27 (Mirror Ball round 18 — fixed lamps, softer facet ramp, brighter, OTA) + THE ROTATION QUESTION SETTLED: owner sent a clip of a REAL mirror ball and asked for (1) light from fixed sources rather than a band sliding across, (2) softer per-facet gradients, (3) THE TILES THEMSELVES TO TURN, (4) brighter tiles.
  (3) IS NOT POSSIBLE OVER THE AIR AND THE PROOF IS NOW ON RECORD — do not spend another round on it. A tile's screen x is `R*cos(b)*sin(lam)`, so the transform needed to rotate it depends on its LATITUDE as well as its longitude; one transform per column therefore cannot serve the rows inside that column. Measured with a least-squares affine fit per column over a full turn (scratchpad/ball/affine.py): worst error 89px on a 170px radius, and it does NOT improve with more columns (48/96/160 all ~88px) because the error is driven by the latitude spread, not the longitude spread. Every column also passes through the ball's centre line each turn, where the required scale goes to infinity. That is the same wall rounds 13's two rejected approaches hit, now quantified. Real rotation needs a per-tile transform (~1200 animated views, and RN has no preserve-3d) or a GPU canvas — react-native-skia, which is a NATIVE module and so a fresh-build item.
  (1) SUBSTITUTE THAT WORKS: a static grid cannot hold a lit patch still while mirrors move through it — whichever mirrors are lit sit at fixed screen positions, so if the lit SET changes the lit REGION must move. What can be done is make the light read as beams rather than a wash: `LAMPS` assigns every mirror to one of three fixed lamps, each with its own `tilt` (shifts the mirror's flash timing by latitude, so a lamp's lit mirrors form a DIAGONAL patch instead of a full-height stripe), its own `hue`/`sat` from stationPalette, and its own `phase`. Costs nothing — the mirrors just land in different buckets of the existing set. FLASH_CORE/WIDTH 0.034/0.070 -> 0.012/0.052 so a mirror snaps on and off instead of swelling, and the flash opacity split went to 0.94-1.00 vs 0.02-0.14: on the reference a lit mirror is beside a dead one, and it is that contrast, not the brightness, that makes each read as a separate mirror.
  THROB TRAP, and the reason `phase` exists: a beam on the FRONT of a sphere lights a wide area, the same beam at the limb lights a sliver, so with all lamps in step the ball swung between 22% and 4% of pixels above 200 twice per turn — visible pulsing. Staggering the lamps a third of a cycle apart holds it at 12.8-16.1%. Measured with scratchpad/ball/sweep.sh, which samples brightness across a turn; reuse it after ANY flash change. FLASH_GROUPS was raised to 40 chasing this and it made no difference (26 gives the same spread) — it was never a bucket-resolution problem, so it is back at 26 and costs no extra animated views.
  (2) FACET RAMP: 3 stops -> 6. The hard line was two steep segments meeting at a corner (white 0.66 falling to 0.10 across the first 46%, then re-darkening); eased through the middle and softened at both ends it reads as a curve.
  (4) BRIGHTER: SHADE_ANCHORS' dark end lifted off near-black (#101320 -> #1b2030 and up the ramp) and faceStep +1.0 with jitter 4.8 -> 5.4. Median luminance 106 -> 113-125 across the turn, share above 200 12.5% -> 12.8-16.1%. The round-6/13 warning (a brighter RESTING surface kills the sense of motion) is respected because the flash contrast went up at the same time — the gap between a caught mirror and its neighbour is now larger than it was, not smaller. Path count unchanged at 1540.
- DONE 2026-07-27 (mode header copy, OTA): the eyebrow above the station name in all 8 fullscreen modes reads “YOU’RE LISTENING TO” instead of “PLAYING FROM” (owner). Curly apostrophe, not a straight one — better in a letterspaced small-caps line and it also sidesteps eslint's react/no-unescaped-entities. All eight already shared identical styling (fontSize 10, weight 700, letterSpacing 2, white at 0.45), so it was a straight swap; checked at 393pt and at 320pt (the narrowest phone still around) and it fits on one line at both.
- DONE 2026-07-28 (Mirror Ball round 19 — station colour that actually shows, beams instead of stars, dark when paused, OTA): four owner asks.
  (1) "SUNSET AM SEEMS DULL" — the cause was structural, not a tuning number. `SHADE_ANCHORS` is a strongly BLUE ramp (#1b2030 ... #c2cbe2), and every station's colour was being mixed INTO that navy grey. Opposite hues average toward grey however much you add, so Sunset's amber and hot pink were arriving as dusty mauve while the cool stations happened to look right. Tinting harder cannot fix it. The ramp is now rebuilt per station by `stationShade(eq)`: each anchor is flattened to a pure grey of the same brightness (killing the baked-in blue), then pushed toward a DEEPENED copy of the station's mid stop by `0.80 * (1-t)^1.6`. Two things about that curve are load-bearing — it must be steep, because a shallow one puts heavy colour in the MID tones and since `deep` is darkened that halves the ball's bright pixels (measured); and the target must be deepened, because Mountain Pass's mid stop is literally WHITE and mixing that into the shadows lifts the floor until no mirror can be dark. `shadeAt` now takes the ramp as an argument. Alongside: stationPalette's pale variant 0.42 -> 0.28 toward white (42% into white is most of a pastel, and nine of those drive every mirror's cast), its deep variant 0.34 -> 0.42, per-mirror `cast` 0.18+0.48 -> 0.24+0.56 applied at 0.95 rather than 0.72, and LAMP saturation 0.10/0.26/0.18 -> 0.20/0.46/0.36 — the beams carry most of the colour, because an unlit mirror shows almost none of its own. Measured on Sunset: mean saturation 0.239 -> 0.376 with median luminance held at 115 (round 18's approved band was 106-125). Night Run stays cyan/violet, Mountain Pass stays pure silver (sat 0.038), Coastal warm-teal.
  MEASUREMENT NOTE: the ">200 luminance" share used since round 6 PENALISES colour — a fully bright saturated orange sits near 190 and doesn't count. Once the lamps are tinted, read median luminance plus a max-channel test instead, or you will chase a number that says "dimmer" while the screen says "more vivid". Also fixed: scratchpad/ball/render.js had FLASH_CORE/FLASH_WIDTH hardcoded at the round-13 values and had silently drifted out of step with the shipped flash curve; it now reads them out of the component.
  (2) STARS -> BEAMS: `BallGlitter`, `StarDust` and the now-dead `Glint` are deleted (the owner: the tiny 2D stars "don't enhance the style" — they read as sparkles pasted over the picture). New `LightStreaks`: seven long, thin, very faint white lines at shallow angles (14-36deg), each breathing on its own slow loop. They fade out at BOTH ends via a gradient — a flat white bar has two hard stops and reads as a drawn line the moment one lands on screen — and their y positions are STRATIFIED down the screen, not seven independent rolls, which had put two beams ten pixels apart at similar angles. They sit behind the ball and deliberately do NOT travel with it: a beam comes from a fixed lamp.
  (3) PAUSED = DULL AND UNLIT: `live` now animates to 0, not 0.15. `lit` is threaded through `MirrorBall` into `MirrorFlash`, so the caught mirrors fade out and the ball drops back to its bare mosaic; `BallSheen` and `LensFlare` are wrapped in the same gate (they are light PLAYING on the ball, so nothing may still be sliding across a stopped ball); `VolumetricRays` and `BallBloom` likewise, being light LEAVING it. With the streaks, bokeh and light field already gated, a paused ball now sits in a genuinely dark room.
  (4) THE ROOM'S REFLECTIONS TRAVEL WITH THE BALL: the light field used to orbit on its own independent 26s loop, so the dots drifted while the ball was stopped and moved at their own rate while it turned. `fieldSpin`/`fieldRotate` are gone; the layer now rides `spin` itself via `fieldDrift = spin.interpolate([0,1] -> [0, winW])` — one screen-width of travel per turn, in the direction the surface travels (right). Two `LightField` copies a screen apart (`offsetX` 0 and -winW) make the wrap seamless. Same Animated.Value as the ball, so they cannot drift apart or outlive it.
- DONE 2026-07-28 (MIRROR BALL round 20 — REBUILT AS CHROME, mood becomes lighting, OTA): owner sent two reference photos (a real ball throwing dozens of thin shafts, and a lens glint) with a full brief: "the mood should control the lighting, not the mirror ball itself", "the silver chrome should always remain visible beneath the coloured reflections", "the mirror ball should almost never be the brightest thing on screen". This SUPERSEDES the colour direction of rounds 11-19 — every one of those pushed the station's colour further into the material, and the cumulative result was a coloured sphere rather than a mirrored one. Round 19's `stationShade()` is DELETED; do not reintroduce a per-station base ramp.
  MATERIAL: `SHADE_ANCHORS` is a fixed slightly-cool silver, #07080d -> #ffffff. The WIDE range is the point — a narrow ramp reads as matte plastic. Per-tile brightness scatter is pushed toward the ENDS of its range (`Math.sign(roll) * |roll|^0.68 * 4.6`) rather than clustered around the mean, because a real mirror reflects a completely different part of the room from its neighbour; the owner drew this as a checkerboard of dark and light squares and it is most of what sells chrome. Colour cast now applies to about a QUARTER of tiles (`hash01 > 0.74`) at 0.16-0.44, not all of them at 0.24-0.80 — that is what keeps bare silver visible.
  LIGHTING: `ColorCycleWash` (three full-ball colour slabs) is GONE, replaced by `ColourReflections` — five soft coloured patches, r 13-28 in a 100 viewBox, each breathing on its own period. Radius matters: at r=40 one patch is 80% of the diameter and you are straight back to a tinted sphere. They do NOT ride the spin (a lamp is bolted to the room). LAMPS: the key light is now essentially white (sat 0.05); the other two carry the mood at 0.52/0.40. `dbShade`'s station-coloured stop removed and `dbWarm` (a hardcoded orange!) deleted. NeonSweep's peaks halved to 0.10-0.13 and it is now gated on `lit` — two colour systems on one surface add up to the whole-ball wash both exist to avoid.
  NEW `MirrorGlints`: eleven four-point stars (two crossed HAIRLINES plus a small hot core — anything thicker reads as a plus sign drawn on the ball) that flash for ~0.55s on their own long, mutually non-multiple periods, so the pops look random with nothing random at runtime. They sit ABOVE MirrorFlash, with a small blown-out white core on the hotspot, because both are the light itself rather than a lit surface. Placement is polar with rr <= 0.40, which keeps every arm inside the ball's clip.
  NEW `LightShafts` replaces `VolumetricRays`: 22 hairline beams radiating from the ball, whole fan turning on 74s. TWO THINGS LEARNED ON THE RENDER: (a) the first cut was up to 24px wide and read as a flat starburst — widths are now `size * (0.004 + hash^2.6 * 0.020)`, i.e. ~1-6px; (b) a single hard-edged bar still reads as a drawn line however thin, so each shaft is TWO gradients — a 6x-wide copy at 0.22 standing in for the bloom (no blur filter exists) with the bright core inside it. Each is a full-diameter view rotated about its own centre with the gradient in its top half only, because RN has no transform-origin.
  ROTATION: `BALL_SPIN_MS` 6000 -> 15000 (4 RPM, inside the owner's 3-5). Pausing now STOPS EXACTLY WHERE IT IS — the old 1100ms coast meant pause/resume never landed on the same angle. Scrub already drove the turn both ways.
  ROOM: LightField spread wider (x1.35/x1.25) and made small-and-bright with a 1.6x shadow radius — rendered fat they read as coloured bubbles floating in front of the ball; the big soft shapes are BokehField's job. Counts 12->16 and 7->9.
  HARNESS: scratchpad/ball/room.js mocks the WHOLE mode in HTML from the component's own maths (tiles via ts.transpileModule as before, plus the shaft/glint/patch parameter generation copied across) at 393x852. That is what caught both shaft problems and the bubble-dots; the tiles-only render could not have. Keep it in step with the component by hand — it is a mock, not a transpile, for everything except buildSphereTiles.
- OTA COMMAND CORRECTION + MAC SETUP (2026-07-28): the owner switched to a MacBook and asked how to ship from it. Setting it up turned up a REAL BUG IN OUR OWN INSTRUCTIONS: from Expo SDK 55 onwards `eas update` REQUIRES `--environment <name>` and IGNORES local `.env` files entirely (docs.expo.dev/eas/environment-variables/usage + /faq; the SDK<=54 fallback-to-.env behaviour is what everyone remembers and it no longer applies). Cruise FM is SDK 56, so every place that documented the bare `eas update --branch preview` was wrong — AGENTS.md and docs/launch/ota-updates.md are now fixed; `.github/workflows/ota-update.yml` was already correct. WHY IT MATTERS: without the flag the bundle publishes with EMPTY EXPO_PUBLIC_SPOTIFY_CLIENT_ID/SECRET and looks completely successful, then nobody on the channel can sign in to Spotify. Verify with `eas env:list --environment preview --include-sensitive --format short` before publishing from any new machine — PRESENCE IS NOT ENOUGH: a var stored with `secret` visibility still appears in `env:list` by NAME but eas-cli cannot read it back (only EAS Build can), so it inlines as empty while a name-only grep reports green. That was the bug in our own workflow's guard; it now asserts a readable, non-masked value and anchors the match to `^KEY=`. CONSEQUENCE FOR SECOND MACHINES: `.env` does NOT need copying between computers — the keys live server-side in EAS; `eas env:pull --environment preview` fetches them into `.env.local` if the app is to be run locally. New guide: docs/launch/mac-setup.md. Facts verified against the repo rather than guessed: React Native 0.85.3 and metro both declare `engines.node: ^20.19.4 || ^22.13.0 || ^24.3.0 || >= 25.0.0` (so "Node 20" is NOT sufficient — the patch numbers bind), `package-lock.json` is committed so `npm ci` is the right install, and `main` is 161 commits behind `claude/cruise-fm-v4wk5f` — a fresh `git clone` lands on `main`, so the checkout step is load-bearing or the owner would silently publish a months-old bundle to their phone and every TestFlight tester. Guide uses `npx eas-cli` rather than a global install (the official Node .pkg makes `npm install -g` fail with EACCES, which is where non-coders get stuck), and tells the owner to check `node -p "process.arch"` prints arm64.
- OTA COMMAND — WHAT ACTUALLY HAPPENS IF YOU FORGET `--environment` (2026-07-28, verified by reading eas-cli 21.3.0's own source, `npm pack eas-cli@21.3.0`; docs.expo.dev returns 403 through this proxy so the shipped code is the authority): the docs say the flag is "required", which reads as "it will error and you'll notice". IT DOES NOT. `update/utils.js` `environmentFlagNeededForSdk550OrGreater()` triggers an INTERACTIVE PICKER — and the picker's answer is a red herring, because `commands/update/index.js:190` keys the env loading on the raw FLAG, not the prompt: `const maybeServerEnv = environmentFromFlags ? {...await getServerSideEnvironmentVariablesAsync(), EXPO_NO_DOTENV:'1'} : {}`. `environmentFromFlags` is destructured at line 134, BEFORE the prompt at line 152 assigns to a different variable used only for update metadata. So you can be prompted, pick "preview", publish successfully, and still ship EMPTY Spotify credentials. Owner-facing rule now in both guides: IF A LIST OF ENVIRONMENTS APPEARS, THE FLAG WAS FORGOTTEN — Ctrl+C and start again. Worse in CI: that same function returns false whenever `CI` or `EAS_BUILD` is set (GitHub Actions always sets CI=true), so in the workflow there is no error, no prompt and no warning at all — only the explicit flag prevents it. Comment added at the Publish step saying exactly that.
- SECRET-VISIBILITY TRAP (same session, same method): `eas env:list` WITHOUT `--include-sensitive` returns secrets too and prints them by name with a `*****` placeholder (`utils/variableUtils.js:31`), while `loadServerSideEnvironmentVariablesAsync.js` filters secrets out (`byAppIdWithSensitiveAsync` then `.filter(({name,value}) => name && value)` — secrets come back `value: null`). So a var stored as `secret` passes a name check and publishes blank. Our workflow's guard had exactly this hole; it now uses `--include-sensitive --format short`, anchors on `^KEY=` (the old substring grep would have matched a stray `..._OLD`) and fails on an empty or `*****` value. NOT CONFIRMED and worth checking before the next publish: whether the two Spotify vars are actually plaintext/sensitive rather than secret in the `preview` environment. Builds would work either way (EAS Build can read secrets), so the working TestFlight build proves nothing about this.
- SMALLER CORRECTIONS from the same verification pass, all now fixed in docs/launch/mac-setup.md: `eas login` defaults to BROWSER login (`commands/account/login.js`, `browser` flag `default: true`) — it does not prompt for a password in the terminal, so don't tell the owner to expect one. There is NO separate Apple Silicon Node installer: nodejs.org/dist lists only `osx-x64-pkg` for macOS (a legacy label on what is now a universal .pkg) plus arm64/x64 TARBALLS, so "download the Apple Silicon .pkg" is unactionable — if `node -p "process.arch"` says x86_64 the cause is Rosetta on Terminal.app and the fix is to untick it, not to reinstall. `eas update` refuses to run on a dirty working tree and offers "Commit changes to git?" which runs `commitAllFiles: true` — the owner is told to answer NO. `eas env:pull` writes `.env.local`, and eas-cli #2834 reports `.env.local` still being read during updates despite the documented exclusion, so the guide says delete it when finished. Also: `main` is ~160 commits behind but its last shared commit is 21.07, i.e. SIX DAYS not "months" — the guide now names the concrete stake (publishing from main reverts the 27.07 background-timer crash fix onto every tester) instead of a commit count that drifts.
- DONE 2026-07-28 (Mirror Ball round 21 — soft glints + Sunset's real hues, OTA; owner shipped round 20 and sent a close-up + the flare reference): three asks. (1) GLINTS WERE HARD: the round-20 MirrorGlints were literally two 1px solid Views crossed plus a solid white circle — on device that is a plus-sign drawn on the mirrors (owner screenshot proves it). Rebuilt as ALL FALLOFF: halo+core is one radial gradient per glint (per-glint ids `dbGlintHalo${i}` — 11 separate Svg roots, duplicate ids across roots render blank), arms are LinearGradients transparent at BOTH tips, inset from the box, vertical arm reaching further than horizontal (an even cross reads as a marker). (2) TOO FAST: 170ms in / 380ms out read as strobing — now 1100ms in / 1600ms out on Easing.inOut(sin), periods 3600+0..4600ms. A glint is a slow shine, not a camera flash. (3) SUNSET LIT COLOUR: the two coloured LAMPS used stationPalette indexes 4/7 = LIGHTENED eq[1]/eq[2], so Sunset's lit mirrors came out washed coral. Now indexes 0/6 = raw eq[0]/eq[2] at sat 0.58/0.46 (Sunset: true amber + magenta), and ColourReflections weights the OUTER stops via eq[[0,2,1,0,2][i%5]] with peaks 0.20-0.36 — the outer stops are where a station's character lives; the middle one mostly averages them. Chrome-base rule from round 20 untouched: all of this is light, none of it is material. room.js mock updated in step (soft glints + patch colours).
- DONE 2026-07-28 (Mirror Ball round 22 — the dark-teal tiles on Sunset, OTA): owner screenshot showed steel-teal mirrors scattered through the silver on Sunset AM. CAUSE: round 20's "slightly cool silver" SHADE_ANCHORS — slightly cool literally means blue-tinted, invisible on cool stations, but sat over Sunset's warm backdrop those blue-greys read as TEAL. A hue that only shows on half the stations is worse than no hue. FIX: every structural colour on the ball is now DEAD NEUTRAL — SHADE_ANCHORS pure greys (#0a0a0b..#ffffff), FACET_DIRS dark stops #05060d→#060606, ball body #0a0912→#0b0b0c, LightFace base #08070f→#080809, dbShade darks neutralised, seam strokes #07070f→#08080a, meridian lit stroke #e8eeff→#eeeff2, and stationPalette's deep-variant mix target #141726 (a NAVY — it was dragging every station's deep variant off-hue) → #161617. The metallic look survives entirely on range + scatter, exactly as the round-20 note predicted. MEASURED on the room.js mock: pixels with B−R>12 inside the ball went 4.4% → 0.0% on Sunset; Night Run unchanged (its blue arrives via its own eq casts/lighting, which is the rule working). RULE: nothing structural on this ball may carry a hue — if a tint is wanted, it goes through the lighting layers.
- DONE 2026-07-28 (owner's daily-use round 1 — sheet gesture trap, YOUR STATIONS block, intro animation removed, all OTA): three reports from real use. (1) PLAYLIST SHEET DRAGGED THE PAGE AWAY: PlaylistSheet has no gestures of its own and is drawn inside screens that DO (StationDetailModal + every mode's pull-down dismiss). Whenever its ScrollView declined a vertical drag — list at top, or shorter than maxHeight 300 so never scrollable — the parent claimed it and pulled the whole page out from under the sheet. FIX: a PanResponder `gestureTrap` on the sheet body (`onStartShouldSet false`, `onMoveShouldSet` when |d|>6, refuses termination) that swallows declined drags before the parent sees them. Lives INSIDE PlaylistSheet, so every caller everywhere is fixed at once — don't re-fix this per-screen. The list still scrolls because the deeper responder is asked first. (2) STATIONS PAGE — custom stations no longer interleave with the default AM stations by frequency (26.07 behaviour): they now sit in their own "YOUR STATIONS" block under the defaults, each group still dial-ascending. New `SubHeader` row carries the railLine so the dial's left edge runs through unbroken. Verified in the real web build with a seeded `cruise_custom_stations` localStorage entry (Playwright addInitScript — the trick for testing custom-station UI on web with no device). (3) OPENING ANIMATION REMOVED: BrandIntro.tsx DELETED and unmounted from _layout. It was a JS rAF loop calling setState every 33ms during the busiest moment of boot — jagged by construction, unfixable, and the native expo-splash-screen underneath already shows the identical still logo, which is what the owner asked for. assets/images/intro/ left in the repo (orphaned; contains the layer-generation scripts) — delete whenever convenient.

Verified in the real web build with a seeded custom station. NOTE: taglines are gone from rows by design — they live in the OnAir banner and the station detail page. - DONE 2026-07-28 (CD spins like the record, OTA): the CD's horizontal swipe-scrub is replaced by the vinyl's ROTATIONAL gesture, owner's ask ('the CD should respond to the full turn like how the vinyl performs'). Drag anywhere on the disc and it follows the finger around its centre (atan2 from the case's measured centre, shortest-way-round angle diff so the ±180° seam doesn't read as a reverse spin), 360° = 5s of song — the vinyl convention — with the same light haptic tick per 5 wound seconds and the same tap-to-play/pause (judged by pixel travel, NOT degrees — near the centre a wobble reads as many degrees; the vinyl lesson). Wired through CD's existing wall-clock phase machinery (phaseRef/readPhase — never reads back a native value) and the useTrackClock scrub API. TAP TRAP: scrub.end() ALWAYS seeks, so a tap must bypass it entirely (togglePlay flips `playing` and the clock effect re-syncs) or every play/pause stutters the song with a stale seek. The disc now claims its touches on START like the record does, so swipe-down-dismiss doesn't work from the disc itself — the chevron and the area around the case still dismiss. Verified in the web build: a ¾-turn circular drag rotates the hub and the mode survives; tap toggles.
