Three fixes shipped: (1) 'Welcome back' greeted first-time users — now 'Welcome' until a cruise exists (lastCruise is the signal); (2) WakeSpotifyHint grew a second voice: `connected` prop — not-connected track-less drives get 'Visuals only for now — play music in any app and cruise on. Connect Spotify from the home page for full control.', auto-fades after ~9.5s (nothing to resolve, so it must not sit all drive); all 8 modes now pass show={playing && !track && !handoff} connected={spotify.connected}; (3) ConnectSpotifyCard carries the beta line 'Connections are in limited beta — if sign-in doesn't complete, you still get the full visual experience.' — the dev-mode 403 must read as a known limit, not a broken app. NOT FIXED, noted: the platform sheet's ✕ dismisses WITHOUT saving 'none' while Skip saves it, so two dismissal paths yield different gate behaviour later; PREMIUM badges on station-page mode chips during launch-free are deliberate (shop window).
- APPLE MUSIC — GREENLIT TO BUILD NOW (owner, 29.07: "it's almost August... best to get the app done, so we can fail faster and improve faster"). Owner-side steps written up in docs/launch/apple-music-setup.md (enable MusicKit on com.driftlore.CruiseFM App Services + have an Apple Music sub on the test phone). BUILD PLAN, in order, honouring the fingerprint rule: (1) JS-FIRST, ALL OTA-SAFE: refactor the mode-facing playback layer (useSpotifyPlayback is imported by all 8 modes + home) into a platform-agnostic hook behind musicPlatform.ts, with Spotify as the first implementation — zero native edits, shippable and testable before any build; (2) the AppleMusic implementation of that interface against a thin native bridge INTERFACE (JS side complete, returns unavailable until the module exists); (3) ONLY WHEN CUTTING THE BUILD: the native MusicKit module + app.json entitlement/usage-string/plugin edits, batched with expo-sharing + react-native-view-shot + dropping expo-audio (see NEXT FRESH BUILD list). Estimate given to owner: ~2-3 working sessions of code, 1-2 build cycles, plan-around-a-week elapsed, range 2-3 days to 2 weeks if MusicKit misbehaves. Apple review nudge advice given same day (submitted 23.07, ~6 days waiting = longer than Apple's usual 24-48h): check the submission isn't sitting in a needs-action state, then request EXPEDITED REVIEW via developer.apple.com/contact/app-store/?topic=expedite citing first-launch timing; do NOT cancel/resubmit (resets queue position).
- OWNER DECISION 2026-07-28 (music ceiling): owner was reminded of the 5-user Spotify dev quota ('that's embarrassing... the self-animating modes on top of music they can't control is a bit embarrassing when the app itself looks premium') and CONFIRMED Apple Music/MusicKit as the priority build so launch users aren't waiting on Spotify slots. Their idea, recorded: an in-app 'notify me when connections open' capture + notification when a music connection becomes available for them. MusicKit is a NATIVE integration → goes in the NEXT FRESH BUILD batch below.
- DONE 2026-07-29 (APPLE MUSIC step 1 — the music switchboard, OTA): playback is now platform-agnostic at every call site. NEW `src/utils/useMusicPlayback.ts` — the switchboard every mode + home + NowPlayingHost talks to; it calls BOTH `useSpotifyPlayback` and the new `useAppleMusicPlayback` unconditionally (rules of hooks) and returns Apple's result only when saved platform === 'appleMusic' AND the installed build carries the native module, so behaviour today is byte-identical to before. NEW `src/utils/useAppleMusicPlayback.ts` — a STRUCTURED STUB with the full interface (connected/track/contextName/shuffleOn/repeatMode/play/pause/next/prev/shuffle/repeat + `available`), documenting the native bridge contract (NativeModules.CruiseMusicKit: requestAuthorization/currentEntry/play/pause/next/previous/seekTo/setShuffle/setRepeat/playPlaylist/userPlaylists). All 13 call sites swapped mechanically (10 UI files use the hook; ShareCard/ModeActionRow/useTrackClock import the NowPlaying/RepeatMode types, now re-exported from the switchboard). Local variable is still named `spotify` in the modes — historic, renaming ~hundreds of refs was pure churn. Verified in the web build with zero page errors. NEXT STEPS for Apple Music: (2) real useAppleMusicPlayback against the bridge interface + connect flow UI + apple playlist linking; (3) the native Swift module + app.json entitlement/plugin — FRESH BUILD ONLY, batched.
- DONE 2026-07-29 (APPLE MUSIC step 2 — the whole JS side, OTA): everything except the native module now exists. NEW `src/utils/appleMusic.ts` — the peer of spotify.ts, a typed front door to `NativeModules.CruiseMusicKit` where EVERY call no-ops safely when the module is absent (`safe()` wrapper + `appleMusicAvailable()`), because this ships OTA into builds that don't have it. Playlists share the LinkedPlaylist store with Spotify, told apart by a `applemusic:playlist:<id>` uri prefix (`isApplePlaylist`/`applePlaylistId`). `startApplePlaylist` returns Spotify's StartResult vocabulary so the player's existing notices work unchanged; 'no-device' can never occur (playback is local, not a remote device). NEW real `useAppleMusicPlayback` — same shape as useSpotifyPlayback so the switchboard's callers can't tell them apart; simpler by design (no wake nudge, no start-result reporting, no playlist-context chasing — MusicKit plays on THIS phone). CRASH RULE OBEYED: its 5s poll is pollRef + AppState-gated exactly like Spotify's (bug_type 309 SIGKILL lesson; any repeating timer added here must do the same). ROUTING: `playStationMusic` in NowPlayingContext now takes an Apple branch FIRST when the build has MusicKit and the saved platform is appleMusic — a Spotify-uri playlist saved earlier returns 'no-playlist' rather than failing silently, since relinking is the fix. UI: NEW `ConnectMusicCard` (home, Apple-only, red) which NEVER auto-prompts — Apple's single dialog fires on a deliberate tap (owner decision 29.07), and it distinguishes DENIED from authorised-but-no-subscription, saying so plainly. ConnectSpotifyCard now stands down for appleMusic listeners (two connect cards = noise). PlaylistSheet lists the user's Apple playlists and HIDES the paste box for them (Apple has no shareable playlist link — offering one is a dead end). StationDetailModal copy is platform-aware, and the strict playlist gate extends to Apple ONLY when `appleMusicAvailable()` (gating a visual-companion listener would block a drive we could have shown them). VERIFIED in the web build, zero page errors, on BOTH paths: today's Spotify user (untouched) and an appleMusic-platform user on a module-less build (degrades to visuals exactly as before). Storage key is `cruisefm_platform` — worth remembering when seeding tests. REMAINING: step 3, the native Swift module + app.json entitlement/usage string — FRESH BUILD ONLY, batched.
- DONE 2026-08-01 (SHARE CARDS → FIVE DESIGN STYLES + a Pinterest pin, OTA): owner asked for design styles "like how Spotify lets users share design cards", and for the artwork to be savable as a Pinterest pin. NEW `src/components/ShareCardStyles.tsx` owns every layout; `ShareCard.tsx` keeps only the sheet, the capture plumbing and the picker. THE ONE INVARIANT: every style draws the SAME `ModeHero` (ShareModeArt) and differs only in how the card is FRAMED around it — the mode is the part nobody else has, so it is never the variable. NOW PLAYING (the shipping card, unchanged in look) = full-bleed station photograph, mode on it, song credited under. POSTER = station name as the HEADLINE at 88pt with the song demoted to a credit, photograph left much brighter (0.30/0.16/0.40/0.95 vs Now Playing's 0.46/0.30/0.52/0.90) because a poster IS the photograph. TICKET = the drive as a stub: rounded panel, dial number in dot-matrix, the mode in a window, torn perforation with two notch circles filled in the page's own dark, song printed on the counterfoil over a barcode. RECEIVER = the card as a head unit, matching the Stations page and the Tuner: brushed-metal hairlines, amber dot-matrix frequency, recessed window, dot-matrix song line, dial and red needle. MINIMAL = no photograph at all, the station's colours as a gradient, the mode floating in them, song set large with a lot of air. PIN FORMAT: same 1080 width, height 1350 (4:5) or 1620 (2:3); `FORMAT_H` is the only switch and every y in every style is derived from `cardH`, never hardcoded. SAVING: iOS shares a real PNG so the OS share sheet's Save Image puts it in Photos ready to pin; Android still shares text only until `expo-sharing` lands (already item 1 on the fresh-build list), and the sheet SAYS so rather than offering a button that won't work.
  FOUR THINGS THE OFFLINE RENDER CAUGHT, all the same class of bug — a block sized against an ASSUMPTION rather than a measurement: (1) the ticket's dial and station name shared a row with the name budgeted against a fixed 340px, but "103.5 FM" is half as wide again as "810 AM" in dot-matrix, so "Mountain Pass FM" ran straight through its own dial number — they get a row each now, which cannot collide whatever the widths; (2) the ticket's and the receiver's windows were sized off the panel's WIDTH, making them 592 and 639 tall, which pushed the counterfoil through the barcode and the song readout through the dial — both are now sized off their own HEIGHT and the hero is scaled to fit, centred; (3) a taller pin gave its extra 270px to whatever gap happened to be at the bottom, so the ticket opened a dead band above the barcode — the extra now goes into the window (0.62/0.60 of it) and into Minimal's hero scale, never into a gap; (4) Minimal at 82pt ellipsised a song title mid-word, so it is 72pt over three lines.
  IN-APP: the sheet grew a scrolling row of style chips and a Card 4:5 / Pin 2:3 toggle. TRAP: the chip ScrollView must be given a REAL width (`winW - 32`) — inside a centred column with no width of its own it sizes to its content, so five chips ran off both edges instead of scrolling. Checked in the real web build at 393x852 and 375x667, all five styles plus both formats, zero page errors; and offline across Night Run, Sunset, Mountain Pass (the pale station), a custom station with no photograph, and the no-track fallback. Poster skips its credit block when there is no live track, because the title falls back to the tagline it already prints above.
  STILL SVG PRIMITIVES ONLY, so this is a normal OTA — no view-shot, no new dependency. `heroTransform()`/`<G transform="translate(...) scale(...)">` is how a style repositions the hero; react-native-svg 15 parses string transforms (extractTransform.ts:174), and the harness emits them as plain SVG attributes so both paths agree. HARNESS: scratchpad/share/render.js now takes a job — `styles` (every design, one station), `pins` (the same at 2:3), `modes` (every mode in one style, `STYLE=` env) — and transpiles ShareCardStyles' own source, so it tests the shipped code. Run it with `NODE_PATH=/home/user/CruiseFM/node_modules`.
- THE PHONE WENT QUIET 2026-08-02 — a day of OTA updates published successfully and reached NOBODY, and the cause is a trap this repo had not written down. The owner: "I think we've been building on the wrong build I just updated to build 14 just now." Correct. MEASURED with the workflow's own diagnose mode (Actions → Ship OTA update → mode `diagnose`, which prints each iOS build's number beside its Profile and Channel — this is the tool for any "did it arrive?" question): build 14 = profile `production`, channel **production**; build 12 = profile `testflight`, channel **preview**. The production BRANCH has never carried a single update ("Recent update groups:" empty, Runtime Version N/A), so build 14 was running its own baked-in bundle from commit a1abf9f, thirteen commits behind, and every publish that day had gone to preview.
  ROOT CAUSE, and it is a documentation failure rather than a code one: the resubmission steps written on 02.08 correctly say to build the store binary with `--profile production` — but EVERY EAS build shows up in TestFlight, so the owner installed it, and a production-profile build listens on the production channel. Nothing warns you. The app simply stops improving, which is indistinguishable from "the fixes didn't work". Note this is NOT the pinned-runtime trap (runtimeVersion was 1.0.0 on both builds and matched perfectly) — the runtime was never the problem, the CHANNEL was, and the workflow's runtime guard passed happily because a preview build was indeed listening.
  RULE, now written into docs/launch/app-store-submission.md next to the build command: the owner's phone stays on the newest `testflight`-profile build; a `production`-profile build is for the store and must never be installed from TestFlight onto the testing phone. Getting back is free — TestFlight keeps the older build under **Previous Builds**, and reinstalling it rejoins the preview channel, at which point the already-published updates land on the next launch. The alternative, publishing to the production channel, is the one thing to weigh rather than reach for: it changes the app Apple is looking at if that build is in review.
  RESOLVED SAME EVENING, and the production channel was deliberately published to: the owner's point was that build 14 is the binary APPLE will run, and she wanted the reviewer to see the song picker and the mood picker rather than the barer version baked into it. The standing "never publish to production during review" note was raised first and she reaffirmed the request, so it stands as her call, not a slip — recorded here because the next person will read that rule and should know it has been consciously set aside once. VERIFIED after publishing rather than assumed: `eas channel:view production` now shows an update group at Runtime Version **1.0.0** against build 14's own Runtime Version **1.0.0**, an exact match, which is the only thing that decides whether an update is ever fetched. Worth knowing for next time: OTA is check-on-launch / apply-on-NEXT-launch, so the app has to be opened, fully closed and opened again before the change appears — on a phone or on a reviewer's device.
- APPLE MUSIC FAULTS ROUND 2026-08-04, off build 19 (OTA): the song list said "no playable songs" in a playlist that was AUDIBLY PLAYING. The screenshot located it precisely — the sheet opened and routed as Apple (so canList and the platform split worked), meaning the emptiness came from the READ. Cause, proven by reading not guessing: SongListSheet passes the FULL `applemusic:playlist:<id>` uri as the playlistId, `playAppleTrack` strips the prefix, but `getApplePlaylistTracks` handed it RAW to the bridge — whose MusicLibraryRequest id filter can match no playlist whose id literally starts "applemusic:". One-line fix (normalise inside getApplePlaylistTracks, accepting either form) and it is pure JS → OTA to build 19. RULE: any function taking a playlist identifier must accept the uri AND the bare id — the caller cannot be trusted to know which it holds.
  ARTWORK SETTLED BY THE INSTRUMENT 2026-08-04: the check reported `artwork MISSING` with songs listing fine — so MusicKit returns nil from BOTH `entry.artwork` and `song.artwork` for library-playlist tracks, a known MusicKit gap for library items. FIX (native, needs the NEXT BUILD — build 20): third fallback through MediaPlayer, which watches the same system player — `MPMusicPlayerController.applicationQueuePlayer.nowPlayingItem?.artwork` holds the actual IMAGE, rendered once per item to a cache file and returned as file:// (RN's Image loads it like any url). On MainActor; keyed on persistentID so it writes once, not per 5s poll. VERIFY after the build with the same quick check — the line should flip to `artwork yes`.
  (superseded detail) ARTWORK was still missing after the round-1 native attempt (entry.artwork then song.artwork, both nil). NOT guessed at a third time: `diagnoseAppleMusic()` in appleMusic.ts is the Apple twin of diagnoseSpotify — access status, subscription, playlist id asked, songs returned, and whether currentEntry carries an artworkUrl — surfaced via the same "Run a quick check" button in the sheet's Apple empty state. A screenshot of that output decides whether artwork is a native-returns-nil problem (next-build Swift work) or a JS mapping problem (OTA).
  IN-REVIEW CONSTRAINT: build 18 (in review) carries the same prefix bug, and the fix CANNOT be published to the production channel until the review ends (no mid-review production publishes). After approval, publish to production BEFORE the owner presses Release so day-one users never see it. Reviewer exposure is low — reading an Apple playlist requires linking one, which requires a subscription.
- ONE SERVICE AT A TIME, IN THE INTERFACE TOO 2026-08-04 (OTA): owner — "the app should pivot once Apple Music or Spotify is selected… I've accidentally pressed Spotify connect multiple times where I either unlink Spotify on accident or link Spotify when I'm using Apple Music. The link Spotify should come under the Spotify option, rather than having its own section." Correct, and it is the SAME mistake the playback switchboard was making underneath (see the both-players entry): the app offered two live services at once. `SpotifyConnectRow` sat in Profile directly under Music Platform on EVERY platform, so an Apple Music listener had a Spotify connect/disconnect control one thumb-width from the row they actually wanted. It is now gated on `platformId === 'spotify'` — the connection belongs to the CHOICE, not to the app at large. ConnectSpotifyCard on the home page already stood down for Apple listeners (checked, not assumed), so Profile was the only leak. RULE: any per-service control belongs inside that service's context and must be invisible when another service is selected.
- BOTH PLAYERS WERE RUNNING AT ONCE 2026-08-04 (OTA) — the owner's screen recording of a Spotify drive on build 22 settled this in eight frames: the transport alternated play/pause and the clock ran 0:09 → 0:06 → 0:10 → 0:11 → 0:12 → 0:13 → 0:10 → 0:15. Two sources reporting one song. CAUSE: `useMusicPlayback` passed the same `visible` to BOTH `useSpotifyPlayback` and `useAppleMusicPlayback`, and the comment above it claimed the unselected one "idles cheaply". It does not — each hook polls its own service and calls `adoptPlayState` to mirror reality, so on any build carrying MusicKit both were writing the SHARED play state from different services. Spotify said playing; Apple's leftover queue said paused; the app flapped, and the modes' animations (which gate on `playing`) lagged and stuttered along with it. FIX: `useSpotifyPlayback(visible && !useApple)` / `useAppleMusicPlayback(visible && useApple)` — one flag gates the poll, the adopt AND the AppState listener, because all three are keyed on it. Until the saved platform loads, Spotify is active, which is the historic default. LESSON: two hooks that both write shared state are not "one active, one idle" unless something actually stops the loser; a comment asserting it is not a mechanism.
- SCRUB, ROUND 2 — THE OTHER TWO MODES 2026-08-04 (OTA): routing `useTrackClock` by platform fixed CD and left VINYL broken, because Vinyl predates the shared clock and imports `seekTo` from spotify.ts DIRECTLY (two call sites: the record's rotational scrub and the seek bar). The bar moved, the record turned, and the song snapped back to its real position. CASSETTE had the identical import and was never reported — found by grepping `seekTo` across src/components once Vinyl proved the pattern, not by waiting for it. Both now call the exported `seekActive` from useTrackClock. THE RULE IS WORTH REPEATING BECAUSE IT WAS WRITTEN THE DAY BEFORE AND STILL MISSED THREE FILES: nothing below the UI layer may import a platform's transport directly. `grep -rn "from '@/utils/spotify'" src/components` is the check — anything transport-shaped in that list is a bug waiting for a second platform.
  PLAYLIST PICKER wore Spotify green on an Apple Music list (tick, active row, spinner) — it now takes Apple's red via the `apple` flag the sheet already had for hiding the paste box.
- HORIZON REBUILT: STATIC SCENE + NATIVE-DRIVER OVERLAYS 2026-08-04 (OTA) — owner: "horizon mode... currently experiencing lag — slowing down the entire app". Correct on both counts, and the cause was architectural: Horizon was the LAST mode animating by re-rendering React — a rAF loop calling `setPhase` ~15×/s, which re-rendered the ENTIRE fullscreen component (controls, action row, everything) plus a ~90-element SVG tree through react-native-svg's reconciler, all on the JS thread. A saturated JS thread lags the whole app, not just the mode — every tap and JS-driven animation anywhere queues behind it. The 24.07 25→15fps throttle halved the burn but kept the architecture. THE REBUILD: almost nothing in this scene moves — sky, sun, slats, spill, horizon line and rays are now STATIC (three stacked Svgs, drawn once per station/orientation); the only motion is (1) RollingLines — 11 plain Views on ONE looping native Animated.Value, each line's translateY/opacity/scaleY sampled into keyframe interpolations from its fixed journey (q = frac(offset + t), y = HORIZON + q^2.2·GRID_H); (2) TwinkleStars — the old per-frame star math had exactly FIVE distinct rates (0.7 + (i%5)·0.23 rad/s), so five shared loops serve every star, each star reading its bucket through its own sine keyframes; (3) the sun glow's pause-dim — the old `amp` was constant 1 while playing and 0.4 paused, i.e. never per-frame at all, so it is one animated opacity wrapper. Zero JS-thread work per frame. GEOMETRY RULE: the scene Svg's viewBox must EQUAL the window (`makeGeom(winW, winH)`, both orientations — GEOM_WIDE/GEOM_PORTRAIT deleted), because the overlay Views share the scene's numbers as screen pixels; a fixed-size geom under `slice` would rescale the Svg but not the overlays and the lines would float off the grid. THE TRAP THAT COST A ROUND: `Animated.loop(Animated.timing(v, {toValue: 1}))` — a sawtooth that RELIES on loop's reset — ran ONE pass and parked at 1 (measured: the probe's computed transform froze at the journey's exact end). Every loop proven in this app wraps an `Animated.sequence` that travels back down by itself; a sawtooth needs its restart written out — `.start(({finished}) => { if (finished && alive) run(); })` with `v.setValue(0)` at the top, no value read-backs (the Mirror Ball stall rule). Verified in the web build: lines measured moving, stars twinkling, landscape docks correctly, zero page errors. NOTE for measuring: 11 evenly-offset lines map onto themselves every cycle/11 ≈ 300ms, so two samples at a near-multiple read as "not moving" — the round-12 aliasing trap again.
- THE "NO UPDATES ARRIVING" RECOVERY PATH IS ALREADY ON THE PHONE (2026-08-04): when the owner reports updates not landing (twice today), check the server side with the workflow's diagnose mode FIRST (it verified: updates live on preview at runtime 1.1.0, builds 17-22 all listening) — and then remember that BUILD 22 BAKES IN both `UpdateCheckRow` (Profile → Settings card → "Check for updates": checks, downloads with visible status, second tap restarts into the new code) and the Profile footer version line (`appVersionLabel`: "base build" = no OTA ever applied vs "updated <date, time>" = which one). Both committed 30.07, ancestors of build 22's commit 1eacf99 — verified with `git merge-base --is-ancestor`. So there is no catch-22 of "the fix for updates ships by update": the button predates the drought. The footer line is the instrument for "which code is my phone running?" — ask for it before theorising.
- APPLE MUSIC ALBUM ART — THE PHONE DOES NOT HAVE THE PICTURE 2026-08-04 (OTA): third round on blank artwork in Vinyl and CD, and the first two both failed for the SAME unexamined reason — they asked the DEVICE for an image the device does not hold. Route 1 was MusicKit's `entry.artwork`/`song.artwork` (nil for library items, measured 04.08). Route 2 was MediaPlayer's `nowPlayingItem.artwork` (build 22+, still nil). The likely cause of route 2, and worth stating because it kills the whole family of local fixes: a STREAMED library is made of CLOUD items — the song is in the library but neither the file nor its artwork is on the phone, so there is no local image to return. Downloading every album is not something to ask of a driver. ROUTE 3, which ships: `src/utils/appleArtwork.ts` asks Apple's own public catalogue (`itunes.apple.com/search`, no key, no token, no permission — the same catalogue the song came from) and returns an https URL that `<Image>` loads like any other. PURE JS, so it reached the build already on her phone instead of waiting for build 24; the two native routes are still tried first because a local image is exact and needs no signal. NOT AWAITED BEFORE THE SONG: `chaseArtwork` runs beside the poll and patches `albumArt` into the existing track when it lands — build 21 already proved what happens when artwork sits between the poll and the title (the whole deck reads "no track" for the drive). The cache key is claimed BEFORE the chase starts so a 5s poll cannot launch the same lookup repeatedly. MATCHING IS THE RISKY PART and it is tested offline (scratchpad/artmatch.mjs transpiles the shipped file and stubs the endpoint — 15 cases): a title matches if it is equal after normalising or one contains the other (a library title is usually the shorter — "Africa" vs "Africa (Single Version)"), and THE ARTIST MUST AGREE TOO or a common title confidently returns the wrong album. NORMALISER TRAPS the tests caught: accents must be folded by an explicit map (`String.normalize('NFD')` is not worth betting on under Hermes) or "Déjà Vu" and "Beyoncé" never match; `&` must become "and"; apostrophes must be DELETED rather than turned into a word break ("Don't"/"Dont"). `artworkUrl100` is upscaled by rewriting the `/100x100bb/` path segment. Misses are cached in memory for the session (never on disk) so a failed lookup is not retried every poll; hits persist to AsyncStorage, capped at 150. NOT VERIFIED LIVE: outbound hosts are blocked from this build environment, so the response shape is from the endpoint's long-standing contract and every field read is defensive — a shape change degrades to no artwork, which is exactly today's behaviour. The quick check now prints a third line, `Catalogue artwork: found / no match`, so a future failure names its own route. PRIVACY: the policy had no Apple Music section at all, and the website's copy had drifted from the source by hand (missing a section the in-app one carried, different date) — there is now an Apple Music section covering the connection AND this lookup, and `scripts/gen-legal.mts` writes `website/` as well as `docs/legal/` so the two cannot diverge again.
- THE QUEUE-SCROLL FREEZE, ROOT-CAUSED BY THE OWNER 2026-08-04, late (OTA): her repro nailed it — "when I scroll fast to look at the queue, sometimes the station card also follows with the swipe and the card collapses to the bottom and then whole screen freezes". MECHANISM: SongListSheet presents in its own iOS window but its React tree lives inside the mode's, so a drag its list declines bubbles to the mode's dismissPan (the 03.08 (1b) finding); the 03.08 gestureTrap catches slow drags on the sheet BODY, but a FAST flick — or one starting on the sheet's backdrop, which the trap doesn't cover — still leaks. The card then dismisses UNDER the open sheet and tearing down both stacked iOS windows at once wedges the screen. FIX IS A RULE, NOT ANOTHER TRAP: while ANY sheet is open above a mode, the card's dismiss gesture STANDS DOWN — every mode's dismissPan claim is now gated on `sheetUpRef` fed from `useNowPlaying().sheetCount` (the counter AutoDim already uses; the responders are built once, so it must be a ref). All 8 modes patched identically; PlaylistSheet (mounted==open, so `useSheetOpen(true)`) and ModeSheet (`useSheetOpen(visible)`) now register in the count alongside SongListSheet and ShareCardSheet. RULE EXTENDED: any sheet that can appear over a drive registers via useSheetOpen — both so AutoDim stands down AND so the card cannot be dragged out from underneath it. Verified: full health walk green, and the 8-mode dismiss harness still passes with no sheet open (the guard must not eat normal dismissal).
- SHARE CARDS ROUND 4 — THE CARDS TAKE THEIR SHAPE FROM THE PHONE 2026-08-05 (OTA): two owner asks off device screenshots, and both dissolve the fixed 4:5/2:3 card. (1) SNAPSHOT: "remove the outer border so the share option only shares the part that is snapshotted… leave a bit of room for the border to include cruisefm.app at the bottom… 'You're listening to' is really close to the border." New `cardHeightFor(styleId, format, snapshot)` in ShareCardStyles DERIVES the card's height from the capture, so the export is the picture band edge to edge over slim strips: `SNAP_TOP 40` of air above the eyebrow, `SNAP_BOT 108` carrying cruisefm.app, `SNAP_SIDE 20` so the rounded white frame still reads as a frame. Backdrop dropped from this style (BaseWash strips only). `grabModeSnapshot`'s cropTopPt also widened +46→+44 for 8pt of in-picture air above the eyebrow — +44 is the FLOOR, below it the chevron's ring peeks into the top-right corner. (2) TICKET REBUILT STRUCTURED: "get the ticket template as the same dimensions as the snapshot mode — the ticket mode is a bit compressed for the different mode sizes." Correct: the full-bleed cover was showing only ~59% of the capture. Now header zone (`TK_HEAD 214`) / picture band / tear (`TK_TEAR 34`) / counterfoil (`TK_FOOT 352` — at 330 the barcode touched NOW PLAYING), where the band is the capture between TWO NEW crop lines that travel with the snapshot: `identBotPt` = max(insetTop,20)+92 (below the station identity — the header prints the station, which also killed the header GHOSTING visible in her 05.08 screenshots) and `songTopPt` = max(insetBottom,24)+300 (above the song block — the counterfoil prints the song and she wants the scrub out; the cut lands inside the title block's own 18pt top padding, so glyphs are hidden, the visual untouched, and the 4pt per-mode transport-margin variance is absorbed). Drawn CONTAIN at the panel's full width — no mode is ever squeezed or trimmed — with NO fades over the band at all; header and counterfoil sit on the ticket's own paper. Landscape captures fall through to the captureless drawn-hero ticket (Snapshot handles landscape better). PLUMBING RULES: cardHeightFor is THE height authority — the sheet and the styles both call it, and the ticket derives panelBottom from cardH so the two cannot disagree; the 4:5/Pin toggle hides whenever a capture exists (both styles take the phone's shape, there is nothing for it to change); `SnapshotInfo` is exported from ShareCardStyles, structurally identical to ModeActionRow's ModeSnapshot, defined twice on purpose so ShareCard doesn't import from the file that imports it. Verified offline (render.js fixture now carries identBotPt/songTopPt and `NO_SNAP=1` renders the captureless regression — unchanged) + full health walk green.
- SHARE CARDS ROUND 5 — BLACK SURROUND, TICKET PAPER, AND THE CASSETTE CROP BUG 2026-08-05 (OTA; owner: "we're getting really close"): four asks off device. (1) The card around both styles is PLAIN BLACK now (solid #000 Rect; Snapshot's BaseWash and the ticket-with-capture's BaseWash+Backdrop dropped) — "make the border just black so the snapshot and ticket stand out". Captureless cards keep the old washed look. (2) TICKET PAPER: the counterfoil sat on near-black, which would have vanished against the new black card — the whole panel now carries ONE vertical station-tinted gradient (`tkpp` — mixHex(d.wash,'#12131f',0.30) / mid 0.52 / 0.26) under the guilloche weave, so header and counterfoil are matching etched theme-coloured paper and the picture band covers the middle. Checked on Sunset (terracotta), Night Run (royal blue) and Mountain Pass (glowCol turns the white station charcoal-silver — text stays legible). Tear holes + side notches take `holeFill` = #000 with a capture so they read as punched through to the card. (3) THE CASSETTE CROP BUG WAS A MODE BUG, NOT A CARD BUG: pill tops and the song title's crown poked into Cassette's captures because `bottomPad` had drifted — Cassette AND Vinyl sat at max(insetBottom,24)+24, Equalizer at +20, while the five other modes (and grabModeSnapshot's crop lines) use **+16**. All three normalised to +16 — the 8pt shift is imperceptible in-app and makes every mode's furniture sit at identical heights, which is what point-anchored crops require. If a mode's bottom stack is ever restyled, +16 is load-bearing for the share cards. (4) `TK_HEAD` 214→246 — the picture band was "just touching" the dial number's dot-matrix (ends ~256; picture now starts at 290). Verified offline on all three stations + health walk green.
- PRE-SUBMISSION CHECKLIST 2026-08-06 — `docs/launch/pre-submission-checklist.md`, written after the second rejection at the owner's request ("write the checklist"). RUN IT BEFORE EVERY STORE BUILD. Both rejections so far were findable from the repo alone: 2.5.4 was a `UIBackgroundModes` claim injected by a plugin nobody wrote a line for, 2.1(b) was a live paywall in a no-IAP submission. Section A is copy-pasteable and was verified to run clean against this repo (resolved-Info.plist introspection, permission-string inventory, OWNER_MODE/LAUNCH_FREE, both paywall guards, tsc, version/runtime), Section B is the human half (empty Monetization tab, current screenshots, WHICH COMMIT the build carries via the workflow's diagnose mode, never install a production-profile build on the test phone), C is the build/submit commands and the bump rule, D is the in-review rules, E is what to do if rejected anyway. THE HABIT IT ENCODES: verify from the app's own resolved configuration, never from app.json or from memory of a same-day fix — build 18 was cut 19 minutes before its fix landed and nothing on Apple's side would ever have said so.
- THE "ARE YOU DRIVING?" CARD CAUGHT UP TO THE APP 2026-08-06 (OTA): owner saw it appear over Settings for the first time in months — "it has the old features. Can we update the card to the app's design". Correct: `DriveCheckCard` was the last screen still wearing the pre-July violet — a violet-rimmed slab with a violet→purple gradient CTA and a lilac steering icon, i.e. exactly what the platform picker was restyled away from on 03.08. Now the same language as everything else: card `#0a0a10` with a `rgba(255,255,255,0.12)` hairline, icon ring white-on-white-6%, the steering wheel in plain white, and the app's standard SOLID WHITE pill with dark type for "Still cruising". LinearGradient and GlossSheen imports dropped with it. Violet now survives in the app only on Switch accents. HOW IT WAS SEEN before shipping (reuse this — the card is otherwise unreachable in a test): temporarily set `PROMPT_AFTER_MS` to 4s, drive the real web build to a mode and then TOUCH NOTHING, screenshot, then revert the constant (confirm with `git diff` that neither timer appears in it). Two traps in that harness: the drive must be started from a station page's Start Drive (the stations list's "Tune in" banner does not open a mode), and you must NOT `page.goto` afterwards — a navigation reloads the web build and throws the session away, so the card can never arm.
- "SAVE IMAGE" — THE REAL CAUSE IS A MISSING PERMISSION STRING, AND IT NEEDS A BUILD 2026-08-06: the entry below (dropping the caption so the share carries one item) shipped, the owner updated, and Save Image STILL did not appear — so that theory, however well-sourced, was not the blocker. The actual cause is checkable from the app's own config rather than reasoned from iOS internals: `npx expo config --type introspect --json` showed the resolved Info.plist carried NSAppleMusicUsageDescription and NOTHING ELSE — no NSPhotoLibraryAddUsageDescription. `UIActivityTypeSaveToCameraRoll` writes to the photo library, so iOS requires that add-only usage string; without it the action is withheld from the sheet (historically it crashed the app instead, which is why the key is non-negotiable). Every other action she saw is consistent with this: Copy, Print and Assign to Contact need no photo-library access. **This is a NATIVE change and CANNOT ship over the air** — the key is added to app.json now so it lands in the next binary, with version/runtimeVersion deliberately NOT bumped (the JS is compatible with builds either side of it, so preview OTA to build 22 keeps flowing; bump both when the build is actually cut). Build 23 is in review and must NOT be disturbed for this. INTERIM ROUTE for the owner: the share sheet's "Save to Files" needs no photo permission, and Pinterest/WhatsApp/Messages all accept the card directly. LESSON, the same one as the ticket cut line: when a fix based on a mechanism doesn't move the symptom, stop reasoning about the mechanism and go read the app's own resolved configuration.
- "SAVE IMAGE" WAS MISSING FROM THE SHARE SHEET, AND WE HAD REMOVED IT OURSELVES 2026-08-06 (OTA): owner, off an iOS share sheet screenshot — "is there a way to download the card?" Her sheet showed Copy / Print / Assign to Contact and no Save Image, while our own hint read "Tap Share card, then Save Image to pin it". CAUSE, read from RN's own source rather than guessed (`node_modules/react-native/React/CoreModules/RCTActionSheetManager.mm`): `showShareActionSheetWithOptions` pushes the `message` string AND the `url` into ONE `activityItems` array, so `Share.share({url, message})` is a TWO-ITEM share — and `UIActivityTypeSaveToCameraRoll` cannot accept a text item, so iOS drops the action entirely. FIX: the card button now shares `{ url: path }` alone. Nothing is lost — the card prints cruisefm.app itself and the caption still has its own "Share as text" button — and the hint is now true, shown on every iOS share rather than only in the Pin shape. RULE: an iOS share meant to be SAVED must carry the file and nothing else; adding a caption silently removes Save Image (and Assign to Contact's presence is not evidence against this — the modern share sheet applies some built-in actions to a primary item, but Save Image is not one of them).
- SHARE CARDS ROUND 6 — THE CUT LINE IS MEASURED, NOT DERIVED + NO LANDSCAPE SHARE 2026-08-05 (OTA): (1) the ticket band's bottom cut still caught ~13pt of title crown on Vinyl/Equalizer/Cassette (owner zoom photo). The styles' arithmetic said the title's glyph top sits at +290-from-bottom; her screenshots MEASURED it at ~+313 — the real stack runs ~20pt taller than its declared style numbers (seek-bar touch padding, MarqueeText) — so `songTopPt` went +300 → **+330**, set from the measurement, with the comment in grabModeSnapshot saying so. LESSON, again: when a layout number disagrees with a device screenshot, measure the screenshot — the style sheet is not the rendered truth. (2) SHARE IS PORTRAIT-ONLY: ModeActionRow hides the share pill in landscape (`winW > winH`) — the cards are built around portrait captures and the owner pulled the option until a landscape ticket design exists ("I have to work on the ticket design again"). Re-enable by deleting that one gate when a landscape design lands. NEXT AGREED (owner, same message): once the cards are confirmed perfect, a FULL app rundown — functions, aesthetics, features, freezes/bugs/crashes — the harnesses for it are health.mjs, fresh.mjs, dismiss8.mjs and the 8-mode rotation sweep. FOLLOW-UP same evening: the +330 cut confirmed clean on device for Cassette and Vinyl, but the EQUALIZER's meter sat flush against the ticket's tear — its bars run right down to the title block, so no crop line can open a gap. Fixed IN THE MODE: `fs.vizSection` gained `marginBottom: 12`, floating the meter a step above the song title so the band keeps a strip of backdrop under the bars — then raised to **52** the same night (owner: quiet songs left the ticket band reading as empty sky over a meter glued to its bottom edge; taller bars considered and rejected by her — lift, don't stretch). A mode whose OBJECT hugs the title block will always need this treatment, not a crop tweak.
- SNAPSHOT SHARE CARDS, ROUND 2 — TWO STYLES, THE REAL THING EVERYWHERE 2026-08-04, late (OTA): the owner saw snapshots on device ("Amazing! It works now" era) and called the line-up: "remove the receiver and sleeve mode and keep the snapshot and the ticket mode". SleeveStyle, ReceiverStyle and their exclusive helpers (ModeStage, BAND_RANGE, LAMP_RED, AMBER, dmFit/dmWidth imports) are DELETED — ~400 lines. SNAPSHOT (final crop, set from the owner's own cropped reference): `CROP_TOP_FRAC 0.120 / CROP_BOT_FRAC 0.118` of a PORTRAIT capture — cuts the status strip, mode label and chevron at the top (picture starts at the YOU'RE-LISTENING-TO line) and the pill row at the bottom, KEEPING the transport; landscape captures aren't cropped. The capture travels with the screen's point size (`ModeSnapshot = {uri,w,h}` from Dimensions.get('screen')); the window is fitted CONTAIN at the visible band's aspect and the clip takes both trimmed strips. The halo glow around the window is DELETED (owner: "make sure the atmosphere stops at the edge of the screenshot border"). No song block on this style: the page IS the song block. CROP LINES ARE NOW POINTS, NOT FRACTIONS (owner, 05.08: "make sure the cut off is consistent... the photo with the vinyl mode has a good distance", plus "allow \'you\'re listening to\' in"): a fixed fraction grazed the Tuner\'s play button while giving Vinyl a comfortable gap, because the transport sits at different heights per mode while the PILLS are anchored — so `grabModeSnapshot` now computes `cropTopPt`/`cropBotPt` from the live safe-area insets against the modes\' real layout numbers: top = max(insetTop,20)+46 (clears the chevron, whose circle ends at +42, and keeps the YOU\'RE-LISTENING-TO eyebrow, which starts at +52), bottom = max(insetBottom,24)+62 (6pt above the pill row on any phone). The style divides by the capture\'s own height; the old fractions remain only as a fallback for captures without the fields. FINAL DRESS (owner, same night): NO header of any kind above the window — the page names its own station, printing it twice was clutter — the frame is a thin WHITE stroke (0.30; an accent-coloured frame was tried and rolled back on her call the same night), and the footer is the bare `cruisefm.app`. TICKET REFINED off her Circular EQ share: the full-bleed capture is BIASED DOWNWARD (`TK_TOP_FRAC 0.163`, clamped to the overflow) instead of centred — centring hid the mode's crown under the header — and the ticket's OWN song text is deleted when a capture exists (her "Holiday" ghost-layered under the ticket's "Holiday"; the screenshot carries title + seek bar itself, and she wants the scrub visible). ROUND 3 (owner, 05.08: "drag down the mode's position… leaving out the music scrub and pulling the music title and song artists' name down", plus "a lot of vignette around the ticket's edges — remove that"): `TK_TOP_FRAC` 0.163→0.05 — the capture starts just under the status bar, which pushes its own song block and seek bar OFF the panel's foot for the modes that carry them low, so the ticket's PRINTED song block returns without ghosting: NOW PLAYING/title/artist are back, bottom-anchored at `npY = panelBottom−208` (artist shares the foot row with cruisefm.app, left/right). VIGNETTE: with a capture the top fade is 232 tall at 0.74 (was 286 at 0.92) and the bottom fade is BOTTOM-ANCHORED (`panelBottom−310`, 0.90) so the picture zone between header and tear carries no shading at all. Barcode stays tucked under the tear at `perfY+42`, cover tile right. Captureless fallback keeps the original counterfoil and fades. TICKET with a capture is FULL-BLEED — the screenshot covers the whole panel edge to edge under the header/tear/counterfoil/barcode ("as if the ticket template just sits over the mode playing"; the framed window cut the mode off) — `slice` cover trims where the status bar and pills live, so the object band fills the card; captureless builds keep PhotoFill + the drawn ShareModeArt hero. Verified offline (render.js fixtures carry a 393x852 stand-in snapshot) + full health walk green.
- SNAPSHOT SHARE CARDS 2026-08-04, late (OTA — the native half has been in every build since 15): task #18 done. The share pill now PHOTOGRAPHS THE RUNNING MODE the instant it is tapped (`grabModeSnapshot` in ModeActionRow — react-native-view-shot's `captureScreen`, raced against 1.5s, every failure → null) and a new SNAPSHOT style — first chip, default when a capture exists — frames the real screenshot in the card: quiet backdrop, phone-shaped window rx46, song block, cruisefm.app footer. THE CROP IS FREE: the window's ratio (660 wide vs the tall screen) under `preserveAspectRatio slice` trims the top and bottom of the capture symmetrically — exactly where the status bar/eyebrow and the transport/pills live — so the object-and-title band survives on any phone shape with no image manipulation. CAPTURE ORDER IS LOAD-BEARING: the screen is photographed BEFORE `setSharing(true)` renders the sheet, or the screenshot would be of the sheet itself. Degradation: web/older builds/native failure → snapshotUri null → the Snapshot chip is hidden and the dispatcher falls back to Ticket, byte-identical to the pre-snapshot sheet (health walk re-run green). The drawn styles (Ticket/Sleeve/Receiver + ShareModeArt) remain both the fallback and the choices. Verified offline via scratchpad/share/render.js (fixtures now carry a stand-in `snapshotUri`); the true end-to-end capture can only be judged on device — owner has it next launch. NOT YET VERIFIED ON DEVICE: that Svg.toDataURL rasterises the file:// capture inside the exported PNG (album-art https images already do, so expected to hold; if the shared PNG's window comes out black, that is the suspect).
- THE "CD DISMISS FREEZE" WAS NEVER A BUG — IT WAS THE TEST LYING 2026-08-04, late (task #27 CLOSED): full health pass (15-step walk, 8-mode rotation sweep, 8-mode dismiss harness, fresh-user run — all green) left one deterministic repro: dismissing CD "froze" the Modes page in scratchpad/dismiss8.mjs. Forensics: the marked scroller after CD's dismissal had scrollTop 470 — EXACTLY max scroll (1322−852) — and every scroll operation on it worked perfectly. The probe tested by ADDING 200 to scrollTop, which at exact max clamps in place, so a fully-healthy page read as FROZEN. Why only CD: closing a mode returns browser focus to the card that opened it, which scrolls that card into view — Vinyl (mid-list) parks the page at 339, CD is the LAST card so it parks at exactly max. dismiss8's probe now tries BOTH directions. TWO LESSONS, both already written down once and worth repeating: a probe must not be able to pass (or fail) vacuously — test the negative direction before crying frozen; and "deterministic, one item only" should prompt "what is special about that item's POSITION", not just its code (CD's only specialness was being last in a list). The owner's real on-device freezes were the genuine bugs fixed 03.08 (terminate handlers, song-sheet gesture tree, auto-dim over sheets) — nothing remains reproducible.
- ARTWORK CONFIRMED WORKING ON DEVICE + THREE POLISH ITEMS 2026-08-04, evening (OTA): owner: "Amazing! It works now" — task #30's covers land on CD and Vinyl. Three adjustments off her screenshots: (1) TONEARM — THREE rounds ending in a SOLID STRAIGHT LINE, her words ("Just make it a solid straight line"). Round 1 hugged the pivot→stylus chord with a gentle S — "sort of jaggered"; round 2 copied her reference photo (straight tube two-thirds down, one sweep into the shell) — still not right; final: every spine point sits ON the pivot→stylus line (−9.4° off vertical), every tangent IS that line, and ARM_HEAD_A shares the same axis, so both cubics degenerate to rules and the arm is one straight rod, pivot to needle. Do not reintroduce an S without her asking. THE CONSTRAINT THAT MATTERS: the stylus landing point is load-bearing, so every reshape was SOLVED to keep S at (−0.158, 1.025)·armLen exactly — any future change must re-solve B/HEAD_A/HEAD_L together or the needle walks off the grooves. (2) SUNSET'S VINYL RING — went dusk-pink → amber '#FFA24B' → and the amber read as MUSTARD on device; the override is now DELETED so the ring falls through to eqColors[1] ('#FF6F5A'), the same peachy accent slot every other mode wears for Sunset. Lesson: don't invent a third colour for a station — the accent slot exists. (3) FIREFLIES SOFTENED — the "bounce back motion" she saw was structural: each flight was FOUR STRAIGHT LINES between random waypoints, snapping direction at every corner. Flights are now two sine harmonics per axis sampled into 18 keyframes — position AND velocity continuous everywhere including the loop seam (whole periods wrap by construction), so there is no corner to bounce off. Loops converted to explicit-restart sawtooths (the Horizon Animated.loop lesson, same day). Palette: ~half the flies now carry the station's colour nearly neat (mix 0.16-0.30 toward white vs the old 0.45-0.5), the rest stay warm-white so the swarm reads as light, not confetti. Verified: sunset ring + arm by web screenshot, firefly motion by the 48-boxes/14-moved probe.
- THE FOUND-BUT-BLANK ARTWORK BUG, SOLVED BY THE INSTRUMENT 2026-08-04 (OTA): fourth artwork round, and the upgraded quick check ended it in one screenshot — `Now playing: … — artwork yes / Backup artwork: found (file…) / Catalogue artwork: found`, ALL THREE routes healthy over a blank Vinyl label. Only one reading fits: the bridge's artwork URL is truthy but UNLOADABLE — MusicKit returns `musicKit://` scheme URLs for library artwork, renderable only by Apple's own ArtworkImage view; RN's `<Image>` draws nothing, silently, and because the string was non-empty the JS fallback chase NEVER RAN. The check itself colluded by printing "artwork yes" — it now prints the RAW url's first 34 chars, and the rule is recorded there: an instrument may never summarise the one detail under investigation. FIXES, all verified end-to-end in the web harness (scratchpad/artpipe.mjs — fake `__fakeAM` bridge via a TEMP hook in appleMusic.ts (added/removed in the same session, never committed enabled), Playwright route interception for the catalogue and image hosts; the musicKit:// case now chases and lands the cover on the deck): (1) `getAppleNowPlaying` nulls any artworkUrl not matching `^(https?|file|data):` so the chase runs; (2) the Swift `artworkURL(for:)` filters the scheme at the source — next build; (3) chase resilience: the artwork claim carries a timestamp and a missing cover is re-chased every ~20s instead of claimed once forever (a chase that died mid-flight — backgrounded app, dropped signal — used to blank the deck for the whole song), the chase's completion is deliberately NOT gated on cancelledRef (a completed lookup is valid data; dropping it left the claim blocking retries), and a network-failed catalogue lookup no longer poisons the session miss-cache (only a genuine no-match is cached). Also on record from this round: the OTA drought ended — the owner confirmed the red playlist cards, so the Profile update button works and the phone is receiving again.
- A `color` PROP ON AN ICON IS NOT THE LAST WORD, AND THAT IS WHY THE GREEN SURVIVED 2026-08-04 (OTA): owner, after the picker round above — "I still haven't got the Apple Music colour on the playlists, they're still green". Two things, and the second is the reusable one. (a) The station page's linked-playlist CARD was never touched: the previous round changed the little note icon and nothing else, so the wash, the border, the pencil and the toast were all still hardcoded green. It now takes one `PLATFORM_TINT` table (solid/wash/washHero/rim/rimStrong per platform) applied at the call site, so the card, its hero state, its icons and the toast cannot drift apart again. (b) THE ICON FIX HAD NEVER RENDERED AT ALL. react-native-vector-icons builds its style as `[{fontSize, color}, style]` — the STYLE WINS OVER THE `color` PROP — and both `styles.playlistBtnIcon` (StationDetailModal) and `ps.check` (PlaylistSheet) carried a hardcoded `color`, so a per-platform colour passed as a prop was silently discarded. Colour for these icons must come from the style, or from a style with no competing colour in it. SWEEP DONE: a script matched every `<MaterialCommunityIcons|Ionicons|…>` carrying BOTH a `color` prop and a `style={x.key}` whose stylesheet entry defines `color` — exactly one other hit (ThemeGenerator's `pv.playIcon`), and both colours there are `#fff`, so it is harmless. Re-run that sweep rather than eyeballing if a colour prop ever appears not to take. VERIFIED by forcing `appleMusicAvailable()` true in the web build (temporary, reverted) and seeding `localStorage['cruisefm_platform']` — the card measured `rgba(250,36,60,…)` on Apple Music and `rgba(29,185,84,…)` on Spotify. NOTE the platform key is stored as a BARE STRING, not JSON: seeding it JSON-encoded silently falls through to the Spotify path, which cost a run.
- THREE OFF A REAL APPLE MUSIC DRIVE 2026-08-04 (OTA): (1) SCRUB NEVER SEEKED. `useTrackClock` imported `seekTo` from spotify.ts and called it directly, so on Apple Music the record and the disc turned under the finger and the song stayed put — the gesture was fine, the seek went to an app that was not playing. New `seekActive()` in that file routes by platform (cached at module load, re-read after each Spotify seek so a mid-session switch lands on the next scrub rather than never). RULE: nothing below the UI layer may import a platform's transport directly — go through the switchboard or a router like this one.
  (2) A PLAYLIST SAVED FOR THE OTHER PLATFORM IS NOT A LINK. Stations kept showing their Spotify list while the listener was on Apple Music: it looked linked, Start Drive went ahead, and `playStationMusic` then refused it because an Apple player cannot open a `spotify:` uri — the card and the drive disagreed. Both now judge it the way playback does (`linkUsable` in StationDetailModal, `usableUri` in ModeActionRow): a link counts only if it belongs to the platform in use, the sub-line NAMES the mismatch rather than hiding it ("… is a Spotify playlist — pick an Apple Music one"), and the note icon takes Apple's red instead of Spotify's green. NOT FIXED, and worth doing properly later: linked playlists are still stored in ONE slot per station, so switching platform loses the other side's choice. Per-platform storage is the real answer.
  (3) CD FREEZE still hit-and-miss on build 21 — the terminate fixes helped but did not finish it. Task #27 stays open; the reproduction harness is scratchpad/dismiss8.mjs.
- APPLE MUSIC USES THE **SYSTEM** PLAYER, NOT THE APPLICATION PLAYER 2026-08-04 (native, build 22+): owner — "when i swipe Cruise FM to close, it closes the music also". Exactly what `ApplicationMusicPlayer` does: the queue lives in OUR process and dies with it. The deeper problem is the driving case — anything iOS does to a backgrounded app takes the music with it, and switching to Maps mid-drive is the NORMAL case, not an edge one. `SystemMusicPlayer` hands the queue to the Music app: survives force-quit, survives backgrounding, needs no background-audio capability (the one Apple rejected build 7 over), and matches the arrangement Cruise FM already has with Spotify, where their app plays and we are the visual layer. THE TRADE, accepted: the listener's Music app now shows what Cruise FM started, because it genuinely is playing it — which is also what makes the lock screen and CarPlay controls work during a drive. Implemented as ONE `private typealias CruisePlayer` so the choice lives in a single place; if it is ever reverted, `MPMusicPlayerController.systemMusicPlayer` in the artwork fallback must go back to `applicationQueuePlayer` — the two must always name the same player or artwork reads from the wrong queue.
  NOTE this may also be why artwork read as missing on builds 19-21: the MediaPlayer fallback asked `applicationQueuePlayer` for the now-playing item, and that is a different queue from the one the listener could hear if anything had shifted playback. Both now name the system player.
- REJECTED BY APPLE (SECOND ATTEMPT) 2026-08-06 — Guideline 2.1(b), App Completeness: the reviewer reached the £1.99 "Unlock Premium" paywall (their iPad screenshot shows it) in a submission with no submitted In-App Purchase products. ROOT CAUSE, proven by the workflow's diagnose mode (the instrument for "which code does build N carry?"): **build 18 = commit 5beadf1 (04.08 04:14), and the fix hiding the Profile upgrade card = 62cf67d (04.08 04:33) — landed NINETEEN MINUTES after the build was cut.** So build 18 shows "Upgrade to Premium" on Profile to every user; one tap opens the paywall. (The entry above and the 04.08 submission notes claiming build 18 "carries the hidden Premium card" were WRONG — always diagnose the commit, never trust the same-day assumption.) The 62cf67d commit message had literally predicted this rejection. FIX 06.08, belt and braces: `src/app/premium.tsx` now refuses to render any offer while `isPro` (launch-free = everyone) — renders the bare backdrop and navigates away (deferred + `canGoBack() ? back : replace('/')`, because a deep link mounts it as the FIRST screen and immediate navigation throws "Attempted to navigate before mounting the Root Layout"). Verified by deep-linking /premium in the web build: no £1.99/Unlock/Restore/trial text ever renders, lands on home, zero errors. RULE: the screen that takes money guards itself; entry-point gating alone is one forgotten button away from a rejection. RESUBMISSION = new production-profile binary from current HEAD (bakes the whole week: Apple playlist fix, share cards, freeze fixes, Swift artwork filter, big icon — which also retires the "publish to production before Release" dance for this cycle), version stays 1.1.0, runtime stays 1.1.0 (same natives as builds 19-22), build number auto-increments. Owner must also check App Store Connect → Monetization for any lingering subscription products in "Ready to Submit" and remove/park them — CHECKED 06.08: the Subscriptions tab is empty, no products ever existed, so the rejection was purely the UI offering a purchase with nothing behind it.
- SUBMITTED TO APP REVIEW (THIRD ATTEMPT) 2026-08-06: **build 23**, production profile/channel, version+runtime 1.1.0, cut from HEAD (carries the whole 04-06.08 week: paywall guard, Apple playlist fix, share cards, freeze fixes, Swift artwork filter, big icon). Resolution Center reply sent stating the app is fully free with no IAP products. RULES WHILE IN REVIEW: build 23 listens on the production channel at runtime 1.1.0 — do NOT publish to production at 1.1.0 until the review ends (preview stays safe); the owner's phone stays on testflight-profile builds and must NOT install build 23 from TestFlight. POST-APPROVAL: **PUBLISH PREVIEW'S WORK TO PRODUCTION BEFORE THE OWNER PRESSES RELEASE** (she confirmed this explicitly on 07.08). An earlier version of this note said no publish was needed because "build 23 bakes everything" — that was WRONG and is exactly the same assumption that sank build 18. Build 23 = commit 108c7e7, and six commits landed after it; two change what users see: `a45fa6e` (share the card alone so iOS offers Save Image) and `5c9d8f6` (the "Are you driving?" card restyled to dark glass). Both are JS, so a production publish at runtime 1.1.0 delivers them. `714d8fa` (NSPhotoLibraryAddUsageDescription) is NATIVE and cannot be delivered this way — it waits for the next binary along with the Save to Photos button. VERIFY AFTER PUBLISHING, never assume: `eas channel:view production` must show an update group at runtime **1.1.0**, matching build 23's own runtime, or no phone will ever fetch it.
- SUBMITTED TO APP REVIEW (SECOND ATTEMPT) 2026-08-04: **build 18**, production profile/channel, version+runtime **1.1.0**, cut from current code — carries MusicKit/Apple Music, the freeze fixes, the song list, the fireflies, the platform-picker restyle and the hidden Premium card. It REPLACES build 14 (1.0.0, submitted 02.08): the owner removed that submission from review knowingly — the queue reset was weighed and accepted, because 14 could never carry Apple Music and Spotify's 5-user cap makes MusicKit the launch story. Notes for Review kept the 2.5.4 explanation + no-sign-in walkthrough, plus one line about optional Apple Music via MusicKit. Manual release. The owner's phone stays on testflight-profile builds (17+, preview channel) — build 18 must not be installed from TestFlight.
- PLATFORM PICKER CAUGHT UP 2026-08-03 (OTA): owner spotted the "Connect Your Music" sheet still wearing the pre-July look — violet sheet rim, a violet glow orb, six brand-coloured slabs and a violet gradient CTA — months after the rest of the app went to dark glass. It is the one screen a first-time user sees before anything else, so it was setting the wrong expectation for everything behind it. Now: sheet `#0a0a10` with a white hairline (the Profile page's black), cards `rgba(255,255,255,0.05)` + a 0.12 rim, the brand colour surviving only on the icon chip, the tick and a whisper of gradient on the SELECTED card, and the CTA as the app's standard solid-white pill with dark type (GlossSheen import dropped with it). COPY WAS ALSO OUT OF DATE and that mattered more: Apple Music read "Visuals now · full control coming" — written before MusicKit existed, and truncating to "full cont…" on a real phone. With the 1.1.0 build it is simply 'Full in-app control', and the blurb now says Spotify and Apple Music both play inside Cruise FM. NOTE this update publishes at runtime 1.1.0, so it reaches build 16 and later ONLY — build 12 and the production build 14 are on 1.0.0 and will never see it.
- APPLE MUSIC STEP 3 — THE NATIVE MODULE 2026-08-03 (FRESH BUILD REQUIRED, not OTA): `modules/cruise-music-kit/` implements the bridge contract `src/utils/appleMusic.ts` has documented since 29.07, name for name. It is an EXPO local module (expo-module.config.json + podspec + one Swift file), autolinked — verified with `npx expo-modules-autolinking search --platform apple`, which lists it. CONSEQUENCE WORTH KNOWING: an Expo module is NOT in React Native's `NativeModules` map, so the JS lookup now goes through `requireOptionalNativeModule('CruiseMusicKit')` from expo-modules-core — that returns null rather than throwing when absent, which is the exact contract this file needs since it ships OTA into builds without the module. The legacy NativeModules lookup is kept as a fallback.
  SWIFT: MusicKit throughout rather than mixing MPMusicPlayerController with ApplicationMusicPlayer — `MusicAuthorization` for the prompt, `MusicSubscription` to tell authorised-but-not-subscribed from denied (the connect card says which), `ApplicationMusicPlayer.shared` for transport, `MusicLibraryRequest<Playlist>` for the user's own playlists. Most of it is `@available(iOS 16.0, *)`, degrading to no-ops below that. TRAP recorded in the file: a library playlist arrives WITHOUT its tracks, so `playPlaylist` must call `.with(.tracks)` before queueing or it starts nothing at all. Every function degrades rather than throws — a thrown promise mid-drive surfaces as a dead transport with no explanation.
  app.json: `NSAppleMusicUsageDescription` ONLY — set declaratively, no config plugin needed. **DO NOT ADD `com.apple.developer.applemusic`.** Build 15 failed on exactly that: "Entitlement com.apple.developer.applemusic not found and could not be included in profile. This likely is not a valid entitlement and should be removed." Native MusicKit — `MusicAuthorization` + `ApplicationMusicPlayer` + `MusicLibraryRequest`, i.e. everything this module uses — needs the usage string and NOTHING else; the user's own subscription is what authorises catalogue playback. That entitlement belongs to the MusicKit *web/REST* flow with developer tokens and a private key, which Cruise FM does not use. Corroborating signal in the same log, worth reading next time: `Synced capabilities: No updates` — EAS did not recognise it as a capability to enable either. `version` AND `runtimeVersion` bumped together 1.0.0 -> 1.1.0, which the pinned-runtime rule requires whenever native code changes — it is the only thing stopping an update landing on a build that lacks the module. VERIFIED with `npx expo config --type introspect --json`: usage string present, entitlement present, and NO UIBackgroundModes (the key that got build 7 rejected).
  BATCHED INTO THE SAME BINARY: `expo-sharing` ~56.0.23 and `react-native-view-shot` ^5.1.1. VERSION TRAP worth remembering — `npm install expo-sharing` grabbed **^57.0.8**, i.e. the SDK 57 line, because expo-* packages are versioned to their SDK; every other expo package here is ~56.x, so it had to be pinned to ~56.0.23 by hand. `npx expo install` would have picked it correctly but the registry call failed through this proxy. Android's share now goes through `Sharing.shareAsync(path, {mimeType:'image/png'})` — RN's own Share cannot attach a file there — with the text form still behind it as the fallback. react-native-view-shot is installed but NOT yet wired: having the native library in the binary is what matters, and the capture rewrite in ShareCard is pure JS, so it can ship OTA afterwards without another build.
  BUILD ON THE `testflight` PROFILE, NOT production, or the owner's phone leaves the preview channel (see the 02.08 entry). Verified before handing over with `npx expo config --type introspect --json` (usage string, entitlement, no UIBackgroundModes, 1.1.0/1.1.0) and `npx expo-modules-autolinking search --platform apple` (cruise-music-kit and expo-sharing both listed; view-shot is not an Expo module and autolinks through the RN CLI instead).
- MIRROR BALL: FIREFLIES 2026-08-03 (OTA), owner: "let's go softly into the mirror ball… add more glitter floating and moving around. It doesn't have to be moving in the same direction as the spinning sphere. It can be as simple as moving fireflies when music is playing." NEW `Fireflies` — 14 soft motes that WANDER the room, gated on `live` so they only appear with the music. THE POINT IS WHAT THEY DON'T DO: the room already had glitter that twinkles in place and a static ray burst, so the ball was the only thing moving; every earlier attempt to fix that made the room travel WITH the ball and each one ended up reading as a second light system arguing with the first (see the scrolling-field and CastReflections entries, both retired). A firefly answers to nothing, which is exactly why it sidesteps that whole trap — and it is what the owner asked for. COST: five shared loops however many are drawn. Each fly picks a loop and traces its OWN path from it, so bucket-mates never move in step; the path returns to its start at t=1, so a LINEAR loop cycles seamlessly with no retracing (a sine here would make every fly pause at the same two points — the easing has to live in the path, not the timing). Brightness reads the same value through different stops so a fly doesn't dim at a fixed point on its journey. Same soft radial-gradient recipe as GlitterSpeck — never a hard circle. VERIFIED in the web build by snapshotting every small glow box twice 2.6s apart: 48 boxes, exactly 14 changed position, i.e. the fireflies travel and the glitter correctly stays put.
- SONG-LIST SHEET LOSES ITS DRAG 2026-08-03 (OTA), after the owner reproduced the freeze exactly: "I was looking at the upcoming songs and I swiped down to close the tab but it brought the station card with me as well and now all the buttons can't work." So the 28.07-style `gestureTrap` added earlier that day was NOT sufficient on its own — the sheet's own header drag is a second downward gesture living inside the mode's React tree, and the two can disagree about who owns a swipe. HER FIX, and it is the right one: "make the cross at the top right hand corner more obvious when leaving the queue, rather than putting both the top tab end and the cross." The grabber, the `drag` PanResponder and their styles are DELETED — SongListSheet now has NO gesture of its own, so there is nothing to hand to the wrong owner, and the ✕ went 28pt/0.08 fill/16pt glyph -> 38pt/0.16 fill + rim/21pt white glyph. RULE for any future sheet that opens from inside a mode: give it a button, not a drag. The trap stays, to swallow whatever the list declines.
- RECEIVER SHARE CARD — ROOM TO BREATHE 2026-08-03 (OTA): owner, on the card (not the Tuner mode — note the two exist separately, see the 03.08 tonearm lesson): "the top station information looks cramped can we free up some space between the text?" Measured: the frequency's 124-tall digits ended NINE pixels above the station name, so the three rows read as one block. Glass GH 276 -> 330 with the rows re-spaced off GY (30 / 94 / 258). That height has to come from somewhere: the hero moved down with it and shrank (0.76 -> 0.645 of card width), and the SONG BLOCK IS NOW ANCHORED UP FROM `dialY` instead of down from the hero — hanging it off the hero is why the artist line landed on the dial's tick marks the moment the hero changed size. Verified by rendering all eight modes through scratchpad/share/render.js (`STYLE=receiver node render.js modes night-run`, then screenshot the .html — the harness writes HTML, it does not shoot it).
- TWO OFF A REAL DRIVE 2026-08-03, evening (OTA): (1) THE EQUALIZER ONLY ANIMATED IF YOU PRESSED PLAY. Owner: "it only animates when you press play even though a song is running… you'd have to stop the song and replay, but replaying takes a second — which makes it seem like Spotify snoozed off". Real, and Equalizer was the ONLY mode with it: its bar loops were driven IMPERATIVELY from exactly two places, `togglePlay` and the open effect, with a third for orientation. So every OTHER route into a playing state left the meter dead — opening onto music already going, the mini-player's play button, and the AppState foreground re-sync adopting Spotify's own state. Replaced with ONE declarative effect on `[visible, playing, isLandscape]`; `togglePlay` no longer touches the bars at all, so a new caller cannot miss it. Checked the other seven while in there: Cassette and Vinyl have real `[playing]` effects, Horizon and Circular EQ read a `playingRef` inside a continuously-running rAF loop (so a change takes effect the next frame, no restart needed), and CD/Mirror Ball gate on a `spinning` derived from playing. Only Equalizer was hand-wired.
  (2) THE MOODS WERE BURIED. Owner: "all mood backgrounds have been heavily blurred and darkened… it sort of makes the mood themes insignificant." MEASURED before touching anything, which split it into two separate causes: the pre-blurred assets in `assets/stations/blur/` retained only 4-8% of the originals' detail (matching a ~radius-8 gaussian on a 1080-wide image) while their mean luminance was IDENTICAL to the sharp originals — so the blur was the assets and the darkness was NOT. Assets regenerated at RADIUS 3.5, picked by rendering 8/5/3.5/2.5 side by side on four stations and looking: at 2.5 the detail starts competing with the mode's own object, at 5 the scene still reads as colour rather than place. 612KB -> 774KB total, all still JPEG and well inside the ~600KB-per-file rule. NOTE the live `blurRadius` prop stays unused for stations that have a blur asset — re-blurring on the main thread every re-display is what got the app SIGKILLed once already (see StationBackdrop's comment); to change the blur, regenerate the assets. The DARKNESS is the 5-stop scrim, which only EqualizerMode and VinylMode carry (the other six rely on the backdrop alone) — eased 0.20/0.15/0.30/0.46/0.58 -> 0.10/0.06/0.18/0.32/0.46, keeping most of the weight at the foot where white type and the transport sit and giving up the top two thirds, which carry nothing but the picture.
- FOUR ITEMS OFF A REAL DRIVE 2026-08-03 (OTA), and item 1 is a bug that had been latent in ALL EIGHT MODES since the day pull-to-dismiss was written.
  (1) THE PAGE FREEZES AFTER DRAGGING A CARD AWAY. Owner: "Every time I leave the station card… by dragging the card down to the bottom. The whole page likes to freeze and not let me scroll immediately… I keep swiping up and down to get the page moving, or I cross out the song playing, or I eventually leave and fully close the app." Every clause of that is a symptom of ONE cause. iOS CANCELS a touch that leaves the bottom edge of the screen rather than releasing it — and dragging a card "down to the bottom" is exactly that gesture. All eight modes' `dismissPan` defined `onPanResponderRelease` and NOTHING ELSE, so a cancelled gesture simply stopped: `slideY` stayed parked wherever the finger left it, the mode stayed `visible` with its content translated off-screen, and its transparent modal window stayed over the app. Taps fell through to the page beneath (hence the tab bar still working — which she used as her "is the app dead?" test) while scrolling did not; swiping again re-grabbed the stranded card and closed it properly; the mini-player ✕ ended the session and unmounted it; force-quitting did the same. FIX: every mode now has `onPanResponderTerminationRequest: () => false` plus an `onPanResponderTerminate` that runs the SAME `settleDismiss(g)` as release — past the threshold it closes, otherwise it springs back, so the card can never be left stranded. The close also goes through a `dismissCloseRef` now, because these responders are built once with `useRef` and were closing over the first render's `winH` (wrong after a rotation). CD's own disc responder had a related defect — its terminate branch sprang back unconditionally instead of settling — and now shares the one rule via `settleDismissRef`.
  STILL OPEN, AND REPRODUCIBLE: in the web build, dismissing **CD** leaves the Modes page's scroller refusing `scrollTop` — deterministically, CD only (the other seven pass), not order-dependent, not transient at +4s, with no full-screen layer left behind, no stranded modal, and `body{overflow:hidden}` identical across all eight so that is not it. Harness scratchpad/dismiss8.mjs (opens each mode, drags off the bottom edge, then probes every scrollable div). CD is the only mode whose object claims touches on START with capture and refuses termination, which is the obvious suspect but is NOT yet proven. Do not "fix" it by guessing — reproduce with that harness first.
  (1b) AND THE OTHER HALF OF IT, 03.08 after the fix above went out — owner: "CD worked fine it was scrollable once and then the page froze. So it's kind of on and off", plus "when I scroll through to change songs… the card likes to also scroll down with the tab". Her screenshot shows the Modes page visible ABOVE an open mode, i.e. the card sitting ~130pt down its own screen with the song sheet open over it. SCROLLING THE SONG LIST WAS DRAGGING THE MODE. iOS presents SongListSheet in its own window, but RN's responder system is TREE-based, not window-based, and the sheet's React tree sits inside the mode's — so a drag the ScrollView declined (list already at its top) bubbled up to the mode's `dismissPan`. That also explains the "on and off" freeze: the card gets left part-way down without ever crossing the dismiss threshold, which is the stranded state from (1) by a different route. FIX: the same `gestureTrap` PlaylistSheet got on 28.07, on the sheet body — the list still scrolls (deeper responder asked first) and the header's drag-to-close still wins. NOT REPRODUCIBLE ON WEB: a control run with the trap removed also held the card, so RN Web does not model this. Shipped on the strength of the mechanism plus the 28.07 precedent, not on a passing test — if the card still drifts, that is the thing to re-examine.
  (1c) MODES TAB WASN'T STARTING THE MUSIC. Owner: "the redirect doesn't open for … modes page -> stations mood -> plain mode". Correct, and the redirect was a symptom rather than the bug: `modes.tsx` called `np.open(..., { paused: !pending.locked })`, so a free/owned mode opened IDLE and `startStationMusic` never ran — no playlist, no wake, no music. That flag made sense while tapping a mode meant "let me look at this one", but since the mood sheet landed (03.08) you choose a mode AND a station before anything opens, which is a drive by any reading. Now opens unpaused, exactly like the Stations page. Note the old expression was also backwards on its face: previews (locked) auto-played while the modes you actually own did not.
  (2) MOOD PICKER HID THE TAGLINE. `StationSheet`'s active row was a solid `#ffffff` fill, and `tagline` is `rgba(255,255,255,0.42)` — white at 42% ON white, i.e. literally invisible, so the station you had chosen was the one you could read least. Replaced with the Stations page's own "tuned" language: a 4px bar down the left edge in the station's accent, an accent rim, an accent checkmark, name and dial to full white, tagline lifted to 0.66. Row keeps the dark glass, so every row is the same colour, the same height and legible. The row needs `overflow:'hidden'` for the edge bar to clip to its 14px radius.
  (3b) REDIRECT ON THE FIRST PLAY, owner's refinement 03.08 after the conditional version above: "I only want the redirect for the first play. Like a new opened app or even after a time limit." NEW `src/utils/spotifyWake.ts`, and the whole design is in when it does NOT fire — a redirect on every play would throw people out mid-drive, which is the opposite of what it is for. Two signals only, both meaning "Spotify has probably dozed off": the first play after a COLD START (module state, so it resets itself), and the first play after the app has been BACKGROUNDED for more than 15 minutes (AppState listener). A mid-drive station switch is neither, so it stays out of the way exactly where the music is definitely already awake. `markSpotifyWoken()` is called by BOTH branches, so one redirect per stretch of use at most.
  NOT A HANDOFF, and this is the load-bearing part: `handoff` is only ever cleared by the NEXT start attempt (`reportStartResult`), so marking a proactive redirect as one would drop the listener back into a "Playing in Spotify" panel with a dead transport even though Spotify was now awake and the API would work. It is a new `'waking'` StartResult instead — a plain note saying what happened, no handoff flag, transport stays live, and the existing poll adopts the track when it arrives.
  (3)+(4) SPOTIFY SNOOZING, and these were ONE bug. Owner: "at what time does Spotify like to knock off… when I switch stations Spotify likes to snooze, then it becomes a game of opening, closing and switching apps", plus a request to redirect to the Spotify playlist on the first station tap. Spotify drops off Connect once its own app has been paused and backgrounded for a while, so `startPlayback` comes back `'no-device'` — and `playStationMusic` returned that verdict STRAIGHT OUT while `'error'` and `'restricted'` fell through to `openInSpotify(uri)`. So the one case where waking Spotify was the entire fix was the one case we didn't wake it: we showed a notice telling her to go and do it by hand, which is precisely the errand worth deleting. One-line fix — `'no-device'` now falls through to the deep link too, which wakes the app AND starts the right playlist in a single tap, then the existing "Playing in Spotify" handoff panel explains itself. DELIBERATELY NOT an unconditional redirect on the first station tap as asked: throwing people out of the app even when playback would have worked is the opposite of keeping them in it, and in practice the first tap IS the no-device case, so the conditional version fires exactly when it helps.
