The `'no-device'` notice now only appears if the deep link itself fails, and says so.
- THREE BUGS OFF ONE DRIVE 2026-08-03 (OTA) — owner: "the app keeps pausing, crashing and preventing me from swiping, when I pull a card down… and when I tried to change the song, the playlists songs aren't found". Two real bugs and one non-bug, and the interesting part is that ONE sentence described two separate faults.
  (1) THE PULL-DOWN THAT PAUSED THE MUSIC. The disc's rotational gesture (03.08) claims touches on START and sets `onPanResponderTerminationRequest: () => false`, so ANY touch beginning on the disc owned the gesture for good. Pulling down therefore never reached the mode's own dismiss — and worse, a short pull fell under the 12px tap threshold on release and hit togglePlay, so the pull-down PAUSED the song. Every word of the owner's sentence, from one cause. The record has been built the same way since 22.07, so it was fixed identically. FIX: the disc's own responder now classifies the drag and owns both outcomes — within ~35 degrees of straight down (`|dy| > |dx| * 1.4`) it drives the mode's `slideY` and closes on the same 120px/0.8 thresholds as the mode's dismiss; anything else winds. The scrub no longer BEGINS on grant (it can't, until the drag is judged), so a pull-down never raises the scrub pill or seeks.
    WHAT WAS TRIED AND MEASURED FIRST, so nobody rebuilds it: a physical discriminator, since pulling down through a disc's middle is RADIAL and winds nothing while a winding drag is tangential. Two rounds of it. Swept ANGLE is useless — near the hub, four pixels of offset swings the angle through tens of degrees (the same reason the tap test is in pixels, not degrees). Arc length in pixels (`|dθ| · r`) fixes that but fails differently: the call is made after only ~16px of travel, by which point a perfectly tangential drag has wound just 16px too, so an absolute threshold called everything a pull-down. A RATIO (arc ÷ travel) is scale-free and finally behaves — and is still wrong, which the harness showed: 90px right of a 150px disc a straight-down drag genuinely IS 0.86 tangential, so physics says "wind" while the person plainly meant "leave". WHERE PHYSICS AND INTENT DISAGREE, INTENT WINS. Knowingly traded away: you cannot wind a disc by dragging straight down; you wind it with a circular sweep or a drag across, which is what anyone does anyway. Harness: scratchpad/classify.mjs replicates the classifier against real 393x852 geometry and prints arc/travel per gesture — reuse it, because THE BROWSER CANNOT TEST THIS: RN Web's event target has no `measure()`, so `centerRef` stays {0,0} and every angle in a web run is nonsense. The final rule needs no centre at all, so drag3.mjs does verify it end-to-end.
  (2) AUTO-DIM FROZE THE APP OVER AN OPEN SHEET — a third modal window. iOS allows the mode's own Modal plus ONE sheet on top; a third never presents and swallows every touch anyway. AutoDim mounts an invisible full-screen catch Modal after 30s, and `activityTick` only fires on PLAYBACK CONTROLS, so reading a song list for half a minute trips it every time. The screen dimmed (brightness really is set to 0.22), the catch layer never appeared, and the taps that leaked through to the disc paused the music. This had been latent since the share card shipped on 26.07; the song list just made 30 idle seconds inside a sheet the normal case. FIX: `sheetCount` on NowPlayingContext + `useSheetOpen(visible)`, which every modal sheet that can appear mid-drive now calls (SongListSheet, ShareCardSheet). AutoDim requires the count to be zero — it is the only one of the three the user did not ask for — and PlaybackNotice/DriveCheckCard defer rather than cancel, appearing when the sheet closes. RULE: any new Modal that can present during a drive either calls useSheetOpen or must not exist.
  (3) "THE PLAYLIST'S SONGS AREN'T FOUND" was mostly an honesty bug. `spotifyFetch` folds EVERY failure into null — offline, 4s timeout, 401, 403, 404, 429 — which is right for the polling paths (a drive must not stop to explain itself) and wrong for a screen someone is reading: `getPlaylistTracks` turned all of it into `[]` and the sheet said the playlist had no songs. NEW `spotifyFetchDetailed()` returns a reason, with its own 12s timeout — the shared 4s exists so a failed START falls over to the handoff path quickly, and it is far too impatient for a list fetched over a moving car's signal, which is the single likeliest cause of what she actually saw. getPlaylistTracks now returns a RESULT, pages to 300 songs, and asks for `total` alongside the items precisely so "genuinely empty" (total 0) can be told from "the projection returned nothing" (total > 0), which retries WITHOUT `fields` instead of reporting an empty list. The sheet names the cause in plain words and offers Try again only where retrying could help. Also: the sheet had a grabber that did nothing — pulling it down now closes it (header block only, so the list keeps scrolling).
  (4) THE TWO .ips FILES SHE SENT ARE NOT A BUG. Both bug_type 309, EXC_CRASH/SIGKILL, codes 0x0, `procRole: "Non UI"` — killed 24 and 43 minutes after launch while BACKGROUNDED, i.e. ordinary iOS reclaiming a suspended app. Audited before saying so: every repeating timer in src/ is AppState-gated (the two polls and Vinyl's spin net), which was the real 27.07 fault, and the image working set is far smaller than it looks — the stations list rows are colour glass, not photographs, so only the hero banner and the current mode's backdrop are decoded, not all ten. Don't chase these; do chase any 309 that lands with procRole "Foreground".
  (5) FOLLOW-UP, same evening: the honest message worked and she immediately asked the right question — "how can I fix this?" The read was coming back 403, and Spotify uses that one status for refusals with completely different fixes, so `classify403` now also sniffs for "scope". A 403 for INSUFFICIENT SCOPE means the token was minted before Cruise FM asked for a permission it now needs — retrying can never change that answer and only reconnecting can, so the sheet offers a Reconnect Spotify button that runs the sign-in from where she is standing (the music is Spotify's, so it keeps playing). Any other playlist 403 means Spotify is refusing that particular list, which for a playlist means one of its OWN — a Daily Mix, Discover Weekly, an editorial list — and the message says so and points at using her own playlist. NOTE the two vocabularies: the START path speaks `StartResult` and has no 'scope' member, so `startResult403()` narrows for it; only `spotifyFetchDetailed` (screens someone is reading) sees the finer answer. RULE this round established twice: a message that only names the problem is a dead end — name the fix, and where the fix is one tap, put the tap there.
  (6) AND THE OTHER HALF OF THE SAME REPORT — "during the drive I tried changing the song but the options didn't come up". `canList` required `contextUri`, which is whatever SPOTIFY reports is playing: empty until the poll answers, and empty for good whenever the context isn't a playlist. The pill then silently fell back to the playlist PICKER, so tapping it opened the wrong sheet. The station already knows which playlist it drives, so ModeActionRow now loads `getStationPlaylist(session.stationId)` itself — no new prop through eight modes and two call sites each — and the song list uses it whenever Spotify's own context isn't a playlist. Only a station with nothing linked (or Apple Music, which has no queue-source id) still opens the picker. Jumping to a track works unchanged, since the linked uri is a perfectly good context to start within. SCOPE HISTORY, checked rather than assumed: `playlist-read-private` was added 08.07 (commit 7291509), and a Spotify refresh token NEVER gains scopes it wasn't granted — it just keeps rolling — so an account that authorised before that date is still missing it today and nothing showed it until something read a playlist's contents. That is the likeliest reading of her 403 on a playlist she owns, and it also explains why the picker still listed playlists (`/me/playlists` degrades to public-only rather than failing).
  (7) BOTH GUESSES AT THAT 403 WERE WRONG, and the lesson is the round's real output. The owner reconnected repeatedly and it never helped: the message stayed on the generic branch, which means Spotify's body did NOT contain "scope", so the insufficient-scope theory is dead — and she had already ruled out the editorial-playlist theory ("Europa is my playlist I made"). Two confident diagnoses, two wasted trips for her, both argued from plausibility rather than evidence. The `fields` projection is now retried bare on any forbidden/error first page in case the projection is what is refused, but the actual fix is to STOP PARAPHRASING: `classify403Detailed` keeps Spotify's own `error.message` and `spotifyFetchDetailed`/`getPlaylistTracks` carry it through as `detail`, which the sheet prints verbatim in small italics ("Spotify said: …"). The forbidden copy no longer offers a diagnosis at all, just the fact and the reassurance that playback is unaffected. Also fixed while in there: `restricted` was being folded into `forbidden`, so a dev-mode allowlist rejection would have shown the wrong explanation entirely; it has its own message now. NOTE the body can only be read ONCE — everything that wants it goes through classify403Detailed, never a second res.text().
  (8) SOLVED 03.08, AND ONLY BY MEASURING. The diagnostic added in (7) settled it in one screenshot: `/me` 200, `/me/playlists` 200, **`/playlists/{id}` 200**, `/playlists/{id}/tracks` **403 Forbidden**, on TWO different owner-made playlists. So it was never the account, the token, the scopes or the playlist — Spotify refuses that one SUB-ENDPOINT while happily serving the playlist object beside it. Every earlier theory was about the wrong noun. THE FIX is to read the songs off the playlist object, which carries its own first page in `tracks.items`.
  AND THE TRAP INSIDE THE FIX, which cost a round on its own: that fallback was already in the shipped code and STILL failed. A bare `/playlists/{id}` expands each track's album and artists AND their `available_markets` — roughly 180 country codes per track and again per album — so a 100-song playlist arrives as MEGABYTES and reading it blew the 12s budget, landing on `reason: 'error'` and falling through to the same forbidden message. Adding `?fields=tracks(items(track(uri,name,duration_ms,artists(name))))` collapses that to kilobytes. RULE: any Spotify read that returns full track objects needs a `fields` projection, not for tidiness but because `available_markets` alone will sink it.
  WHY THE PROBE DIDN'T CATCH IT, worth remembering when writing any diagnostic: the probes only read the STATUS on success and the body on failure, so `/playlists/{id}` scored a clean 200 while the real code path was drowning in the body it never fetched. A probe that doesn't do the same work as the code proves nothing about the code. There is now a fifth line that does the real read and reports how many songs came back.
  METHOD NOTE FOR THE WHOLE ROUND: three confident diagnoses (missing scope, an editorial playlist, then a working fallback) were all wrong, and each cost the owner a trip. What ended it was shipping an instrument instead of a theory — four probes and the granted scopes, printed verbatim. Reach for that on the SECOND wrong guess, not the fourth.
  (9) AND THE ANSWER, 03.08: SPOTIFY WILL NOT GIVE A DEVELOPMENT-TIER APP A PLAYLIST'S CONTENTS, BY ANY ROUTE. The instrument settled it in one screenshot — `/me` 200, `/me/playlists` 200, `/playlists/{id}` **200**, `/playlists/{id}/tracks` **403**, the projected read off the playlist object **0 songs**, and the granted scopes printed in full: `playlist-read-private, playlist-read-collaborative, user-modify-playback-state, user-read-playback-state, user-read-currently-playing`. Every permission we need, granted, and the contents withheld anyway. So the scope theory, the editorial-playlist theory and the which-endpoint theory are ALL dead, and no amount of code changes it. Do not spend another round trying to read a playlist.
  WHAT SHIPPED INSTEAD: the player's QUEUE (`/me/player/queue`, `getPlaybackQueue()`), which is player STATE rather than playlist CONTENT and rides `user-read-playback-state` — demonstrably working, since the whole app is built on it. When the playlist read fails the sheet shows what is queued next under a "Coming up" heading that says plainly that Spotify won't share the full playlist. Tapping one still jumps, because `playTrackInContext` seeks by uri WITHIN the existing context and the queued tracks are in it. Smaller than the feature asked for and honestly labelled as such; the real fix is Apple Music, which has no such tier.
- PICK A SONG WITHOUT LEAVING THE APP 2026-08-03 (OTA): owner, after a real drive — "I wanted to change the song without always hopping over to Spotify… without having too many functions that crowd the card." Skip only walks forward and back, so reaching a particular song meant leaving. NEW `src/components/SongListSheet.tsx`, and the design is entirely about WHERE it lives: nowhere new. The middle pill already names the playlist, so tapping it now opens THAT PLAYLIST'S SONGS instead of a picker of other playlists, and "Change playlist" becomes one row at the top of the same sheet. No new pill, no sixth transport button — the card is unchanged. Deliberately plain: a list and a tap, no search, no reordering, no drag; picking a song is already more interaction than skipping and this is used at the wheel.
  PLUMBING, all of it already permitted by the existing scopes: `getPlaylistTracks(id)` (100 rows, Spotify's own page size; local tracks and podcast episodes have no uri and are dropped), and `playTrackInContext(contextUri, trackUri)` which threads an `offset` through the existing `startPlayback` so its device-waking sequence is reused. THE OFFSET IS LOAD-BEARING: start a track on its own and Spotify forgets the playlist, so next/back stop walking it and the "playing from" pill empties. `useSpotifyPlayback` now exposes `contextUri` (it already tracked it privately) and `NowPlaying` gained `uri` so the list can mark the playing row; `useAppleMusicPlayback` returns `contextUri: null` for parity, since Apple's system player exposes no queue-source id. The pill FALLS BACK to the old picker whenever there is no playlist context — companion-mode and Apple listeners see exactly what they saw before.
  MODAL OR NOT, and the rule is subtler than "never": this sheet IS a Modal, while StationSheet on the Modes tab must not be. The difference is where it opens from. Inside the player's own modal a second one works — `ShareCardSheet` has done exactly that since July — whereas from a TAB page, with `NowPlayingHost` holding a modal elsewhere in the tree, it presents an invisible touch-swallowing window. Being a Modal is also the only escape from the mode's own layer order: `AmbientGlow` is a later sibling and painted its haze straight across the list (seen on the render; a nested zIndex cannot lift a child above an outer sibling). Any modal that can appear mid-drive needs `supportedOrientations`.
  VERIFIED: the no-context fallback drives for real in the web build, and the list itself was rendered by temporarily stubbing seven tracks — the browser has no Spotify, so it is the only way to see the screen before shipping it. Stub removed after. Rotation sweep clean.
- HEALTH CHECK 2026-08-03, and the one real bug it found: `WakeSpotifyHint` sat at `insets.top + 64` in portrait while every mode puts its station identity block at `Math.max(insets.top, 20) + 52` — a block about 46 tall. Those overlap AT ANY INSET, so the hint was lying across the station's own name; I had earlier told the owner this was a browser-only artefact, which was wrong (the arithmetic was never checked). It only shows when there is no track, which is precisely what a first run looks like for anyone who hasn't connected Spotify — so it was one of the first things a new user saw. Now `Math.max(insets.top, 20) + 106`, derived the same way the modes derive theirs so it cannot drift out of step again. HARNESSES worth reusing, all in scratchpad/store: `health.mjs` (15-step smoke walk over every page, sheet, the drive flow and the mini-player, collecting page AND console errors), `fresh.mjs`/`fresh2.mjs` (brand-new-user first run — note the home hero's button reads "Let's cruise", NOT "Start Drive", and it is found by shape rather than text). TWO VACUOUS TESTS TO AVOID REPEATING: clicking where the share button "should" be passes silently because the share pill only renders when `spotify.track` exists, so it can never be exercised in the web build — check the cards with scratchpad/share/render.js instead; and a step that only catches exceptions will report `ok` for a click that hit nothing, so assert on what appeared afterwards.
- MOOD PICKER ON THE MODES TAB + CIRCULAR EQ DETAIL PASS 2026-08-03 (OTA): (1) THE DOORWAY. Owner: "I find it annoying that Night Run FM is the default — which makes me jump back to Spotify or the stations page to get the right mode." Exactly right, and it was a one-word cause: `np.open(mode, undefined, …)` from modes.tsx, and `open`'s signature defaults `stationId = 'night-run'`. New `src/components/StationSheet.tsx` — tapping a mode now asks which mood first, AM band then FM then YOUR STATIONS, each climbing its own dial, matching the Stations page's reading order. Pre-ticked with the last cruise's station (`loadLastCruise`), falling back to `defaultStationForNow()` — the hour's own pick — so the common case is one extra tap on something already highlighted. This does NOT reinstate the in-drive mood sheet dropped on 28.07 ("one pill, one job"); mid-drive the mood is still the Stations page's business. **DO NOT MAKE THIS A MODAL** — the first cut was one, so it could cover the floating tab bar, and the owner reported the app FREEZING on device the moment a mode was tapped. iOS will not stack a second Modal window over the one `NowPlayingHost` already holds for the fullscreen player, so it presented an invisible, touch-swallowing window over the app: no sheet, no response, everything dead. Exactly the trap PreviewGate hit on 24.07 ("iOS won't stack a second Modal window — card never appeared"), and the fix is the same — stay in the page. It works fine in the web build either way, so this can only be caught on device or by knowing the rule.
  CONSEQUENCES OF STAYING IN THE PAGE, both handled: the floating tab bar is a sibling rendered AFTER the screen and floats over the sheet, and so does the MINI-PLAYER when a drive is running — so the list pads past both (`TAB_SAFE_INSET + insets.bottom + extraBottom`, with modes.tsx passing `np.session ? 76 : 0`). Kept mounted through the exit animation via a `mounted` flag or it vanishes instead of sliding away, and the open animation always does `y.setValue(winH)` first from the LIVE window height: relying on whatever the value happened to hold is how a sheet ends up parked below the screen with a live backdrop over the app, which is the same invisible-blocker symptom by a different route.
  (2) CIRCULAR EQ, which had never had a detail pass and it showed — the entire mode was one soft radial halo plus 64 radial dashes. Four additions, all in service of it reading as an instrument rather than a pattern: a hairline RAIL at R0 for the bars to stand on (without a baseline they float); a SHORT INWARD mirror of each bar at 0.34 of the outward length, which is what turns a sunburst into a spectrum wrapped round a circle — deliberately short, the hollow centre is this mode's whole silhouette; PEAK DOTS riding each bar's recent maximum, the polar version of the Equalizer's peak caps the owner kept, and computed with NO per-bar state by sampling the same wave backwards through `phase` (`max` of phase, phase−0.30, phase−0.62); and a BLOOM drawn as an annulus rather than a disc, breathing with the level so the whole ring brightens on a burst. All still one `<Path>` each for 64 bars — a zero-length dash with a round cap is a dot. FOUND ON THE RENDER: `amp` can run past 1, and the longest bars were being sliced off flat against the viewBox — a straight vertical cut down the right of the ring. Everything is clamped to `R_MAX = VB/2 - 3` now.
  (3) HORIZON got its main pass earlier the same day (see below); added here only the stars twinkling, driven off the `phase` the scene already advances every frame so it costs nothing. A sky of perfectly steady dots is the one thing that gives a drawn night away.
- CASSETTE ROUND 2026-08-03 (OTA) — three items off the owner's device screenshots, and the interesting one was a WRONG DIAGNOSIS caught by measuring. (1) THE COUNTER IS GONE from the shell. A real cassette has no counter — the counter lives on the DECK — so a big digital readout floating on the plastic was the one element reading as app-on-object rather than object; it also printed straight across the `CR-02 · HIGH BIAS · MADE FOR THE ROAD` micro-text, and said exactly what the elapsed time under the seek bar already says. `timeText` removed from `CassetteBody` and all three call sites. (2) FLOATING NOTES now render BEFORE the shell instead of after, so they pass behind it; drawn on top they landed between the reels and on the top edge, and on a physical object that reads as dirt rather than atmosphere. The shell is translucent so they still show faintly through it, which is correct and much softer.
  (3) THE TWO-TONE REELS, and the method matters more than the fix. The obvious diagnosis was that the tape packs were only 0.80 opaque, so the blurred station photograph bled through and tinted them differently. Plausible, and WRONG: measured before and after raising them to 0.97, the left/right gap in R−B went 13.7 → 14.1, i.e. nothing. (First measurement was junk too — the sample squares were centred on the reel hub rather than the tape, so always measure the ANNULUS between hub edge and pack edge.) The real cause was `SHEEN`: six diagonal iridescent bands, each a DIFFERENT hue — mint, sky, lilac, pink, peach, yellow — spaced 44 apart at −18°. The reels sit at the same height but 106 apart horizontally, which at that angle is 34 units of vertical shift, almost exactly one band. So each reel sat under a different colour of the rainbow. SHEEN is now six ~90%-white tints: the bands still read as light travelling over plastic, but no band can recolour what is under it. Gap 13.7 → 5.1. Same rule the mirror ball settled on — THE MATERIAL MAY NOT CARRY A HUE; mood arrives through the shell's station tint, which is applied evenly. The 0.97 pack opacity was kept because it is physically right (you cannot see the road through wound tape), with a comment saying plainly that it did not move the number.
  STILL OPEN: the frozen clock (two shots two minutes apart, bar at ~1:46 then ~2:17, readout stuck on 01:42 in both). Not touched here — it needs a reproduction against a real track and `elapsedTxt` comes off `progress.addListener`, so a guess would be an unverified change to playback timing. Note for whoever picks it up: RN's `AnimatedValue._updateValue` always calls `__callListeners`, so `setValue` during a scrub is NOT the explanation; check instead whether `progress` is being forced native by something sharing its props node.
- APP STORE SCREENSHOTS REGENERATED 2026-08-03, AND THE MODE LABEL FIX THAT WENT WITH THEM: the owner: "it's getting hard to do these screenshots while being weary of the playlist names and marketing. The modes won't show unless I click a song most of the time." Both halves are real. The old `screenshots-appstore/` set was shot 23.07 and had gone badly stale — `05-soundwaves-raindrive.jpg` advertises SOUND WAVES, a mode retired and deleted on 25.07, so the live listing was selling a feature that no longer exists; the home and stations shots predate the receiver rebuild; and Mirror Ball and CD, the two best modes, were not on the listing at all. Regenerated all six from the real web build at 428x926 logical / deviceScaleFactor 3 = **1284x2778**, Apple's 6.5" slot and the same size as the old set. HARNESS: scratchpad/store/shot.mjs (`node shot.mjs "<station>" "<Mode>" out.png`, or `stations` for the dial page).
  THE POINT OF DOING IT THIS WAY: with no Spotify connection there is no track, so every mode falls back to the STATION'S OWN TAGLINE — no song title, no artist, no playlist name, no album art anywhere in the set, which removes the whole rights/marketing worry at source and reads better than a song title would. Set: Mirror Ball/Downtown, Horizon/After Hours, the Stations dial, Cassette/Daylight, Tuner/Night Run, Vinyl/Coastal.
  TWO TRAPS IN THE HARNESS, both cost a round: (1) **Start Drive already leaves the mode PLAYING** — pressing the transport to "start" it PAUSES it, and a paused Mirror Ball is deliberately dark and unlit (28.07), so the first set had a dead grey ball. Touch nothing. (2) The Mirror Ball rests its chrome after a few untouched seconds, so a wake tap is needed — but `(214, 200)` is NOT neutral: on Vinyl and CD that lands on the record/disc, which toggles playback, and it paused the deck. Tap `(40, 118)`: above every mode's object, clear of the chevron and the grabber. Also wait ~13s before shooting so the "visuals only for now" hint self-clears.
  SHIPPED WITH IT: `CASSETTE` and `VINYL` mode-name tags, top-left, matching the other six. Six of the eight modes had one and those two never did (Equalizer's is inline rather than via `fs.modeLabel`, which is why a grep for the shared style under-counts it) — a plain inconsistency, and it would have shown across the store set.
- HORIZON DETAIL PASS 2026-08-03 (OTA) — owner's device screenshots, and the flat sun had a CAUSE, not a tuning problem: `hzSun` fed the station's three eqColors straight into a top-to-bottom gradient, and several stations have almost no internal contrast in that ramp. After Hours is `#FF4444 / #FF1111 / #FF0000` — three near-identical reds — so the sun painted as a FLAT red disc; Mountain Pass (three whites) painted flat white. A station's eq ramp is tuned for equalizer bars and mirror facets, and is the wrong input for a big smooth gradient. FIX: `sunRamp(eq)` DERIVES five stops — a hot near-white top (`mixHex(eq[0], '#FFFFFF', 0.62)`), the station's own three through the middle, and a deep bottom mixed 0.55 toward `#140F2A`. Deepening toward a very dark INDIGO rather than black is load-bearing twice over: black flattens into a silhouette, and the cool bias is what keeps the all-white station reading as ice instead of dirt. Checked on After Hours (hot red), Mountain Pass (silver/ice over fog), Sunset (gold→coral→pink) and Night Run (blue, landscape).
  THE SLATS were the other half, and also structural: `SUN_CUTS` spaced six cuts in flat viewBox units downward from the sun's own CENTRE, but the sun is masked at the horizon and its centre sits only 0.42 of a radius above it — so "from the centre down" is the bottom THIRD of the visible disc, and three of the six cuts landed under the horizon and were never drawn. That is exactly the owner's screenshot: a solid dome with two thin slots near the bottom. `sunCuts(cy, r, horizon)` now spans from `cy - r*0.36` (the middle of the VISIBLE disc) to just above the horizon, thickening `r*0.022 → r*0.097` on a `f^1.5` curve, everything a fraction of the radius so it holds at any size. Also added: a two-stage bloom (a tight hot core AND a wide soft haze — one gradient cannot do both, widen it and the core washes out), a sky that warms toward the horizon instead of staying dead black, and the sun's light spilling onto the grid.
  THE GRID WAS STRIKING THROUGH THE TYPE — song title, seek bar and transport all sat on red stripes. Cause: the backdrop's scrim (`LinearGradient` at the top of the tree) sits UNDER the full-bleed scene layer, so it protects nothing; the scene is deliberately drawn behind the controls (31.07) and there was nothing between it and the text. Fixed in two places rather than one, because a scrim alone would have had to be heavy enough to kill the scene the owner likes: (a) the rolling lines now FADE BACK OUT in the near foreground (`near = (q-0.34)/0.66`, opacity × `1 - near²·0.86`) — their opacity grew with `q`, which is right for perspective and wrong for legibility, so they were brightest exactly where the words are; the rays take a `userSpaceOnUse` gradient stroke doing the same thing with one def for all 15. (b) a gentle second scrim ABOVE the scene, portrait only, starting at nothing at 46% of the screen. Landscape needs none — the docked panel carries its own background.
  TWO THINGS THE RENDER CAUGHT: the light spill was a Rect and its two hard vertical edges showed as seams down the sides of the road (it is an Ellipse with a radial gradient now — same lesson as the mirror ball's rim and the share card's fade bands: any hard-edged light layer eventually reads as a drawn shape); and the first slat spread still bunched into a stripe because it started at the sun's centre rather than the middle of what you can see. HARNESS: scratchpad/arm/station-mode.mjs opens any station in any mode in the real web build (`node station-mode.mjs "After Hours" Horizon out.png`) — worth keeping, the Modes tab alone can only ever show the default station. Verified after with the 8-mode rotation sweep: zero page errors, zero misfit haze canvases, no leaked transforms.
- RESUBMISSION BUILD 2026-08-02 — AND WHY `runtimeVersion` DELIBERATELY DID **NOT** MOVE: the owner had not resubmitted after the 29.07 rejection (five days out of review), so the priority became getting a binary to Apple with the fewest possible failure modes. FINDING: the rejection fix (63b9f77) removed expo-audio but never bumped `version`/`runtimeVersion`, both still `1.0.0`, which looks like a violation of the pinned-runtime rule. It is not, and the reasoning is worth keeping because the rule reads stricter than its purpose. The seatbelt exists so an update that NEEDS a new native module cannot land on a build that lacks it. Here the native delta is a REMOVAL, and nothing in `src/` has referenced expo-audio since 22.07 — so a bundle built from today's source runs identically on a build that still contains the (unused) module and on one that doesn't. No update can break in either direction. Bumping would have cost real money: the store build goes out on the `production` profile while the owner's phone is a `preview`-channel build, so a bump would have stranded her phone with no OTA until she installed a SECOND build — during the week we most need to keep shipping polish to it. So: `version` stays 1.0.0 (the app has never been released; a first release at 1.0.1 is noise on the listing) and `runtimeVersion` stays 1.0.0. `eas.json` has `appVersionSource: "remote"` + `autoIncrement: true` on the testflight/production profiles, so the BUILD NUMBER — the only thing Apple actually requires to increase for a rejected submission — is handled server-side. Net: the resubmission needed ZERO code changes, which is also why nothing else was batched into it (expo-sharing and view-shot were considered and deliberately held for the Apple Music build — a build failure tonight costs another day in the queue, and one Android user is not worth that). VERIFIED before handing it over with `npx expo config --type introspect`: no `UIBackgroundModes`, no `NSMicrophoneUsageDescription`, no mention of audio anywhere in the resolved iOS config. Owner steps written up at the TOP of docs/launch/app-store-submission.md, including the reply to paste into the App Review conversation. NOTE THE CHANNEL SPLIT for whoever picks this up: the store build is `production` profile → production channel (build 7 was too), the owner's phone and TestFlight testers are `preview`; publishing to production while in review counts as changing the app mid-review, preview stays safe.
- VINYL TONEARM REBUILT 2026-08-03 (OTA) — AND THE MISS THAT MADE IT NECESSARY: the owner sent a reference photograph of a real arm on 02.08 and said the tonearm was "a funky line"; I rebuilt the one in `ShareModeArt.tsx` — the SHARE CARD's hero — and never touched `VinylMode.tsx`, so nothing changed on the screen she actually drives with. She sent the same photograph back: "it hasn't lived up to the expectation... I asked to change it with the same image in a previous round. Let's start from scratch." LESSON, and it generalises past this file: several modes now exist TWICE — once live and once as a share-card drawing — so a note about "the vinyl's tonearm" or "the cassette's hubs" must say WHICH, and a change to one is not a change to the other. Grep for the component before assuming.
  THE OLD ARM was a vertical line with `BEND = min(17, armLen*0.08)` hooked in over the last 42% and a rounded rect stuck on the end at a fixed 18° — a stick with a kink, which is exactly what "funky" described. REBUILT, and three things carry it, all structural: (1) THE SPINE IS AN S. Module constants `ARM_A/ARM_J/ARM_B` give a point and a tangent angle in fractions of armLen (`ARM_J` is the INFLECTION), and the tube is two cubics joined with MATCHING tangents — that is what puts the inflection where it belongs; a single cubic bends wherever its control points happen to land, which is how the old one went wrong. A real S-arm leaves the bearing leaning slightly OUTWARD (+10°), bows back across the middle (−18°) and straightens into the headshell (−8°). (2) THE TUBE IS ROUND — four strokes on the SAME path (outline, body, inner light, hot hairline offset to the lit side). One flat stroke is the whole difference between a tube and a drawn line. (3) THE HARDWARE IS REAL — bearing plate with radial vents and a slotted centre screw, counterweight on a SHORT stub (set further back it reads as a lollipop), collar, wedge headshell with vent slots, mounting screws and a finger lift, then the shell's bright front FACE and the cartridge nose with the stylus at its point.
  GEOMETRY THAT IS LOAD-BEARING: everything is a fraction of armLen, so the same code serves the fullscreen deck (armLen = platSize*0.70) and the preview card. The stylus must land ~1.03 armLen from the pivot and ~0.158 of it toward the spindle — that is what puts it at 82% of the record's radius when the arm drops and 69% when the song ends, both well outside the 45% label. Change `ARM_HEAD_L`/`ARM_HEAD_A` and the needle walks onto the artwork. SVG NOTE: `rotate(a)` turns a DOWNWARD vector toward −x, so a group that should point along `armDir(θ)` is rotated by −θ; both the headshell and the counterweight rely on it.
  THREE THINGS THE OFFLINE RENDER CAUGHT: the headshell was barely wider than the tube and read as a dark blob (it is a WEDGE now — `wTop = armW*1.15` to `wBot = headW`, headW 20→26); the finger lift floated beside the shell instead of touching it (it is anchored on `edge(f)`, the shell's real half-width at that fraction, and tapered); and the shell plus cartridge merged into one long dark shape so only the needle read — the fix is the shell's BRIGHT FRONT FACE between them, not a lighter cartridge. HARNESS: scratchpad/arm/shape.mjs mocks the arm's own maths over a record at three rotations and PRINTS where the stylus lands as a percentage of the radius; it is a mock, so keep it in step by hand. Verified after in the real web build (portrait + landscape deck) and by the 8-mode rotation sweep: zero page errors, zero misfit haze canvases, no leaked transforms.
- CD MODE REVERTED 2026-08-03, same day (OTA): owner, after seeing it on the device: "undo the changes done to the CD mode, it was better previously, but keep the new album cover opacity level." So `CDMode.tsx` is restored to its 42393d4 state — the ORIGINAL `JewelCase` (full-height hinge spine, three clips, corner brackets) and the ORIGINAL `buildFan` (`|cos(ang)|^3` with the `strength < 0.04` cull, wedge opacity 0.20/0.13) — with ONLY the album-art change re-applied on top (art 0.44 → 0.60, pewter wash 0.13 → 0.09). DO NOT re-fix the "dark areas in the rainbow": those two soft sectors at the top and bottom of the disc are the shipped look and she prefers them. The slimline-case rebuild and the unbroken-sweep fan are described in the entry below purely as a record of what was tried and rejected; the cassette half of that entry DID ship and stands.
- DONE 2026-08-03 (CD + CASSETTE detail round, OTA — the CASSETTE half shipped; the CD half was reverted, see above): five owner notes off the device, all against reference photographs.
  CD (REVERTED) — (1) THE DARK AREAS IN THE RAINBOW were `buildFan`'s own shaping: `strength = |cos(ang)|^3` reaches ZERO at the top and bottom of the disc, so a third of the wedges were skipped outright by the `strength < 0.04` guard and the sweep had dead sectors. Now `0.34 + 0.66 * |cos|^1.5` — never zero, still brightest left and right (a real disc's diffraction peaks along the light's axis), unbroken all the way round. Per-wedge opacity trimmed 0.20/0.13 → 0.165/0.108 because all 64 wedges draw now where ~22 used to be culled, and the overlap was stacking into spokes. (2) ALBUM ART 0.44 → 0.60 with the pewter wash 0.13 → 0.09 ("so each song feels more personal"). (3) THE CASE rebuilt off her slimline-jewel-case photograph: generous corner radii, a double wall PLUS a tray wall inside it, an L-shaped rib at each corner, moulded lugs straddling the top and bottom walls, and the hinge clip PROTRUDING from the left. The old full-height left "spine" was the wrong part entirely — a slimline case has a clip at mid-height, not a spine. All dimensions proportional to `size`, so it holds together at any scale; the hinge lives inside a `P = size*0.052` body inset, since anything drawn outside the Svg's own canvas is clipped.
  CASSETTE — (4) THE HUB. Owner: "the inner tape circles with the teeth look sort of cheap. Realistically they're not shaped like that." Correct: the old one was spokes radiating OUTWARD from a glowing centre, which reads as an asterisk. A real hub is the opposite — a white plastic ring with a dark spindle hole punched through it and six square teeth projecting INWARD into that hole, which is what the deck's spindle grips. The teeth are hub, not gaps. (5) THE BOTTOM EDGE, the end you actually hold and the busiest part of a real shell: head window with the felt pressure pad on its leaf spring, a shield behind the tape, two pinch-roller openings with the rollers sitting in them, two capstan holes, locating holes either side, guide rollers on their posts, and the moulded lip running the full width. (6) The three `Sparkle` stars in the corners are DELETED, component and all. (7) LANDSCAPE `cassetteW` 1.30/0.60 → 1.46/0.66 — the deck docks at 0.86 scale beside the panel, so the old numbers left a band of empty table either side; it now runs to about 20px of the pane's edges.
  VERIFIED: the 8-mode rotation sweep (portrait → docked → rested → back-portrait) with zero page errors, zero misfit haze canvases and no leaked transforms.
- BALL: GLITTER 2026-08-02, latest (OTA): owner: "add some glitter, sparkles and fun. It's a mirror ball! It should look fun and pretty! Place it all over!" NEW `GlitterField` — 64 tiny lights scattered across the WHOLE screen, each twinkling in place. It obeys both standing rules at once: nothing travels (a twinkle is brightness, not motion — which is what real glitter does), and every drawn thing is gradient falloff — each speck is a soft radial dot, and the brighter ~28% carry HAIRLINE cross-flares whose gradients are transparent at both tips (the round-21 soft-glint recipe, the one drawn-sparkle construction that has survived on device; never a stroked star). COST: EIGHT shared Animated loops however many specks are drawn — each speck borrows one of 8 phases AND one of 3 response curves (peak-mid / rising / falling), so bucket-mates pop at different moments instead of blinking in unison; 24 effective twinkle patterns from 8 animations. Mounted ABOVE the vignette (specks must sparkle in the corners too), gated on `lit` like every other light. Colours: white + the three whitened eq stops.
- BALL: STATIC FINE RAY BURST, CLEAN ROOM 2026-08-02, latest (OTA) — SUPERSEDES the scrolling-field entry below. Owner, with a reference photograph of a real ball throwing a dense burst of fine beams: "it's much odd now the rays move the opposite direction of the reflections. Start with a clean background and add realistic rays coming off of the mirror ball. Don't rotate them, just make it beam finely... Remove the small reflective squares." She is right about the conflict: two light systems moving at different rates in one room always read as disagreeing — the fix was never tuning one to match the other, it was having ONE.
  `CastReflections` (the scrolling rows/squares) is DELETED — component, constants and mount. The room is now: dark wash + `LightRays` + DustField + Vignette. `LightRays` REBUILT STATIC: 44 fine filaments at UNEVEN angles and lengths (jittered spacing — a machined fan reads as a drawing), a ~20% minority of longer brighter "heroes", widths 0.014-0.045 of the ball, every ray one stretched radial gradient soft in all three directions. NO ROTATION — a real beam is bolted to the geometry of lamp and mirror; instead each ray BREATHES between 55% and 100% of its own brightness on one of SIX shared staggered Animated loops (`RAY_PHASES = 6`) — alive without motion, and six animations however many rays are drawn. Gated on `lit` like everything else.
  RULE, now proven three ways on this mode: the room may have at most ONE moving light system, and whatever moves must ride `spin`. The ball's own surface is that system; everything else holds still and breathes.
- BALL: THE ROOM IS A SCROLLING FIELD 2026-08-02, later (OTA): the ring model was right for a ceiling and wrong for this screen. Owner: "since we are facing the mirror ball head on... the background should just have the light rows move right to left at the same pace as the mirror ball, covering the entire screen... the top and bottom rows move in opposite directions, which gives me a headache." That headache is inherent to ANY rotating pattern — one pattern turning cannot have every row travelling the same way — so the rotation is gone.
  `CastReflections` is now a SCROLLING FIELD: 9 rows of patches sliding right to left, every row the same way, across the whole screen. Perspective supplies what the rotation used to: rows crowd toward the top (`yBase = winH * (0.02 + 0.98 * u^1.55)`), patches grow and SPREAD toward the foot (count `17 - k*1.35` per screen, so ~17 at the top and 5 at the bottom — the owner's "more dispersion between the light reflections"), and each row bows on a periodic curve rather than ruling a straight line. THREE copies a screen apart with `translateX 0 → -2*winW` per revolution makes the wrap seamless; the bow is evaluated from the patch's own x so every copy computes the same offset and the seam cannot show. Colours whitened much further (0.62/0.66 toward white, opacity 0.20-0.40) — "enhance the light reflections/spots to be lighter". It also mounted in the wrong place before: it lived inside the BALL's anchor box, which is why it cut off at the bottom; it is a room layer now, positioned against the screen.
  MEASUREMENT NOTE, and it finally worked: with every row travelling the same way a horizontal cross-correlation between frames IS a valid instrument (it was not when the rows opposed each other — see the entry below). Median −40px per 420ms step, left, in 9 of 11 steps. TRAP: the first two attempts read exactly 0 because the bands I sampled were dominated by STATIC UI — the notice card at the top and the transport at the foot. Sample between the ball and the song title, where nothing but the field is drawn.
  `LightRays` REPLACES the deleted MirrorBeams ("bring back the light rays"). The old ones gave every beam its own mirror and its own trajectory, so one could park near the right-hand limb and sit there — that is what "stubborn" meant. These are 18 EVENLY SPACED shafts turning as one fan with the spin: identical to each other, always moving, no single one able to go stubborn. Each is one stretched radial glow, soft along its length, across its width and at its far end.
  `UnderGlow` DELETED — the broad pool beneath the ball was the "bottom half bubble". The per-patch halo ellipses went with it: a soft disc behind every spot is what made them read as bubbles rather than light on a wall.
- BALL: THE ROOM'S LIGHT REBUILT AS TRAVELLING ROWS 2026-08-02 (OTA): owner sent a recording plus two reference photographs of real mirror balls and three asks. (1) THE SPARKLE IS GONE — the four-point star that rode inside SpotlightPan ("a light star moves across the ball making it look cheap"). Fourth time a drawn highlight on this mode has been reported; the rule holds. (2) `MirrorBeams` DELETED outright ("the light rays that live on the right is stubborn and won't be removed"). Three rounds of feathering never stopped a beam parked near the limb reading as a streak laid over the picture, and with the cast light rebuilt the room no longer needs them. The component and BEAM_COUNT are removed, not just unmounted. (3) `CastReflections` REBUILT. It was ~34 patches in one group rotated 0→720deg about the ball — a rigid clockwise pinwheel, which is exactly what she described as "light pieces circulate the ball like a circle".
  THE MODEL, and it is the whole component: a real ball throws its mirrors onto the room as a grid arranged in RINGS, and as it turns each patch travels along its own ring. THE TRICK: an ellipse is a rotated circle that has been squashed — so the patches sit on plain CIRCLES inside a view that rotates, and that view sits inside another that scales it vertically (`CAST_FLATTEN = 0.52`). Points therefore travel along ellipses: wide sweeps at the sides, tight compressed arcs at the top and bottom, which is the row structure in her photographs. Still native-driver transform only; the entire room is two nested views and ONE animated rotation. Count 34 → 120 across 5 rings (12/18/24/30/36), each patch drawn TALL (h / CAST_FLATTEN) so the wrapper's squash leaves it near-round instead of a flat dash.
  DIRECTION, and the honest part: rotation is now 0 → −720deg, i.e. ANTICLOCKWISE, so along the top rows the light travels RIGHT TO LEFT as asked. The bottom rows necessarily travel the other way — one pattern turning cannot have every row moving the same direction, and the reference photographs behave the same. It rides `spin`, so it can never disagree with the ball: stops when it stops, follows a finger drag, and stays at 2× the ball's rate because reflecting off a turning mirror doubles the angle.
  TUNING that mattered: at `reach` 0.92 of the screen and a high lift, most rings landed off-screen and the arcs never crossed the upper background — reach 0.64, lift R*0.30 puts arcs across the whole frame. MEASUREMENT NOTE: a horizontal cross-correlation between frames CANNOT verify the travel direction here and three attempts at it produced noise — the arcs curve, so patches move diagonally, and any band wide enough to sample mixes the top rows (moving left) with the bottom rows (moving right), which cancel. The rotation SIGN is the thing to reason about, not the pixels.
- DONE 2026-08-02 (HOME HEADER EQUALIZER — the bars never moved on device, OTA): owner: "the equalizer at the top of the home page STILL doesn't animate when a card is open and music is playing... looks like it's active but something is stopping the equalizer to move." The gate was fine and the label was right; the ANIMATION was the problem, and it was invisible here because it works perfectly in the browser. METHOD, reuse it: force `live={true}` in cruise.tsx, run the web build and sample the bars from the DOM (scratchpad/share/eqprobe.mjs reads each 3px bar's computed height / transform matrix ten times over 2s). Under Reanimated the heights cycled 3..24 exactly as designed — so the bug could not be the gate, `np.playing`, or the 15s poll. WHAT IT ACTUALLY WAS: `EqualizerHeader` was the ONLY component in the live app still using react-native-reanimated, AND the only one animating HEIGHT — a layout property, which cannot take the native driver. Every other animation in Cruise FM is an `Animated` transform on the native driver and every one of those runs on her phone. One component, different engine, the one property that can't be native-driven: not a coincidence worth defending. FIX: rewritten on React Native's own `Animated`, animating `scaleY` instead of height. The bar is drawn at its FULL height and scaled down; scaling happens about the centre, so it is paired with a `translateY` of `(maxH - REST_H) / 2` that puts the bottom edge back on the baseline — RN has no transform-origin, and without it short bars float. VERIFIED by measuring again: scaleY sweeps 0.14..1.00 out of phase across all seven bars, and every bar's bounding-rect BOTTOM is pinned at the same pixel in all ten samples, so nothing drifts off the baseline. Also fixed while in there: the per-bar `delay` now runs ONCE before the loop instead of inside it, where it made every bar pause at the bottom of every cycle and the row breathe in unison. Reanimated stays in package.json (react-navigation depends on it); the only remaining importers are unrouted Expo template files.
- DONE 2026-08-02 (SHARE CARDS round 5 — full-bleed photograph, and a tonearm that's a curve, OTA): three owner notes off her phone.
  (1) "MAKE THE IMAGE COVER THE ENTIRE BACKGROUND of the ticket and receiver — allow for etching and blurring for the top and bottom. But don't cut off the image." Two different things, and the split matters: the PHOTOGRAPH now covers the whole card and is allowed to be trimmed (`PhotoFill`, always cover — that is what photographs are for), while the mode's OBJECT is laid over it CONTAINed at a fixed width and is never cropped. A framed window is gone from both styles. Type reads because the top and bottom fade rather than being boxed off (`FadeBand`, fading to nothing at its inner edge — a scrim with a visible boundary is just a rectangle, the lesson the mirror ball's rim taught). The ticket's top fade carries a little of the station's colour, which is where the old opaque header band went. LAYOUT NOTE: at the panel's FULL width the ticket's hero left the counterfoil too short and the artist ran into the barcode, so the object sits at 0.95 of it; the receiver's at 0.76, growing to ~0.83 on a 2:3 pin so the extra height buys a bigger object and a lower fascia instead of a dead band of metal.
  (2) "The equalizer in the receiver mode is quite hilarious" — a set inside a set. TWO causes, both fixed: the mode used to sit in its own recessed WINDOW, so it read as a second screen inside the first (gone with the full-bleed change), and the receiver drew its own needle directly under the Tuner's. `ownDial = modeId !== 'radio'` now prints a plain scale there instead of a second working dial.
  (3) TONEARM. She sent a reference photo of a real arm and said the headshell points the right way but the tube is "a funky line" — correct, it was a straight `<Line>` with a wedge stuck on the end. It is now a real J: one cubic bezier leaving the bearing downward and bending left into the headshell, stroked THREE times on the same path (shadow, chrome, hairline catch — a tube is round, and one flat stroke is the whole difference), with a proper bearing housing, an angled counterweight, a headshell drawn from the tangent at the tip with the cartridge and stylus at its end, and a finger lift. The stylus also had to move OUT: at the first try it sat on the label, which is the one place a needle never is — it now lands about 0.55 of the way out on the grooves.
- DONE 2026-08-02 (SHARE CARDS round 4 — THE HEROES NOW MATCH THE MODES, OTA): owner, from her phone: "the image that renders from the mode — it's much of a different depiction from what the modes actually look like. The cassette mode is probably the best depiction." She was right, and the cause turned out to be ONE decision, not eight: **only VINYL and CD show album artwork on screen** (verified by grepping `albumArt` through all eight mode components — 6 hits in VinylMode, 5 in CDMode, ZERO in the other six). Every hero had been drawing the cover into the picture anyway, so the mirror ball had a square floating under it, the horizon's sun WAS the cover, the equalizer stood in front of one and the tuner had one in its panel — none of which exists in the app. Pasting the cover in was most of why each hero looked like a different product.
  THE FIX, and it is also her layout request: the cover moved OUT of the artwork and into the credit line beside the song (`CoverTile` in ShareCardStyles), and each hero was rewritten off its real component. CASSETTE is now ported coordinate-for-coordinate from CassetteMode's own 340x210 drawing (LX/RX/RY 118/224/118, pack r52, the cream paper label at 30,24,280,40 carrying "A · STEREO · C90" + the song, the ribbon down to the capstans at 74,168 and 268,168, the bottom lip, the screws). MIRROR BALL lost the cover and gained the mode's own shading model — brightness from the reflection direction r = 2(n·v)n − v against three fixed lamps, scatter pushed to the ends of its range, brick bond, hairline gaps top and bottom ONLY. EQUALIZER is 30 bell-curved bars on a baseline with white peak caps and nothing else. CIRCULAR EQ has a HOLLOW centre, which the mode's own comment insists on. TUNER is the dot-matrix display: ON AIR alone on its row, song and artist full width, then STEREO · AM/FM keys · TUNED on ONE row (where the mode puts the band button), the frequency under it, then the dial. HORIZON's sun is a GRADIENT sliced by six widening cuts at the mode's own makeGeom offsets, not the album cover. CD went to the mode's real numbers (translucent disc, art ghosted to 0.86R under a pewter wash, hub 0.155R / grippers 0.235R / hub ring 0.31R / stacking ring 0.62R) and its diffraction fan dropped to 0.065 — 50 overlapping wedges at 0.20 stack into a flower.
  NEW `ModeStage` (ShareCardStyles) draws what a mode ACTUALLY is: the station photograph full-bleed, the mode's scrim over it, the object on top. All three styles use it, so the window is a picture of the app rather than an object on a flat panel. `heroBox(x,y,w,h,'cover'|'contain')` replaces heroFit; cover is the default because a window with bars down its sides doesn't read as a picture. CONSEQUENCE, and it is a standing rule now: a window wider than 1080:740 trims the stage top and bottom, so **every hero must keep its content ≥40 units inside the stage** — the Tuner's panel lost its head at the first attempt, and the CD's jewel case had to come in from 352 to 330.
  TICKET REBUILT to the owner's own running order: header and text / rule / the mode full bleed across the whole ticket / tear / NOW PLAYING · song · album cover / barcode. Header band deepened again (0.68 → 0.80 toward black) as she asked. SLEEVE's board now carries the station photograph at 0.30 under the board tint and the grain — a flat tint was the last thing making the jacket read as a coloured rectangle rather than card off a press.
  STILL A DRAWING, NOT A SCREENSHOT. A real capture of the live mode needs `react-native-view-shot`, which is native and therefore item 2 on the NEXT FRESH BUILD list; this round closes most of the gap without it. Verified: all eight modes in all three styles, both formats, on Night Run, Sunset, Mountain Pass and a custom station with no artwork; driven in the real web build at 393x852 and 375x667 with zero page errors.
- DONE 2026-08-01 (SHARE CARDS round 3 — CUT TO THREE, then a detail pass, OTA): owner picked TICKET, SLEEVE and RECEIVER out of the eight ("8 is too many to choose from... we'll improve the graphics and design on these"). `NowStyle`, `PosterStyle`, `PolaroidStyle`, `PostcardStyle` and `MinimalStyle` are DELETED — including the original Now Playing card, so `DEFAULT_SHARE_STYLE` is now `ticket`. With three chips the row fits without scrolling, so `sc.chips` gained `flexGrow:1` + `justifyContent:'center'` (it still scrolls if the set ever grows again).
  WHAT THE DETAIL PASS ACTUALLY CHANGED, and the principle under all of it: each of these is pretending to be a physical object, and what makes an object read is the marks a MACHINE would have left on it — not more colour. TICKET: a coloured header band in the station's own light (deepened 0.68 toward black — at 0.42 it came out a bright saturated blue and read as a UI header, not card stock), a diagonal guilloche weave under everything, a stable serial number (`derive()` seeds it from station+mode+dial so the same drive always prints the same number — a serial that changed every render is the one detail that gives the whole thing away), "ADMIT ONE" set on its side down the counterfoil edge via `transform="rotate(90 x y)"` (string transforms confirmed working on device paths and in the harness), and the perforation rebuilt as PUNCHED HOLES in the card's own dark rather than a dashed stroke — a dash reads as a border style, holes read as a tear. SLEEVE: board grain (64 hairlines), RING WEAR from the record inside (the single detail that says "owned"), a shrink-wrap sheen as a soft multi-stop diagonal, a bumped corner, and a HYPE STICKER roundel carrying the dial number. RECEIVER: a moulded bezel with four slot screws, a recessed GLASS display holding the lamp row + readout with one soft reflection sweep across it, the amber readout glowing onto its own glass (an ellipse of amber under the digits), the station name inside the glass in dot-matrix, and — the one that matters — THE NEEDLE NOW SITS WHERE THE STATION REALLY IS (`BAND_RANGE` FM 87.5–108.5 / AM 530–1600, needle at the station's own dial value). Parked dead centre on every card it quietly said none of this was real.
  THREE COLLISIONS THE RENDER CAUGHT, all the same failure as rounds 1 and 2: (1) the receiver's foot had a volume knob, an etched wordmark, the install line AND the dial's band labels sharing 90px, and all four printed over each other — the knob was the least load-bearing and went; (2) the receiver's frequency and the station name inside the glass touched, so FREQ_DOT 15→13.5 and the glass grew 300→316; (3) the ticket's counterfoil block is now CENTRED between the tear and the barcode instead of hung off the tear, because pinned to the top the 2:3 pin opened a dead band under the artist.
  VERIFIED: all three styles x all eight modes offline, plus both formats on Night Run, Sunset, Mountain Pass (pale + FM, so the long name and the wide dial) and a custom station with no artwork; and driven in the real web build at 393x852 and 375x667, zero page errors.
- DONE 2026-08-01 (SHARE CARDS round 2 — THREE MORE OBJECTS, OTA): owner picked the OBJECT direction out of round 1 ("expand on the mirror ball and ticket") and asked to see it across the other modes. Three additions, all in ShareCardStyles.tsx, all drawing the same `ModeHero` as everything else: SLEEVE = a record jacket with the disc easing out of the right-hand side, station/song/artist PRINTED on the board and a catalogue number under it (CFM-<dial>); INSTANT = an instant photo, white border with a deep chin and the caption in DARK type — the inversion is the point, it is the only light card in the set and it is what stops a feed; POSTCARD = "GREETINGS FROM <station>" over the picture with a paper half underneath carrying the song, a perforated stamp holding the dial number, a postmark and address rules. The postcard is the closest fit to the product's own premise, since the mood stations are places. `SHARE_STYLES` is now eight, ordered so the object family sits together.
  NEW HELPER `heroFit(x,y,w,h)` — fits the whole 1080x740 hero stage inside an arbitrary box, CONTAIN not cover. Cropping is not available to us: the modes draw to the stage's full width (the cassette shell alone is 896 of 1080), so trimming the sides to fill a square window slices the object rather than the background.
  FIVE THINGS THE RENDER CAUGHT, and note that four of them are the SAME failure as round 1 — a block sized against an assumption: (1) the sleeve's station name and artist shared a baseline with the artist right-aligned, so a long name printed straight over it — a line each now; (2) the sleeve's disc was drawn at a record's own near-black and simply vanished into the card, taking the whole "it's a sleeve" idea with it: only a sliver clears the jacket, so that sliver has to be LIT (lifted base, sheen gradient, rim stroke, brighter rings); (3) the sleeve also has to be NARROWER than the card or the disc has nowhere to come out to — a jacket that fills the frame is just a square; (4) the instant's chin was eyeballed at 176 and could not hold two caption lines plus the artist plus the footer, so the artist fell off the bottom of the print and the footer ran through the caption — 262 now, and the caption's width budget leaves the dial stamp alone; (5) the postcard fitted its hero from 30px ABOVE the card's top edge, which clipped the vinyl's tonearm off the picture.
  PIN FORMAT: the sleeve centres the jacket AND its catalogue line as one block. Pinned to the card's foot instead, the 2:3 version stranded the sleeve in the top half with 370px of nothing under it — the same "where does the extra height go" question every style has to answer explicitly.
  VERIFIED: all eight styles x all eight modes offline (scratchpad/share/render.js, `STYLE=<id> node render.js modes <station>`), plus both formats on Night Run, Sunset and the no-artwork custom station; and driven for real in the web build at 393x852 and 375x667 through every chip, zero page errors.
- BALL: THE AWKWARD STREAK 2026-08-01 (OTA): owner's recording showed a broad band on the right with visible parallel edges and a blunt end, plus thin lines crossing near it. TWO SOURCES, both fixed. (1) `MirrorBeams`' feathered build was three stacked strips of a LENGTHWISE gradient — the gradient faded along the beam but each strip's SIDES were hard lines, and on device the widest beams read as drawn bands hanging in the room. Each beam is now ONE stretched radial glow (an Ellipse centred on the beam's root at the ball, radial gradient in bounding-box units) — soft along its length, across its width and at its far end; there is no edge anywhere to see. (2) `LightStreaks` DELETED: the seven static diagonal hairlines were hired when the room was empty; with CastReflections filling it, fixed lines crossing the scene read as scratches on the picture. RULE consolidating three rounds of this: any light drawn as a stroked or hard-edged shape eventually gets reported as an artefact ("Os", "wire cage", "awkward streak") — light layers on this mode must be pure gradient falloff.
- BALL: THE ROOM CATCHES THE LIGHT 2026-08-01 (OTA): owner's recording showed the background empty — "the reflections aren't hitting the background, it doesn't circulate around the sphere". TWO CAUSES: the vignette round had darkened the room, and the orbiting specks rotated about the SCREEN's centre (an absoluteFill transform), which sits well below the ball — so nothing ever read as circulating the sphere. `LightField` DELETED; new `CastReflections`, the thing everyone knows a mirror ball does: ~34 mirror-shaped patches of light cast onto the room, sized UP with distance (a projected square spreads) and stretched along their own radial direction (it lands at an angle, which smears it), each a soft halo ellipse under a rounded core. Mounted INSIDE the ball's anchor box so the container rotates about the ball's true centre, at 2x the ball's rate (reflection doubles the angle). ONE Svg root, static shapes, ONE animated rotation — the whole effect is a single animated view. TUNING that mattered: the first cut's patches were 2x too big with a 0.30-opacity halo and read as pills floating in front of the scene; halo 0.10, core 0.75, op 0.05-0.16, w 0.022-0.072·size. Patches are the station's LIGHT colours (two whitened eq stops + neutral).
- BALL: THE CINEMATIC ROOM 2026-07-31 (owner's full lighting brief — premium studio, not club — OTA): four additions on top of the specks/beams round, all restrained by instruction. (1) RAYS FEATHERED: MirrorBeams' laser look was two things — a near-white spark at the gradient's start and a hard edge down each side of the rect. The gradient now starts at the station colour at 0.50 (no white tip), and each ray is THREE strips of the same gradient (full-width at 0.5, middle at 0.7, core at 1.0) so the edges dissolve. Widths up ~3x (0.020–0.095·size) and peaks down to 0.14–0.36: wide-and-faint is what reads as volumetric, thin-and-bright is what read as lasers. Still per-mirror trajectories riding `spin`. (2) `Vignette`: static full-screen radial, transparent to 62% then #020208 at 0.52 in the corners, mounted over the room layers and under the chrome. (3) `DustField`/`DustMote`: 14 near-invisible motes (1.5–3.7px) drifting slowly upward through the light, each catching a brief highlight mid-loop (opacity 0.02→0.20→0.02 on a 5–10s cycle); a third are tinted toward the station colour. Deliberately dust-in-a-still-room, not snow; gated on `live`. (4) THE ONE SPARKLE: two gradient-falloff arms + nothing else, drawn INSIDE the SpotlightPan pool's own Svg so it rides the pan and only ever sits on the brightest mirrors. All soft — no stroked shapes, which is what made the old glints and lens-flare Os read as stickers. Checks: tsc clean, 3125 paths / 65 roots, open-to-settled 2.9s, rotation cycle + all pages clean.
- BALL: SPECKS ORBIT, AND A REAL BEAM BUG 2026-07-31 (OTA): (1) BUG — `MirrorBeams` gated brightness on `1 - Math.max(0, nz)`, which is 1 for the ENTIRE BACK of the ball, so every mirror round the back was throwing a full-strength beam. Added `vis = clamp(nz/0.14)` so a mirror has to be on our side to throw anything. That, plus BEAM_COUNT 15 -> 30 and shorter beams (reach winW*1.7 -> 1.0), turned "one stray bar crossing the screen" into a burst of rays round the ball, which is what the reference photographs show. (2) THE SPECKS NOW ORBIT rather than sliding sideways. The slide was a `translateX 0 -> winW` on two wrapped copies, and the owner read it as going the OPPOSITE way to the surface — a horizontal slide is ambiguous (a spot above the ball and one below appear to go opposite ways when the real motion is rotational) and the wrap is what made them read as "floating". They now rotate about the ball at TWICE the ball's rate, which is the actual physics: reflecting off a turning mirror doubles the angle, so the spots sweep twice as fast as the surface that made them. One copy instead of two, since a rotation wraps by itself.
- BALL ROUND 2026-07-31 evening (five owner notes: the Os, sphere-accurate beams, mirror specks, colour that shows, spotlight on the ball only — OTA): (1) THE "Os" WERE `LensFlare`. It drew three hollow white circles (`fill="none" stroke`) at fixed points across the ball — lens-flare ghosts, which over a detailed mosaic read as stray O outlines sitting on the mirrors. DELETED. Worth noting the first guess was wrong: the floating bokeh circles were removed first and the Os survived, so if a ring ever reappears, look for a stroked Circle, not a soft gradient. (2) BEAMS NOW COME OFF THE SPHERE. `LightShafts` was a rigid fan of 22 shafts radiating from the centre and turning as one piece — a flat pinwheel (owner: "it is circulating the mirror ball like a 2D shape"). New `MirrorBeams`: each beam belongs to a MIRROR at a fixed latitude/longitude, so as the ball turns that mirror orbits and the beam is drawn from wherever it currently sits, pointing along its outward normal. KEY CHOICE: opacity peaks at the LIMB and falls to nothing at the centre — a mirror square-on throws its beam down the lens where the ball is in the way, and the shafts you see in a photograph come off the edges. That is what makes the light travel around a sphere instead of spinning like a wheel. Whole trajectory sampled once into interpolation tables driven by `spin`, so no per-frame work and it stops/scrubs with the ball. (3) FLOATING CIRCLES -> MIRROR SPECKS: `BokehField`/`BokehDot` deleted; `LightField`'s dots are smaller and sharper and are pushed OUT of the ball's own disc (a reflection lands on the room; drawn over the mirrors it reads as a blemish). (4) COLOUR THAT SHOWS: the spotlight pool now carries the station's colour (white core, station mid, station deep), and in the flipbook a mirror catching a coloured lamp is LIFTED before it is tinted — `base = t + cast*0.42` and the cast goes toward a WHITENED station colour, never a deepened one. Mixing a mid-grey tile toward a deep palette entry is exactly what left the colour "in the background and in deep shades" (owner). Colour lobe widened ^40 -> ^28, cast ceiling 0.42 -> 0.72. (5) SPOTLIGHT ON THE BALL ONLY: `SpotBeam` (the wedge reaching in from off-screen) is deleted — it overran the ball and was being clipped into a hard-edged rectangle below it. The pool alone, clipped to the ball, is what she asked for. CHECKS: tsc clean, 3125 paths / 49 SVG roots, open-to-settled 2.8s, full rotation cycle and all five pages with zero errors.
- THE FLIPBOOK IS THE BALL 2026-07-31 (panning spotlight, and the old ball deleted, OTA): owner: "the diagonal light moves across the mirror ball and the stars that light up from the ball... look decorative"; the ball itself now looks good enough that the add-ons read as stickers on top of it. (1) `BallSheen` (a diagonal band sweeping corner to corner at a fixed angle) and `MirrorGlints` (four-point stars) are DELETED. (2) NEW `SpotlightPan` + `SpotBeam`, built from the geometry rather than drawn: a light from direction L lights the part of the sphere facing it, so the pool sits at screen point R·L, and because that patch is a disc on a sphere seen at an angle it projects to an ELLIPSE — squashed along the line back to the ball's centre by exactly L.z and turned to face it. So the pool narrows and leans as the light swings toward the limb. L traces a slow Lissajous (sideways swing and up-down drift at different rates) so the light arrives from a genuinely different angle each pass instead of orbiting on rails. THE BEAM IS WHAT SELLS IT: a bright patch alone is just a patch, which is precisely why the sheen band read as decoration — `SpotBeam` is a wedge reaching the ball from off-screen, drawn BEHIND the ball so the ball occludes its tip. Pool and beam share ONE `spotPan` value created in the mode body; two loops of the same length would drift apart and a beam that misses its own pool is worse than no beam. The ball's baked lamps stay: a room has fixed lamps AND a moving spot, and the fixed ones are what make individual mirrors flare. (3) `LightShafts` now rides `spin` instead of its own 74s loop (owner: "make sure the reflected light move at the same pace as the ball") — these are beams thrown OFF the mirrors, so they can only travel at the speed of the surface throwing them, and riding spin also stops them when it stops and follows a finger drag.
  TIDIED INTO THE MAIN PATH, as asked: the `FLIPBOOK` switch is gone and with it `buildSphereTiles`, `SphereGrid`, `MirrorFlash`, `RotatingMeridians`, `Meridian`, `buildMeridianBase`, `buildLatitudeArcs`, `LightPatches`, `BallSheen`, `MirrorGlints`, plus the constants only they used (`Tile`, `BEVEL`, `FACET_DIRS`, `facetDir`, `SHADE_STEPS`, `shadeAt`, `FLASH_*`, `LAMPS`). DiscoBallMode.tsx 2223 -> 1583 lines, 640 deleted. The ball's geometry and lighting now live entirely in MirrorBallFlipbook.tsx. Checks: tsc clean, 3127 paths / 45 SVG roots (down from 56 — the deletion paid for the spotlight), open-to-settled 2.96s, full rotation cycle and all five pages with zero errors.
- FLIPBOOK ROUND 2 2026-07-31 (denser, silver, and less blur — owner picked the flipbook over the pre-render, OTA): she compared it against an offline Python render (scratchpad/prerender/ball.py, 34x64 mirrors with a real bevel and bloom) and chose the flipbook — "it's missing that interactive touch" — but wanted three things from the render. (1) DENSER: 17x32 -> 23x44, paid for by a MIN_AREA cull on each tile's own drawn area (silhouette slivers cost a shape and show nothing). 3126 paths vs the shipping ball's 2368; open-to-settled unchanged at ~2.9s. (2) SILVER, NOT MATTE — three causes, found by measuring rather than eyeballing. The mirror FILLS were already fine (mean saturation 0.065, essentially the same as the offline render's 0.078), so the matte look was entirely layers on top: LightPatches is now dropped outright under the flipbook (it exists to move light over a STATIC grid; over mirrors that carry their own it is just a veil), ColourReflections -> 0.22, NeonSweep -> 0.24, dbShade's inner white stops and dbHot cut, dbRim halved. Then the highlights: the ball had almost no blown-out pixels (1.4% above luminance 200 vs the render's 7%), so the specular lobe went from dot^15 to TWO lobes — a WIDE one (^15) for brightness and a NARROW one (^40) for colour. Keeping them apart is the point: widening a single lobe lit more mirrors but tinted half the ball at the same time. Finally the resting surface was lifted, which the shipping ball could never do (rounds 6/13: a bright resting surface swallowed light sliding over a still grid) — the flipbook's motion IS the grid, so that ceiling no longer binds. (3) LESS BLUR: the frames cross-faded continuously, so every moment was a blend of two — permanent motion blur that read as spinning far too fast. Now a TRAPEZOID: each frame holds at full strength across the middle of its slot and blends only over the last 40%. HOLD must stay under 0.5 or the two active weights stop summing to one and the ball pulses; five samples per slot lands exactly on the plateau corners and both ramp ends, and because the sampled weights sum to one at every sample the interpolated sum is one between them too.
  MEASUREMENT NOTE for next time: do NOT chase the offline render's saturation number. That ball is rendered in a void; this one hangs in a blue-lit room with the station's own light on it, so a much higher on-screen saturation is correct rather than a fault. Compare HIGHLIGHTS (share above 200) and the fills' own saturation instead — those are like for like.
- PROTOTYPE 2026-07-31 (THE FLIPBOOK — mirrors that actually turn, OTA, switchable): owner picked this from five proposed options. `src/components/MirrorBallFlipbook.tsx`, wired into DiscoBallMode behind `const FLIPBOOK = true` (one line to fall back to the shipping ball). THE TRICK: a mirror ball is regular in longitude, so rotating the grid by exactly one tile's width maps the tile SET back onto itself — six frames spanning that one tile-width therefore loop forever with no seam, and the mirrors genuinely move between them. Six grids are built once and never touched; the only animation is which is showing, i.e. opacity on six views, cross-faded (triangular weights summing to 1, so the ball never dims between frames) and derived from `spin` itself via a multi-segment interpolation so the frames stay locked to the ball even under a finger drag.
  WHAT IT FORCED, and it is an improvement not a compromise: the shipping ball keys each tile's brightness to its IDENTITY (hash of j,k), which cannot survive the wrap — tile k lands where k+1 was and would arrive with the wrong brightness. Brightness here is a function of where the mirror POINTS: reflection direction r = 2(n·v)n − v, sampled against smooth 3D value noise (the room) plus three sharp specular lobes (the lamps). Neighbouring mirrors point ~11° apart and reflection doubles that, so they land in different noise cells and come out wildly different — the dark-beside-bright checkerboard that sells chrome — while a single mirror changes SMOOTHLY as it turns, which is what stops six frames strobing. MirrorFlash is not rendered under the flipbook: faking exactly this was its only job.
  MEASURED, 14-frame burst over ~6s, temporal std per pixel inside the ball: flipbook median 10.08 / 69.1% of pixels changing >8 levels, vs 8.17 / 51.1% for the shipping ball as it stands and 9.90 / 61.3% for the old version WITH the rotating meridians. So it beats the wire-cage version without drawing a single line, and static vertical-edge energy falls 0.85 → 0.23 (nothing is pinned any more). COST: 1846 paths vs the shipping ball's 2368, 56 SVG roots vs 77, and 6 animated views instead of 26 — cheaper on every count. TRADE the owner is judging: grid drops from 25x48 to 17x32, so the mirrors are chunkier. Side-by-side GIF built with scratchpad/landscape/gif.mjs (clip-cropped Playwright frames assembled with PIL).
- DONE 2026-07-31 (four owner fixes: tagline, one gutter, Horizon's size, meridians off, OTA): (1) Night Run's tagline "Empty expressways. Violet dashboards." → "Blue-lit dashboards." — the station went jet blue, so the copy had stopped describing it. (2) ONE PAGE GUTTER. Cruise and Modes sat on 22, Stations on 20 and the floating tab bar on 16, so the bar's edges hung outside every page's text column — that is what "the nav panel isn't central on the page titles" was (owner, 31.07; the pill itself measured dead symmetric on her screenshot, 48px each side, so the asymmetry was never within the bar). New `PAGE_GUTTER = 20` in theme.ts, used by all four. Verified by measuring the pill's edges against the titles on the real renders. (3) HORIZON WAS BEING MAGNIFIED, not drawn too big. Its portrait scene was a fixed 360x460 viewBox with `preserveAspectRatio="slice"`, so on a 393x852 screen the scale is max(393/360, 852/460) = 1.85 — the sun blown up to ~84% of the width and only 59% of the drawing's width visible. Worse, the Svg was sized winW x winH inside the middle flex slot, so it OVERFLOWED its box, and that overflow was the only reason the scene appeared behind the controls at all. The owner likes it behind the controls, so that is now deliberate: the portrait scene is a full-bleed absoluteFill layer like the landscape one, with `geomPortrait = makeGeom(winW, winH)` so the viewBox matches the screen and nothing is cropped or magnified. Sun radius falls out at min(W*0.239, H*0.20) ≈ 48% of the width, the stars spread across the real width instead of being cropped, and the sky above the sun stops being empty. (4) ROTATING MERIDIANS REMOVED from the ball (owner: "try the mirror animating without the vertical lines") — on device the drawn verticals read as a wire cage over the chrome. `RotatingMeridians`/`Meridian` are kept in the file, unused, because this is a straight one-line swap back. MEASURED both ways with a 14-frame burst over ~6s (scratchpad/landscape/burst.mjs), temporal std per pixel inside the ball: WITH meridians median 9.90 and 61.3% of pixels changing >8 levels; WITHOUT, median 8.17 and 51.1%. So the ball still visibly changes — the flashing-mirror layer from round 13 is carrying it — but the specific "the grid is turning" cue is weaker (travelling vertical-edge energy 4.33 → 1.56). If the rotation stops reading on device, put the one line back.
- DONE 2026-07-31 (modes-page ball — a REAL sphere at last, OTA): third attempt, and the first two failed for the same reason. The thumbnail was a square lattice squeezed toward the edges: perfectly straight horizontal rows, perfectly straight vertical columns. Round 1 added foreshortening, round 2 added Lambert shading off each tile's own normal — both correct, both irrelevant, because no sphere has straight rows and the eye reads the GRID before it reads the light (owner: "this 2D mirror ball is not cooperating"). LESSON: curvature has to be in the geometry; shading can only support it.
