Each mirror is now a genuine latitude/longitude quad on a unit sphere with a ~17° downward viewing TILT, back-face culled — so latitude rows project to arcs that dip toward the viewer and columns converge at the pole. That is the full mode's construction; the mode keeps its own tilt near zero because it TURNS and has lighting to sell it, but a still picture needs the tilt to say "ball" on sight. TILT IS NEGATIVE — we look UP at the ball, because that is how you meet one, hanging above you (owner, 31.07). Two things follow and both are load-bearing: the rows bow UPWARD across the front, and the pole facing us is the BOTTOM one, so the fitting that covers the column convergence sits at the bottom (drawn as a squashed ellipse, inside the clip) while the stem simply runs to the top of the silhouette and stops — looking up, the top fitting is on the far side of the sphere. ROWS/COLS 13x24 at hero size, 8x14 at 62px (checked by temporarily demoting the hero — the ball is normally the hero, so the small path is otherwise never rendered). Each quad is shrunk 0.90 toward its own centre so the dark body shows between mirrors: the GAP is the grid. Kept from the earlier rounds: Lambert + end-weighted brightness scatter, the one shared facet gradient (bounding-box units, so a single def rescales to every quad), the offset shadow crowding the lower-right limb and the key highlight at the light. NEW: a dark cap at the top pole — a real ball has a fitting there, and it covers the one place this projection looks busy, where the columns converge.
- DONE 2026-07-31 (Night Run goes blue everywhere, gold premium chip, and DAYLIGHT, OTA): (1) JET BLUE STATION-WIDE — the 31.07 orb-only override is DELETED and `night-run`'s own colours moved instead: eqColors `['#C6ECFF','#2E7DFF','#1340E6']`, plus gradientColors/cardGradient/glowColor/iconBg off violet, because a blue mode over a purple card is worse than either. NOTE LEFT FOR THE OWNER: the tagline still reads "Empty expressways. Violet dashboards." — her copy, so not touched. (2) PREMIUM CHIP under the profile name now wears `PREMIUM_GOLD` (#F7B733, the upgrade card's eyebrow) rather than the app violet; FREE PLAN keeps the accent. (3) DAYLIGHT — new setting (`cruise_daylight`, default OFF) on MotionContext with `useDaylight()` shorthand, plus a Profile toggle that sits directly above Auto-dim and HIDES it (a toggle that cannot fire is worse than no toggle). DELIBERATELY NOT A WHITE THEME, and this is the reasoning to keep: in sun the problem is not that the app is dark — black behind white type is the highest contrast a phone can make and the cheapest on OLED. What vanishes outdoors is everything drawn at half strength. So Daylight lifts the quiet things and deepens the scrims UNDER type: `StationBackdrop` gains a 22% veil over the photograph (one component, so all 8 modes + the hero + the landscape deck at once), StationIdentity's eyebrow/dial/name go to near-full white, ModeActionRow's glass pills go opaque with a 0.42 rim, LandscapeChrome's panel wash deepens and its artist line brightens, and the Stations list lifts its dial numbers (0.42→0.86), names, chevrons and hairlines. Auto-dim stands down entirely — dimming with the sun on the screen makes the driver reach for the brightness slider, which costs more battery than the dim ever saved. WHAT IT DOES NOT DO: the modes themselves are still dark cinematic scenes; if the owner wants genuinely light LIST pages that is a much bigger piece of work and a different product decision. Verified with a seeded `cruise_daylight` localStorage entry (the same Playwright trick used for custom stations) — off/on compared side by side on the Stations page and inside a mode.
- DONE 2026-07-31 (band letters + a jet-blue orb, OTA): (1) SEVEN SEGMENTS CANNOT DRAW AN M. The station page's dial line set the whole string in DSEG7, and band letters inherit the parent Text's fontFamily, so "94.7 FM" came out "94.7 FN" (owner screenshots). A seven-segment cell has no diagonals and no vertical centre bar — the glyph does not exist to be fixed. Band letters now take DSEG14, which does have them, and which the Stations page's own band headers have always used. `useDsegFont()` is now a wrapper over `useDsegFonts()` returning `{seg7, seg14}` from one `useFonts` call; every future caller that prints a BAND rather than a NUMBER must reach for seg14. Only StationDetailModal printed one (StationIdentity carries the band inside the station's plain-font name), so that is the only site changed. Note the `loaded` flag now gates both faces, so a missing DSEG14 asset would drop the numbers to mono everywhere — both ttfs are committed under assets/fonts. (2) CIRCULAR EQ — new `ORB_PALETTE` in CircularWaveMode overrides the station's eqColors for THIS MODE ONLY; `night-run` is jet blue (#C6ECFF → #2E7DFF → #1340E6). Why an override rather than editing the station: eqColors is a three-stop ramp tuned for bars and mirrors, and the orb maps the LAST stop onto the ring's whole outer edge, so Night Run's magenta was carrying a station whose own photograph is blue. The station keeps its cyan/violet in all seven other modes. Order is centre → out, matching the cwStroke gradient's 0.5/0.75/1 stops. First ramp landed the tips in near-navy and read dull against the backdrop; the shipped one keeps them luminous.
- DONE 2026-07-31 (owner round: six asks off the device, OTA): (1) CD WIND MARKER — the disc's rotational scrub now shows the record's centre pill (`▶▶ +7s`), same shape, position and 1s-then-fade timing, in the STATION's colour rather than the vinyl's gold so it belongs to that deck. A tap still bypasses it entirely (the pill is set straight to 0), because a tap must not go through scrub.end. (2) TONEARM CARTRIDGE — the headshell was rotated -18°, and since the pivot sits top-right while the shaft hooks LEFT toward the spindle, a negative SVG angle kicked the cartridge back OUT and broke the J. It is +18 now (`HEAD_TILT`), which also matches how a real arm is built: the headshell's offset angles the stylus in toward the spindle. (3) STATIONS PAGE — the Create pill is the app's primary button (solid white, dark type, a `+`); as grey glass on a photograph it read as chrome rather than the thing to press. The CREATE SHEET's preview is no longer a violet-rimmed card: the stations page is a printed list now, so the preview is an actual ROW off the dial — number, name, MINE chip, the chosen colour on the icon, chevron, hairlines — with the tagline as a caption beneath (it doesn't appear on the dial). The dial NUMBER IS REAL: `custom-<Date.now()>` is settled when the sheet OPENS rather than at save time, so the number previewed is the number the station gets. CTA went from a slab in whatever colour the user had just picked (the button changed identity as you scrolled the swatches) to the white primary pill. Column widths and type sizes are copied from stations.tsx — if they drift the preview stops being one. (4) MODES-PAGE BALL — foreshortening alone still read flat, and it would: the old shading was a RADIAL dim, which is symmetric, and a symmetric dim is a vignette on a disc — it says nothing about where the light is. Each tile is now lit by its own surface normal: under an orthographic view a sphere's normal falls straight out of screen position (nz = √(1−nx²−ny²)), so Lambert against a light up/left/slightly-front gives a real lit side, terminator and shadow side. Plus per-facet gradient (ONE shared def — SVG gradients are in bounding-box units so they rescale per tile), scatter pushed to the ends of its range, square-cut tiles (the rx rounding read as candy), N=15 at hero size and 10 at row size, and the key highlight moved to where the light actually is. The square LATTICE stays — a lat/long projection is truer but its pole tiles read as noise at 62px. (5) MIRROR BALL PORTRAIT KEEPS ITS CONTROLS — portrait resting was this mode's own invention and no other mode does it, so it was the one screen where the controls vanished while you held the phone normally. The rest timer is now gated on `isLandscape`; the landscape deck still docks and rests, which is the agreed grammar every mode shares. (6) BIGGER — CD case winH*0.86→0.94 (cap 350→384) sideways and winW*0.93→0.97 / winH*0.44→0.47 (cap 410→430) upright; Equalizer meter winH*0.60→0.68 sideways with the docked scale 0.62→0.68, and VIZ_H 0.26→0.285 upright. Checked at 667pt, where the upright meter is the binding constraint.
  CHECKS AFTER: tsc clean; the 8-mode rotation sweep (portrait → landscape → rest → wake → portrait) with zero page errors, zero misfit haze canvases and no leaked transforms; all five pages plus the create sheet and the CD at 393x852 AND 375x667 with zero page errors; and the rotational scrub driven for real with a circular drag on both the disc and the record.
- DONE 2026-07-30 (ROTATION ROUND 5 — the two cut-offs, and a rotation smoothness sweep, OTA): owner on device: "a cut off in the equalizer and in the mirror mode... it looks like it's from the atmosphere", plus the vinyl left half off the screen in portrait, plus "run checks on the smoothness for the rotations". TWO ROOT CAUSES, both found by measuring her screenshots rather than eyeballing, and both a whole CLASS of bug rather than one-offs.
  (1) THE ATMOSPHERE CUT-OFF — `AmbientGlow`'s `Haze` was `<Svg width="100%" height="100%">` with `50%` geometry inside, the ONLY percentage-sized SVG in the app. A percentage canvas is resolved natively and does NOT re-resolve when the window changes shape, so after a turn the ellipse was still being drawn at its PORTRAIT proportions inside a view that had already re-laid-out to the landscape box — and the view clips, so the haze was sliced off in a straight horizontal line. PROOF, and reuse this method: the step is ONE pixel with a ~3× brightness ratio, so it is a layer ending, not a gradient. Solve for which layer: on her 926×428 screen the portrait hero canvas is 582×463 anchored at the landscape box's top-left, which puts the ellipse's right edge at x=0.448 (measured: step reaches zero at ~0.45) and the clip at y = 141 + s·107 for breath scale s∈[0.92,1.1] → 0.560..0.605 (measured: 0.568 on Mirror Ball, 0.604 on Circular EQ — the same layer at two breath phases). Nothing about the INTENDED geometry fits those numbers; the stale-canvas geometry fits both axes to a couple of percent. FIX: every haze now gets a real pixel width/height and draws through a `0 0 100 100` viewBox with `preserveAspectRatio="none"`, so the canvas is a prop that changes on rotation and the drawing no longer depends on how any percentage resolves; the layer is also keyed on orientation so a turn rebuilds it outright. Boxes moved from left+right and `%` tops to left+width/top+height in px — arithmetically identical, just concrete. Swept the same treatment through the two other percentage canvases (Mirror Ball's UnderGlow, Horizon's scene — both had a viewBox so they could only stretch, not clip, but `slice` cropping for the old screen is the same failure). RULE: never size an `<Svg>` in percentages in this app.
  (2) THE VINYL CUT-OFF — `isLandscape ? deckScene : null`. A native-driven transform lives on the NATIVE view; dropping it from the style prop sends no transform at all, so the view simply KEEPS the last one it was given — the record stayed shrunk to 0.86 and pushed −0.2·winW left, forever, in portrait. FIX: `useDeckScene(chrome, winW, scale, active)` — portrait flattens the interpolation to identity instead, and every mode now renders the style unconditionally. Never write `isLandscape ? <animated style> : null` again, in this codebase or any other.
  SMOOTHNESS SWEEP (all 8 modes, portrait → landscape → rest → tap-wake → portrait, in the real web build; harness scratchpad/landscape/sweep.mjs, which also dumps each haze's canvas-vs-box fit and every non-identity transform on the page): zero page errors, no misfit canvases, no leaked transforms. It also caught four real things. The arrival flashed one frame of the deck glide because the turn re-renders as landscape while `chrome` is still parked at 1 — the reset now runs in a LAYOUT effect (`useIsoLayoutEffect`, exported from LandscapeChrome; Mirror Ball's own re-dock effect uses it too). Vinyl's rotational scrub called `evt.target.measure(...)` unguarded (threw on web, and a throw in a grant handler kills the gesture) and BOTH Vinyl and CD anchored the gesture's starting angle before the async measure answered, so the first move after the centre had moved — i.e. right after a turn, or mid-glide — jumped; the starting angle is now re-anchored inside the measure callback. Tuner still slid off by the module-load PORTRAIT height, so sideways it exited 2.2× further than the screen and the last half of the 320ms was blank. Cassette's 400-dot grain field was seeded against portrait dimensions, so sideways almost all of it fell below the screen and what showed was crammed into the left half. WakeSpotifyHint hung at `insets.top + 64` full-width, which sideways lands across the object — it now tucks into the top strip of the left pane, past the chevron and short of where the panel docks.
- DONE 2026-07-30 (LANDSCAPE round 3 — the DOCKING DECK replaces the bottom strip, OTA): owner, after living with the L1 grammar on device, picked "Option C" from three prototyped panel behaviours (fixed deck / sleeps-in-place / docking; scratchpad/landscape/options.js): AWAKE = an L2 head-unit panel docked on the right (DECK_FRAC 0.40 of the screen) with identity, title, panel-width seek, transport and pills — the scene sits in the left pane clear of every control; AT REST (6s) = the panel slides off the right edge and the scene glides to centre stage at FULL SIZE; tap anywhere docks it back to the same fixed spot (driver muscle memory). WHY: L1's rest state inherited the awake spacing (object high + small over dead space), and its seek bar was a full-screen reach. MECHANICS: same `chrome` value drives everything — panel translateX (slide replaces fade; wake now 260ms, teleporting panels read wrong), and NEW `useDeckScene(chrome, winW, scale)` gives each mode's scene a transform-only glide (translateX −DECK_FRAC/2·winW + scale). Per-mode scales: objects 0.86 (default), EQUALIZER 0.62 (full-width meter must fit the pane) and its meter now anchors LOW (bottom: 44 — owner: "not dead centre", a meter rises from a baseline), HORIZON 1 (slide only — shrinking a full-bleed scene reveals its edges; the sun lands at the left pane's centre, panel covers the rest). Disco's ROOM stays full-bleed, only the ball group glides. `LS_CHROME_CLEAR` is deprecated (nothing spaces off the bottom any more). Verified full cycle in the web build (awake → rest → tap re-dock), zero page errors. WATCH ON DEVICE: Vinyl/CD rotational scrub measures the object's centre — the deck transform may offset touch mapping while the panel is docked.
- DONE 2026-07-30 (Mirror Ball round 23 — the premium-detail brief, OTA): owner sent an Apple-style reference sheet with a full spec (micro-bevels, chrome variance, speculars, bloom, under-glow, white-dominant lighting) and — crucially — said NOT to adopt its per-station full recolours, which VALIDATES the round-20 chrome architecture outright (silver material, mood arrives as light only). Deltas, all additive within the measured rules: (1) MICRO-BEVEL: a third path per visible tile referencing ONE shared vertical gradient `dbLip` (lit top lip 0.8→0, shadow bottom lip 0→0.65) — horizontal edges ONLY (vertical edges are the round-12 static-seam trap), opacity jittered per tile (uniform lips read stamped), skipped where depth<0.35 (silhouette slivers). (2) `KeySpecular` — one soft breathing radial at the key light's upper-left, 0.10–0.22 opacity on a ~8s cycle, gated on `lit`; all falloff, never a hard ellipse (round 4). (3) `UnderGlow` — the mood-tinted pool beneath the ball (light, not material), lit-gated, all-gradient. (4) ColourReflections grew a SIXTH patch that is deliberately NEUTRAL cool-white (ambient room light) at a lower peak, so mood colour always sits beside plain light. Composite measured after: median 121, >200 share 16.4% — inside the round-18/19 approved band, no rebalance needed. Everything else on the brief (multiple soft sources, rays, flares, particles, shimmer, sparkle cadence) already existed from rounds 18–22.
- DONE 2026-07-30 (LANDSCAPE round 2 — the other five modes + a readiness gate, OTA): owner on device: "a lot of them do squish, the mirror ball works the best" — the un-built modes were rendering their portrait columns sideways. (1) NEW GATE: `LANDSCAPE_READY` set in orientation.ts — OrientationGate only unlocks rotation for modes listed there; a mode without a landscape composition stays portrait (squish is worse than upright). TUNER is the one holdout — the dial deserves its own sideways design round. (2) CASSETTE/VINYL/CD/CIRCULAR-EQ/HORIZON all got the L1+L3 treatment via the same template as round 1: hero sized off HEIGHT alone (the portrait formulas' winH*0.44-ish terms are what shrank everything), portrait chrome behind `!isLandscape`, LandscapeChrome + useChromeFade + root wake sniffer, AmbientGlow gated out (portrait-derived smoke geometry). Cassette's stale early-dev landscape branch (two columns, FAKE track list, Play-on-Spotify button — it shipped by accident the day rotation unlocked) is DELETED. (3) LandscapeChrome grew an optional `seekBar` node prop — Vinyl predates the shared clock and passes its own ScrubProgressBar; progress/scrub are now optional. (4) HORIZON WIDE GEOMETRY: slice-cropping the portrait viewBox into a wide window blew the sun up 2.4x (only its midriff showed). `makeGeom(W,H)` now derives the whole composition (horizon line, sun off the short edge, stars across the real width, slats scaled with the sun) — GEOM_PORTRAIT is bit-identical to the old constants, GEOM_WIDE (800x370) feeds the landscape's full-bleed scene. TRAP hit twice this round: inserting a hook anchored on the playlist-effect line can land BEFORE `isLandscape` is declared — declare it at the useWindowDimensions line, not at the sizing line.
- LANDSCAPE TRAP FOUND ON DEVICE 2026-07-30, FIXED SAME DAY (OTA): rotation unlock alone does NOT rotate the modes — every fullscreen mode presents inside an RN `Modal`, and on iPhone `RCTModalHostView.supportedOrientationsMask` returns PORTRAIT-ONLY when the `supportedOrientations` prop is absent (verified in node_modules/react-native/React/Views/RCTModalHostView.m), independently of the app-level mask. Symptom: fade worked, screen never turned. FIX: `supportedOrientations={['portrait','landscape']}` on all 8 mode Modals AND every Modal that can present during a drive (AutoDim's catch layer, PlaybackNotice, DriveCheckCard, ShareCardSheet) — a portrait-pinned Modal presenting OVER a landscape mode snaps the whole screen upright. Page-level modals (CreateStation, PlatformSelector, Settings, StationDetail, ThemeGenerator) deliberately left portrait. ANY NEW MODAL that can appear mid-drive must carry this prop. Also verified from expo-screen-orientation's own Swift: JS `OrientationLock.DEFAULT`(0) → native allButUpsideDown, and the registry mask from lockAsync OVERRIDES Info.plist at the root VC — so runtime rotation works despite app.json "orientation": "portrait", no fresh build needed.
- DONE 2026-07-30 (LANDSCAPE round 1 — rotation + Mirror Ball & Equalizer, OTA): owner picked "L1+L3 together" from three prototyped directions (scratchpad/landscape/build.js — HTML mocks at 852x393 with real station photos + the ball cropped out of ball/room.png; L2 "Deck" split-screen REJECTED). The grammar: full-bleed scene, portrait furniture redistributed along the bottom (title left, pills right, seek across, transport centred, chevron+eyebrow top-left), and after 6 untouched seconds of playback ALL chrome fades to a pure scene with a whisper of the station name — the Mirror Ball's portrait chrome-fade generalised. PLUMBING: `src/utils/orientation.ts` (defensive wrappers over expo-screen-orientation — module is in EVERY build since 08.07, so this whole feature ships OTA) + `OrientationGate` in NowPlayingHost: rotation allowed ONLY while a mode is expanded, PORTRAIT_UP everywhere else; closing a sideways mode snaps the app upright. The list pages never rotate — deliberate, matches Apple Music. SHARED: `src/components/LandscapeChrome.tsx` = presentation (`LandscapeChrome`) + fade machinery (`useChromeFade` — gate read through a ref, the wake callback lives in a once-built capture sniffer) + `LS_CHROME_CLEAR` (px the awake chrome needs; scenes keep their object above it). The wake MUST be `onStartShouldSetResponderCapture={() => { wake(); return false; }}` on the mode's ROOT — an absoluteFill box-none overlay cannot reliably see taps on the scene beneath. ModeActionRow slots in via its `style` prop (zero out marginTop/paddingHorizontal/alignSelf). EQUALIZER: the ORIGINAL early-dev landscape branch (two columns, own VolumeSlider, safety banner, dim button — never shipped, app was portrait-locked, predated every shared piece) is DELETED along with VioletProgressBar/VolumeSlider/platform-row state; new branch = full-width meter standing at bottom: LS_CHROME_CLEAR above the chrome. MIRROR BALL: reuses its OWN chrome/wakeChrome machinery for the landscape overlay (one timer, one behaviour); ball sizes off height alone in landscape (winH*0.56 cap 280 — the portrait formula's winH*0.39 makes a grapefruit) and the flex box's paddingBottom is LS_CHROME_CLEAR*0.54 (verified at 852x393: less sinks the ball into the seek bar). TRAP: AmbientGlow is gated `!isLandscape` — its smoke geometry derives from PORTRAIT screen height at module load, so in a 393-high window the haze starts below the screen; running it is invisible cost. Verified in the real web build at 852x393 + portrait regression shots, zero page errors, full rest→wake cycle. REMAINING 6 modes (Cassette/Vinyl/Tuner/Horizon/CircularEQ/CD) follow the same recipe after the owner approves these two on device; Tuner and Cassette will want their own scene compositions, not just the overlay.

Violet survives only on Switch accents. Also exports `useDsegFont()` + `stationDisplayName()`. Names already end AM/FM; custom stations get the band appended. (5) MODES-PAGE BALL CURVED: the uniform grid read flat, so the lattice is now warped through `c + R*sin(u*π/2)` on BOTH axes — squares foreshorten into slivers at the silhouette (plus per-tile shade falloff), which is the curvature cue; poles never pinch because it's a square lattice, not the lat/long projection that sank the first thumb. All verified in the web build; zero page errors.
- REJECTED BY APPLE 2026-07-29, FIXED SAME DAY (build 7, submission c3094be4-98c0-4bac-be68-e51408b78f8c, reviewed on an iPad Air 11-inch M3): **Guideline 2.5.4 Performance — Software Requirements**. "The app declares support for audio in the UIBackgroundModes key... but we are unable to play any audible content when the app is running in the background." CORRECT AND OUR FAULT: Cruise FM never plays audio itself — Spotify does — so it has no business claiming background audio. CAUSE: nothing in app.json declared it; the **expo-audio plugin** adds it automatically (`node_modules/expo-audio/plugin/build/withAudio.js` pushes 'audio' into UIBackgroundModes). expo-audio was installed 20.07 for mic-reactive visuals, those were REMOVED 22.07, and the plugin was deliberately kept only to protect the OTA fingerprint — i.e. this rejection was caused by the one piece of dead weight already sitting at #4 on the NEXT FRESH BUILD list. FIX: plugin removed from app.json AND `npm uninstall expo-audio` (nothing in src/ imported it — verified). That also removes the unexplained microphone permission from the store listing, which was the other reason it was queued. VERIFIED with `npx expo config --type introspect`: no UIBackgroundModes, no NSMicrophoneUsageDescription anywhere in the resolved iOS config. This is a FRESH BUILD event by definition (app.json + package.json both changed), which is fine — a rejection requires a new build to resubmit anyway. LESSON: an Expo plugin can inject Info.plist keys you never wrote; before any submission, run the introspect command above and read what Apple will actually see.
- FULL SWEEP 2026-08-13, after the light theme (owner: "run a full sweep"). Everything green except one class of finding, and the finding is the reusable part. NEW INSTRUMENT, kept in the repo: `scripts/test-contrast.mjs` + `scripts/contrast-probe.js` — walks every text node on every page in BOTH themes, composites the real backdrop down the ancestor chain, and reports anything under 2.6:1. It exists because a light theme fails SILENTLY: white type on white paper throws nothing, shows up in no diff, and both times it happened on 13.08 the OWNER found it on her phone, which is the wrong instrument. Needs the web build running and Playwright (`PLAYWRIGHT_MODULE=/abs/path/node_modules/playwright/index.mjs` if it is installed outside the project — NODE_PATH does not apply to ESM imports).
  WHAT IT FOUND, and it was a whole family rather than one bug: **the app's two warm accents are the only colours used as TEXT on the page rather than on a card**, and both were tuned for black. `#F59E0B` measures **1.95:1** on paper and `#F7B733` **1.62:1** — visible but not readable — while the band captions, the ON AIR chip and the PREMIUM badge are all set in them at 8-10pt. They are palette tokens now (`p.amber` / `p.gold`), deepening to `#A85E06` / `#9A6B00` in light (4.5:1 and 4.3:1) and unchanged in dark. RULE: any colour used as small type must be checked on BOTH grounds; a hue that works on black is a different colour on paper.
  TWO PROBE TRAPS, both of which made the first run useless: filter icon glyphs by CODEPOINT (private-use ranges), not by font name — the family comes back as 'ionicons' for some and a plain fallback for others, so a name test lets half of them through and the report fills with sun and rain symbols; and skip any text whose nearest positioned box also contains a picture, because every hero in this app lays its photograph in as an absolutely-positioned SIBLING that an ancestor walk cannot see. The Stations hero still reports ~1.1:1 and is a KNOWN FALSE POSITIVE — verified by sampling the pixel behind it instead.
  EVERYTHING ELSE: tsc clean; the three offline suites pass; preflight 11/11; health walk (15 steps) green; fresh-user run green; the 8-mode dismiss harness green (dismissed / page scrolls / no stranded modals, all eight); the 8-mode rotation sweep green (zero page errors, zero misfit haze canvases); settings pages clean in both themes. PERFORMANCE unchanged from the 11.08 baseline — Mirror Ball 43fps at 3125 paths, everything else 59-60fps. NOTE FOR NEXT TIME: the first perf run reported single 56-74ms long tasks on Circular EQ, Tuner and Mirror Ball and the second reported ZERO everywhere. Machine contention, not a regression — always run that harness twice before believing a long task.
- SEVEN-ITEM ROUND 2026-08-18 (owner's list, all OTA). Everything below is on the preview channel.
  (1) **THE ELAPSED TIME FROZE FOR THE REST OF THE DRIVE** — owner: "sometimes the time at the start stays on 1:19 even when i scrub the bar." A REGRESSION FROM MY OWN 14.08 COAST CAP. Reproduced deterministically in the web build with a stubbed service told to go quiet mid-song: the signal drops at 0:41, the bar coasts its 30 seconds to 1:12, and then sits there while the song plays on — because a fresh reading was the only thing that ever restarted the coast. THE MISTAKE WAS TREATING ONE SILENCE AS ANOTHER, and this is the rule to keep: a service that SAID it stopped is already handled properly (the clock refuses to start until `isPlaying` confirms — 11.08 — and the poll adopts a real pause after two idle answers); a service we merely cannot REACH is a patchy signal, which on a drive is the ordinary case, and Spotify's own app is still playing locally. There, holding the bar is a lie in the other direction and a WORSE one, because the error grows without bound and reads as the app having died. A coast now RE-ARMS while the last word was "playing" and only holds after `MAX_SILENCE_MS` (150s, ~30 missed polls, shorter than most songs so the bar cannot wrap onto a track we know nothing about). `shouldKeepCoasting` is exported and tested. ALL THREE CLOCKS, because they must agree — the shared one, Vinyl and Cassette — and CASSETTE HAD NEVER HAD THE BOUND AT ALL, so it still carried the original 14.08 bug of running to the end of the song over silence; its four copies of the wind-forward animation are now one helper.
  (2) **A 204 MEANS SUCCESS, AND `spotifyFetch` FOLDS IT INTO `null` ALONGSIDE EVERY FAILURE.** Caught while wiring shuffle to revert on refusal — the revert would have fired on every WORKING press and broken both buttons outright. Player commands with no useful body now go through `spotifyCommand`, which returns the status honestly. Check this before testing any spotifyFetch result for truthiness.
  (3) SHUFFLE AND REPEAT: three faults. **Repeat was a two-state button over a three-state feature** — every mode sent `'track'`, so the only reachable repeat was repeat-ONE, which on a station whose whole point is its playlist means the playlist stops; and the state was flattened to a boolean, so a player genuinely set to repeat the playlist showed the repeat-one icon with no press that could get back to it. It cycles off → the PLAYLIST → this song → off now (`nextRepeat`), and the playlist comes first because it is the one people mean. Apple's native side already understood all three. **A press was overwritten by a poll that predated it** — `after()` re-polls at 220ms, long before Spotify has necessarily applied the change, and that stale reading was believed, so the button dropped back and looked dead. Same shape as the seek that sprang back (10.08), same answer: `acceptReported` holds what was asked for until a reading AGREES or the guard expires. **The eight modes each kept a mirrored copy** of both settings, synced in eight identical effects; the copy was what let a button disagree with the music, so they read the player directly now.
  (4) MIRROR BALL LIGHTENED (owner: "it can look a bit dim sometimes"). **BRIGHTNESS ON THIS BALL IS A COUNT, NOT A LEVEL** — how many mirrors are catching a lamp at once. Raising the ambient floor and lifting the ball's body moved the median from 61 to 82 and DESTROYED it: the share above 200 fell 4.1% → 0.6% and the dark mirrors went with it, i.e. a uniform grey sphere. Widening the bright lobe (15 → 10) raises the median AND the highlights AND keeps a lit mirror plainly brighter than its neighbour, which is the cue that reads as chrome; it also answers "sometimes", since a wider lit band means fewer dim moments in the turn. `lambert` is now a small term on purpose: a mirror is not diffuse, its brightness is what it REFLECTS, so leaning on lambert left one whole side near-black as though the ball were matte. After, on Downtown: median 81, >200 8.0%, >160 17.1%, 4.2% still genuinely dark; paused still has no blown highlights at all. Measure by screenshotting the running mode and masking the ball's own disc — /tmp harnesses are lost on container reset, so rebuild it from that recipe.
  (5) TUNER LANDSCAPE: the head unit fills its pane. **The box was small because of HEIGHT** — portrait stacks four rows, that stack is taller than a 393pt screen once the dial and hint sit under it, so the panel had been shrunk to 44% of the screen. Shrinking the panel to fix an overflow is treating the symptom. A real head unit in a wide slot puts the frequency BESIDE the rest, which halves the height. TWO COLLISIONS ON THE RENDER, both the same failure — a block sized against an assumption: three items on a space-between row need the panel's whole width, not a third of it (AM/FM + STEREO + TUNED overlapped, so they take a full-width row underneath, where a receiver keeps them anyway); and a frequency column sized while looking at AM's "AM 810" is two cells too narrow for FM's "FM 94.70", which printed straight across the song title. The song line cannot shrink out of the way — a dot-matrix window is a whole number of character cells, so it carries an explicit width. The frequency's width is knowable: `dmWidth`, not a constant.
  (6) SCRUB FOCUS ON CD AND VINYL (owner: "a haptic and enlarge and blur the background, so it feels focused"). **THERE IS NO ANIMATABLE BLUR** — `blurRadius` re-blurs the whole photograph on the main thread every re-display and iOS killed the app for exactly that (the reason stations ship a pre-blurred FILE); animating it would be that fault continuously. So `useScrubFocus` builds the focus from the other two rack-focus cues: the subject grows and the background darkens AND drifts wider. The PUSH carries what the blur would have — at 1.05 it was invisible next to the veil, at 1.10 the picture plainly falls away. Measured: ~a third darker on the backdrop's lit areas, object 1.06×, back to exactly 1.000 on release. **THE CD SCALES ITS DISC, NOT ITS CASE**: the case already runs to 97% of the window, so growing it only sliced its own corners off; a derived "whatever fits" scale came out at 1.015, i.e. nothing. The disc sits at 85% of the case and has somewhere to go. Haptics gained `grab`/`release` — the grain used to fade in from nothing, so the gesture had no beginning; the grab also resets the rate gate or it swallows the first detent.
  (7) **EVERY MODE NOW RESTS IN PORTRAIT** — six untouched seconds of playback and the controls fade, the scene re-centres, any touch brings them back. This REVERSES the 30.07 decision to gate the Mirror Ball's portrait rest to landscape (good ground at the time: no other mode did it); every mode does it now. `restShiftFor(contentH, sceneY, sceneH)` gives the exact distance and must stay MEASURED — a constant would be wrong in a way that is hard to see, and it also settles the DIRECTION for free: the Equalizer's meter is pushed low by a flex spacer so re-centring moves it UP 75pt while every other scene moves down. Measure the scene's BOX, not the object in it — the ball is deliberately offset high inside its box and that should survive. `pointerEvents` matters as much as the opacity: an invisible play button is still a play button, so the bottom stack and the close chevron stop taking touches when rested, and the three modes whose OBJECT doubles as play/pause (record, disc, cassette shell) needed the same guard on their tap paths or waking the deck pauses the song. Scrubbing stays live while rested — winding the record is the scene, not a control. HARNESS: `scripts/harness/rest.mjs`, which reads the eyebrow's COMPOSITED opacity down the ancestor chain (a presence check would pass whether it worked or not) and holds the rule that a PAUSED drive never rests. It earned its keep at once: Cassette passed awake and rested and failed `woken` because its portrait root was missing the touch sniffer.
  (8) CREATE-STATION SHEET THEMED — the last screen still hardcoded dark. Two things could not simply be tokenised because they were tuned for black: the SAVE PILL inverts (the app's primary button is a solid fill in the opposite of the page, and white on off-white is not a button), and the selected swatch's RING sits on the SHEET rather than on the colour, so it has to be whatever the sheet is not. The glass wash over the inputs and icon tiles becomes ink for the same reason.
- WINDING THE DECK NO LONGER WAKES THE CONTROLS 2026-08-18, later (OTA) — owner, on the round above: "could we have the vinyl and CD not have the controls come back in when I try to scrub. It tends to move back up and then it acts like the page wants to move down." BOTH HALVES ARE ONE CAUSE. The root touch sniffer woke the chrome on ANY touch, and waking slides the scene back to its awake position — under the finger, mid-gesture. The object then moves relative to the thumb, which the drag classifier reads as downward travel, so the card starts to dismiss. THE ORDERING IS THE WHOLE PROBLEM: the sniffer is `onStartShouldSetResponderCapture` on the ROOT and capture runs top-down, so it always fires before the object's own responder is granted — at that moment there is no way to ask "did this land on the record?" FIX: `requestWake()` defers by one tick (`setTimeout 0`, which lands after the responder negotiation, since that is synchronous inside the touch dispatch) and the deck's `onPanResponderGrant` calls `cancelWake()`. A gesture that turns out to be a plain TAP hands the wake back on release — losing tap-to-wake would be the worse bug, and while rested that tap does nothing else (it must not also pause the music). SEPARATELY, `useChromeFade` gained `hold`: a scrub must also stop the chrome RESTING mid-gesture, for the same reason in reverse. It could not go through `sheetOpen` — that flag re-runs the hook's effect, which calls `wake()`, so feeding a scrub into it brings the controls back, i.e. exactly what is being avoided. `hold` cancels the countdown and re-arms it on release only if the chrome is currently up. HARNESS: `scripts/harness/scrub-rest.mjs`, and it carries its own control — it asserts the deck's wind marker appeared, so the test cannot pass by the drag doing nothing, and it was verified to FAIL against the broken version (mid-drag reads 1).
- STARTING A DRIVE NO LONGER RESTARTS MUSIC IT IS ALREADY PLAYING 2026-08-18 (OTA) — owner: "when i'm already playing music from spotify and i already know what playlist it belongs in, i click the right playlist but then the music changes. i think it would be nice for the music to continue playing. unless its a different playlist and then it should change." THE CAUSE: Spotify's `PUT /me/player/play` with a `context_uri` starts that context FROM THE TOP, so handing it the playlist it is already playing throws away wherever you had got to — from the outside, the app skipping your song for no reason. `playStationMusic` now probes first and `startActionFor` decides between three genuinely different outcomes: same playlist playing → **leave** it alone; same playlist paused → **resume IN PLACE** (call play with NO uri — passing one restarts it, which is the same bug wearing a different hat); anything else, including no usable answer from the service → **start**. Costs one extra request at drive start, the same one the poll makes every five seconds anyway, and only on the path that was about to make a much more expensive one. The rule is pure and exported because it decides whether the app throws away the song someone is in the middle of; `scripts/test-start-action.mjs` covers all three plus the no-answer default and exact uri matching. NOT DONE for Apple Music: the bridge exposes no queue-source id, so there is nothing to compare.
- "I CAN HEAR THIS — CRUISE WITH IT" 2026-08-18 (OTA) — owner: "there should be something at the top, like 'i hear this song playing…', 'do you want to continue' and then it selects a station and a mood. because sometimes the playlists don't stick and users play whatever song comes up." `src/components/AlreadyPlayingCard.tsx`, above the hero on the home page. THE POINT IS THAT IT STARTS NOTHING: every other way into a drive tells the music service what to play, and this one takes what is already playing and wraps a station and a mode around it — the case the app had no answer for, where someone's only route in was to have their song replaced. New `open(..., { adopt: true })` on NowPlayingContext opens the session, sets the transport to playing (the music genuinely is) and does NOT call startStationMusic. WHICH STATION: `stationForPlaylist(uri)` — the reverse lookup added to stationPlaylists.ts — so if the playlist playing is one a station is linked to, that station, which is a fact rather than a guess; otherwise the hour's own pick. Mode is the last one driven. The playlist NAME leads the sub-line when known, because spotting that your music is not the playlist you thought it was is the thing that prompted this. Never renders during a drive or without a real song. On Apple Music it still works with less to say — no queue-source id means no playlist name and no station match, so it falls through to the hour's station. VERIFIED by temporarily stubbing a now-playing track into useMusicPlayback (removed after): the card renders in both themes, opens a drive, and the "waking Spotify" notice — which fires on EVERY ordinary start — does not appear, which is the proof that nothing was started.
- A SOLO SONG GETS TO CHOOSE 2026-08-18 (OTA) — owner: "for a solo song that is playing (not associated with a playlist) and Cruise FM can hear, allow the user to choose the station and [the mode]." THE RULE IS "ASK WHEN WE DO NOT KNOW", one rule rather than a special case per source: if `stationForPlaylist()` matches, the station is a FACT and the card opens the drive in one tap; a solo track, an album, the radio or a playlist no station owns all land in the same place, where the card asks for a mood and then a look. THE BUTTON SAYS WHICH IT WILL BE — a play triangle starts something, a chevron opens a chooser; one glyph doing both is the kind of thing this app keeps removing. `ModeSheet` gained `onPick`/`currentId`/`title`/`extraBottom` so it can be used BEFORE a session exists (its default still switches the running drive's visual, which is what the in-drive pill wants), and `startAdoptedDrive` is exported so the one-tap path and the asked path cannot drift.
- A WRAPPER MUST NOT IMPOSE ALIGNMENT ON A PARENT THAT STRETCHES 2026-08-18 (OTA) — owner, with two screenshots: "the song titles have now shifted centre. Make sure they come back to the left hand side." MY OWN REGRESSION from the portrait-rest work the same day: the bottom stack in each mode was wrapped in an `Animated.View` so it could fade out and stop taking touches, and in the Equalizer and the cassette that wrapper was given `alignItems: 'center'` (plus `width: '100%'`). Both parents — `fs.content` in the Equalizer, the flex column in the cassette — declare NO `alignItems`, so their children default to STRETCH, and `trackBlock` depends on that: it is full width with `alignItems: 'flex-start'` inside, which is what puts the title against the left edge. Centring the wrapper shrank the block to its own content and put it in the middle of the screen, so the title moved with it. THE RULE: a wrapper inserted purely to animate opacity must be transparent to layout — match the parent's alignment or impose none (`alignSelf: 'stretch'` and nothing else). Vinyl correctly keeps `alignItems: 'center'`, because its parent genuinely centres; that is why only two modes moved. VERIFIED by measuring rather than looking: a probe opened all eight modes and read the song block's left edge — every one starts at x=28, the `paddingHorizontal` of `trackBlock`. Reuse that; "looks left-aligned" cannot tell 28 from 96.
- ONE STATION IS ON AIR, NOT FOUR 2026-08-18 (OTA) — owner, off the stations page at 22:24: "can we have only 1 FM station on air", with Rain Drive FM, Downtown FM and Tunnel FM all wearing the chip at once (and Night Run AM above them). THE PAGE WAS CONTRADICTING ITS OWN HERO, which has always announced exactly one station under ON AIR THIS HOUR. Cause: one prop was carrying two different claims. `onAir` was fed `live.includes(id)` — every SCHEDULED station inside its window — and it drove both the dimming AND the chip; windows deliberately overlap (`schedule.ts` needs them to, or there would be dead hours), so at any moment three or four are open, and the round-the-clock stations passed `: true` on top of that. A badge four rows wear at once is decoration, not a signal. FIX: split the claims. `onAir` keeps driving the DIMMING only — a station that keeps hours and is outside them sits back, everything else stands at full strength — and a new `featured` prop drives the CHIP, fed from the same `primaryOnAir()` the hero uses, so the two can no longer disagree. Three legible levels: quiet / open / on air. Nothing is gated on any of it; off air is still presentation only (see schedule.ts). THE CONTROL IS WHAT MADE THE TEST WORTH RUNNING: the probe counts chips and reads back which station each sits on, and it was run against the STASHED old code first — 3 chips (Daylight AM, Rain Drive FM, Coastal FM) versus 1 (Coastal FM, matching the hero). Without that, "found 1" is equally consistent with a selector that matches almost nothing.
- THE RECORD AND THE SHELL CAST A SHADOW NOW 2026-08-19 (OTA) — owner: "add some shading behind the vinyl and cassette mode like how we did to the cd mode… add depth". New `src/components/CastShadow.tsx`, one component for both (and available to any future object), mounted as the FIRST child of each deck. THREE THINGS ARE LOAD-BEARING, and every one of them was caught by measuring rather than looking. (1) THE CANVAS MUST BE BIGGER THAN THE OBJECT — the 13.08 CD lesson: a shadow falls BESIDE the thing casting it, and each mode's object sits on a canvas exactly its own size. (2) AND THE PARENT MUST NOT CLIP IT. Both decks' root Views are exactly the object's box, so `overflow: 'visible'` is required on each; without it the halo measured strongest on the LIT side, which is the giveaway — below the cassette's shell it was −0.29 clipped vs −8.89 free. Controlled for: outside the deck's own box nothing got lighter by more than 2 levels, so making the box visible revealed no previously-clipped content, only the shadow. (3) THE CORE IS TRANSPARENT, which is not an optimisation. Both objects are deliberately TRANSLUCENT — the record is a clear pressing, the shell a tinted one, and the blurred station scene shows through both, which is most of what makes them read as objects in a place. A filled shadow shows straight through the thing casting it and turns it opaque. So what is drawn is only the part you could actually see: the halo outside the edge. SHAPES: a disc takes one radial gradient; a rounded rectangle takes a stack of 14 outlines, each slightly larger and fainter, because there is no blur filter available to us (the standing rule since the mirror ball's bloom). THE ARITHMETIC TRAP IN EACH. The gradient's stops are percentages of its RADIUS, so the object's edge sits at `(width / w) * 100` — writing `* 50`, which reads perfectly plausibly, peaks the halo half way inside the record and darkens its whole outer half. And the stacked outlines OVERLAP by design, so roughly two of them cover any one distance: each must be `1 - (1 - want)^(1/OVERLAP)`, not `want` divided by a guessed number — the guess made the cassette's halo a third of the record's. MEASURED, by quadrant, against the same shot with the component removed: the record's ring went −10.5 down-right, −7.5 down-left, −3.2 up-right, −1.1 up-left; the shell −8.9 below, −6.6 right, −3.6 left, −2.5 above. That ordering IS the test — a cast shadow from this app's upper-left key light has to be darkest down and to the right, and a number alone cannot tell you it landed on the correct side. Static, memoized, ~15 extra paths in total; checked in portrait and landscape, both decks, zero page errors.
- A HUG IS NOT A SHADOW UNDERNEATH 2026-08-19, later (OTA) — owner, off device shots of the cassette and the vinyl: "the shading is a bit off for these two modes. Tweak it so it sits slightly under the mode. And can we lift the darkness of the cassette mode, it reduces the effects of the shadowing." SHE WAS SEEING A RING. The reach above an object is `SPREAD - DY` and below is `SPREAD + DY`, so at the shipped 0.1/0.034 the shadow stood **22.7px proud of the cassette's top edge** — arithmetic, not opinion. THE FIRST FIX WAS WRONG, AND THE MEASUREMENT CAUGHT IT: pushing DY to 0.072 to slide the ring downward reads as obviously correct and is not, because rule (3) makes the hug's CORE TRANSPARENT — so moving it down moves the hole down with it, and the hole lands exactly where the shadow was wanted. Measured: the band just below the cassette came out **lighter by 8.5**, the opposite of the intent. Never offset a hollow ring and call it a cast shadow. WHAT SHIPS IS TWO SHAPES. The HUG is now tight (SPREAD 0.1 → 0.045, DY 0.034 → 0.020) and does one job — giving the edge form. A separate **POOL** — one soft ellipse under the bottom edge, `POOL_DROP/POOL_WIDTH/POOL_PEAK` — is what actually grounds the object, which is how a contact shadow is built anywhere. The canvas grew downward to hold it, because a clipped pool ends in a straight line and that is the one thing a shadow may never do. Measured after: above **+5.1 lighter** on the cassette and +0.1 on the record, below **−2.5** and **−8.3 darker**. Above lighter and below darker at the same time is the whole test; either number alone can be got by accident. THE CASSETTE'S DARK MASS: the tape packs were `#17100a`, near-black, which made the two reels the darkest thing on the screen and left the new shadow nothing to read against. Lifted to `#3a2a18` — measured 121.4 → 130.3 — while the 0.97 OPACITY is untouched, because that one is physical (you cannot see the road through wound tape) and was settled on 03.08.
  THREE THINGS THAT BIT, all worth knowing. (1) **AN ABSOLUTELY-POSITIONED SHEET CANNOT LIVE INSIDE THE PAGE'S ScrollView** — it is placed against the scroll CONTENT, so it mounts somewhere down the page and is simply never seen. Both sheets are rendered as siblings of the ScrollView in cruise.tsx, which is exactly how the Modes tab already does it; the card asks the page via `onAsk`. (2) **StationSheet calls `onClose()` immediately after `onPick()`** (`onPress={() => { onPick(r.id); onClose(); }}`), so two independent state flags do not work — the close wipes what the pick just set and the second sheet never opens, which looks identical to a sheet that failed to render. One object with an explicit step, and a close handler that cancels ONLY when nothing was chosen. (3) **ModeSheet's chip row sat UNDER the floating tab bar** and could not be tapped at all; the caller passes `TAB_SAFE_INSET`, because in a drive there is no tab bar and the sheet must not assume one.
- NIGHT RUN IS TEAL NOW, AND THE PHOTOGRAPH DECIDED IT 2026-08-19 (OTA) — owner: "have the accents on night run station a teal colour from the background image". NOT PICKED BY EYE. The picture was measured the way `scripts/marketing/tints.py` measures a screenshot — weight every pixel by saturation x brightness — and **83% of its colour sits between hue 180 and 195, with a weighted mean of 182**, i.e. the photograph is teal and the accents were jet blue at 218. That is the same mismatch the 31.07 round fixed one step short of: it moved the station magenta → blue when the picture had never been blue either. The new anchors are sampled rather than invented — the lit neon in the shot averages rgb(95,181,200) and the deep tones rgb(22,90,99). EVERY COLOUR ON THE STATION MOVED TOGETHER, which is 31.07's rule and still right: gradientColors, cardGradient, eqColors, glowColor and iconBg. AND THE OVERRIDE THAT WOULD HAVE FOUGHT IT: `VINYL_ACCENTS` in VinylMode carried `'night-run': '#2B4CFF'`, a hardcoded deep blue for the record's rim — exactly the mistake the comment directly above it records for Sunset's amber (04.08: "don't invent a third colour for a station — the accent slot exists"). Deleted, so the ring falls through to eqColors[1] like every other mode. When a station's palette changes, grep for its id: a per-mode override is the thing that silently disagrees. Verified by reading the rendered colour rather than looking at it — the mood ring measures rgba(25,198,212) in both orientations.
- THE LANDSCAPE PANEL TAKES APPLE'S PROPORTIONS 2026-08-19 (OTA) — owner, with a screenshot of Apple Music's landscape player beside ours: "I like how Apple has their landscape view with nice and enlarge buttons — can we replicate that style? Currently it's small and sits at the bottom. Keep the bottom pills at the bottom and enlarge the title and the artist name. Slightly make the top text smaller." THE CAUSE WAS ONE SPACER. `panelInner` had a single `flex: 1` under the station caption, so the song block, the seek bar, the transport AND the pills were all shoved into the foot of the panel under a tall empty gap. There are now TWO balanced spacers, with the pills outside the lower one — so the control block floats in the middle of the space between the caption and the pills, and the pills stay pinned to the bottom as asked. SIZES: title 20 → 27, artist 13.5 → 16, play button 58 → 68, skip glyphs 36 → 44, pause bars 7x24 → 8x28. The panel is wider than a portrait column and was setting the title at portrait size, so the song read as a caption rather than as the subject. TOP TEXT: `StationIdentity`'s `compact` variant is what shrinks — and it turned out to shrink almost nothing, because `nameCompact` was byte-identical to `name` (15pt both) and the eyebrow had no compact variant at all. Now 13.5pt with a 9pt eyebrow. LandscapeChrome is its only caller, so nothing else moves. Checked with a track stubbed into the switchboard (removed in the same session — `git diff` it before committing), since without one there is no artist line and no seek bar and the layout cannot be judged. Fits at 852x393 and at 667x375, the smallest phone still around; on the SE the Add Playlist pill still abbreviates, which it did before this and is a pre-existing narrow-panel condition.
  AND THE HARNESS TRAP THIS CREATED, now fixed for good in `scripts/harness/visible.mjs`: the home page now mounts a mode picker and a mood picker, parked off-screen, so the SAME WORDS exist two or three times in the document and every harness reaching for `.first()` silently started aiming at a parked chip (measured: the phantom "Equalizer" at y=1531, the real card at y=420). Three health-walk steps failed while the app was perfectly fine. THE VIEWPORT ALONE IS NOT A SUFFICIENT FILTER and the first fix was caught by it — `scrollIntoViewIfNeeded()` on the phantom scrolls the page TO the phantom, which duly makes it "on screen". The honest test is REACHABILITY: a parked sheet renders with `pointer-events: none`, so a person cannot touch it however far you scroll. `visibleClicker` prefers reachable-and-on-screen, falls back to reachable-then-scroll, and is shared by health/rest/scrub-rest rather than copied.
- LONG TITLES REEL EVERYWHERE NOW, AND THE WEB BUILD COULD NEVER HAVE TOLD YOU 2026-08-19 (OTA) — owner, off a landscape cassette on Garage AM: "make sure that the long song titles reel across the tape and on landscape mode. It only works on portrait mode, but it needs to work for landscape mode and across all modes and stations", plus "increase the size of the play buttons, a bit more so it's easy for drivers to push". THREE FINDINGS, and the middle one invalidates a lot of past screenshot evidence. (1) TWO PLACES GENUINELY DID NOT REEL. The landscape panel's title was the one title left in the app set as a plain `<Text numberOfLines={1}>`, and the panel is NARROWER than a portrait column, so it is where a long title runs out of room first. And the cassette's INLAY label was hard-truncated at 21 characters in SVG. SVG text cannot marquee — panning it means animating an SVG prop, which is a JS-thread re-render of the whole shell every frame, the architecture this app spent 08.08 removing — so the title moved OUT of the drawing into a real RN `MarqueeText` overlay positioned in the SVG's own units x scale (label x 30..310, baseline y 52 at font 11). It sits above the two broad glass reflections, which are 0.045 and 0.025 opacity, so nothing is lost. Every mode shares LandscapeChrome, so "all modes" is one fix. (2) **`onTextLayout` IS NOT IMPLEMENTED IN react-native-web AT ALL** (grep its Text exports — zero hits). MarqueeText measured the true line width only through that callback, so in the web build the ghost never reported, the overflow always computed as zero, and NO title in the entire app has ever panned in a browser. Every marquee screenshot ever taken here shows a still, ellipsized line — indistinguishable from a broken marquee. It now measures through `onLayout` as well (implemented on both), with the ghost text sitting at its natural width inside a 10000-wide row so nothing can truncate; the larger of the two wins and each is stored separately so a change of song replaces both. That is what makes this feature TESTABLE from here at all. (3) THE WIDTH HAD TO MOVE OFF THE TEXT AND ONTO A VIEW. Once web could measure, the pan ran and carried an ellipsized line clean out of view — a title that scrolled away to nothing. Measured: the visible Text's computed width stayed at the box's 294px while its own content was 777px, i.e. `width: textW + 8` on a Text is honoured on iOS and not in RNW's flex resolution. A View honours it on both. Verified by INK, not by eye: sampling the title's row, the panning text's pixels stop at x=828 against a text column ending at 830, so it is genuinely clipped to its box rather than bleeding over the scene. TRANSPORT: play button 68 → 80, which is exactly the portrait play circle — landscape was the smaller of the two targets — with skip glyphs 44 → 48 and the pause bars 9x32. HARNESS: scratchpad marquee probe opens a mode with a long title stubbed into the switchboard, samples the title element twice ~4s apart and reports MOVED/still plus rect, scrollWidth, computed width and display. A still reading is the useful one — it is what a broken marquee AND an unmeasurable one both look like, so check `scrollWidth` against the computed width before concluding anything.
- NEXT (owner, 26.07 sign-off): "polish on those card artwork" — pick this up first next session. Ask WHICH cards they mean before starting: the Stations-page station cards (smoked glass just landed, owner said "keep the cards for now, might change them later") or the share card's per-mode artwork. The four-direction mockup harness is worth rebuilding for either — see the smoked-glass entry above for how it was made.
- THE HOME HEADER WAS NAMING A STATION THAT WASN'T ON, AND ITS NAME WAS WHITE ON PAPER 2026-08-19 (OTA) — owner: "sometimes the top 'now playing section' plays when no station is really playing… and the station title comes out white when it's on light mode", plus "run a check between light mode and dark mode to see if there's visual clashes". (1) THE HONESTY BUG. `EqualizerHeader` rendered whenever the music service reported ANYTHING playing — and with no drive there is no station to name, so `nowStation` fell back to the HERO'S SUGGESTION and the app announced NOW PLAYING over a station that was not on. Her screenshot proves it: the "I can hear Welcome to Paradise" card is right above it, so the music was real and the station named was not. The header belongs to a DRIVE and now renders only when `np.session` exists; music playing outside one already has an honest home in that card, which names the actual song and offers to wrap a station round it. Motion now goes through `confirmedPlaying(np.playing, track, musicSwitching)` — the same rule every mode's scene uses, so the meter waits for the service's verdict instead of the transport's optimism. (2) THE WHITE TITLE. That component predates the light theme and was never converted: both lines were hardcoded dark-theme literals, and `Cruise.textPrimary` is `#FFFFFF`, so on paper the station's name simply vanished. Now themed, with each token's dark side byte-identical to the literal it replaces (utils/appearance.ts's own rule). NOTE the contrast sweep could never have caught this — it walks the pages with NO drive open, and this header only exists WITH one. Measured after: rgb(23,23,27) on rgb(246,244,239) in light, white on black in dark. (3) THE SWEEP FOUND A THIRD THING, AND IT IS THE 14.08 RULE AGAIN: a colour arriving from outside the palette and drawn as a MARK must go through `readableOn`. Mountain Pass's eqColors are literally three whites, so on the light Stations page its icon was invisible — measured 0.58 of a luminance level from the empty row beside it against 7.3 and 7.9 for its neighbours. Through `readableOn` it lands at 13.55 and keeps its hue; Tunnel, already dark enough, moves 7.87 → 7.92, i.e. untouched. Applied at the dial's accent and to the home meter's bars, which stand on the page for the same reason. `readableOn` returns early in dark, so the dark theme cannot be affected. THE FIVE STATIONS-PAGE WARNINGS ARE STILL FALSE POSITIVES, and this time verified rather than assumed: sampling the real pixels behind each flagged line (they sit on the station photograph, which the probe's ancestor walk cannot see) gives 5.4:1, 5.9:1, 7.9:1, 11.6:1 and 19.8:1.
- MODE LINE-UP DECISION (2026-07-25, owner): supersedes the 22.07 freemium split. Retire SOUND WAVES (`waves`) — it only ever existed as the redundant-with-Equalizer giveaway, and the bar moved once Vinyl and Disco Ball got their detail passes. Add CD as a PREMIUM mode; promote CIRCULAR EQ (`orb`) to FREE in Sound Waves' place — same "second EQ is the right giveaway" logic, but a better-looking mode, so the free tier gets an upgrade while the newest work stays a paid draw. Resulting split: FREE = Cassette, Equalizer, Circular EQ. PREMIUM = Vinyl, Tuner, Horizon, Disco Ball, CD. TRAP when removing a mode: `lastCruise` (and the Modes tab / per-station selections) store the mode as a BARE STRING, so deleting `waves` strands anyone whose saved cruise points at it — add an unknown-mode → Equalizer fallback in the same change. Cheapest moment to delete is BEFORE public release (nobody has it saved); build on the preview channel only while iOS is in review.
- AN OFF-AIR STATION ASKS BEFORE IT PLAYS 2026-08-19 (OTA) — owner: "should the stations not on air be locked until when it's available? It says back at 'certain time' but you're still allowed to play the station. I suggest to keep them locked." SHE IS RIGHT ABOUT THE FAULT: the station page printed BACK AT 5PM over a button that started the drive regardless, which is a promise the app does not keep. She chose an ASK over a lock after seeing the numbers, and the numbers are the reason to record this: the windows deliberately overlap only three or four at a time, so a true lock would put SIX OR SEVEN OF THE TEN stations out of reach at any given moment and somebody who loves Sunset AM could reach it for one hour a day — and the FM band already wears a padlock for free users, so a second padlock on the same page would read as "pay to unlock". The rule at the top of schedule.ts therefore still stands: off air is presentation, not permission. `needsOffAirAsk(id)` is the whole rule (scheduled AND outside its window, so a round-the-clock station and the driver's own creations never ask) and `src/components/OffAirAsk.tsx` is the card. It is an IN-PAGE OVERLAY, not a Modal — it is raised from inside StationDetailModal, which is itself a Modal, and iOS will not stack a second one over the window NowPlayingHost holds (the PreviewGate and mood-sheet trap). THREE ENTRY POINTS, and they are the complete set: StationDetailModal's Start Drive (which every card, shelf and dial row leads to), the Modes tab's mood sheet, and the home hero — which resumes the LAST cruise, so it is the one that can name an off-air station without the user choosing it. Mid-drive station switching (the Tuner's dial, Change Mode) deliberately does NOT ask: that is someone already listening, and a question at the wheel is worse than the inconsistency. The adopt card is exempt too — the music is already playing, so "shall we play it?" is incoherent. NOTE the Modes path must carry its own copy of the mode: StationSheet closes itself the instant it picks, which clears `pending`. **AND A REAL BUG THE TESTING FOUND**: the card read "Back at 4pm" for a station that comes on at five. `backOnLabel` worked the hour out as `now + a ROUNDED gap in minutes`, so at 04:38:57 the 741-minute gap added back to 16:59:57 — the same trap `upNext` already records, in a smaller disguise, and it fires on any call made part-way through a minute, i.e. every real one. The hour now comes from `nextOnAirHour`. Six seconds-of-the-minute cases are in scripts/test-schedule.mjs. HARNESS: every harness that starts a drive from the mood sheet now meets this ask, and WHETHER it appears depends on the clock — so ignoring it would pass in the evening and fail all afternoon, the worst kind of flake. `answerOffAir(page)` is shared from scripts/harness/visible.mjs and wired into health, rest, scrub-rest and shots rather than copied four times.
- DONE 2026-07-25 (CD MODE BUILT + line-up change, OTA): `src/components/CDMode.tsx`, premium, id `cd`, icon `disc` (verified in the MCI glyphmap). Album-as-object per the references: mirrored disc behind clear jewel-case plastic (hinge spine + clips, corner brackets, hub rosette, micro-text ring, hairline scratches, dust). LAYERING IS THE WHOLE TRICK and it's the Disco Ball lesson applied up front: the iridescent fan is STATIC (diffraction belongs to the light, not the plastic) and only the printed label, wear and hub turn; the label sits ABOVE the fan because a printed face is matte and rainbow streaking across the artwork kills it. Album art turning with the disc is also what makes the rotation legible at a glance — the exact failure that cost four rounds on the ball. Fan = 50 overlapping low-opacity wedges (heavy overlap or they read as spokes); concentric data rings are rotation-invariant so they stay in the static layer. Rotation reuses the clock-derived phase (never reads back from the animation, so it cannot stall), 3.4s/turn vs Vinyl's slow drift, swipe-to-scrub. Built by prototyping offscreen first, then verifying by transpiling the component's OWN builder functions with `ts.transpileModule` and rendering their real output — reuse that trick, it checks the shipped geometry rather than a lookalike. LINE-UP: Sound Waves retired + file deleted, Circular EQ promoted to FREE in its slot, CD added premium. Free = Cassette/Equalizer/Circular EQ; premium = Vinyl/Tuner/Horizon/Disco Ball/CD. Added `knownMode()` in modeCatalog so a saved cruise naming a retired mode falls back to Equalizer instead of resuming into nothing.
- STORE BUILD CUT 2026-08-19 — production profile/channel, version and runtime both **1.3.0**, from commit **0dfbd54** ("An off-air station asks before it plays"), started by the workflow's `build` mode with `--auto-submit-with-profile production`, so it delivers itself to App Store Connect when it finishes. VERSION DELIBERATELY NOT BUMPED, and the reasoning is the pinned-runtime rule read correctly rather than mechanically: preflight reported **no native module changed since 057c023**, the last build seen to LAUNCH, so this binary is native-identical to a known-good one and nothing requires a new runtime. Holding 1.3.0 is worth something concrete — the store build can still fetch production-channel updates published at that runtime, and bumping would have cut it off from every update already there. 1.3.0 was also never released (build 29, 14.08, was built and never submitted), so the version string is free. WHAT IT CARRIES: two releases' worth. The public is on 1.2.0, so this is the whole of the unreleased 1.3.0 scope — the station photo, per-service playlists, the Y2K card — PLUS 53 commits since: the light theme, the broadcast schedule, session tickets, the driving/listening switch, notifications, self-updating, shuffle/repeat, the marquee sweep, the off-air ask. `docs/launch/release-notes-1.3.0.md` was rewritten for that real scope; it had been written against build 29 and covered a third of it. RULES WHILE IN REVIEW, the usual three: do NOT publish to the production channel at runtime 1.3.0 (preview stays safe); the owner's phone must NOT install this from TestFlight, or it leaves the preview channel and stops receiving updates (the 02.08 drought); and if it is still Waiting for Review after ~2 working days, request expedited rather than cancelling. THE BUILD IS NOT THE SUBMISSION. `eas submit` delivers the binary to App Store Connect — it does not create a review submission. Someone still has to open the 1.3.0 version there, attach this build, paste What's New, and press Submit for Review. And on release day, remember D2 in the checklist: an approved, released app whose page 404s is set to removed from sale, fixed under Pricing and Availability.
- …AND `--auto-submit` DID NOT SUBMIT IT 2026-08-19. Build 30 was started with `--auto-submit-with-profile production`, finished cleanly at 07:04, and at 07:33 AND AGAIN at 07:42 diagnose still printed **"submitted to App Store Connect: no submission recorded against this build"** — the exact line that exposed build 29's five-day sit in August. Running the workflow's `submit` mode uploaded it in fifteen seconds: `Build number: 30, App Version 1.3.0, Submitted your app to Apple App Store Connect!` LIKELY CAUSE, recorded as a hypothesis rather than a finding: the build line also carries `--no-wait`, so eas-cli exits the moment the build is queued and nothing is left attached to fire the submission when it completes. Not worth proving — the reliable move is to CHECK. The workflow's notice used to say "it will submit itself", which was simply false here; it now tells you to run diagnose when the build finishes and read that one line, then `submit` mode if it is missing. THE RULE, third time it has bitten: **a finished EAS build is not a submitted build, and "it finished" is not evidence that anyone can install it.** Check the submission line every single time.
- THE 1.3.1 BUILD CANNOT BE CUT UNTIL 1 SEPTEMBER — THE EAS FREE PLAN'S iOS BUILDS ARE SPENT 2026-08-19. Every check was green — tsc clean, all nine offline suites passing, the 15-step health walk landing every step, the eight-mode rest harness green, the new input sweep clean on all three text fields in both themes, preflight 11/11 — and the build failed anyway, sixteen seconds in, for a reason NOTHING IN THE REPOSITORY CAN SEE: `This account has used its iOS builds from the Free plan this month, which will reset in 12 days (on Tue Sep 01 2026).` eas-cli then prints the generic `Error: build command failed.`, so **the line that explains the failure is the one ABOVE the error, not the error itself** — read the whole tail of the Start-an-EAS-build step, not just the last line. TWO CONSEQUENCES. (1) A FAILED BUILD STILL BURNS A BUILD NUMBER: `appVersionSource: remote` + `autoIncrement` increments server-side BEFORE the quota is checked, so this attempt took 30 → 31 and produced nothing; the second attempt was cancelled rather than left to run for exactly that reason. Build numbers are cheap and need only increase, so this is noise rather than damage, but do not be surprised when the next successful build is 32. (2) THE OPTIONS ARE WAIT OR PAY, and they are the owner's call, not a technical choice — the quota resets on 1 September, or an Expo paid plan lifts it immediately (expo.dev → account → settings → billing). NOTHING IS BLOCKED IN THE MEANTIME: the preview channel is unaffected (an OTA publish is not a build), so the owner's phone and every TestFlight tester keep receiving work as normal; it is only the store binary that waits. THE LESSON, and it belongs beside the submission rule above: preflight and the harnesses verify THIS REPOSITORY, and a build depends on an account, a quota, Apple's credentials and a queue that none of them can inspect. A green sweep is a statement about the code and never a prediction that a build will run.
- STORE BUILD CUT 2026-08-19, SECOND ATTEMPT — **build 32, version 1.3.1, runtime 1.3.0**, production profile/channel, from commit **5724773**, build id `a19a655f-a5c7-47b6-9d01-4a2b6f52f39c`. The owner upgraded the Expo plan, so the quota wall in the entry above is gone; the same command that died in sixteen seconds went through unchanged, which is the cleanest possible confirmation that the failure was never the code. Build 31 was consumed by the failed attempt and never existed as a binary. WHAT IS DIFFERENT FROM BUILD 30, and it is worth watching rather than trusting: this run printed `- Scheduling iOS submission` → `✔ Scheduled iOS submission` with a submission id (`f368f497-1662-4d4a-b608-6a392b002b80`) AT BUILD TIME, which build 30's log did not — so `--auto-submit` may genuinely fire this time. The rule stands regardless: **a finished EAS build is not a submitted build**, so when it finishes run diagnose and read the "submitted to App Store Connect" line before telling anyone it is with Apple. NOTE a scheduled submission is not a completed one either — it waits for the build and can still fail on its own (build 30's `ascAppId` failure was of that family). RULES WHILE IN REVIEW, unchanged: no production-channel publish at runtime 1.3.0 while it is being reviewed (preview stays safe), and the owner's phone must NOT install this from TestFlight or it leaves the preview channel. THE BUILD IS STILL NOT THE SUBMISSION FOR REVIEW: someone has to open the 1.3.1 version in App Store Connect, attach build 32, paste What's New from docs/launch/release-notes-1.3.1.md, swap in the reshot screenshots, and press Submit for Review.
- BUILD 32 SUBMITTED, AND CONFIRMED — 2026-08-19 12:07 UTC. The workflow's `submit` mode ran `eas submit --platform ios --profile production --latest --non-interactive` against build 32, and the log ends with `✔ Submitted your app to Apple App Store Connect!` — a real success line, not just a scheduled one. Submission id `af9204b2-8cd5-4d07-952f-fefffe0110d5`. Apple's own processing (the binary becoming visible in App Store Connect / TestFlight) takes 5–15 minutes from there, separate from this step. THE POLLING LESSON FROM THIS SESSION: the GitHub Actions job-status API returned `in_progress` on every poll for over 20 minutes after the step had actually finished at 12:07:20 — the job's real `completed_at` was under 4 minutes after it started. Do not trust a long run of "still in_progress" polls as proof something is stuck; re-check with a wider gap or just read the job log directly, which carries the true timestamps. THE BUILD ITSELF finished earlier (see the entry above) with `error: null` — so the "build failed" the owner saw on the Expo dashboard was almost certainly the SUBMISSION page, not the Builds page; the two are shown separately there and only one had actually failed (build 30's `--auto-submit` never registered). NEXT: same as before — someone still has to open the 1.3.1 version in App Store Connect, attach build 32 once it finishes Apple's processing, paste What's New from docs/launch/release-notes-1.3.1.md, swap in the reshot screenshots, and press Submit for Review.
- CD MODE design settled 2026-07-25 (round 2, owner reference = a Rumours CD in a clear case): disc is TRANSLUCENT (the drive shows through — that's what makes it read as an object), album art is GHOSTED faintly across nearly the whole face with a pewter wash over it (etched, not a printed label) and it doubles as the rotation cue, hub is small per the reference (0.155R centre hole, 4 gripper holes at 0.235R, hub ring 0.31R, stacking ring 0.62R) — the first cut's 0.52R printed label was the "inner dark circle too big" complaint, and with no track it fell back to a solid dark half-disc. Case dimension comes from PAIRED strokes: lit outer edge + dark line just behind + faint inner highlight = moulded plastic. TRAP: diffraction opacity is coupled to disc transparency — 0.30/0.22 is subtle over opaque silver but goes psychedelic over a transparent disc; it's 0.20/0.13 now. Still open: jewel case (shipped) vs dash slot-load framing, which is arguably more on-theme for driving. Do NOT commit the reference images: third-party album art, public repo.
- NEXT (post-launch, AMBITION — agreed 2026-07-25, and deliberately still inside the driving theme): (1) BREAK THE MUSIC CEILING FIRST. Spotify's 5-user dev quota means that for essentially every real user the visuals are disconnected from their actual playback — handoff mode, station tagline instead of song titles. Everything else is built on sand until that's fixed. Apple Music via MusicKit has no equivalent quota gate and works natively on the platform we launch on, so it is the single highest-value post-launch build, ahead of any new mode. (2) MAKE THE MOOD LIVE. The modes are a skin over someone else's player and could be copied; the taxonomy ("organises by how a drive feels") can't. Ambitious version = stations that respond to the actual drive — time of day, length, motion, weather — so it stops being a visualiser and becomes a category nobody occupies. (3) GIVE IT A REASON TO SPREAD. Nothing currently leaves the phone. A POST-DRIVE recap (route, songs, mood) rendered in the app's own visual language turns each drive into something shareable — growth with no ad budget. Post-drive only, never mid-drive; it must not become a reason to touch the phone while moving. NOT ambition: a tenth visual mode. Fewer and better beats more — six modes worth screenshotting beats nine that aren't.
- NEXT (post-launch): realism detail pass on other modes — owner loved the vinyl hardware round (album-art label, silver J tonearm w/ counterweight/dial/cartridge, groove-riding needle, 2026-07-23) and wants the same treatment elsewhere. Cassette is DONE (24.07). Remaining tail by the new bar: HORIZON and CIRCULAR EQ have never had a detail pass — bring them up to Vinyl/Disco standard or retire them the way Sound Waves is being retired. Reference-image-driven, SVG-drawn, all OTA-able.
- NEXT (post-launch): Apple Music support via MusicKit (platform concept already in code); Google ID verification clears → upload build, £1.99 product in Play Console, swap test_ key for goog_ key → closed test (12 testers/14 days); iOS home-screen widgets (owner asked 2026-07-23) — order: Start Drive widget (station-art tile deep-linking into last station/mode) → drive-stats/streak tile → "Tonight's pick" card → Live Activity during drives (lock screen/Dynamic Island, genuinely live). All native (Swift via config plugin, e.g. @bacons/apple-targets), EAS cloud-buildable without a Mac, fresh build per iteration (no OTA). NOTE: live Spotify now-playing in a widget is NOT feasible (no Apple Music-style access + ~15min refresh) — don't promise it
- DONE 2026-07-26 (ship from the phone): `.github/workflows/ota-update.yml` — a MANUAL GitHub Actions button (`workflow_dispatch` only, never on push, so the owner still decides what ships during launch) that runs `eas update` for a chosen channel. Owner presses it from the GitHub mobile app / browser, no PC needed. REQUIRES a repo secret `EXPO_TOKEN` (expo.dev → Account settings → Access tokens → robot/personal token). Before publishing it asserts EXPO_PUBLIC_SPOTIFY_CLIENT_ID/SECRET exist in that EAS environment — `.env` is gitignored so CI has no local copy, and a bundle published without them would silently break Spotify sign-in for everyone on the channel. Workflow files are NOT a fingerprint source, so adding this did not disturb OTA. **SUPERSEDED IN PART 13.08 — see the auto-publish entry above: a push to the development branch now publishes to preview by itself, and the button remains for everything else.**
- APP STORE SCREENSHOTS ARE NOW MARKETING SLIDES 2026-08-08 (owner, after seeing TinySong/Hayu listings: "how we get the App Store photos like these for free"): the listing used raw screenshots; it now uses captioned slides built here, no tool and no subscription. `scripts/marketing/build-slides.mjs` reads `screenshots-appstore/*.jpg` and writes `screenshots-marketing/*.png` at Apple's 6.5" size (1284x2778) via HTML + Chromium.
