THREE LAYOUTS, and the split is the owner's pick after comparing four directions side by side: slide 1 is **B** — the screenshot full-bleed with the headline over it, because the first slide has to stop a thumb and that is the biggest picture available; slides 2-9 are **A** — the phone floating on a glow, which gives the set a calmer rhythm; slide 10 is **CARD** — the share card alone, no phone. TRAP in B: the scrim must be FULLY OPAQUE down past the headline's foot (0% to 15.5%), or the app's own header ghosts through it — the first render had "Downtown FM" sitting inside the H1. TINTS ARE SAMPLED, NEVER PICKED: `scripts/marketing/tints.py` weights each screenshot's pixels by `saturation x luminance` and lands every result at one fixed lightness/saturation, so the surround belongs to its own picture but the ten slides still read as one set. Re-run it after ANY screenshot change — the stations reshoot below flipped that slide from teal to amber, which is correct because the new picture is the dial rather than the mountain. TWO THINGS THE RENDER CAUGHT: the raw share screenshot is the share SHEET (six buttons and a Cancel) and reads as a menu, so slide 10 crops the card out of it by measured pixel box (`CARD_BOX`, found by thresholding the bright block — the crop is done in CSS so the script needs no image library); and the stations screenshot was scrolled to the top, which sliced the last row against the screen's bottom edge — `scripts/marketing/stations-shot.mjs` re-shoots it at MAX scroll, which shows all ten stations, both bands and the red tuned marker, so the "Ten moods. Not ten genres." headline is proved by its own picture. `screenshots-appstore/03-stations-dial.jpg` was replaced with that reshoot. TYPEFACE IS A STAND-IN: this machine only has Liberation Sans (the free Helvetica clone). Drop a real font in and point the stack at it. NOT YET UPLOADED — these ship with version 1.2.0's listing update.
- NOTIFICATIONS: THE ENGINE WAS BUILT, THE PROMISES WERE NEVER TESTED 2026-08-12 (OTA) — third move against the retention score, and it turned out to be less "build it" than "prove it and fix what proving it found". `src/utils/notifications.ts`, `notificationCopy.ts`, `NotificationHost` and `NotifyPrompt` all already existed and were mounted in `_layout`. What did not exist was any evidence that the rules the app promises — at most two a week, 48 hours apart, nothing in the quiet hours, nothing on a day you drove, and the back-off ladder — actually hold. Three offline suites now run against the SHIPPED code: `scripts/test-notifications.mjs` (copy), `scripts/test-notification-budget.mjs` (the engine, with an in-memory AsyncStorage and a fake expo-notifications that records what it was asked to schedule), `scripts/test-schedule.mjs` (the timetable underneath). Run all three after touching any copy or any rule.
  **THE HONESTY BUG THE SCHEDULE EXPOSED.** The system's own rule is that a notification is a statement about the world — "Sunset AM is on air" has to be so. The day the schedule landed it made exactly one line false: `beat-traffic` announced **Cars & Coffee FM on weekday mornings**, and its window is weekend mornings only. Found by machine, not by eye, and the line now names Mountain Pass, which genuinely broadcasts at 8:20 on a Tuesday. THE REAL FIX IS STRUCTURAL: `plan()` now drops any candidate whose station would not be on air when it fires, so no future copy edit can ship a lie even though copy ships over the air and nobody has to run a test to do that. A `sunday-coffee` line was added at the same time, since the weekend window covers Sunday too.
  **TWO TOGGLES WERE DECORATIVE**, which is the same fault as a clock running over silence and the app had it twice on one page: `prefs.badges` was never read because nothing ever called `noteBadgesEarned`, and `prefs.newStations` was never read at all. Badges are now judged when a drive ENDS (the only moment one can be won) — and the FIRST check writes down what is already earned and says nothing, because "Night Owl, earned" about a badge won three weeks ago is untrue. `announceReleaseIfNew()` + a version-keyed `WHATS_NEW` announces a release once; a first sighting is recorded silently, so a fresh install is never told about a feature on day one. A version with no line written for it announces nothing, which is the right default. The row was relabelled **New in Cruise FM** — it carries whatever a release adds, and 1.3.0's is about photos rather than a station or a mode.
  **TWO REAL CONCURRENCY FAULTS**, both invisible until the tests existed. (1) Every read-modify-write on the notification state raced: tapping a notification opens the app, which BOTH zeroes the ignored streak (they wanted it) and reschedules (which reconciles and counts fired notifications as ignored) — two readers, two writers, last one wins, so a tapped notification could still shrink the allowance. All state changes now go through a serialised `updateState()`. (2) `reschedule()` returned silently when one was already running, and the commonest second caller is a settings change — which would then be planned against the OLD preferences and appear not to have taken effect. It sets `rescheduleAgain` and re-runs once at the end.
  MEASURED, and this is what the ladder actually does: ignored ×0 → 2 planned, ×2 → 1, ×4 → one every 13.5 days, ×6 → nothing until they open the app themselves. TWO OF MY OWN TEST EXPECTATIONS WERE WRONG and the code was right, which is worth recording: "never on a day they drove" can only be enforced for TODAY, because a drive log holds the past and next Tuesday is unknowable when the notification is laid down (what keeps the promise is replanning when a drive ends); and a fortnightly user who was sent one yesterday SHOULD still have one planned — thirteen days out. Assert on the gap, not on the count.
  NOT BUILT, deliberately: weather (phase two, needs a rough location, opt-in) and the learned-from-your-own-driving lines (need ~2 weeks of history).
- THE DIAL IS ALIVE 2026-08-12 (OTA) — second move against the retention score, and the biggest one available without new content: **the app is themed as radio and behaved like a static menu.** Ten stations, always the same ten, in the same order, identical every time you opened it. Real radio's whole appeal is being DIFFERENT when you tune in, which is what makes "what's on tonight?" a question worth asking. NEW `src/constants/schedule.ts` owns the timetable and every surface reads from it; the five-way hour switch that used to live inside `defaultStationForNow()` is gone, and that function is now a thin wrapper over `primaryOnAir()` so a dozen call sites keep working.
  **THE ONE RULE: off air is a PRESENTATION state, never a lock.** Every station stays fully playable at every hour — a listener who wants Night Run at two in the afternoon must never be told no, and on a real receiver the frequency is still there when nobody is broadcasting on it. What the schedule changes is what the app SUGGESTS and how the dial LOOKS. Nothing gates on it.
  SCHEDULE SHAPE: windows are half-open `[start, end)` in hours and may WRAP midnight (`{start:20, end:1}`), with an optional `days` array — that exists for Cars & Coffee, weekend mornings only, because "cold mornings, warm cups, engines idling" is a Saturday and not a Tuesday. `'always'` is a third state for Rain Drive, whose real schedule is the WEATHER; until that ships it is permanently available rather than given an invented time of day. THE HEADLINE STATION is whichever is nearest the MIDDLE of its own window (`primaryOnAir`), which rotates the pick through the day for free and needs no priority list to maintain; a day-limited window gets a 0.25 bonus so Saturday at 8am is Cars & Coffee rather than Mountain Pass. `'always'` stations never headline — they have no window to be in the middle of.
  THREE STATES, NOT TWO, and this is worth keeping: a station is lit (scheduled + on), dimmed (scheduled + off), or **neutral** (unscheduled — Rain Drive and every custom station). `isScheduled()` is what separates the third case, and the row prop is `boolean | undefined` so undefined means "this claim does not apply". Lighting a lamp that never goes out only makes the lamps that do mean less.
  UI: the dial rows get a 6px broadcast lamp in the gutter between the number and the name — **absolutely positioned**, because in the flex row it would push every on-air station's name across and the whole page depends on names starting at the same x; and a dot costs no row height, which this dial cannot spare (every row must stay the same height, 26.07). Off-air rows sit at 0.62 opacity — a step back, never a wall. The hero gained an `UP NEXT · <station> at 7pm` line under the button, which is the half of a schedule that gives anyone a reason to look again. The station page prints `● ON AIR` or `BACK AT 8PM` on the row that already carries the dial identity — that page has the room the rows do not. Recomputed on FOCUS, never on a timer: the page is looked at, not watched, and a repeating timer would be one more thing to gate on AppState (the 27.07 SIGKILL rule).
  TESTED, and the test is in the repo at `scripts/test-schedule.mjs` — it transpiles the shipped module and stubs only its one import, so it exercises the real code. The property that matters is **COVERAGE: no dead hours** — every hour of every day must have at least one SCHEDULED station on air, ignoring Rain Drive, which would otherwise mask a gap. That check earned its keep immediately: the first draft ended After Hours at 4am and started Mountain Pass at 5am, leaving 04:00–05:00 with nothing on air anywhere. Re-run it after ANY change to the windows. Also covers midnight wrap, the weekend-only window, the headline always being genuinely on air, and the "Back Saturday" / "Back at 8pm" wording.
  PHASE TWO, not built: weather for Rain Drive (needs a rough location, so it is opt-in and later), and notifications keyed to the schedule — which is the pairing that matters, because "Rain Drive is on air" is a reason to open the app and "come back to Cruise FM" is a nag. Build the dial first, then the notifications have something true to say.
- YOUR STATIONS COME BEFORE OURS 2026-08-12 (OTA) — first move against the retention score, which was the lowest thing on the honest scorecard the owner asked for (4/10; nothing accrues, nothing changes, nothing calls). THE REASONING: ten moods somebody else chose is a product, four you made is yours, and the more of yourself you have put into an app the more you come back to it. Custom stations already carried all of that — name, colour, playlist, and since 1.3.0 your own PHOTOGRAPH behind the drive — and were filed as a side feature under a YOUR STATIONS subheading on the stations page, invisible until you had already made one. So this round is positioning, not engineering: three changes, no new capability. (1) HOME gets a **"Your stations" shelf ABOVE Recommended**, absent until one exists (an empty shelf teaches nothing the invitation card says better), with a `NewStationCard` outline tile at the end — the end of your own shelf is where "one more" occurs to you. (2) `MakeStationCard`, an EARNED invitation on the home page: shows only after **two real listening sessions** (`getDriveStats().totalDrives`, the app's own 2-banked-minute definition, so mode-peeking doesn't count), never on day one — asking a stranger to build something before they know what a station IS gets ignored, and teaching people to dismiss your prompts costs more than the prompt is worth. It stands down permanently the moment they make one OR dismiss it; the shelf takes over the job. Same house pattern as SpotifyNudgeCard (AsyncStorage dismiss key, useFocusEffect). (3) STATIONS PAGE gets an **empty slot on the dial** while you have none — a vacant frequency (`— —`, a plus, no hairline) sitting at the foot of the AM band, because the only affordance before this was a small Create button on the hero and someone scrolling the dial could never learn the feature existed. It disappears the moment YOUR STATIONS appears. NOTE the last AM default's `last` prop had to go: something always follows it now, so suppressing its hairline left a gap.
  NEW `src/utils/createStationRequest.ts` — the create sheet lives on the Stations page and ONLY there (one home for making and managing, rather than a second copy of a 600-line modal mounted on home), so the home invitations set a flag and route to Stations, which opens the sheet on arrival. A MODULE FLAG, NOT A ROUTE PARAM, deliberately: `router.push('/stations?create=1')` reads better but the param STICKS, so returning to Stations an hour later re-opens the sheet out of nowhere unless every consumer clears it. Reading the flag takes it, so it cannot misfire twice — verified end to end in the web build (tap invitation → lands on Stations with the sheet open → close it → leave and return → quiet). Full 15-step health walk green, zero page errors, both states (with and without custom stations) screenshotted. NOT DONE and worth knowing: the home shelf does not pass `onEdit`/`onDelete` to StationDetailModal, so the ⋯ menu only appears from the Stations page — home is for playing them, Stations is for managing them.
- A LITTLE CAR ON THE SCRUB 2026-08-13 (OTA) — owner's idea, and the cheapest character the app has bought all week: the seek bar already reads as a road (the played part lit, the rest ahead of you), so the playhead is now a car driving the length of the song. `SeekCar` in SeekBar.tsx replaces the white dot; nothing is lost, because the touch target was always the whole 36pt row rather than the dot.
  TWO THINGS THAT MATTER IF IT IS EVER RETOUCHED. (1) IT SITS ABOVE THE BAR, NOT ON IT. At the playhead the bar changes from solid white to dark, so a white marker there is half over each and reads as a smudge — the first render had the wheels disappearing into the played half. `CAR_SIT = 4` puts the wheels on the surface without crossing into it, and a text shadow (vector icons are text) grounds it and separates it at any position. (2) VINYL HAS ITS OWN COPY OF THE BAR — it predates the shared one and grows its track while scrubbing — so the car is EXPORTED and used in both, or the deck would be the one mode without one, which reads as a bug rather than a choice.
  **MPH WAS ASKED ABOUT AND DECLINED, and the reason is honesty rather than clutter** (owner: "I was thinking the cars mp/h but it might just crowd the page"). The app has no speed: there is no location permission, and adding one for a decorative number would mean a prompt, battery, an amended privacy page and App Review attention — for a figure that would be invented. A made-up readout is exactly the class of thing this app has spent weeks removing (the clock running over silence, the scene animating over nothing, the notification naming a station that was off air). If a number is ever wanted there, the honest ones are already on the bar: elapsed and remaining.
- DRIVING, OR JUST LISTENING 2026-08-13 (OTA) — owner: "having a driving mode and a non-driving mode for those users who want to just display them on the phone while working… there might be a question like 'heading anywhere?' Nah/yeah. So drive quantities are recorded. And that wording isn't always about driving." It was already on record (11.08) that the driving frame had a question mark over it, and she is the case in point — she uses it in her room.
  IT ALSO FIXES A VAGUENESS. The app cannot tell whether anyone is driving; `DriveCheckCard` exists precisely because it is GUESSING. Asking once replaces the guess with an answer, so the numbers are true for the first time and the card can stand down entirely for someone who has said no — which retires the most intrusive thing in the app for half the audience.
  ASKED ONCE, THEN REMEMBERED (`src/utils/sessionKind.ts`, `HeadingAnywhereCard`). A fork in front of every session would tax the one-tap start, which is the app's best moment; a once-and-forever answer is equally wrong, because the context genuinely changes — car today, desk tomorrow. So: the question appears once on the home page, and after that a two-word switch sits under the hero. NOTHING IS GATED on the answer — every station, mode and control behaves identically. It decides what the app COUNTS and what it CALLS things, and that is all.
  **THE BRAND RULE, and it is the important one: KEEP THE DRIVING VOICE, SOFTEN ONLY THE CLAIMS.** ~540 drive/cruise references across src, but the driving character IS the product — it is what nobody else has, and the station names work at a desk anyway ("Empty expressways. Blue-lit dashboards." is evocative wherever you are sitting). So the atmosphere never changes: taglines, station names, "Let's cruise.", the on-air copy. What changes is every line that ASSERTS something about the person — the start button, the stats labels, the badge descriptions. `words(kind)` in sessionKind.ts is the one place that decides.
  STATS: `DriveEvent` gained a `kind`, and **absent means driving** — every session logged before today was recorded under the old assumption, and rewriting that history would be its own small lie. `DriveStats` keeps `drivesThisWeek`/`totalDrives` meaning DRIVES ONLY (so nothing that reads them quietly starts counting desk time as road time), adds `listensThisWeek`/`totalListens`, and adds **`totalSessions` for anything asking "have they used this app yet?"** — MakeStationCard was gating on `totalDrives`, so a pure desk listener would never have been invited to make a station.
  BADGES ARE **NOT** SPLIT, against the first instinct, and the reasoning is worth keeping: read what each one measures — nights, streaks, hours, stations, moods — and every single one is about USING THE APP, not about being in a car. Only the wording was drive-shaped. Splitting would have meant inventing a near-identical second set and leaving the desk listener with an empty shelf until it existed. They judge on all sessions and are worded to be true of both; the split that IS visible lives in the stats strip, which counts the two apart and says which is which.
  TWO REAL BUGS FOUND DOING IT. (1) `HeroCard` derived its eyebrow by sniffing `buttonLabel.startsWith('continue')` — so "Keep Listening" would have told a returning desk listener TONIGHT'S PICK for a station they were halfway through. Meaning now comes from a `resuming` prop, not from parsing copy. (2) `scheduleRecapIfDue` counted `drivesThisWeek`, which is zero for a desk listener for ever — they would never have received a weekly recap at all. It counts their own kind now, and `recapCopy` takes the kind.
  HARNESS NOTE, cost a round: after clicking "Skip for now" on the platform picker you must WAIT FOR THE SHEET TO GO before touching anything. Its backdrop lingers and swallows every later click, which reads as the app ignoring you when it is fine.
- EVERY SESSION LEAVES A TICKET 2026-08-13 (OTA) — the retention idea from 09.08, built after the owner approved it and asked "when can I test the recap?". When a session ends past the existing 2-banked-minute bar, a paper stub rises: the station, the mode, how long, which one of the week it was, and the songs it played. Profile grows a log of them under a seven-day strip; tapping a row reopens its stub.
  **IT IS A STUB, NOT A DRIVE RECAP, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE DESIGN.** 11.08 settled that the app may not claim a drive happened — it genuinely cannot tell, which is exactly why the "Are you driving?" card exists — so this states only what was OBSERVED: a station, a mode, a stretch of listening, some songs. No map, no distance, no route; there is no location permission and inventing one of those numbers is the class of thing this app has spent weeks deleting (the clock running over silence, the scene animating over nothing, the notification naming an off-air station). Wording comes from `words(kind)`, so someone who answered "just listening" is never told they drove.
  THE CARD IS PAPER IN BOTH THEMES (`#F4F1E8`), deliberately against the 13.08 light-theme rule that translucent panels follow the page. It is an OBJECT, not a panel — a printed ticket does not invert when the page does — and it is the one surface in the app meant to feel like something you kept.
  STORAGE: `DriveEvent` gained `mode` and `tracks`, capped at `MAX_TRACKS = 40` so a four-hour session cannot grow without bound. Titles are noted from the playback switchboard as they change and deduped on `lastTrackKey`, or a five-second poll would write the same song over and over. `recordDriveEnd()` now RETURNS the finished event, which is what `NowPlayingContext.stop()` hands to `justFinished` for the stub to read; old history has neither field and simply prints without them.
  **AND THE PRE-EXISTING BUG IT EXPOSED, which is why it was found at all: `modes.tsx` never called `recordDriveStart`.** A session begun from the Modes tab was invisible to the stats, the badges AND the streak — since the mood sheet landed on 03.08 — and nothing printed those numbers closely enough for anyone to notice until a stub had nothing to print. Fixed there, and `cruise.tsx`/`stations.tsx` now pass the mode through as well. LESSON: a feature that DISPLAYS accumulated data is the cheapest audit of the code that RECORDS it; if numbers have never been shown, assume they have never been checked.
  HOW TO TEST IT END TO END without waiting two minutes: temporarily set `QUALIFYING_MINUTES = 0` in driveStats.ts, drive, minimise with the chevron, press the mini-player ✕ — the stub rises. Revert the constant and confirm with `git diff` that it is not in the change, the same discipline as the DriveCheckCard timer.
- THE APP UPDATES ITSELF NOW 2026-08-14 (OTA) — owner: "if there was a way to automatically update the app when it's closed for them — instead of pressing 'look for new updates' — that would be good, so they aren't stuck without the update." `src/components/AutoUpdateHost.tsx`, mounted in `_layout`.
  WHAT WAS ALREADY HAPPENING, and why people still fell behind: `updates` in app.json carries only a `url`, so `checkAutomatically` defaults to ON_LOAD — check at launch, download in the BACKGROUND, run it on the launch AFTER that. A short visit never gives the download time to finish (the exact reason `UpdateCheckRow` was built on 30.07), and even a completed download idles until the next launch. The button was the right patch and the wrong permanent answer.
  WHAT IT DOES: on returning from the background after a REAL absence it checks, fetches, and `reloadAsync`es, so from outside it looks as though the app updated while it was shut. No timer anywhere — it hangs off AppState alone (the 27.07 SIGKILL rule) and does nothing at all while backgrounded.
  **THE RESTART IS THE ENTIRE RISK AND THE RULES EXIST TO CONTAIN IT**: never while `np.session` exists (a drive ending because the app updated itself would be the worst bug in the app — this covers a MINIMISED drive too), never with `np.sheetCount > 0` (same reasoning as AutoDim, 03.08), never within 60s of a cold start (reads as a crash, and could loop), and only after 3 minutes away so an app-switch doesn't trigger it; 30-minute cooldown. The busy guard is re-read AFTER the download as well as before, because a fetch on a moving car's signal is slow and a drive can start inside it. Guards live behind a REF — the listener is attached once, so a captured `session` would be permanently null (the modes' stale-closure trap).
  IF THE MOMENT ISN'T SAFE NOTHING IS LOST: the update stays downloaded and expo-updates' own launch behaviour runs it next time. Waiting is always allowed; interrupting never is.
  `shouldUpdateNow()` is EXPORTED AND PURE on purpose — everything it decides ends in throwing the running app away, so the rules must be readable and testable rather than inferred from an effect. `scripts/test-auto-update.mjs` transpiles the shipped component and covers each rule at its boundary, the drive guard against the most favourable conditions available, AND that the window is actually reachable on an ordinary day (a rule that can never fire is as bad as one that fires constantly). UpdateCheckRow's idle line is now "Happens by itself · tap to check now", so nobody who ignores it is falling behind. expo-updates is in every build since 15, so this is a normal OTA.
- THE 1.3.0 STORE BUILD WAS BUILT AND NEVER SUBMITTED 2026-08-14 — owner: "Are these updates coming through to other app users?" No, and the diagnose run (Actions → Ship OTA update → mode `diagnose`, channel `production`) named the cause in one line: **build 29 (version 1.3.0, production profile/channel, commit 959fa88) is FINISHED with `submitted to App Store Connect: no submission recorded against this build`.** A finished EAS build is not a TestFlight build and is certainly not a store build — App Store Connect never hears about it until something submits it — so the newest thing the public can have is build 27 (1.2.0), and everything since (photos, per-service playlists, the light theme, the Y2K card, drive tickets, the 14.08 round) exists only on the preview channel. The 08.08 entry added `--auto-submit-with-profile` to the build step and a `submit` mode for exactly this, which is the fix if it recurs; a build cut outside that workflow will not submit itself.
  SECOND FINDING, same run, and it is the channel/runtime trap in a new shape: the production channel's newest update groups are at **Runtime Version 1.0.0** (a week old — "Fireflies drifting through the mirror ball's room" and its neighbours) while the production BUILD on the store is **1.2.0**. An app fetches only updates stamped with its own runtime, so those publishes reached nobody either, and the store app has been running its own baked-in bundle since it shipped. Nothing to repair — a store build carries its own bundle — but it means "we published to production" has not implied "store users got it" since the 1.2.0 build went out. ALWAYS read the runtime on both sides before claiming an update landed.
  RULE THIS ROUND CONFIRMS: "did it reach anyone?" has THREE independent answers to check, not one — the CHANNEL (preview vs production, 02.08), the RUNTIME VERSION (30.07), and whether the binary was ever SUBMITTED (08.08). Diagnose prints all three; check all three.
- THE LOG GOT ITS OWN PAGE, AND THE LIGHT THEME'S LAST TWO BLIND SPOTS 2026-08-14 (OTA): three owner items off the first day of real use, and the third is a general rule rather than a one-off.
  (1) **THE LOG MOVED** ("the driver log is really long can it be collapsed into a page"). Printed inline, it pushed the settings further down every day, so Profile stopped being a profile. New `src/components/DrivesPage.tsx` — a Modal over `SettingsPageShell`, so it gets the family's header, card and edge-swipe-back for nothing, and it is the same shape as Account/Privacy/Notifications/About, which are all pages behind a row. Profile keeps ONLY the seven-day strip, which is the part with a reason to be seen every visit, and the whole card is the way in. The inline version's cap of twenty older entries is gone — that was right when it shared a page and wrong on a page whose only job is the log. `logTitle()` is shared by both so the heading and the page title cannot disagree, and it prints "Your drives & sessions" when the log genuinely holds both kinds rather than picking whichever is first.
  (2) **CONNECT YOUR MUSIC WAS STILL BLACK ON PAPER**, and it is the first screen a stranger ever sees, so it set the wrong expectation for everything behind it — the same mistake it was restyled away from on 03.08, in the other direction. Themed throughout; the ONE decision worth keeping is that the primary pill INVERTS (`pal.mode === 'light' ? pal.text : '#ffffff'` with the opposite ink), because the app's primary button is defined as a solid pill in the opposite of the page, and a white pill on a near-white sheet is not a button. `SpotifyConnectRow` was themed at the same time — the last row on Profile still on `Cruise.textPrimary`, i.e. white on white, which is the class of bug a light theme causes SILENTLY.
  (3) **A BRAND COLOUR IS TUNED FOR ONE BACKGROUND, AND SOME OF OURS ARE TUNED FOR BLACK.** Tidal's mark is `#CBCBCB`; on paper the glyph vanished into its own chip, and the same colour is set as TEXT on Profile's platform row. New `readableOn(color, mode)` in appearance.ts: returns the colour untouched on dark, and in light deepens anything above 150 perceived luminance toward `#1c1c20`, scaled to how pale it is and capped at 0.78 so pure white lands on dark grey rather than black. HUE IS PRESERVED — measured 0.0° shift on all six brands — which is the whole point of a brand colour, and deepening is usually what the brand itself does (Tidal's logo on a white page is black). Tested offline against the real brand set (`readableOn`: dark untouched, all six read on paper, the five that already read are byte-identical, hue shift, and the white/black/malformed edges). RULE: any colour arriving from outside the palette and used as text or as a mark must go through it; the app's own amber and gold are the hand-tuned version of the same problem.
  ALSO: the picker's brand chips take the brand's own colour in light instead of a white glyph, `PlatformIcon` already accepted a `color` prop so nothing else changed.
  HARNESS TRAP, the vacuous-pass one AGAIN and worth restating because it produced a screenshot that looked like a finding: `boundingBox()` is VIEWPORT-relative, so tapping an off-screen element clicks whatever is at those (possibly negative) coordinates and the step passes having hit nothing — the first stub run silently photographed the settings page instead. `scrollIntoViewIfNeeded()` first, and assert the thing that should have opened is actually on screen. SECOND trap, which faked a real bug: the drive log is APPENDED, so storage is oldest-first and `getFinishedDrives` reverses it — a seed written newest-first inverts the list and makes the ordinal count from the wrong end ("YOUR 1ST DRIVE" on the newest). The ordinal was correct; the seed was not. Seed data must match how the app actually writes.
  `scripts/test-contrast.mjs` now takes `BASE_URL` (it had :8081 hardcoded and a second web build was on :8085). Checks after: tsc clean, all five offline suites pass, health walk 15/15 with zero errors, contrast sweep clean on Profile in both themes with only the five known Stations-hero false positives (white type on a photograph the ancestor walk cannot see).
- PUSHING IS NOT SHIPPING — AND FOR A WHOLE DAY NOBODY NOTICED 2026-08-13: the owner, after three rounds of work: "Those updates still haven't gone through." Correct, and entirely my doing — everything was committed and pushed, and NOTHING was published. The OTA workflow was `workflow_dispatch` only by design (launch week, the owner deciding what shipped), so a push moved code into the repo and no further. Saying "it's OTA, build 28 will pick it up" was true of what the code COULD do and false about anything having been sent. FIX: `.github/workflows/ota-update.yml` now also fires on `push` to the development branch and publishes to **preview** — the owner's phone and the TestFlight testers, i.e. people who opted into unfinished work. PRODUCTION STAYS MANUAL, always: that channel is the public App Store app, and a commit must never change what a stranger is running or what Apple is reviewing. THE MECHANISM, worth copying: a push carries no `inputs`, so the job resolves `CHANNEL`/`MODE`/`BUILD_PROFILE`/`NOTE` once into job-level `env` with `inputs.x || default`, and every `if:` and every command reads those instead of `inputs` — so a push and a button press follow exactly the same path, through exactly the same guards (stale-main refusal, preflight, the readable-Spotify-keys check, and the runtime-version check). A `paths:` filter keeps it to `src/`, `assets/` and the manifests, because an update carries JS and assets only and republishing for a docs edit is noise.
- A STATION'S PICTURE IS TWO DIFFERENT THINGS, AND RN's `Image` ONLY TAKES ONE 2026-08-13 (OTA): owner, off a screenshot of the new home shelf — "the backgrounds don't come up on the cards for my stations". A built-in station's `image` is a bundled asset, i.e. a NUMBER from require(); a custom station's is a file on the phone, i.e. a STRING path. React Native's `Image`/`ImageBackground` accept the number and silently draw NOTHING for the string, which has to be wrapped as `{ uri }`. So every custom station rendered as an empty dark square. THE TRUTHINESS CHECK IN FRONT OF IT MADE IT WORSE — `station.image ? <Image/> : <Gradient/>` — because a non-empty string is truthy, so the gradient fallback never ran either and the card came out near-black rather than falling back to the station's colours. Why it had never shown before: the full-screen backdrop and the home hero use **expo-image**, which accepts both forms; only the three surfaces still on RN's own Image were affected — the home shelf card, the mini-player thumbnail, and the Stations hero (that last one latent, since a custom station can never be the on-air pick). All three now go through `stationImageSource()` in `src/utils/stationImage.ts`, which returns the number as-is, wraps a string, and returns null for anything else so the gradient fallback works again. RULE: anything still using RN's `Image` for station art must come through that helper; anything new should just use expo-image.
- THE LISTING TURNS APPLE-FIRST, AND THE CARD SLIDE STOPS BEING A SCREENSHOT 2026-08-12 (listing only, no app change beyond one share-card fallback). Three things, all off the owner. (1) **DESCRIPTION IS APPLE-FIRST** — "can we change the description to focus more on Apple users". It opened "Spotify organises music by artist" and put Apple Music second throughout, which is backwards twice over: this is an iOS-only app, so every reader is already in Apple's world, and Apple Music is the platform where Cruise FM actually works for everyone — Spotify's developer tier caps full in-app control at FIVE accounts, so for almost every reader "connect Spotify" means the visual layer and nothing else. Leading with Spotify was selling the thing most people cannot have. Spotify is still named in the same three places, one step later; nothing was dropped and nothing overclaims. Same swap on the vinyl slide's caption. Promotional text refreshed for 1.3.0 — that is the ONE field editable with no review, so it is where the newest thing belongs. (2) **A STUB'S DECLARED DIMENSIONS ARE LOAD-BEARING**: the 1.3.0 "your own photo" slide shipped visibly stretched (owner: "that's stretched funny -- i wouldn't use it") because the temporary `choosePhoto` stub declared 1600x1067 for a file that is really 1080x1620. `PhotoFrameSheet` lays the image out at those numbers under `contentFit="fill"`, so the portrait photo was force-fitted into a landscape box — the harness distorted it, not the app. Re-shot with the true size. RULE: a stub that stands in for a native module must carry the real asset's real metadata, or the screenshot is testing the stub. (3) **THE SHARE-CARD SLIDE IS NOW RENDERED, NOT SHOT** (owner: "id like to have the y2k share card option displayed on the preview cards"). New `CARDS` layout in build-slides.mjs shows the Y2K card in front with the Ticket tilted behind it, so the slide says "styles, plural" with no word for it. Both come from `scratchpad/share/render.js` against the SHIPPING components (`NO_ART=1 NO_SNAP=1`) rather than from a device, which fixes two stale things at once: the old raw screenshot was the share SHEET (six buttons and a Cancel — reads as a menu) and it printed `cruisefm.app`, the domain that was never registered. tints.py now samples `.png` as well as `.jpg` so the surround is still measured rather than picked. The old `CARD` layout and its hand-measured crop box are deleted. (4) FOUND DOING IT, and it ships in the app: the Y2K card's no-cover fallback was a flat tinted square — the state every companion-mode listener sees, and the one thing on that card that reads as a bug. It now falls back to the STATION'S OWN PHOTOGRAPH under a 0.22 wash (custom stations with no picture keep the tint), and the scrub bar's decorative `pct = 0.42` is 0, because the times either side read 0:00 and a bar sitting 42% along beside them says two different things at once.
- BUILD 24 (1.2.0) FAILED, AND A FAILED BUILD LOOKS EXACTLY LIKE ONE THAT NEVER RAN 2026-08-08 (owner: "its not updated on test flight"). Two separate things, and the first is the reusable lesson. (1) THE INSTRUMENT WAS LYING BY OMISSION: `eas build:list` reported build 24 as `Status errored` and nothing else, so the first read of it was "the workflow never submits, that's why TestFlight is empty" — true, but not the cause. The workflow's diagnose mode now prints the newest iOS build's status AND its `error` object (`eas build:list --limit 1 --json`), because a status word alone cannot distinguish "failed" from "never started" and both present to the owner as an empty TestFlight. (2) ROOT CAUSE: `expo-notifications` applies its own iOS config plugin AUTOMATICALLY — it is in package.json, so Expo picks it up without being named in `plugins` — and that plugin sets `aps-environment` unconditionally (node_modules/expo-notifications/plugin/build/withNotificationsIOS.js). That entitlement is for REMOTE push and needs the Push Notifications capability on the Apple app ID; ours has never had it, so Xcode refused the profile. Cruise FM's notifications are entirely LOCAL, which needs no entitlement at all, so the fix is `plugins/withoutPushEntitlement.js` — a four-line plugin that deletes the key, listed LAST in app.json because auto-applied plugins run before the ones you name. Enabling the capability instead was rejected: it means an Apple-side change, a new provisioning profile, and a push capability advertised for a feature that does not exist. Verified with `npx expo config --type introspect --json`: entitlements now `{}`. THE SAME FAMILY AS THE 2.5.4 REJECTION (29.07, expo-audio injecting UIBackgroundModes) — an Expo plugin can add native keys you never wrote, and the only way to know is to read the RESOLVED config. (3) SEPARATELY, AND ALSO REAL: a finished EAS build is NOT a TestFlight build. EAS builds the binary and stops; App Store Connect never hears about it until something submits it, and TestFlight only lists what App Store Connect has. `build` mode now passes `--auto-submit-with-profile`, and there is a `submit` mode for a finished build that was never sent.
- `--auto-submit` NEEDS `ascAppId` IN eas.json 2026-08-08: the first build with auto-submit uploaded fine, queued the build, and THEN exited 1 with "Set ascAppId in the submit profile (eas.json) or re-run this command in interactive mode. / Submission failed / Error: build command failed." Non-interactive eas-cli cannot look the App Store Connect app id up for itself. Both submit profiles now carry `"ios": {"ascAppId": "6793233679"}` — the number is the one in the App Store URL, apps.apple.com/app/id6793233679. READ THAT FAILURE CAREFULLY IF IT RECURS: the BUILD is unaffected and keeps running on EAS (it is already uploaded by then), so a red workflow run here does NOT mean there is no build — only that it will not submit itself. Note also this is exactly the sort of eas.json edit that used to be a fresh-build trap; since the runtime version was pinned on 30.07 it is not, so editing submit config is free.
- BUILD 25 CRASHED 40 ms INTO LAUNCH — A NATIVE MODULE NEWER THAN THE SDK'S CORE 2026-08-08 (owner: "every time I try opening the app it keep crashing"). SOLVED FROM THE .ips ALONE, and the method is the point. FIRST the timing, which reframed the whole search before any theory: `procExitAbsTime - procStartAbsTime` was 6.48M ticks on the first attempt and ~0.9-1.1M on the four retries, and at the 125/3 ns timebase that is ~270 ms then ~40 ms — i.e. the process died BEFORE the JS bundle ran, which instantly rules out every line of app code AND rules out fixing it over the air. `procRole` was **Foreground**, the case the 27.07 entry says to chase (the backgrounded 309s are ordinary reclamation). THEN the `termination` block named it outright: `namespace DYLD, indicator "Symbol missing", Symbol not found: _$s15ExpoModulesCore6RecordPAAE4from10dictionary10appContextxSDySSypG_AA03AppH0CtKFZ` — demangled, `Record.from(dictionary:appContext:)` — `Referenced from: ExpoMediaLibrary.framework`, `Expected in: ExpoModulesCore.framework`. Confirmed against the sources here: core 56.0.15 has `Record.convert(from:appContext:)` and NOT `from(dictionary:appContext:)`, so expo-media-library 56.0.10 was compiled against a newer core than SDK 56 ships. THE TRAP, and why no check would have caught it: `bundledNativeModules.json` publishes `expo-media-library: ~56.0.6` for SDK 56, and **56.0.10 satisfies that range**, so `expo install --check`/expo-doctor pass happily; `npm install expo-media-library@~56.0.6` also resolves to 56.0.10. The linker is happy too — only dyld on the device fails. FIX: the three modules added for 1.2.0 are now pinned EXACTLY, no tilde — expo-media-library 56.0.6 (the SDK's own version), expo-notifications 56.0.16 (was 56.0.23, seven patches beyond the bundled line and unproven; dyld stops at the first missing symbol so it may well have been next), expo-sharing 56.0.23 (kept, because build 23 is in the App Store running it against this same core — proven; an accidental bump to 56.0.24 during this round was reverted). RULE now in docs/launch/pre-submission-checklist.md: any build that adds or changes a native module must be LAUNCHED on a real phone before it goes near Apple — a launch, not a lint, precisely because no static check can see this class of fault. Also: expo-modules-core is pinned by `expo` itself, so the answer is to move the module down, never to move core up.
- THE BUILD PROCEDURE IS NOW MECHANICAL 2026-08-08 (owner, after build 25: "we should ensure to conduct a procedure to ensure nothing hasn't been missed"). `scripts/preflight.mjs` runs every check that can be made from the repo, the build workflow REFUSES to start a build or publish an update unless it passes, and each check names the build that taught it. What it reads: the RESOLVED iOS config via `expo config --type introspect` (never app.json — a plugin can add native keys nobody wrote: expo-audio added UIBackgroundModes and got build 7 rejected, expo-notifications added aps-environment and broke build 24), version==runtimeVersion, entitlements (must be empty — the profile carries no capabilities), permission strings (an unexpected one is a rejection risk, a missing one silently removes a feature, as NSPhotoLibraryAddUsageDescription did to Save Image), OWNER_MODE, the paywall self-guard (build 18's 2.1(b)), the working tree and HEAD, and tsc.
  THE CENTRAL IDEA, and the reason it is not just another checklist: **a native version is trusted only once a build carrying it has been OPENED on a real phone.** `scripts/preflight-allow.json` names that commit as `provenCommit`, and preflight reads the versions straight out of THAT commit's package-lock.json. Anything unchanged since then is proven; anything newer than the version this SDK ships AND changed since then is the build-25 shape and fails until it is pinned down or justified with evidence. Move `provenCommit` forward only after a build has actually been opened — never because it merely built. NOTE a plain range check cannot do this job: expo-media-library 56.0.10 satisfies the `~56.0.6` that Expo publishes for SDK 56, so npm, expo-doctor and `expo install --check` all pass it.
  IT EARNED ITS KEEP IMMEDIATELY: the first clean run caught `expo-constants` silently moved 56.0.17 → 56.0.23 by the day's `npm install` commands — unproven, newer than the SDK's own version, and already inside the build that was running at the time (build 26, abandoned). Pinned back to 56.0.17 and build 27 cut instead. Lesson worth keeping: `npm install <anything>` can move UNRELATED packages when it re-resolves the lockfile, so run preflight after every install, not only before a release.
- "YOU MIGHT AS WELL JUST USE SPOTIFY" 2026-08-08 (OTA) — a tester ON THE ALLOWLIST, i.e. with full in-app control, played through the app and told the owner she still had to keep opening Spotify. FIRST DIAGNOSIS WAS WRONG and worth recording as a warning: the obvious reading is the 5-user quota, so the answer drafted was "she never had real control, steer her to Apple Music". The owner corrected it in one line — "she actually got access to Spotify linking to the playback" — and the real cause was OUR OWN CODE. `playStationMusic` opened Spotify PRE-EMPTIVELY whenever `shouldWakeSpotify()` was true, which is the first play after a cold start or after 15 minutes backgrounded (added 03.08 at the owner's request, to avoid "spending a few seconds looking broken"). But a drive normally starts from a freshly opened app, so "first play of a session" is very nearly EVERY play: the app bounced the listener into Spotify before even attempting the API, which is exactly what makes it read as a launcher for Spotify rather than a player. WHAT MADE IT UNNECESSARY: `startPlayback` already carries a complete silent wake sequence — play → `GET /me/player/devices` → play by device_id → `PUT /me/player` transfer with `play:true` → 700ms → nudge onto the playlist — and we were skipping all of it. It is now tried FIRST and Spotify is opened only when the API genuinely answers `no-device`; the instant "Waking Spotify…" notice covers the ~1s wait so it is explained rather than blank. `src/utils/spotifyWake.ts` DELETED (its only caller was this branch). This reverses the owner's 03.08 call knowingly — flagged to her as such, one line to put back. LESSON, twice over: a pre-emptive redirect is a tax on the common case to save the rare one; and when a tester's complaint has an obvious structural explanation (the quota), check WHICH path they were actually on before designing around it.
- SAVE TO PHOTOS FAILED ON BUILD 26, AND THE IMPORT WAS A TRAP THAT PASSES EVERY CHECK 2026-08-08 (OTA): the owner tapped Save to Photos and got "Couldn't save that one. Share card still works." CAUSE: in SDK 56 `expo-media-library`'s ROOT entry still exports `saveToLibraryAsync` and `requestPermissionsAsync`, but they are DEPRECATION STUBS whose bodies are `throw errorOnLegacyMethodUse(...)` (node_modules/expo-media-library/src/legacyWarnings.ts). So the root import resolves, type-checks, bundles, installs and runs — and throws the instant the button is pressed, where our try/catch turned it into a vague message. The real functions live behind the `expo-media-library/legacy` subpath; the alternative is the new class API (`Asset.create`). RULE: a name existing on a module is not evidence the function works — for anything from a package that has published a "legacy" subpath, read the source before trusting the root export. VERIFICATION, worth reusing for any import-shape change: `npx expo export --platform ios` proves the subpath actually resolves in the NATIVE bundle (Metro, not tsc, is the authority there), and then grep the built .hbc for a string unique to the stub — `imported from "expo-media-library" is deprecated` is absent and `saveToLibraryAsync` present, which is positive proof the real implementation is what shipped rather than the thrower.
- PREFLIGHT'S OWN FIRST CI RUN WAS A FALSE RED, TWICE OVER 2026-08-08 — the gate blocked build 27 AND an OTA publish, both wrongly, and both faults were in the check rather than the app. (1) `actions/checkout@v4` clones only the tip, so `git show <provenCommit>:package-lock.json` had nothing to read; with no baseline every package reads as "(new)", so all ten packages that are legitimately newer than the SDK's floor came back as unproven failures. Fixed with `fetch-depth: 0` on the checkout, AND by making an unreadable baseline ONE explicit failure instead of a cascade of ten — a check that can cry wolf is worse than no check, which is the whole lesson of this repo's vacuous-test entries. (2) `npx tsc --noEmit` failed in CI on two `*.css` imports that are fine locally, because `expo-env.d.ts` is gitignored (Expo's own template does that) and is regenerated by `expo start` — a fresh checkout does not have it, and without it tsc cannot see Expo's css module declarations. Preflight now writes that one-line file if it is missing, which is exactly what `expo start` does. LESSON GENERALISED: a check that has only ever run on a warm working copy has not been tested. Run any new gate once on a clean checkout before trusting it to block anything.
- `provenCommit` MOVED TO BUILD 26 (712b43b) 2026-08-08 — build 26 was installed from TestFlight and confirmed to OPEN, which is the only evidence that counts, so expo-media-library 56.0.6 and expo-notifications 56.0.16 are now trusted baselines. It replaced 108c7e7 (build 23, the App Store release). Build 27 never existed: preflight's false red blocked it, and it turned out not to be needed — build 26's natives are sound, and the Save-to-Photos and Spotify fixes are JS, so they reach it over the air.
- THE "RUN A QUICK CHECK" BUTTON IS GONE FROM THE SONG LIST 2026-08-08 (OTA): the owner saw it sitting under a perfectly healthy queue and asked for it to go. She was right, and the reason is worth recording because it is about when an instrument stops earning its keep. That button appeared whenever `trouble` was set — which for Spotify is now the PERMANENT state, not an error, since 03.08 settled that Spotify will not give a development-tier app a playlist's contents by any route. So the queue fallback is forever, and a debug button was riding along under it on every drive. The question it existed to answer has an answer; keeping it only invited a user to run a check whose result cannot change anything. Removed all three surfaces (the Spotify trouble state, the Apple empty state, and the Apple healthy-list row added 04.08) plus the `checks`/`checking` state and five styles. `diagnoseSpotify` and `diagnoseAppleMusic` are KEPT in the utils — they cost nothing unused, they took real work, and re-surfacing one is a few lines if a new question ever needs them. The honest fallback copy stays: the sheet still says plainly that Spotify won't share the full playlist and that these are the queued tracks.
- THE APP FELT SLOW BECAUSE SIX MODES RE-RENDERED 25 TIMES A SECOND 2026-08-08 (OTA), owner: "my friend noticed that the app's response was a bit slow, and I saw freezing occur various times". Same architecture the Horizon rebuild killed on 04.08, still living in two more places — and the JS thread is shared, so it delays every tap and every JS-driven animation ANYWHERE, not just in the mode that owns it. (1) `FloatingNotes` was one rAF clock calling `setTick` every 40ms for as long as music played, and it is mounted in SIX of the eight modes (Cassette, Vinyl, Equalizer, Circular EQ, Horizon, Tuner) — an app-wide performance tax for a decorative touch. Rewritten so each note is an `Animated.View` that animates its own 0→1 life on the NATIVE driver and asks to be removed when it finishes; React now re-renders only when a note is born or dies, roughly twice a second at the default spawn rate. The spawner became a plain interval, so it is `useAppActive`-gated per the 27.07 SIGKILL rule — rAF stopped itself in the background, an interval would not. The eased curve and the piecewise fade are reproduced exactly through interpolations, so the motion is unchanged. (2) `TunerMode`'s 25fps `setPhase` existed ONLY to flicker `StaticNoise`, which is drawn at `offAir` opacity — i.e. invisible the moment the dial locks onto a station, which is the entire drive. Gated on `lock < 0.98`: off-station it runs, tuned in it stops dead. (3) STILL OPEN and the remaining one of this family: `CircularWaveMode` re-renders at 15fps because its 64 bars are recomputed from a wave function of `phase` — genuinely per-frame data, so it needs the Horizon treatment (sample the journey into native keyframes) rather than a gate. `PaywallShowcase` also runs at 30fps but never mounts while LAUNCH_FREE holds the paywall shut. METHOD NOTE: find these with `grep -rln requestAnimationFrame src/components` and then read what each tick actually does — the tell is a `setState` inside the loop, and the second question is how many modes mount the component.
- CIRCULAR EQ: THE CLOCK MOVED INTO THE RING 2026-08-09 (OTA) — the last component of the 08.08 performance family. Unlike the Tuner (gated) and FloatingNotes (moved to the native driver), this one genuinely needs per-frame data: 64 bars recomputed from a wave function of `phase`. What it did NOT need was to drag the whole fullscreen mode along — `phase` lived in the parent, so 15 times a second React re-rendered the header, station identity, song block, seek bar, transport, action row and backdrop to move some bars. New `WaveRing` owns the clock, the level and the beat kick and returns a `<G>`, so the work is confined to that subtree; the parent no longer re-renders at all while playing. Also cheapened the remaining per-frame cost: `BAR_COS/BAR_SIN/BAR_ANG` are computed once instead of 192 trig calls a frame, and `toFixed(1)` — startlingly slow at ~400 calls a frame — is replaced by `d1()`. WHY NOT THE FULL HORIZON TREATMENT (bars as native-driver views sampled into keyframes): the wave only repeats every ~42s, because the phase coefficients 1, 1.3 and 0.6 share a period of 20π, so every one of 64 bars would need a long keyframe table and there would be ~192 animated views. Recorded here so nobody re-derives it; the flipbook trick that saved the mirror ball does not transfer either, since a spectrum at 15fps would need ~600 frames and cross-fading one reads as blur. Verified in the web build: ring renders identically and its path measurably changes between samples.
- BEAT-MAP PROBE, TEMPORARY 2026-08-09 (OTA, preview only): a tester asked "are these visuals even following the music?" — they are not; the Circular EQ runs a hardcoded ~100 BPM pattern and the Equalizer a timed loop, and iOS lets no app hear another app's audio. The only route to genuinely following a track is Spotify's `/audio-features` and `/audio-analysis`, which publish real tempo plus every beat, bar and section with timestamps; combined with the position we already poll, the bars could hit the actual snare, all over the air. Whether we may call them depends on our app's REGISTRATION DATE (Spotify restricted these endpoints for apps created after Nov 2024), which cannot be determined from here — so `probeBeatMap()` in spotify.ts plus `BeatMapProbeRow` in Profile ask once, on a real drive. 200 = build it; 403 = the route is closed and the fallback is a narrow opt-in microphone mode, which is native and batches with widgets. BOTH THE PROBE ROW AND ITS IMPORT IN profile.tsx COME OUT once the screenshot arrives.
- SPOTIFY WILL NOT GIVE US A TRACK'S BEAT MAP — SETTLED 2026-08-09, DO NOT RE-TRY. The probe ran on a real drive (track "Zero — Remastered 2012") and both endpoints answered **403**: `/audio-features` 403, `/audio-analysis` 403. That is the restriction Spotify applied to apps registered after November 2024, and no scope, account or reconnection changes it — the same shape as the 03.08 finding that a development-tier app cannot read a playlist's contents. So the metadata route to beat-locked visuals is CLOSED, and Apple Music publishes no equivalent at all, so there is no metadata route on either platform. `probeBeatMap()` and `BeatMapProbeRow` are deleted; the question is answered and a permanent instrument for a permanent refusal is clutter (same reasoning as the quick-check removal the day before).
  WHAT REMAINS, honestly ranked. (1) THE MICROPHONE is the only thing that gives both the right RATE and the right PHASE, always, on any platform — and it was built on 20.07 and removed on 22.07 for reasons that still stand: over Bluetooth in a car it picks up road noise and distorts, and on iOS the recorder contends with Spotify for the audio session. A narrow return is defensible — opt-in, speaker-and-stationary, off in the car — because that is exactly the situation a tester was in when she asked whether the visuals follow the music. Native, so it batches with widgets, and it needs a microphone usage string on the listing. (2) A BPM LOOKUP from a public database (GetSongBPM, AcousticBrainz) is OTA-able and would at least make the pulse the song's real speed instead of a hardcoded ~100 — the same pattern as the iTunes artwork lookup already shipping. But it is a PARTIAL win and should be sold as one: without beat timestamps the phase is unknown, so the pulse would be at the right tempo and the wrong offset, which reads as "close but not with it". Coverage is also patchy. Recommendation on record: the mic, narrowly, over the lookup.
- YOUR OWN PHOTO BEHIND YOUR OWN STATION 2026-08-09 (JS written; NEEDS THE NEXT BUILD): owner's idea — "can people put their own images as backgrounds", scoped by her to custom stations only, one photo, uploaded by the user, with the flow "make your station, give it your photo, link your playlist". IT FITS BECAUSE THE APP ALREADY HAS ONE HINGE: `station.image` drives the full-bleed backdrop in all eight modes, the hero on the station page and the card in the list, and custom stations have always had it null — which is precisely why they read as colour cards among photo cards. `customToStation` now passes a saved path through, so filling that field makes them first-class everywhere with no downstream plumbing. NEW `src/utils/stationPhoto.ts`: guarded requires (absent until the build, like saveToPhotos), the library picker, and TWO COPIES written to documentDirectory — a 1400px one for card and hero, and a **56px** one for the backdrop. The tiny one is the whole trick: the modes never display a sharp photo (StationBackdrop's note — re-blurring a full image on every re-display got the app SIGKILLed once), there is no blur filter available at import, and blowing a 56px image up to fill a screen IS a blur, free. Same arrangement as the ten built-ins. Files live in documentDirectory NOT cacheDirectory, or iOS could sweep a station's picture away on low disk. TYPE: `CustomStation.image` went `null` → `string | null` (+ `imageBlur`), and every reader must still cope with null — every station made before this has none. NEW `UserPhotoVeil` in StationBackdrop adds a gentle 30% knock-back for user photos only, discriminated for free by `typeof station.image === 'string'` (built-ins are bundled assets, i.e. numbers): the curated ten are shot dark and the app's white type leans on that, and someone's beach photo would swallow the song title. Deleting a station deletes its photos. PREFLIGHT EARNED ITS KEEP AGAIN: expo-image-picker's plugin adds **NSCameraUsageDescription and NSMicrophoneUsageDescription** by default — two permissions the app never uses, on a listing, of exactly the kind that got build 7 rejected, and the mic one right after the owner declined mic features. Both switched off in the plugin config. NOTE the resolved config carries NO `NSPhotoLibraryUsageDescription` either: modern iOS runs the library picker out of process so no permission is needed — VERIFY THAT ON THE FIRST BUILD (checklist item), and if the picker crashes, add `photosPermission` back.
- CRUISE FM IS A **STROFI TECHNOLOGIES** PRODUCT 2026-08-09 (OTA for the app; the website needs a manual deploy): owner wants the app credited to a company rather than to her personally, with the app's name, function and data unchanged. Nothing in the product named a developer at all before this, so it was mostly an ADDITION: `src/constants/legal.ts` gained a "Who makes Cruise FM" section in the Privacy Policy and a "Who you are agreeing with" section opening the Terms (both dated 9 August 2026, regenerated into docs/legal/ and website/ by scripts/gen-legal.mts), the Profile footer carries a quiet uppercase "A STROFI TECHNOLOGIES APP" byline under the version line, the website footer credits it, and the store DESCRIPTION ends "Cruise FM is made by Strofi Technologies."
  WHY THE PARENT CARRIES THE SECOND APP'S NAME, and it is a deliberate strategic choice rather than a naming whim: **Strofi** — the social driving / route-discovery platform — is the bigger bet (docs/strategy.md), and Cruise FM is deliberately its FRONT DOOR. Naming the company after it makes that literal: every Cruise FM user reads the word *Strofi* before Strofi exists, which is free groundwork for the app with the much harder cold-start problem. It trades portability for concentration, which is the right trade when one product is clearly the main event. RISK IT ACCEPTS: if Strofi ever pivots or dies, the company still carries its name.
  TWO EARLIER NAMES, recorded so nobody re-proposes them or the ones checked along the way. **Veyra** lasted about an hour — the reusable lesson is that pretty, vowel-heavy coinages (Veyra, Vayra, Lumina, Nova, Aura) are the most contested naming space there is, because every founder reaches for them first, so another coinage in that family fails identically; the fix is a real-but-uncommon word or a compound of two plain ones. **Northing** replaced it (a surveying term, chosen for portability) and held for a few hours until leading with Strofi made portability the wrong goal. CHECKED AND REJECTED, all with software or App Store namesakes: Skywave, Longwave (an App Store developer with 3M downloads), Catseye, Faro (FARO Technologies, publicly traded), Rumbo (a transport app — same category as us), Sereno (Sereno Software Solutions Ltd is at UK Companies House), Estela, Bruma, Madrugada (a well-known band, bad for a music company), Ocaso, Glovebox (an insurance app), Highbeam (a $42M fintech plus two studios). Nothing in software uses Strofi. METHOD NOTE: the first pass applied too strict a bar — "does ANY company anywhere use this word" — which almost everything fails; the test that matters is whether anyone would confuse you with them, whether they are in music or apps, and whether they would block a trademark.
  THE ONE PLACE IT CANNOT GO, and this is the crux: the App Store's **Developer** line still reads *JESSICA ARROYO*. That field is not metadata anyone writes — on an INDIVIDUAL developer account it is the account holder's legal name and Apple does not allow it to be set to anything else. Changing it needs either conversion to an **Organization** account (a legally registered business plus a free D-U-N-S number; days to weeks, the D-U-N-S lookup alone can take a fortnight) or Apple Support granting a registered trading name with documentation — worth a ticket first, since it is far cheaper if allowed. Do not promise the listing can show a studio name without one of those. NOTE ALSO the bundle identifier stays `com.driftlore.CruiseFM` forever: it is the app's identity on the store, invisible to users, and changing it would mean a different app with no reviews and no existing installs. Same for the Expo account slug.
- WHERE THIS GOES — THE STRATEGY DISCUSSION 2026-08-09, written up in `docs/strategy.md`; read that file before any decision about what to build next, this is the summary. Owner asked whether Cruise FM ends up "another generic aesthetic app" or grows into something bigger, with **Strofi** (a social driving/route-discovery platform) as the next project. FOUR THINGS WORTH CARRYING: (1) the risk to Cruise FM is real but narrower than "generic" — generic means interchangeable and this isn't; the fixable problem is that BEAUTY ALONE GIVES NOBODY A REASON TO RETURN, and the move that fixes it is the POST-DRIVE RECAP (already on the 25.07 ambition list, but more central than that entry implies — Strava's product is not the run, it is the record of the run). (2) THE ONE FACT THAT DECIDES EVERYTHING IS STILL UNKNOWN: does anyone open it a SECOND time? Cannot be answered at ~2 users, and there is deliberately no analytics (the privacy policy promises nothing leaves the phone, so third-party analytics would break a public promise) — ask testers directly. Note the old ceiling is GONE: Apple Music has no user quota, so since build 23 any iPhone owner with a subscription gets full playback; recruit Apple Music users specifically rather than treating Spotify's 5-user cap as the limit. (3) STROFI'S WEDGE IS THE OWNER'S OWN IDEA, not the curated-routes one first proposed: ONE-TIME CODES THAT SHARE A ROUTE PRIVATELY WITH THE GROUP DOING THAT DRIVE (she had been out with drifters who kept switching apps to coordinate). It beats every other Strofi feature because it WORKS AT n=2 — discovery, meetups, garage and brands are all worthless without a crowd, and cold start is what kills community apps — it needs no content from her, and it distributes itself, since using it means sending the code to everyone coming. Order: coordination first, discovery second, community third. Build v1 STATIC (waypoints, not live convoy positions) and know that it needs a SERVER, which is a genuine step change from Cruise FM's no-account/no-server position. POSITION IT AS GROUP COORDINATION, NOT SECRET SPOTS — the same mechanic serves a club run, a track-day convoy or a road trip, and the coordination framing is what keeps it through App Review and out of a liability conversation. PRECEDENT THAT SETTLES THE SHAPE: DriveTribe raised ~£9M, had Clarkson/Hammond/May, ran five years and shut in Jan 2022 — it was a FEED, doing no job for you when alone, so when ad budgets moved there was nothing underneath; what works in this space is TOOLS (Rever, Calimoto — route discovery, community grew on top). (4) THE TWO APPS ARE NOT SEQUENTIAL: Cruise FM is Strofi's FRONT DOOR and the only free cold-start solution available, the RECAP IS THE BRIDGE (a recorded, mapped, shared drive already IS a Strofi route — build it once, it serves both), and the frequencies complement — Cruise FM covers the daily commute while scenic drives are monthly, which is why a log-your-drives product alone struggles. Get the retention answer from the cheap app first.
- 1.3.0 — YOUR OWN PHOTO BEHIND YOUR OWN STATION 2026-08-09 (NATIVE, needs the build; `version` + `runtimeVersion` both 1.3.0). The JS half landed 09.08 (c97dcd0); this entry is the build-readiness pass, and it found three things worth keeping. (1) `allowsEditing` REMOVED from the picker call: it sounds right — let them frame it — but on iOS it forces a **square** crop and IGNORES `aspect` entirely (expo-image-picker's own types say so), which would make someone crop a landscape photo square for something that ends up a tall full-screen backdrop, losing most of it twice. Taking the whole photo is correct because every consumer already fits it with `contentFit="cover"`, and the backdrop, card and hero each want a different shape. (2) THE PERMISSION TRAP, and it will recur with ANY two plugins touching one Info.plist key: `expo-media-library`'s `photosPermission: false` **deletes** `NSPhotoLibraryUsageDescription`, and it was silently deleting the one `expo-image-picker` sets — so the resolved config carried no read permission while app.json plainly appeared to configure one, and the checklist had recorded that absence as a deliberate decision. It was not, it was plugin ordering. Proven by dropping the `false` and re-introspecting (key appears). The `false` is gone and the string ships; PHPicker on modern iOS probably does not need it, but a missing string means a crash on first use plus another build cycle, while a needless one costs a line on the listing that a visible "Add a photo" button explains. RULE: when two plugins configure the same key, only the RESOLVED config says who won. (3) `manipulateAsync` is marked `@deprecated` in expo-image-manipulator but — unlike expo-media-library's root export, which is a THROWING stub — it has a real body that forwards to the new contextual API, including the `release()` calls. Verified by reading the source, which is the rule the Save-to-Photos bug established. `modules()` already guards on its presence, so a future removal degrades to "photo picking unavailable" rather than crashing. Both photo modules are pinned to the SDK's own bundled floor (picker 56.0.16, manipulator 56.0.17) — the safest versions available and the direct lesson of build 25.
- `SafeAreaView` DOES NOT WORK INSIDE A `Modal` 2026-08-09 (OTA-shaped, but rides 1.3.0 — see below): owner's screenshot of Settings → Notifications showed the back chevron level with the CLOCK, and "it happens quite often". CAUSE: `SettingsPageShell` was the only place in the app wrapping content in `<SafeAreaView edges={['top']}>`, and settings pages are presented inside a `Modal` — a separate iOS window. safe-area-context's SafeAreaView derives its padding from its OWN measured frame, and inside that window it measures zero, so the header landed at y=0. Note expo-router DOES render a `SafeAreaProvider` at the root (ExpoRoot.js), so the context is fine — it is the frame measurement that fails, which is why every fullscreen MODE (all in Modals, all reading `useSafeAreaInsets()` directly with `Math.max(insets.top, 20)`) has always sat correctly. FIX: the shell now uses that same explicit padding with the same floor, and the SafeAreaView import is gone. THE FLOOR IS LOAD-BEARING, not decoration — it is what guarantees clearance if an inset ever reads zero again, and the web build (where insets really are 0) is a free regression test for it. SWEPT: the only other `SafeAreaView` uses are visuals.tsx, profile.tsx and premium.tsx, all real routes in the root window, so all fine; `StationDetailModal`'s `insets.top + 12` gained the same floor (identical on a notched phone, protects the zero case). RULE: inside a Modal, read the inset and floor it — never `SafeAreaView`. Same round: the settings back-swipe zone went 44 → 96px from the left edge (a swipe starting further in fell through to the move-based rule, which loses the negotiation to the native ScrollView on iOS, so it silently did nothing), and the back button went 36 → 44 (Apple's minimum target) with a faint ring so it reads as a control rather than a stray chevron on black.
- ENLARGING IS NOT BLURRING 2026-08-10 (OTA): owner opened build 28 (1.3.0) and the first thing she saw was that her own station photo came out "really blurry and pixelated". The bug was a wrong ASSUMPTION in `stationPhoto.ts`, not faulty code. There is no blur filter in expo-image-manipulator, so the soft backdrop copy was saved as a **56px** thumbnail on the theory that stretching a tiny image to fill a screen IS a blur, for free. It is not: enlarging preserves the hard edges between the original pixels and simply makes them bigger — a 7x stretch on a phone, nearer 19x on the station page. Worse, JPEG codes in 8x8 blocks, and at 56px wide there are only seven of them across, so every block boundary becomes a ~170px seam. Measured: seam ratio 1.26 vs 1.00 for a real blur. THE BUILT-INS ARE 1080-WIDE IMAGES, genuinely gaussian-blurred — a completely different thing from a thumbnail, which is what made the comparison look safe on paper. FIX: a real low-pass by resampling twice in one pass (`[{resize:{width:BLUR_W}},{resize:{width:1080}}]` — manipulateAsync applies actions in order), so the detail is genuinely averaged away and the file is stored at the size it is displayed at, with nothing enlarged at draw time.
  AND THE SECOND MISTAKE, which is the reusable one: BLUR_W was first DERIVED from the built-ins' blur radius on paper (radius 3.5 ≈ resample to ~150) and the render showed it far too sharp — building windows still readable, which would fight the mode's object and swallow the white type. Lanczos resampling is not a gaussian and the equivalence does not hold. Measuring edge energy against all four reference backdrops put the real match at 90-120; it is 100, the soft end, because the curated photographs are shot dark and a stranger's photo has no such discipline. RULE: match the reference by measurement, never by deriving from its parameters. Harness: downsample→upsample→JPEG round-trip→compare `mean |∂x|` against `assets/stations/blur/*`, and crop a TEXTURED region to look at — the first comparison was cropped from sky, where every version looks identical and nothing is learned.
- THE TUNER DIDN'T KNOW YOUR OWN STATIONS EXISTED 2026-08-10 (OTA): owner switched to Tuner from a custom station and landed on After Hours FM, with that station's colours instead of her own. NOT a colour bug — the mode genuinely believed it was on a different station. CAUSE: `DIAL` was built from `STATIONS` (the ten built-ins) alone, and the lookups fall back to `'FM'` and `92.1` for an id they don't recognise — 92.1 IS After Hours FM. So every custom station opened the Tuner on top of it. `stationDial()` has always handed any id a stable place (an unknown one hashes into the AM band), so the only thing missing was telling the dial they exist: `DIAL_STATIONS` + `registerCustomOnDial()`, fed from the sync cache on the frame the mode opens and re-fed from `loadCustomStations()` for a cold start. `nearestStation`, `firstOnBand` and the printed scale all iterate that list now, so custom stations get real markers you can tune onto. NOTE the 26.07 decision to keep them OFF the dial was justified by the mood sheet not listing them either — that sheet was replaced on 03.08 by one that does list them under YOUR STATIONS, so the reasoning had expired and nobody noticed.
  AND THE ELASTIC BAND (owner: "it feels like an elastic band where I have to keep pulling to change stations"): `onPanResponderRelease` called `snapToNearest()` immediately and THREW THE RELEASE VELOCITY AWAY, so the needle sprang back to whichever station was nearest at the instant the finger lifted. FM stations sit 1.6-2.6 MHz apart, which at 72px/MHz is 115-187px, so any drag that didn't cross the halfway point pulled straight back — most of them. Fixed by projecting the flick: `glided = freq - (g.vx * GLIDE_MS) / cfg.px` picks the station to settle on, while the animation still starts from where the finger actually left, so a throw reads as one sweep. GLIDE_MS 180 puts a brisk flick two or three stations down. A slow drag has ~0 velocity and is unaffected, so fine tuning still works — do not "fix" this by loosening `px` instead, which trades away precision for the same effect.
- STORE LISTING FOR 1.3.0 2026-08-12: release notes written to `docs/launch/release-notes-1.3.0.md` from the ACTUAL diff since build 27 (44 commits), not from memory — your own station photo leads, then per-service playlists, faster transport and the honest progress bar, the Y2K card and the new colours. Everything invisible was left out; Apple shows about three lines before "more". SCREENSHOTS: the set was FULL at Apple's limit of 10, so adding meant replacing. `08-circulareq-tunnel` was dropped (it and the Equalizer slide both sold "a meter" — the most redundant pair) and a NEW slide put at POSITION 3, because Apple shows the first two or three in search results and nothing on the listing showed that the picture behind a drive can be the user's own. THE SHOT IS THE FRAMING SCREEN, deliberately: a finished mode with a photo behind it just looks like the built-in stations, whereas a viewfinder with your own photo in it can only mean one thing. Taken in the real web build with `choosePhoto` temporarily stubbed to return a bundled car photo, and with `canZoom` forced true so the caption reads "Drag to move, pinch to zoom" as an iPhone shows it rather than the web build's pan-only text — both stubs reverted immediately after. CAPTION FIXED: slide 9 was "A meter that moves with the music", which promised audio-reactivity the app cannot do (no beat data on either platform, settled 09.08); it is now "The meter from an old hi-fi", which describes what it IS. Tints re-sampled by scripts/marketing/tints.py after the swap, as they must be whenever a screenshot changes.
- STROFI: THE NAME'S MEANING, AND THE TRADEMARK REALITY 2026-08-12 (owner answered the brand doc's open questions). MEANING, from her: Strofi is from the Greek **στροφή** (strofí) → English *strophe* — a TURN or a BEND; in literature a structural section of a poem, and in its older sense a turn at a familiar landmark. Recorded in docs/brand.md §2 with why it fits (a bend in the road for a driving product; a section of a poem for products that divide listening into moods and journeys into routes; a turn at a landmark being how people actually give directions). Pronounced STRO-fee. THE TWO NAMESAKES SHE FOUND ARE ALMOST CERTAINLY NOT BLOCKERS, and the reasoning is the reusable part: a trade mark is registered PER CLASS and PER TERRITORY, not over a word. A Greek restaurant sits in **Class 43** (food and drink services); an app needs **Class 9** (downloadable software) and **42** (SaaS/development), possibly 41. Different class, different country, no realistic consumer confusion. A SONG is not a trade mark at all — titles are not ordinarily registrable and a recording is copyright, a different right. The search that actually matters is narrow: an existing mark in class 9 or 42, in the UK or EU, for something confusable — another app, a music service, a navigation product. REGISTERS (there is no single one): gov.uk/search-for-trademark, find-and-update.company-information.service.gov.uk, euipo.europa.eu eSearch plus, tmsearch.uspto.gov, obi.gr for Greece, and branddb.wipo.int (WIPO Global Brand Database) which covers many at once and is the place to start. UK FEES CHANGED: **£205 first class, £60 each additional from 1 April 2026** — the first increase since 1998, so older guidance is wrong; verified by search, not memory. FILE THE WORD, NOT A LOGO — a word mark protects the name however it is set, cannot be outdated by a redesign, and is why the logo can wait. DOMAIN: owner agreed to move off cruisefm.netlify.app, which is printed on every share card that leaves the app. Checked by DNS 12.08 — `strofi.com`, `cruisefm.com` and `cruisefm.co.uk` all RESOLVE (taken); `cruisefm.app`, `cruise.fm`, `strofi.app`, `strofi.tech`, `strofitechnologies.com` have no DNS (NOT proof of availability — confirm at a registrar). NOTE `.app` is HSTS-preloaded so it is https-only by design; `.fm` is priced as a novelty (~£70-150/yr). When it changes, `INSTALL_HOST` in ShareCardStyles.tsx and `INSTALL_URL` in ShareCard.tsx must move TOGETHER.
- THE OWNER IS IN AUSTRALIA, AND A DAY OF TRADEMARK ADVICE ASSUMED THE UK 2026-08-12: she asked "im in Australia, does the EU really affect me?" and the honest answer is that the advice had been wrong-footed from the start — `docs/brand.md` had the UK as home registry and UK fees as the headline, when the App Store record in this very file says **base price AUD 0**. The evidence was already here and was read past; £ pricing in old monetisation notes did the rest. CORRECTED IN THE DOC, as a correction rather than a quiet edit. What actually follows: the home registry is **IP Australia** (`search.ipaustralia.gov.au`, or its own free **TM Checker**), AUD **$250/class** standard or **$330/class** via TM Headstart, which puts an examiner on it before the money is spent. **THE AUSTRALIAN SEARCH HAS NOT BEEN RUN** — the EU, UK and worldwide searches say nothing whatever about who holds *Strofi* in Australia, so it is the gap in the set, not a formality. ON THE EU QUESTION ITSELF: a trade mark follows the USERS, not the desk — the app is on a global store and downloadable across Europe, so an EU right-holder could in principle object to the name there and ask Apple to pull it from EU storefronts.
