But no Class 9/42/41 mark turned up anywhere, so the proportionate answer is to file Australia now and let **Paris Convention priority** (any foreign filing within SIX MONTHS keeps the Australian date) plus the **Madrid Protocol** (one international application through IP Australia designating the EU/UK/US and 120-odd others) carry Europe later, once there is evidence about where the users actually are. The EUIPO SME Fund is EU-established-only, so it never applied. ALSO RECORDED, because it wasted a round: neither the EUIPO nor the UK IPO result page can be shared as a link — EUIPO keeps its search state after the `#`, which its own page reads only on load, and the UK IPO's results sit behind a form POST so `/page/Results` carries no query. Screenshots are the only reliable record of a search on either site.
- BRAND POLICY WRITTEN 2026-08-12 — `docs/brand.md`, at the owner's request ("i also want to create the Strofi Technologies app brand", then clarified: "not logo directions - the branding policy and important information"). Deliberately NO logo: the name is doing all the work, and getting the name right costs nothing while getting it wrong is expensive to undo. What it settles: how the name is written and what is never written (no abbreviations, no legal suffix that isn't real); THE AMBIGUITY RULE — the company and the future app share a word, so in user-facing copy "Strofi" alone means the APP and the company is always written out in full, and in anything legal or commercial both are named explicitly; the full list of live placements (§4) as the checklist if the name ever changes, with the reminder that the legal copy is GENERATED by scripts/gen-legal.mts and must never be hand-edited in website/; the App Store Developer line (§5) which reads JESSICA ARROYO and cannot be changed without an Organization account + D-U-N-S or an Apple-granted trading name; and a STATUS table (§6) stating plainly what is NOT true — no registered entity as far as this repo evidences, no trademark, no logo, no dedicated domain — with the warning that "Ltd"/"Inc" must not be written until an entity exists, which matters more than usual because the Terms already name Strofi Technologies as the party the user agrees with. Also records the name-clearance TEST (not "does anyone use this word" — which almost everything fails and which nearly threw the name away — but would anyone confuse us, are they in music/apps/automotive, could they block a trademark) and the rejected names so nobody re-proposes them. Four open questions only the owner can answer are listed at the end; the most actionable is a real domain, since cruisefm.netlify.app is printed on every share card.
- THE FONT WAS NEVER THE PROBLEM — THE TRACKING WAS 2026-08-11 (OTA): owner disliked the app's type and pointed at the share sheet's buttons as what she wanted ("they look nicer & bolder, and more spaced out") against the station names ("thinner and squashed"). FIRST FINDING, and it reframed the request: the app has NO custom font anywhere — `Fonts.sans/serif/rounded` are declared in theme.ts and used by ZERO files — so every screen already falls through to the platform's own face. The font she liked and the font she was tired of were the same font. What differed was measurable: station names were `fontSize 17, fontWeight '600', letterSpacing -0.3, color rgba(255,255,255,0.94)`, while the buttons are `fontWeight '700'` at full white with NO tightening. Negative tracking is literally squashing, so her word for it was exactly right. FIX BY RULE, NOT BY TASTE: large display text genuinely wants slight negative tracking and small text does not, so a script neutralised `letterSpacing` on every negative style whose own block declares a fontSize BELOW 28 — 38 styles across 28 files — and left the 6 large ones alone. Station rows additionally went 600 → 700 and to full white, matching the treatment she picked out. RULE for new styles: don't tighten anything under 28pt. NOTE the web build renders in a different face from iOS, so a screenshot here shows the weight and spacing change but not the true letterforms — judge the final look on the phone.
  CASSETTE, checked and largely cleared: owner thought it looked "slightly cut off" and hoped "animations aren't slacking off". Measured at 393x852, 852x393 and 375x667 — the shell fits with margins at all three, and the probe's first CLIPPED verdict was a FALSE POSITIVE: it grabbed the largest `<svg>` on the page, which is AmbientGlow's deliberately oversized haze, not the cassette. Her cut-off screenshot is a zoom crop. Performance re-measured across all eight modes after the day's work: 0ms of long tasks everywhere, 60fps on seven, Mirror Ball 43fps with 4.1% jank (it draws 3125 paths — the known cost, and the ROWS/COLS knob is the lever if it ever matters). The reels simply carry less visible detail in PORTRAIT because the shell is smaller there; nothing is failing to render.
- THE NOTIFICATION TOGGLES WERE DECORATIVE 2026-08-11 (OTA): owner — "notification permissions should come up when the person first installs the app. if not, it should be found in the settings page where the permissions can come up." The second half was a REAL BUG and the same fault as a clock running over silence: Settings > Notifications carried five toggles and NO way to grant permission, so without it every one of them saved a preference that could never produce a notification. There is now a PERMISSION section at the top of that page, and it states the real position rather than a hopeful one — `getPermissionState()` returns granted / askable / denied / unsupported, and the card offers "Turn on notifications" only while the one iOS prompt is still unspent; once spent it says so plainly and opens the phone's own Settings instead, because no in-app button can undo a denial. On a build without the module (web, anything before 1.2.0) the state is `unsupported` and the block does NOT render — offering a permission we cannot honour would be the dishonesty being fixed. VERIFIED both ways in the web build: absent on web, and the card renders correctly with the state forced.
  THE FIRST-INSTALL ASK WAS DECLINED, WITH THE REASONING ON RECORD in case it is revisited: iOS allows exactly ONE system prompt and a denial is close to permanent, so asking a stranger on launch — before they know what the app is — is the reliable way to lose notifications for ever. The spec the owner approved on 07.08 waits until the THIRD drive so the ask is earned. Offered as the middle ground if she still wants it sooner: move the earned ask from the third drive to the FIRST. The settings route above is the recoverable half and ships regardless.
- A SHEET MUST OUT-RANK THE PAGE IT COVERS 2026-08-11 (OTA): owner's screenshot showed the custom-station hero — the big icon and the MY STATION badge — floating ON TOP of the Choose-a-playlist sheet while she was pasting a link. In React Native a sibling that declares a `zIndex` paints above one that does not, WHATEVER the order in the tree, so `customHero` (zIndex 5) beat a sheet rendered later with no zIndex at all. Coming later is not enough. Fixed in PlaylistSheet's own backdrop (`zIndex: 60`, above the station page's highest layer at 30) rather than at the call site, so every screen that opens it is fixed at once.
- PULL-TO-DISMISS ONLY FROM THE TOP 2026-08-11 (OTA): owner — "when I select a station and scroll to the bottom and slightly scroll up, the whole card likes to come down, so then I have to be careful scrolling back down". SCROLLING BACK UP MEANS DRAGGING YOUR FINGER DOWN, which is the identical gesture to throwing the card away, and StationDetailModal's `dismissPan` claimed ANY downward drag over 8px wherever the page happened to be scrolled. FIX: the pull-down is offered only when the page is already at its top — a `scrollY` REF fed by the ScrollView's onScroll (a ref because the responder is built once and would otherwise read the first render's value for ever), checked in `onMoveShouldSetPanResponder`. Anywhere else the drag belongs to the list. RULE for any future sheet with a scrolling body: gate its dismiss on the scroll offset, or the two gestures are indistinguishable.
  THE PROBE LIED TWICE BEFORE IT WORKED, and both lies are the standing lesson: (1) the first version had NO CONTROL — it dragged at the bottom, saw the card not move, and reported success, when in fact the page had never scrolled at all and nothing was being tested; a control drag at the TOP (which must still dismiss, and does — 126px) is what makes the bottom result mean anything. (2) It scrolled by setting `scrollTop` on a DOM node from script, which moved the element but never reached RN Web's onScroll — so the component still believed it was at the top, and the instrumented run showed the responder being asked with `scrollY=0` while the DOM said 437. Scroll with the WHEEL, i.e. the way a person does. Verified after: control 126px at the top, 0px at scroll offset 228.
- FRAME YOUR OWN PHOTO 2026-08-11 (OTA — expo-image-manipulator is already in build 28, so the crop needs no binary): owner asked to let people frame a station photo. THE DECISION THAT SHAPED IT: the photo is used at THREE shapes — full-screen and very tall behind a drive, a wide hero on the station page, a short strip on the list row — and one crop cannot flatter all three, so it is framed for the BACKDROP (the one you look at for a whole session) and the other two centre-crop from that. The frame is the SCREEN's aspect, so it reads as a little picture of the phone. `pickStationPhoto` is split into `choosePhoto` (returns the raw asset) and `saveStationPhoto(stationId, src, crop)`, because the crop MUST come off the original — cropping the already-downscaled copy would throw away detail we still need.
  IT IS A SCROLL VIEW, NOT A GESTURE, and that is the round's real lesson. The first build hand-rolled pan+pinch with PanResponder like the modes do, and the browser measured it receiving GRANT, exactly ONE move, then TERMINATE — something in the tree takes the gesture away after a single frame, and `onPanResponderTerminationRequest: () => false` does not stop a native view doing that. The create sheet's ScrollView was the obvious suspect and was RULED OUT (the sheet is mounted after it closes, so it was never an ancestor) — a `scrollEnabled={!framing}` fix based on that wrong theory was written and then reverted. Rather than keep guessing at which view, and ship a gesture unverifiable without a device, the cropper is now built the way iOS builds its own: a ScrollView clipped to the frame, panned and pinched by the platform, with zero gesture code to fight over. react-native-gesture-handler IS installed (via react-navigation) and was considered and rejected — Reanimated demonstrably did not animate on the owner's device (02.08, the home EQ), so introducing that stack unverified is a bad trade.
  THREE BUGS THE HARNESS CAUGHT, all of which would have shipped: (1) STALE CLOSURE — `PanResponder.create` wrapped in `useRef` captured the FIRST render, where `photo` was still null and the image measured 1x1, so every clamp was computed against a one-pixel image and the first drag threw the photo off screen (same trap as a mode's stale `winH` after rotation); (2) RN's ScrollView only scrolls the axis it is TOLD about, and a cover fit overflows in exactly one — a vertical ScrollView sat still however wide its content was, so `horizontal={contentW > frameW}` picks the axis; (3) `off` must be SEEDED with the same centred contentOffset the view is given, because onScroll only fires once something moves — frame a photo, press Use immediately, and it cropped the top-left corner instead of the middle you were looking at. PINCH IS iOS-ONLY (RN ScrollView's zoomScale has no Android implementation); Android pans, and Android is not shipping yet.
  VERIFIED: 42 offline cases against the SHIPPED helpers (scratchpad, coverScale/cropFromScroll/clampCrop over six photo shapes including a panorama and a 1000x4000 — centred-when-untouched, frame aspect, one axis filled, clamped past both ends, 2x zoom halving the crop, and a zero zoomScale not dividing by zero), plus the whole journey driven in the real web build with the native modules temporarily stubbed (choosePhoto returning a generated test image, saveStationPhoto logging the crop). Health walk green.
- A PLAYLIST PER SERVICE, NOT PER STATION 2026-08-11 (OTA): the loose end left on 04.08 — "linked playlists are still stored in ONE slot per station, so switching platform loses the other side's choice. Per-platform storage is the real answer." Now done. Linking on Apple Music used to OVERWRITE the Spotify link and vice versa, so trying the other service once silently threw away every playlist the user had chosen, and the station page went on showing a link playback then refused. THE FIX LIVES IN THE STORE, NOT THE CALLERS: ~30 call sites across the eight modes, the station page and NowPlayingContext all go through getStationPlaylist/setStationPlaylist, and threading a platform argument through all of them is exactly how one gets missed — the same shape as the 04.08 `seekTo` sweep, where two modes kept importing the old function and scrub broke silently on Apple Music. The signatures are unchanged; `stationPlaylists.ts` reads the saved platform itself. A playlist is filed under the service its OWN URI belongs to (`platformOfUri`), not under whatever platform was selected at save time, so a pasted Spotify link lands in the Spotify slot even for a listener who never chose a service, and a stale platform setting cannot corrupt the file. MIGRATION is automatic and lossless: a v1 entry (`{uri,name}`) moves into the slot its uri implies on first read, so nobody loses what they linked before this shipped. CONSEQUENCES: the 04.08 mismatch workarounds are now redundant — `linkUsable` in StationDetailModal and `usableUri(linkedUri)` in ModeActionRow are gone, because the store can only return a playlist this platform can play; what survives is the USEFUL half, a line telling you the other service's choice is still saved ("'X' is saved for Spotify — pick an Apple Music one too"), or the page would read as though the earlier choice had been lost, which is precisely the old bug. `contextUri` still needs judging — that comes from the service, not from us. Deleting a custom station now clears BOTH services' links (`clearStationPlaylistAll`), alongside its photo. VERIFIED offline against a fake AsyncStorage (scratchpad/plt/t.mjs, 12 cases): v1 migration both ways, the overwrite that started this, uri-not-platform filing, unlink leaving the other alone, delete clearing both, a no-platform listener still reading the Spotify slot, and corrupt entries skipped rather than fatal. Health walk green.
- THE APP IS NOT ONLY USED IN CARS 2026-08-11 (owner, declining the post-drive recap): "id hold off from that: some people might even use the app casually in their room for fun -- like myself". SHE IS RIGHT, AND THE REASON GENERALISES. A recap makes a CLAIM — "your drive, 34 minutes, these songs" — and the app has no way to know a drive happened. That is the same fault as the clock running over silence and the scene animating over nothing, both fixed the same day: a feature stating something it cannot verify. The app already concedes the point — the "Are you driving?" honesty check exists precisely because it cannot tell — so building a feature whose entire premise is that assumption would undo the honesty. Do NOT re-pitch a drive recap as designed (drive-framed, driving vocabulary). If the retention idea is revived it has to be framed around the SESSION, which is the thing actually observed: a station, a mood, a stretch of listening, wherever the listener was.
  THE WIDER SIGNAL, worth more than the feature: the OWNER — the person most invested in this being a driving app — uses it stationary, for fun. That puts a question mark over the driving frame itself, which is load-bearing well beyond one feature: the App Store listing, `docs/notifications-copy.md` (whose lines are almost all drive-shaped, and whose phase-two weather idea assumes a journey), and `docs/strategy.md`'s Path B — "the drive is the product" — which is the argument for Strofi's front door being Cruise FM's driver audience. NOT a conclusion from one person; it is a question to ASK TESTERS ("where do you actually use it — car, room, both?"), which costs nothing and is the only way to answer it with no analytics (the privacy policy promises nothing leaves the phone, so there will never be usage data to read).
- THE SELECTED COLOUR WAS THE HARDEST ONE TO SEE 2026-08-11 (OTA): owner, off the Create-station sheet — "can you make the highlight more obvious when I select a station colour?" The old marker was `borderWidth: 2` ON the swatch plus `scale: 1.15`, and RN borders are INSET, so the ring was drawn inside the circle and ate into the very colour it was marking; at 2pt among 25 swatches it read as nothing. `ringOn()` flipped it to near-black on pale swatches, which on her screenshot (the cream one selected) made it fainter still. FIX, and the principle is reusable: the ring now sits OUTSIDE the swatch with a gap of sheet between them (`position:absolute, inset -4`), so it neither eats the colour nor has to compete with it — which means it can simply be WHITE on every swatch, pale ones included, because the gap it sits on is always the dark sheet. Inset -4 into the row's 10pt gap leaves 6pt of clearance from the neighbours. A CHECK MARK was added inside as well: with 25 swatches on screen a ring alone still makes the eye hunt, and the tick says "this one" at a glance. `ringOn` survives as the TICK's colour (dark on Pearl/Ice/Sage, white elsewhere) and its doc comment was rewritten to say so, since it no longer has anything to do with the ring. The `scale` bump is gone — with a ring and a tick it was doing nothing but risking overlap. Verified in the real web build by clicking a pale swatch and a dark one and screenshotting each.
- SHARE CARDS — A Y2K WINDOWS STYLE, PROTOTYPE 2026-08-11 (OTA, opt-in chip; owner's idea with two reference images: "this is a silly idea but I was so intrigued... having a Y2K windows style type... I wouldn't show the mood station or music mode for these — maybe keep the album and just write the stations and the mode as a text"). NOT silly, and her brief is what makes it work: every other card LEADS with the mode's artwork, so a third variation on that theme would have been a recolour. This one leads with CHROME and demotes the station and the mode to text in dialog fields — which is how a nineties player would genuinely have shown them — so it needs no ModeHero, no station photograph and no scrim, and it cannot look like the ticket in different colours. It is also the style that suits the SVG-only rule BEST rather than fighting it: period chrome is 1px light and dark edges with no gradients, no blur and no shadows, so every bevel is four rectangles (`Bevel`, raised or sunken by swapping the lit and shaded edges — that inversion is the whole visual language). LAYOUT RULE LEARNED ON THE FIRST RENDER: the window must be sized from its CONTENTS and then centred, not stretched to the taskbar — the first cut filled the card and left a dead grey band under the scrub bar, and a dialog that ends where its controls end is most of what makes it read as a real window. Same render also caught the second transport row overlapping the Artist field (the right panel's stack is 414 tall against a 400 album, so the fields must clear the taller of the two, not the album). COLOUR: the station's hue survives ONLY as the desktop wallpaper (mixed 0.48 toward a period grey-lilac) and the title bar — no artwork, per the brief — and `inkOn()` flips the title text to black on pale stations; a station above 0.74 luminance additionally has its title BAR deepened 0.42 toward slate, or Mountain Pass (three whites) draws a bar indistinguishable from the window face. The address lives in the taskbar tray, which is the one place it looks native rather than stuck on. Registered as the third chip with DEFAULT_SHARE_STYLE deliberately left on `ticket`. `cardHeightFor` returns the plain format height for it — it never draws a capture, so it has no snapshot-derived shape. THE ONE UPGRADE IT WANTS: a real pixel typeface. It currently sets everything in `monospace` (Courier on iOS), which carries the shape but not the era; a Win95-alike TTF would ship as a font asset, which is OTA-safe and fingerprint-neutral — the DSEG fonts did exactly that on 28.07 — so it needs no build. Rendered offline across Night Run, Sunset and Mountain Pass (scratchpad/share/render.js) and the health walk is green with three chips.
  ROUND 2 (owner: "push the 7 seg display font" + more detail on the deck and the CD): the counter is now a real seven-segment readout — DSEG7 via `useDsegFonts()`, which the app already loads for the station dials, so no new asset and no build — sitting in a sunken olive display well with UNLIT ghost segments behind (`ghostOf` replaces every digit with an 8; that layer is the strongest cue that a readout is a display rather than printed text, and it is the same trick the stations page uses). Only digits and a colon go through DSEG7, never letters — the 31.07 lesson stands, seven segments genuinely cannot draw an M. The DISC is twelve overlapping arcs of a champagne palette (`CD_SHEEN`) across the data area rather than a rainbow: drawn apart they read as a pie chart, which is the same failure the CD mode's diffraction fan hit, and the palette is FIXED rather than the station's, because this is hardware and material carries no mood. The TAPE DECK gained a lit top face and a shaded right face (one small parallelogram each), which is most of why it now reads as an object instead of a rectangle, plus a two-tone power lamp, a sunken cassette window with both hubs, nine three-tone transport keys and the printed lines every such component carried.
  TWO HARNESS BUGS FOUND DOING IT, and the first invalidates earlier renders: `render.js`'s ATTR map had NO `fontFamily` entry, so every `fontFamily` on every card was emitted as a literal `fontFamily=` attribute and silently ignored — meaning all the share-card renders before 11.08 were drawn in the browser's default sans no matter what the component asked for. Second, the `@font-face` block lives inside a TEMPLATE LITERAL, so `' + FONT7 + '` string concatenation landed in the output as literal text; it needs `${FONT7}`, and the ttf must be embedded as base64 because Chromium refuses `@font-face` over `file://`. Check `grep -o 'font-family="[^"]*"'` on the generated html before trusting any type in a render.
  ROUND 3 — THE FONT, SETTLED BY RENDERING IT 2026-08-11: the owner first asked for the whole card in the seven-segment face, then corrected herself with a screenshot of Word 1.x — "instead of 7 seg font, change it to old Microsoft Windows font, I think a suitable style is MS Sans Serif (Helv)". She was right, and the render proves why segments could never have worked: DSEG7 has no diagonals and no vertical centre bar, so "Metro Boomin, Coi Leray" came out **"NEtroboon in,co iLErAY"**; DSEG14 manages capitals but nothing else, and a whole dialog in capitals is not what Windows looked like. Segment faces stay on numbers, where the station dials use them. MS Sans Serif itself is Microsoft's and cannot be shipped, so six OFL pixel faces were downloaded and rendered side by side with the card's REAL strings at the card's real sizes (Silkscreen — forces capitals; Pixelify Sans — too rounded; Jersey 10 — condensed; Micro 5 — too small; Workbench — deliberately distorted; DotGothic16 — closest). Shipped: **DotGothic16, OFL, subset to Latin with fonttools (1.9 MB -> 37 KB)**, at `assets/fonts/DotGothic16-Latin.ttf` with the licence beside it, loaded through `src/utils/pixelFont.ts`. Fonts are ASSETS, so this is OTA-safe and fingerprint-neutral — the DSEG route from 28.07. Verify a subset by rendering the full character set INCLUDING accents before trusting it (Café, Déjà, Beyoncé all needed U+00A0-00FF). The sunken LCD wells went with the segment idea — the times sit straight on the grey, as they do on the owner's own reference. ALSO: the card is now near-square (`Y2K_H = 1150`, not 1350) with the window CENTRED in the wallpaper rather than hung off the taskbar (owner: "cut off the top area to make it look more square and centralized") — a dialog wants a frame close to its own proportions, and 4:5 left a dead band of desktop above it. Blocks shrank to fit: art 400->360, fields 68->64, title 70->66.
- THE SCENE WAITS TOO 2026-08-11 (OTA) — the other half of the entry below, done after the owner confirmed it with a screen recording: "the moving animation misleads me, because the animation SHOULD produce music." THE CLIP, read frame by frame (imageio-ffmpeg at 4fps, then crop the elapsed readout and the transport button and diff): the TRUE position was 00:16 and never moved, while the deck's own clock climbed 01:41 -> 01:42 -> [00:16 x6 while paused] -> 01:44 -> 01:49 -> [00:16 x4], and the record turned throughout. The transport icon's dark-pixel share (0.182 pause vs 0.104 play) dates every state change exactly. NOTE THE FICTION RESUMED after each pause rather than resetting — because the run-ahead is `Date.now() - syncedAt` added to the last known position, so against a service that never started, the error grows without bound; 88 seconds in it was a minute and a half out. NEW `src/utils/confirmedPlaying.ts` — one rule, `playing && !switching && (track?.isPlaying ?? true)`, which every mode already spelled out inline for AmbientGlow's beat and which is now the single place it lives. Applied to each mode's PRINCIPAL scene gate (Equalizer's meter + glow, Cassette's reels, Vinyl's spin/tonearm/shimmer/glow, Horizon's rolling grid and stars, Circular EQ's ring, Tuner's ON AIR lamp, CD's lights, the Mirror Ball's), plus all 11 AmbientGlow `active` props and all 6 FloatingNotes. THE TRANSPORT KEEPS `playing` — a button that hesitates reads as broken, and that split is the whole design. ALSO FOUND: Vinyl and Cassette carry their OWN copies of the clock and each had the identical run-ahead (`t.progressMs + (playing ? Date.now() - t.syncedAt : 0)`); both now gate on the confirmed value, and both effects gained `spotify.track?.isPlaying` in their dependency lists or a held clock waits for the next position change instead of starting on confirmation. THE DEFAULT IS LOAD-BEARING: only an explicit `false` holds anything, so a listener with NO music connection has no track, no verdict, and unchanged visuals — companion mode is the whole app for anyone outside Spotify's 5-user allowlist and must never wait for an answer that cannot come. Verified with scratchpad/store/{scenehold,companion}.mjs: over a service reporting silence, 7 of 8 modes measure EXACTLY 0.00 mean pixel change and run at 3.9-14.4 once confirmed; the Tuner's residual 1.84 is its dot-matrix song title scrolling, which is a long title in a narrow window rather than a playback claim. Companion mode (no track) measures 5.6-13.0, i.e. untouched. TWO VACUOUS PROBES ON THE WAY, both worth remembering: the first diffed raw PNG BYTES and reported ~85 for every mode in both states (two similar images compress to entirely different byte streams — decode to pixels); the second measured the mode's ENTRY animation as though it were playback, and flipped a global without re-rendering React, so the 'confirmed' phase measured a still-held scene. Settle 14s first, and toggle the transport twice to force the tree to read the new verdict.
- THE APP CLAIMED TO BE PLAYING BEFORE IT KNEW 2026-08-11 (OTA), owner: "the user tries to press play and a short pause and the scrub and animations move ahead, the person gets confused and then pauses, which the scrub then moves back to the original spot. Then the user goes to Spotify and plays from there and then everything's okay." She called it a Spotify issue; the waking is, but the CONFUSION is ours and it is a design choice we made. `togglePlay` does `setPlaying(true); spotify.play()` — the shared flag flips before the service has been asked, let alone answered. That is RIGHT for the button (a control that waits reads as broken) and WRONG for the clock, which makes a falsifiable claim about where in the song we are: over a sleeping Spotify the bar ran on over silence, and the next poll — reporting the position never moved — snapped it back. The app was wrong twice, and the listener's own fix was to leave the app entirely. FIX, in `useTrackClock` only: the clock now starts on the SERVICE'S verdict (`track.isPlaying`), not on our optimism. Ordinary case, the chase poll answers in ~300ms and nobody sees anything; asleep case, the bar simply never moves, so there is nothing to rewind. `track.isPlaying !== false` is deliberate — only an explicit false holds it, so a platform that never sets the field behaves as before. `track?.isPlaying` had to be ADDED to the effect's dependency list or a held clock would wait for the next position change instead of starting the moment play is confirmed. VERIFIED end to end in the web build with a stubbed track whose isPlaying is controlled from the test (scratchpad/store/heldclock.mjs): scaleX frozen at 0.150085 across 3.2s while the service reports silence, then 0.156564 -> 0.166538 over 2s once it confirms. TWO VACUOUS RESULTS ON THE WAY, both the same lesson: the first selector required width > 60 and the fill at 15% is only ~50px wide, so it matched nothing and BOTH phases returned 'none' — which reads as a pass; the second matched an Equalizer LAMP (also a white 6px box) and reported a vertical transform. The seek fill's signature is scaleY exactly 1 and no Y shift — filter on that, and run this test in a mode with no white bars of its own. SECOND HALF: every notice in this family ends with the same errand, so the notice card now carries an "Open Spotify" button (`WAKEABLE_NOTICES` in NowPlayingContext, matched by message so a new notice cannot silently inherit a button that doesn't fit — premium-required and no-playlist are deliberately excluded); it calls the existing `returnToSpotify`, which already opens the station's own playlist. NOT DONE, and the remaining half of her report: the SCENE still starts optimistically, so a mode animates for the ~300ms before confirmation and for the whole time a sleeping Spotify never answers. Holding that too means threading a 'starting' state through all eight modes' transports; worth doing if she still reports confusion once the clock stops lying.
- FULL PERFORMANCE SWEEP 2026-08-11 (OTA), owner: "run a full sweep on the app's performance". FOUR FINDINGS, all measured before being touched, and the biggest one had nothing to do with the mode it showed up in. INSTRUMENT: scratchpad/store/perf.mjs opens each of the eight modes in the real web build and watches 12s — total time in tasks over 50ms, worst task, fps, share of frames arriving >32ms late, DOM nodes, path count. Absolute numbers mean little off a phone; the RANKING and the before/after do. It also asserts the node count is above the Modes page's ~828, because the first run reported IDENTICAL numbers for all eight modes — the mood sheet has no "Start Drive" button (you tap a station row), so the probe had cheerfully measured the Modes page eight times. A probe that cannot tell success from failure is worse than none.
  (1) MIRROR BALL WAS FREEZING THE THREAD ONCE A SECOND, and the tell was the TIMING: the long tasks landed 1007, 998, 2008, 1998, 991, 1011, 997, 1004, 1006 ms apart — that is the clock, not the animation. Each was ~55ms of the elapsed-time readout ticking over and re-rendering the whole scene, all 3125 SVG paths. `FlipbookGrid` lays out ~1500 `<Path>` elements and was NOT memoized, so it re-ran whenever its parent did. Now `memo`, along with the nine scene layers (ColourReflections, SpotlightPan, MirrorBall, GlitterField, Fireflies, LightRays, DustField, Vignette, BallBloom) — all take numbers, strings and ref-stable Animated.Values, so shallow compare is exactly right.
  (2) THE ROOT CAUSE WAS IN customStations.ts, AND IT HIT THE OWNER FOUR TIMES HARDER THAN THE TEST DID. Every mode calls `resolveAnyStation(id)` DURING RENDER, and for a CUSTOM station that built a fresh Station object with a fresh `eqColors` ramp every single time (a saved custom station stores no eqColors, so `customToStation` derives one). So every render handed every child new props by identity and every `useMemo` keyed on them missed — the ball was rebuilding its entire flipbook, six frames of sphere projection, from scratch. MEASURED, same mode, 12s: built-in station 587ms blocked / worst 61ms; custom station 2394ms / worst 243ms. A quarter-second freeze once a second, on the kind of station she actually drives. Fixed with a WeakMap keyed on the source object — no invalidation needed, since saving or reloading replaces the objects. AFTER: 0ms on both. LESSON: when a mode looks slow, check what its props' IDENTITY does across renders before optimising the mode.
  (3) VINYL RE-RENDERED 60 TIMES A SECOND. Its `progress.addListener` called `setCurrentTimeMs` unguarded, so the whole deck — record, grooves, tonearm, fireflies, backdrop — re-rendered every animation frame to move a readout that changes once a second. Every other clock already had the guard (useTrackClock's carries a comment saying exactly why; Cassette has its own copy); Vinyl predates the shared clock and never got it. MEASURED with a temporary render counter: 59.6/sec before, 2.0/sec after.
  (4) THE PROGRESS BARS ANIMATED `width`, which is a LAYOUT property and therefore can never leave the JS thread — every frame of every song in every mode crossed into JavaScript and re-laid-out to move a white bar. Now `scaleX` + `translateX`, both native-driver properties: the bar is drawn full width and squashed, and since RN scales about the CENTRE and has no transform-origin, a paired translateX of -W(1-p)/2 pins the left edge (exactly linear in p, so one interpolation does it). scaleX needs a 0.0001 floor — zero is a degenerate matrix. THE DOT MUST SIT OUTSIDE THE SCALED VIEW or it is squashed into an ellipse. Verified by reading the live transform back: matrix(0.5283,0,0,1,-79.49,0) on a 337-wide bar, i.e. the shift is W(1-p)/2 to two decimal places, left edge pinned at the container's padding. Same treatment in SeekBar (all 8 modes) and Vinyl's own ScrubProgressBar.
  STILL OPEN, and NOT a JS problem: the Mirror Ball holds ~42fps in the browser with zero long tasks, so what remains is drawing 3125 paths across six stacked layers, not React. On a phone that is react-native-svg's native drawing rather than Chromium's, so it may not reproduce — check a device before spending anything on it. If it does, ROWS/COLS in MirrorBallFlipbook is the knob.
- THE BLUR CORRECTION WAS UPSIDE DOWN 2026-08-11 (OTA): third round on user-photo blur (owner: "reduce the blur of the selected photos much more", with her source image beside the app's rendering). `StationBackdrop` softened a user's photo with `blurRadius * 3`, on the reasoning that the 540-wide companion file is half a built-in's 1080 so the same radius reads as half the softening. THAT IS BACKWARDS: the blur is applied at the file's OWN size and the result is then ENLARGED to fill the screen, so the enlargement scales the blur up with everything else — a small source needs LESS radius, not more. Being wrong in the wrong direction made it out by roughly the square of the factor. FIXED: `blurRadius / 3`, which is literally the correction the old line attempted. MEASURED, and the measurement is the reusable part: take a built-in station's shipped `assets/stations/blur/*` backdrop as 1.0 (the softness the app has always had and nobody has complained about) and report any candidate as a multiple of its mean |∂x| edge energy after fitting both to a 1170px screen. A user's photo was landing at 0.22-0.60 — two to five times SOFTER than the built-in beside it; at /3 it lands near 2x, so it is now deliberately the sharpest backdrop in the app. Harness: scratchpad/photoblur/{measure.py,sweep.py,compare.py}. TWO THINGS THE MEASUREMENT KILLED, so don't chase them: the 540 SOFT_W is NOT the limiting factor — at radius 0 it retains ~4x a built-in's detail, i.e. more than enough headroom for any future round — and raising it would only affect photos picked afterwards, so old and new stations would stop matching. The radius is the one number to move. Crop a TEXTURED region when comparing (the 10.08 lesson: sky looks identical at every setting and teaches nothing).
- THE APP WAS PRINTING AN ADDRESS THAT DOES NOT EXIST 2026-08-11 (OTA): `cruisefm.app` was never registered, and the share cards print it as their FOOTER — on the snapshot style it is the only route a recipient has to the app, so every card shared since 26.07 sent people nowhere. Switched to the live site, `cruisefm.netlify.app`, in all three places: `INSTALL_HOST` in ShareCardStyles (what the card draws), `INSTALL_URL` in ShareCard (what the share text carries) and the Refer a Friend message in SettingsSheet. The two constants must move together and both say so in a comment. LAYOUT CHECKED BY MEASURING, not by counting characters — the address went 12 chars to 20, the snapshot footer is centred with letterSpacing 4 and the ticket's is right-anchored, so neither width is guessable: rendered the real components offline (scratchpad/share/render.js) and read `getBBox()` off the actual `<text>` nodes. Snapshot 373 wide with 353 clear either side; ticket ends 89 short of the card edge with 401 clear of the artist name beside it, and that gap has a floor because the artist is `clip(d.artist, 30)`. Harnesses kept as hostfit.mjs / hostrow.mjs — reuse them for any change to text that shares a row. Move the domain back the day the short one exists and points here.
- THE SCRUB SPRANG BACK, AND THE POLL WAS DOING IT 2026-08-10 (OTA): owner sent a 2.5s screen recording — Horizon, "Starboy" — saying the scrub "wouldn't move the music". IT WAS NOT UNRESPONSIVE, and the frames settle it: extract at 12fps (the imageio-ffmpeg binary, as on 25.07) and track the bar's white dot by thresholding its row. The dot follows the finger perfectly to x≈27, then JUMPS BACK to 43 on release, and again on a second attempt. So the gesture and the seek were fine; something overwrote them. CAUSE: `useTrackClock`'s re-sync effect depends on `track.progressMs`/`syncedAt`, so it runs on every poll — and Spotify is eventually consistent, so a poll shortly after a seek still reports where the song WAS. That stale reading called `startFrom(base)` and yanked the bar to the pre-seek position. Pre-existing, but 10.08's post-command chase (refreshes at 220/600/1300/2400/3600ms) made it far more likely to land inside the window. FIX: the clock now knows about scrubs. While a finger is down the poll may not touch it at all (`scrubbingRef`), and after a seek incoming readings are ignored until one AGREES with the target (within 2.5s) or a 6s guard expires — the guard matters, or a seek that genuinely failed would freeze the bar forever. Replayed the recording's exact sequence offline against both rules, and drove a real drag in the web build. LESSON, the same one as the mirror ball: for "is it responding?" questions, extract the frames and measure the thing that moved — the answer here was the opposite of the complaint.
